Claims corrected in this plan without a code change (findings 12 to 18 and the contested findings E, F, G, I): rows A3, A4, A5, A6 and A8 of section 4; 5.10's atomicity and confinement sentences; A-P6 in 5.15 and row P-22; the assumption counts of sections 1, 4 and 9.1; the Kani registers of 6.2 and 6.3; the premise of the run-level lemmas in 5.1; R18.1's attribution in 11.11; and two (not three) mutation kills by a solver resource limit. `hard_fault`'s HFSR read is in the register list of UJ-001 since the same date (finding 18).

**The review of the fixes (2026-11-13) and `arch_matches`.** The witness of lead 2.1 shows only the logic ending two Jobs; it does not show that Kernel_Arch then unwinds both. The property under lead 2.1, finding 6 and the INVPC defect of 5.8a is one: Kernel_Arch's level records must correspond to the Kernel's preemption stack. Its statement is `entry::arch_matches` (every Job in progress has its active record, for its Task and naming the Task below it; every active record is such a record). What is proven: in the logic, that every transition but a dispatch leaves the stack a prefix of what it was (`Kernel::stack_prefix_of` on all 35 mutating functions, no resource limit raised), and through `KernelOps` (`spec_depth`, `spec_stack`); in Kernel_Arch, the three steps at which the correspondence was broken: a dispatch puts its Job on the stack and writes that Job's record, active and naming the preempted Job (`irq_entry_core`); an owed unwind is consumed or kept, never replaced (`irq_entry_core`); an unwind stops only at the context of the Kernel's executing Job (`epilogue_core`, which also takes a record that disagrees with the stack for a Kernel fault). **The invariant (2026-11-13, later the same day).** `arch_matches` is now established as an invariant of the entry cores, in the form that also holds inside a Kernel entry: `entry::arch_inv(k, state, hop)`. `ArchState` carries a ghost chain (`chain`, `chain_levels`: the Tasks and levels of the Jobs dispatched and not yet unwound, in dispatch order). `chain_ok` states that the chain describes the level records exactly (every entry's record is active, of its Task, names the entry below as the preempted Job, has a non-zero exception number, and sits at the level of its Task's Priority; levels rise strictly along the chain; every active record is a chain entry) and that the Kernel's stack is a prefix of the chain. `arch_inv` adds that the chain is longer than the stack exactly while an unwind is planned (`hop`, the plan handed to the stub) or owed (`pending_unwind`, 5.8a), and that the planned or owed exception number is the topmost record's. Contracts: `boot_core` ensures `arch_inv(.., 0)` (it clears the records, the chain and the owed unwind itself, so nothing is assumed of the initializer); `start_core`, `svc_core`, `fault_core` and `irq_entry_core` require `arch_inv(.., 0)` and ensure `arch_inv` with their plan; `epilogue_core` requires `arch_inv` with the plan it is called for and ensures it with the next one; `timer_core` and `stack_violation_core` ensure `ended_plan`, from which `irq_entry_core` concludes. `lemma_arch_matches`: `arch_inv(.., 0)` with nothing owed implies `arch_matches`; `epilogue_core` ensures `arch_matches` directly when it names no further level. `KernelOps` gained what the proof needs of the logic: `spec_prio` (the Tasks' Priorities, unchanged by every operation), `lemma_stack_rising` (`Sched::inv`: Priorities rise strictly along the stack), `job_info`'s Priority, and the depth facts of `start`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock` (unchanged), `job_return` (one less) and a Kernel `fault` (unchanged); all proven for `Bound` from the logic's existing contracts, no logic contract changed. The uniqueness of a level's exception number is not needed: the level is its Task's Priority, and Priorities rise along the stack. Code changes the proof required, none a weakening: the unwind plan after a transition is decided by the stack's depth and names the topmost active record (`end_plan_core`, `plan::top_active`), where it named the level of the Job that was executing; `epilogue_core` unwinds the topmost active record and takes a plan naming another level for a Kernel fault, where it searched for the record by exception number, and clears `pending_unwind`; a dispatch decides everything that can refuse it (floor count, frame) before `Kernel::dispatch`, where a refusal after it left a Job in progress without a record on the way to the safe state, and refuses exception number 0. Mutations, each killed by a proof failure: `unwind-chain-condition-reverted` (assertion), `dispatch-record-drops-previous-task` (precondition of `lemma_push`), `unwind-leaves-record-active` (precondition of `lemma_pop`), `end-plan-ignores-ended-job` (postcondition `ended_plan`), `dispatch-starts-job-before-frame-check` (precondition of `lemma_kernel_entry`), `owed-unwind-not-recorded` (postcondition `arch_inv`). `owed-unwind-overwritten` is retired: with the plan naming the topmost record, the invariant gives that a second owed number equals the first, and Verus verifies that mutant (an equivalent mutant; the `consumed or kept` clause stands). No resource limit was raised for this proof (`chain_ok` is opaque and used through lemmas; `epilogue_core` keeps its limit of 80). **Still assumed, by inspection and test** (the stubs' half is proven since section 5.17)**:** the shells and the assembly stubs are outside Verus, so that each core is entered with the invariant the previous one left, and that a stub calls `epilogue_core` with exactly the plan it was handed and again with each next one until 0, are facts of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and the stubs (the HIL suites and the Kani harness below exercise them); the proof is of the build with the deferral of 5.8a (`WINDOW_DEFER`), not of the `defer-nofix` witness build. The Kani harness `arch_records_match_the_stack_over_kernel_entries` stays as a bounded cross-check of the executable: the executable form of `arch_matches` after each of five Kernel entries with their unwinds on the demo's configuration, two Budget events in one timer entry among them; it passes on the reworked cores (2026-11-13; with the chain condition of the lead 2.1 fix reverted it failed on the code before the invariant, `active == depth`). Finding 4's shell fix has its Kani case since the same review (`svc_clear_pending_clears_only_the_pending_bit`); the board's fault-injection run remains on the hardware list.

Standing after the audit, with their reasons: the facts of findings 12 to 14 are by inspection and test, not by contract (a contract with a mutant each would close them); FAULTMASK's clearing by an exception return has no Hardware_Model rule (finding 15); R9.1 and R10.1 do not list the exception of R9.9 (a Release whose deadline lies beyond the operating duration is not made; contested finding G), which matters for Release counts: the last Release inside the final deadline window of a run is suppressed; which handler runs first when an SVC's entry cannot stack (the SVC handler or MemManage, at one priority) is the hardware's, unmeasured on the nRF52840 (QEMU 9.2 takes MemManage first); the Kernel answers correctly in both orders.

### 5.9 Core_Subset items without Ferrocene certification

`profile/core_subset.toml` lists six items with `certification = "none"`. Only `core::arch::asm` (stubs, evidence section 6) and `core::panic::PanicInfo` (the panic handler, `location()` only) are used. `NonZeroU8`, `core::array::{from_fn, map}`, the atomics, and `core::hint::spin_loop` are not used by the Kernel crates, and some recorded substitutes do not match the code (for example, the event-log overflow counter is a plain saturating `u32`, not an atomic). Done 2026-10-22: the four unused items were removed from `profile/core_subset.toml` (19 items remain; `cargo xtask profile` regenerated `docs/profile.md`); an item returns with its evidence when first used.

### 5.10 Analyzer evidence beyond the proof (R32; task 15.4)

- **Property 17 as tested.** R32.2 asks that no computed WCRT decrease when a term worsens. The reported value of a Task found not schedulable is the last iterate before the deadline stop (R29.7), a lower bound that is not monotone in the inputs: from base 4 the iterates 4, 6, 8 stop at 8, and from base 5 the iterates 5, 7 stop at 7, with the same interferer (period 4, bound 2, deadline 6). The property therefore holds, and is tested, on fixed points (both results schedulable) and on verdicts (a worsening never makes an unschedulable set schedulable). This is a refinement of the property's wording, not a weakness of the analysis: the lower bound is reported as such (R30.1).
- **The cross-Partition clause of R29.2 is live.** The simulator produced a set in which a Task of a stop-on-overrun Partition overruns inside its Critical_Section and blocks a higher-Priority Task of another Partition for Budget + Δ_detect + Δ_enforce: the Ceiling blocks every lower Priority across Partitions although the Resource stays within one (PR-30). The Analyzer applies the clause; the test harness's own derivation first did not and was corrected. The demo has no instance (every lower non-accessor Task is below sensor's Priority).
- **Overruns and the simulator's claim.** With injected Overruns the bound is checked for the Tasks of Partitions in which no Job overran: R18.7 protects Partitions from one another, and a Partition's own Overrun inside a Critical_Section lengthens the blocking of its own higher Tasks beyond the site's WCET, which the analysis does not claim to cover.
- **Published sets (R32.7, PAR-04 = 3).** Burns and Wellings' response-time example (T = 50, 40, 30; C = 12, 10, 10; R = 10, 20, 52, the lowest Task missing its deadline); the Sha, Klein, and Goodenough three-task example (C/T = 20/100, 40/150, 100/350; completion times 20, 60, 240); the harmonic textbook set C = (1, 2, 3), T = (4, 6, 12) with R = (1, 3, 10), whose attribution (Buttazzo's response-time example) is to be confirmed at review (section 13). Each Budget equals the published execution time; blocking and overheads are zero.
- **Reference implementation (R32.4).** Written separately in the test file with u128 arithmetic and the recurrence evaluated as written; it agrees with the Analyzer on 500 random sets and the demo. It shares the author, not the code (section 8.5).
- **The term derivation proven (2026-11-06).** `rsk-analyzer::terms` is inside `verus!`: `derive_all` returns, for every Task, O_i as `spec_o` (the Table 13-1 sum of R29.3: four per-Job parameters, Δ_timer per timed event, Δ_lock_in + Δ_lock_out per Critical_Section site, Δ_hm, Δ_entry, the FP pair when the set has an FPU-using Task, Δ_irq + Δ_stamp for a Sporadic_Task), E_i as `spec_e` (R29.4 by the Partition's Overrun_Response, with L_i the longest site), B_i as `spec_b` (R29.2: the maximum of L_kernel, the stop and restart work of R14.10, and the candidates of every site of a lower Task on a Resource whose Ceiling reaches P_i, the cross-Partition clause included) with a site that attains it, and J_i = J_rel; `ticks_to_cycles_down` and `_up` are the rounding of R29.8; `derive_all` returns the overflow of R29.6 exactly when a term exceeds u64 (`prepare` then rejects instead of saturating, which the earlier code did). What stays outside the proof: the resolution of the Task_Model into the module's inputs (`inputs::prepare`: the Partition of each Task, the Resource of each site by name, the WCET_Record of each item, tested per rule), and the composition of `fixpoint` over the Tasks with the verdict of R29.5 (`response_times`, checked against the reference implementation). The three mutations of 9.2 each break one clause and are killed by the postcondition.
- **R29.9 on a scale.** `utilization_exceeds_one` is proven to compute Σ ⌈E_j · 10^9 / T_j⌉ > 10^9 in u128, and `lemma_scaled_term_over` that each scaled term is at or above the exact one, so a set with Σ E_j / T_j > 1 is always failed early; a set within 10^-9 per Task of full utilization can be failed early although the fixed point might have passed it. The step from the per-term inequality to the rational sum is proven since 2026-11-15: `utilization_over_one` states Σ E_j / T_j > 1 over the rationals as the inequality of the sum's numerator Σ E_j · Π_{k ≠ j} T_k and its denominator Π T_k (`util_num`, `util_den`), `lemma_scaled_sum_over` shows by induction that the scaled sum times the denominator is at or above 10^9 times the numerator, and `utilization_exceeds_one` ensures `utilization_over_one(loads) ==> r` (rsk-analyzer: 97 items verified, 0 errors). The converse does not hold and is not claimed (the pessimism above).
- **The intake terms of R19.2 (2026-11-06).** R19.2 asks for ⌈t / MIT⌉ · Δ_irq on every Task whose execution the handling can preempt, and the intake handler runs at the Kernel level, above every Task. The derivation had the cost in O_j of the Sporadic_Task only, so a Task above the source's Task (the demo's `control` above `echo` and `pulse`) carried none of it. `terms::terms_for` (proven to list `spec_terms`) now adds, for every lower-Priority Sporadic_Task j, the term ⌈w / MIT_j⌉ · Δ_irq with no jitter; for a Task of hep(i) the cost stays in E_j, whose ⌈(w + J_j) / T_j⌉ instances are at least ⌈w / T_j⌉. The report lists these terms as `(intake, R19.2)`, the reference implementation carries them, and the UPPAAL model records them as an approximation (R31.2). On the QEMU demo with placeholder timing `control` had been at exactly its deadline (B = `sensor`'s whole Budget), so the 300-cycle term failed it: `sensor`'s Budget in the QEMU declaration is 8.75 ms since (8.8 ms stays in the HIL configurations, whose binaries are unchanged); `control` has 650 cycles of slack.

### 5.17 The assembly entry and exit stubs (2026-11-13)

Until this date the stubs of `unsafe_module/stubs.rs` were justified by review (UJ-020 to UJ-028) and section 5.16 listed as assumed that a stub hands `epilogue_core` each plan it is given until the plan is 0. They are now proven against a machine model, `arch/cm4/stub_model.rs` (ghost code under `cfg(verus_keep_ghost)`; no build compiles it and PAR-01 does not count it).

**The model.** `M` is the processor state a stub touches: R0 to R12, the main stack pointer, LR, PSP, CONTROL, IPSR, PRIMASK, FAULTMASK, the Z flag, S16 to S31, and memory as a function of the address. Each instruction form the stubs use is one function on `M` (`i_*`, 32 forms; a conditional instruction of an `it` block is one function that tests Z). Each stub is the sequence of those functions, one per assembly line, split at its calls and branches (`irq_to_call`, `irq_after_call`, `irq_dispatch`, `irq_restore`, `irq_return`, `irq_to_unwind`; `unwind_entry`, `unwind_to_call`, `unwind_after_call`, `unwind_exit`, `unwind_again`; `svc_to_call`, `mm_to_call`, `fault_to_call`, `timer_to_call`, `kentry_after_call`, `kentry_return`). A call of a Rust half (`call`, `call_out4`, `call_ret`, `call_svc`) keeps what AAPCS leaves to the caller (R4 to R11, SP, S16 to S31, the memory at and above SP) and makes everything else arbitrary; its result enters as the verified core's contract, for which the cores now ensure named relations: `irq_rel`, `epi_rel`, `kentry_rel` (`entry.rs`).

**The tie to the assembly.** `cargo xtask check` (`checks::stub_model`, 5 unit tests) reads the `naked_asm!` block of every naked function and the model functions of its stub and requires that they are the same sequence, instruction by instruction and operand by operand, with the control lines (`bl`, `cbz`, `bne`, `b`, `bx`, labels) in place; it checks each `it` block against the conditions of the instructions under it; an assembly line of an unknown form and a naked function with no model are errors (`job_end_trampoline`, one `svc` instruction, is listed as not modelled). The assembly therefore cannot change without the check failing until the model, and with it the proof, is changed.

**The main stack.** `Frame` is what a dispatch stub leaves on the main stack for its level (R3 to R11 and LR; below them S16 to S31 when the preempted context had a floating-point context). `world(state, mem, base, frames, tpsp)` states that the register records of the chain's entries (5.16) lie on the main stack from `base` upward in dispatch order, that each record's shape and saved LR are those of the EXC_RETURN value its level record keeps, that every level but the first preempted a Job in Thread mode on the process stack with nothing between the two records, and that while a record exists the Thread-mode context is on the process stack.

**The theorems** (all in `stub_model.rs`, 13 lemmas and theorems; `cargo-verus` on rsk-kernel: 250 verified, 0 errors; no resource limit raised):

| Theorem | Statement |
|---|---|
| `theorem_irq_stub` | Entered with `world` and its Rust half answering by `irq_rel`: the stub calls with IPSR, PSP, CONTROL and its LR, under PRIMASK, with the out-pointer inside its own stack room. On a dispatch it installs the plan's PSP and CONTROL (`CONTROL_JOB`), returns with `EXC_RETURN_THREAD_PSP`, PRIMASK clear and FAULTMASK set, and `world` holds with the new level's register record on top and `arch_inv(.., 0)`. Without one it restores R3 to R11, LR, SP and S16 to S31; with no plan it returns as entered (PRIMASK clear, FAULTMASK set) with `world` and `arch_inv(.., 0)`; with a plan it reaches `rsk_unwind` with the plan in R0, PRIMASK held, the topmost record at the stack's top, and `arch_inv` with that plan. |
| `theorem_unwind` | From that state, for any number of rounds: each round calls `epilogue_core` with the plan the stub holds, which satisfies that core's `requires` (so the hypothesis of 5.16 is discharged, not assumed); pops exactly the register record of the level the core unwound (the pop's shape follows the EXC_RETURN value the core returns, which is the one the record was pushed under); goes round again exactly while the core names a next level. It leaves by an exception return with the EXC_RETURN value the last unwound level saved at its dispatch, the PSP and CONTROL of the context that level preempted, R3 to R11 and S16 to S31 as that dispatch pushed them, PRIMASK clear and FAULTMASK set. Then `pending_unwind == 0`, `arch_matches` holds, and `world` holds for the remaining levels. |
| `theorem_svc_stub`, `theorem_memmanage_stub`, `theorem_fault_stub`, `theorem_timer_stub` | The handler passes its arguments (the SVC: the caller's frame address by EXC_RETURN bit 2, and LR; the faults: `in_kernel` = PRIMASK set or taken from Handler mode, MemManage also the frame's address and LR), restores R4 and LR, and with no plan returns as entered with `world` and `arch_inv(.., 0)`, with a plan reaches `rsk_unwind` as above. |

Together with 5.16: from boot, every handler activation that returns leaves `arch_inv(.., 0)` and `world`, and an activation that ends Jobs returns into the context of the Kernel's executing Job with that context's registers. The entry-instruction window of 5.8a is covered by the same theorems: an activation entered from Handler mode plans neither a dispatch nor an unwind (`irq_rel`'s last clause, `fault_core`'s `in_kernel ==> r.0 == 0`), so its stub returns with every register, the stack pointer and `world` as it found them, which is the precondition of the preempted stub's own theorem.

**What the cores gained for this:** `irq_entry_core` ensures `irq_rel` (the records are untouched without a dispatch; a dispatch adds one chain entry whose record keeps the entry's EXC_RETURN and changes no other; an entry from Handler mode returns no frame and no plan); `epilogue_core` ensures `epi_rel` (the chain loses its last entry, no other record changes, and the PSP, CONTROL and EXC_RETURN returned are that record's); `svc_core`, `fault_core`, `timer_core` and `stack_violation_core` ensure `kentry_rel`; `fault_core` ensures `in_kernel ==> r.0 == 0`; `link_ok` bounds a record's exception number by 0x1FF, so `epilogue_core`'s mask leaves a plan as it is (`lemma_plan_number`).

**Findings of the proof.**

1. Fixed. A frame address of 0 from `plan_frame` would have been read by the stub as "no dispatch" (`cbz r0`) after `Kernel::dispatch` had started the Job. Nothing excluded it in the contracts (no stack is at address 0 on the Target). `irq_entry_core` now refuses a zero frame before the Kernel's dispatch, as it refuses an overflow.
2. Recorded, not changed. `rsk_timer_entry` (with `arch::timer` and `rsk_timer_rust`) is in no vector table: the nRF52840's time base is an interrupt line served by `rsk_irq_entry`. Unlike the fault stubs it has no Handler-mode guard and unlike `irq_entry_core` its shell does not turn a plan into an owed unwind, so bound to a vector it would, at a dispatch stub's entry instruction, unwind over the processor's own exception frame. `theorem_timer_stub` states this as a premise (a plan only for an entry from Thread mode). Either the stub is removed or it gets the guard before a Target binds it.
3. Recorded, not changed. A dispatched Job starts with R4 to R11 and S16 to S31 as the stub leaves them: R5 to R7, R9 to R11 and S16 to S31 hold the preempted Job's values, possibly another Partition's; R4 holds an address of the Kernel's main stack and R8 the EXC_RETURN value. No requirement of R15 to R17 forbids it (they bound memory access); it is an information flow between Partitions through registers, and clearing the registers on the dispatch path would close it at the cost of a few instructions per dispatch.

**Still assumed, by inspection** (the trust base of this proof): the meaning of each instruction function and the assembler; the exception model (`hw_entry`: an entry's LR describes the interrupted context and that context's frame is stacked on its own stack, HW-06; an exception return resumes the context its value names and unstacks its frame, and clears FAULTMASK, HW-07; handlers of one priority do not preempt each other, HW-01; an SVC is taken from Thread mode); that the compiled Rust halves follow AAPCS, write neither PRIMASK, FAULTMASK, PSP nor CONTROL, and write main-stack memory only in their own frames, through their out-pointer, and (the SVC handler) in the caller's exception frame; that the shells of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and the `extern "C"` functions of `rsk-entry` call the verified core and return its plan unchanged (safe Rust outside `verus!`, generic over the system crate and instrumented by features; the SVC shell's stale-SVC and stack-check paths return 0 or `stack_violation_core`'s plan); that 32-bit arithmetic does not wrap on the main stack pointer; and, as in 5.16, the proof is of the build with the deferral of 5.8a.

Mutations, each killed by a proof failure: `stub-unwind-pops-before-fp`, `stub-irq-exc-return-not-kept`, `stub-irq-out-pointer-offset` (the model; each would also fail the tie check), `epilogue-returns-other-exc-return`, `fault-in-kernel-not-kernel`, `handler-mode-entry-plans-unwind`, `dispatch-frame-zero` (the cores).

### 5.18 What sections 4, 5.16 and 5.17 left to inspection, now contracts (2026-11-13)

Each item below was "by inspection and test" in this plan until this date. `cargo-verus` on rsk-kernel: 272 verified, 0 errors; rsk-logic: 341 verified, 0 errors. No resource limit was raised. Seven functions are checked in solver instances of their own (`#[verifier::spinoff_prover]`: `apply_core`, `boot_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `epilogue_core`, `svc_core`, `endpoint_reinit`, `dma_request_core`): in the solver context shared with the functions before them their proofs passed or ran out of the limit depending on unrelated changes elsewhere in the file; alone each passes inside the limit it had. `irq_entry_core`'s dispatch arm, intake arm and unbound arm, `svc_core`'s slower services and `boot_core`'s vector loop are verified functions of their own for the same reason (`dispatch_vector_core`, `dispatch_job_core`, `intake_core`, `unbound_core`, `svc_service_core`, `boot_vectors`; inlined).

| Fact | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| The executing Partition and the loaded MPU view (A6; audit finding 12) | The loaded view's value in no postcondition | `arch_inv` states that a level record's Partition is its Task's, that the Partition it names as preempted is the Partition of the chain entry below, and that `ArchState::current_partition` is the last dispatched Job's or none. `view_matches`: while a Job is dispatched and not unwound the loaded view is that Partition's; required and ensured by `irq_entry_core` and `epilogue_core`, kept by every other core. `lemma_executing_partition`: with nothing owed, `current_partition` and the loaded view are those of the Kernel's executing Job, and `current_partition` is none exactly when no Job is in progress (which the stack checks of R16.5 rely on). Mutations `unwind-skips-view-restore`, `dispatch-records-other-partition`. |
| Vector priorities at boot (A3; finding 12) | `HwModel.priority` in no contract | `boot_core` ensures `boot_vector_ok` for every interrupt line: a dispatch vector at `encode_level` of its Task's Priority, an intake or Kernel line at the Kernel level, each enabled, an unbound line disabled. Mutation `boot-vector-priority-kernel-level`. |
| The pended vector (A4; finding 12) | `HwModel.pending` in no contract | `apply_core` ensures (`vectors_applied`) that a vector it pends is the dispatch vector of a Task the Kernel found eligible, and that it pends nothing else. That the selected Task's vector *is* pended is not stated (`select` has no functional specification, only its properties). Mutation `apply-pends-other-vector`. |
| The intake enables (A13) | Not proven: `Tables` stated no distinctness of vectors | `apply_core` ensures that every intake vector is enabled exactly when its source is unmasked and its Partition not stopped. Mutation `apply-enables-masked-source`. |
| The vector tables (A12, A13) | A Generator fact | The tables have specifications (`spec_binding_of`, `spec_dispatch_vector_of`, `spec_intake_vector_of`, `spec_irq_count_of`); `vectors_ok` checks at boot that each Task's dispatch vector is bound to its dispatch and its intake vector to its intake (a new boot check, `BOOT_FAIL_VECTORS`, bit 16), which gives `tables_wf` to every core: no two Tasks share a vector and no intake vector is a dispatch vector, by proof. What remains of the Generator: that the table functions are pure functions of their arguments (constants of the generated crate). Mutation `boot-skips-vector-check`. |
| The Endpoint copy's confinement (R42.2; finding 13) | `endpoint_args` ensured nothing of the pointer; the copies had no `requires` | `endpoint_args` ensures the buffer is word-aligned, of the payload's length, and inside the RAM interval of the calling role's Partition (`message_permitted_spec`), and that the executing Job is the role's Task; `endpoint_slot` that a slot lies inside the Endpoint storage (`storage_permitted`); the cores copy through `copy_in_checked` and `copy_out_checked` only, which require both. Mutations `endpoint-args-skips-message-permitted`, `endpoint-slot-outside-storage`. |
| A-P6 for a Partition's stop or restart (finding 14) | A standing assumption | `clear_channels` and `endpoint_reinit` ensure `reinit_done` for every Endpoint: with a role of the Partition its channels are empty, its delivery latches clear and the roles of its other Partitions told; otherwise its channels are untouched. Mutation `stop-reinit-skips-peer-flag`. |
| Two mutation kills by the solver's resource limit (5.16, contested finding I) | `lock-by-other-than-top`, `dispatch-below-ceiling` | `lemma_no_new_blocking_section` and `lemma_no_lower_dispatch_while_waiting` take the step relation from `lemma_step_rel` and no longer reveal the history's quantifiers; both mutants now fail a postcondition and nothing else. The full register run of this date showed two more that the audit had not counted, `mit-run-spacing-dropped` and `overrun-not-marked-reported`; each now has a refuted obligation in its output (`lemma_mit_spacing_over_run`'s postcondition, taken from `releases_spaced` alone; an assertion that the Overrun is marked reported, in `budget_event`), and for each the solver also runs out of its resource limit on another function of the mutated file (`lemma_history_push`; `apply_response`), which is the first error the run prints. Every mutant of the register is therefore rejected by a refuted obligation; two also show a resource-limit failure. |
| The shells' decisions (5.16, 5.17) | Unverified code of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and `rsk-entry` | Verified cores: `svc_of_ended_job_core` (an entry other than the SVC handler that ended a Job drops the pending SVCall; called last by the timer, intake, unbound, dispatch-vector and fault cores, which ensure it); `svc_check_core` (the SVC's stack check of R16.5: `None` only when the frame is on the main stack, no Job executes, or the frame lies in the executing Partition's stack region, with nothing changed; otherwise the stack violation's plan and the stacking fault discarded); the stale SVC is a branch of `svc_core`; `fault_frame_readable` guards the read of a faulting frame. A shell now fetches the Kernel, the hardware, the tables and the state, calls one core with the stub's arguments, records its instrumentation, enters the safe state when the core says so, and returns the core's plan: that remaining shape is by inspection. `KernelArch` gained `svc_clear_pending` and `stacking_fault_discard`, two trusted functions (proof_assumptions.toml: nine now) that were calls of unverified code before. Mutations `kernel-entry-keeps-pending-svc`, `svc-check-reads-any-frame`, `stack-violation-keeps-stacking-fault`, `fault-frame-read-after-stacking-failure`. |
| The entry-instruction window (5.8a, 5.17) | An argument in prose over the per-activation theorems | `stub_model.rs` states the exception model at a handler as functions (`exc_enter_handler`, HW-06: the frame stacked on the main stack, LR the Handler-mode EXC_RETURN; `exc_return_handler`, HW-07: R0 to R3, R12 and LR back from the frame, FAULTMASK cleared) and proves `theorem_nested_irq` and `theorem_nested_fault`: an interrupt vector or a fault taken at a handler's entry instruction, answered as the cores' contracts state for an entry from Handler mode, returns to that handler with every register, its stack pointers, S16 to S31 and the memory at and above its stack pointer as it left them, and with the main stack and the records in agreement for the new Kernel state: the precondition of the preempted stub's own theorem. The model functions themselves, and that handlers of one priority do not preempt each other, stay assumptions (5.17). |

Behaviour that changed: a boot with vector tables that disagree fails (bit 16); a dispatch refuses a frame address of 0 (5.17); `boot_core` clears the level records and `current_partition`. Nothing else: the functions split out of `irq_entry_core`, `svc_core` and `boot_core` are inlined.

Still by inspection after this section: the shells' remaining shape (above); the purity of the generated table functions; the model's instruction and exception semantics, AAPCS for the compiled Rust halves, and the assembler (5.17); the nine trusted functions of `Cm4Hw`; that `select`'s Task is the one whose vector is pended.

### 5.19 The Config_Checker's recomputation core (2026-11-13)

The Config_Checker (R26) recomputes the derived configuration values from the declared ones, independently of the Generator. Its arithmetic is now verified: `crates/rsk-confcheck/src/derive_v.rs`, inside `verus!`, verified by `cargo verus verify -p rsk-confcheck` in the verification job (19 items, 0 errors). The specifications are stated in the crate from the requirements; nothing of the Generator, the Kernel or their proofs is imported (R26.1: the crate depends on a JSON parser, the ELF reader, and since this date the Verus library).

| Value class (R26.2) | Function | Proven |
|---|---|---|
| Ceilings (R23.1) | `ceiling_of` | The maximum of the accessors' Priorities: at least each, equal to one of them, 0 for none |
| Priority encoding (DD-02) | `encode_level` | `(8 - p) * 32` for 1 to 8, 0 otherwise |
| Priority map | `levels_of` | The Priorities in use, each once, strictly ascending |
| Lock-stack capacity (R23.4) | `sum_checked` | The sum of the nesting depths; `None` exactly on overflow |
| Timer slots (R9.4) | `timer_slots`, `slot_index` | `3n + 2`; `3i + k`; `None` exactly on overflow |
| Regions (DD-07, R16) | `size_of`, `aligned`, `disjoint`, `stack_fits` | Two regions are reported disjoint exactly when no address lies in both; alignment and the stack's fit as defined; no overflow for any base and size |

Defects the proof removed (each a crash or a wrong answer of the checker on a malformed Task_Model, none reachable from a Task_Model the Generator emits): a Priority above 8 underflowed the encoding before its range was reported; a `size_log2` of 64 or more overflowed the shift; a base near the top of the address space overflowed a region's end. One check was added: no Partition's RAM lies over another Partition's code (R16.7); only code against code and RAM against RAM were compared.

Not verified: the reading of the Task_Model (`model.rs`, JSON by name), the decoding of the binary's tables and the CRC-32 (`image.rs`), and `recompute.rs` itself, which decides which values are compared and reports them; their evidence is the crate's tests, the run on every Flight_Build of the verification job, and the Config_Checker mutations of `docs/config_mutations.toml` (R26.7). The proof does not relate the checker's definitions to the Kernel's (`Config::wf`): R26.1 forbids the dependency, and the Kernel validates its tables at boot by its own verified check. Mutations `confcheck-ceiling-keeps-smaller`, `confcheck-disjoint-adjacent-overlap`, `confcheck-encode-level-shifted`.

### 5.20 The multiparty progress theorem in Lean, on the checker as compiled (2026-11-15)

**Status.** `accept_multiparty_progress` (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Proto/Exec.lean`): if `rsk-protocol`'s `multiparty::accept_multiparty`, as rustc compiles it without Verus and Aeneas translates it, returns `true` for a Protocol `g` of `n` roles, then no reachable configuration of the asynchronous product of the Protocol's projections has a waiting set (R41.3). `accept_multiparty_reception`: in every such configuration a role whose head is a receive and whose awaited channel is not empty accepts the channel's oldest label. Both are proven in Lean with no `sorry`; `#print axioms` gives Lean's `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. This is the theorem of 5.15 (Verus, `theorem_multiparty_progress` with `accept_multiparty`'s contract) proven a second time, in another prover, and on the code as compiled rather than on the Verus text of the checker.

**What is proven, in two parts.**

| Part | Files | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| The theorem over the syntax | `Proto/Spec.lean` (the statement: global and local types, `wfn`, the full merge, `projn`, `acceptn`, the semantics `NStep` and `NReach`, the waiting set `NDeadlocked`) and `Refine`, `Merge`, `Runtime`, `RProj`, `Distinct`, `Binders`, `ProjNormal`, `MHead`, `Rel`, `Queue`, `Steps`, `Effects`, `Exists`, `Across`, `Node`, `Invariant`, `Progress` (handwritten) | `multiparty_progress`: `acceptn g n → NReach g n c → ¬ NDeadlocked c n`; `multiparty_reception`. The argument is the Verus one, ported section by section (`crates/rsk-protocol/src/multiparty.rs`, "refinement" to "no waiting set"): runtime global types with messages in transit, the coinductive relation `Fits` between a role's type and the runtime type (a relation closed under `NodeOk`, seeded by "the head refines the head of the merged projection" and carried across a send and a receive), the invariant `MInv`, and the induction on the first-path depth of an awaiting member of a waiting set |
| The checker as compiled | `Proto/Proto.lean` (generated: the Aeneas translation of `accept_multiparty` and what it calls, 705 lines, 34 definitions, no `sorry`, no axiom) and `Proto/Exec.lean` (handwritten) | Whenever the extracted function returns: `merge_exec` is `merge`, `lfree_exec` is the free-variable test, `lshift_exec` the shift, `close_rec_exec` the recursion rule, `projn_exec` the projection, `wfn_exec` decides `wfnAt` (the two loops that build its guard environments by induction), and `acceptn_exec` returning `true` gives `acceptn` (`accept_multiparty_ok`). Each is partial correctness: nothing is claimed of a run that fails or returns `false` |

About 6,700 handwritten lines, 319 theorems.

**What had to change in the Rust for the translation (the Verus contracts are the same; rsk-protocol: 552 items verified, 0 errors).**

| Function | Change | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `wfn_exec`, `wfn_bs_exec` | The guard environment is read (`&Vec<bool>`), and a recursion checks its body under a copy with one more flag; it was pushed and popped in place (`&mut Vec<bool>`). The contract is `r == wfn_at(g@, n, env@)` as before, without the clause that the environment is restored | `Vec::pop` has no model in the Aeneas library (it is translated as an axiom), and the short-circuit condition of the communication case did not translate ("could not match the contexts") |
| `merge_ls_exec` | `match` in place of two `let ... else`; the branch of the other list is taken as a copy (`lse_lookup_copy`, new, with `lse_lookup`'s contract) | Aeneas fails on `let ... else` and on a match over the borrowed `Option<&LE>` |

**What the Lean statement is, against the Verus one.** The same definitions with these differences of form: roles, labels and variables are naturals (integers in Verus, with the bounds in the definitions); a configuration gives the roles' types and the channels as functions (`l : Nat → L`, `ch : Nat → Nat → List Nat`) where Verus indexes sequences (`ch[from * n + to]`); the guard environment of `wfn` is read innermost recursion first, the checker's and Verus's outermost first (`wfn_exec_ok` relates the two by reversal); the waiting set is a function to `Bool` over all naturals. The executable types are read through `vG` and `vL` (the machine integers' values).

**What it does not cover.** The parser and the conversion from the Protocol's text to `GE` (`parse_v`, `gconv`: Verus, 5.15); the flattening into the role tables and the theorem for the automaton (`flatten_n`, `theorem_multiparty_automaton`: Verus; three of its functions do not translate, early returns inside loops); the Kernel's channels being the model's (5.10, 5.15's assumptions A-P1 to A-P8); and the assumptions of 5.15 on how the Jobs use their roles. The translation is of the Verus-erased build: that erasure leaves the compiled function is the toolchain's.

**Mutants** (`docs/proof_mutations.toml`, `prover = "lean"`; 10, each run on 2026-11-15 through `cargo xtask mutations --only` and killed, `lake build` failing in the file named).

| Mutant | What it changes | Fails in |
|---|---|---|
| `lean-proto-code-accepts-a-role-without-projection` | The extracted acceptance loop returns `true` at a role with no projection | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-code-recursion-starts-guarded` | The extracted `wfn_exec` enters a recursion with its variable guarded | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-code-uninvolved-role-takes-the-first-branch` | The extracted `projn_merge_exec` returns the first branch's projection without the merge | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-code-merge-takes-the-other-branch` | The extracted `merge_ls_exec` takes the other list's branch of the same label without merging | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-code-close-rec-keeps-the-binder` | The extracted `close_rec_exec` keeps the numbering of a recursion whose variable does not occur | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-code-accepts-one-role` | The extracted `acceptn_exec` accepts one role | `Proto/Exec.lean` |
| `lean-proto-merge-of-anything` | The statement's merge accepts two unlike types | `Proto/Merge.lean` |
| `lean-proto-waiting-set-without-closure` | The waiting set need not hold the sender an awaiting member waits for | `Proto/Progress.lean` |
| `lean-proto-recursion-starts-guarded` | The statement's `wfnAt` enters a recursion with its variable guarded | `Proto/Runtime.lean` |
| `lean-proto-accept-projects-one-role` | The acceptance condition asks a projection of role 0 only | `Proto/Invariant.lean` |

## 6. Kani (R45.4, R45.6, R45.8; task 6.3)

### 6.1 Limitations and mitigations (R45.6)

| Limitation [RN-16, RN-17, RN-18] | Effect on rsk | Mitigation or TBR |
|---|---|---|
| Sequential code only; data races not detected | Kernel concurrency is preemption between levels | Single-core, non-reentrant precondition (A10, TBR-20); Loom model (section 6.4, R47, since 2026-10-24); review UJ-003 to UJ-006 |
| Inline assembly unsupported; global assembly ignored | All of `stubs.rs` and the asm in `hw.rs` | Stubs KS-01 to KS-07; review identifiers; HIL differential tests (6.2) |
| Harnesses build for the host, so the volatile access, raw write, or `asm!` behind `cfg(all(target_arch = "arm", target_os = "none"))` is compiled out | Since 2026-11-06 the register path has one leaf that differs between the builds, `hw::raw` (one volatile instruction per primitive; the stub KS-01 on the host): every address, value, and order the Kernel computes is code the Flight_Build and the harnesses share, and `Cm4Hw::apply`, the function the verified entry points call, is what `apply_every_action_is_total` runs for every `Action` with symbolic arguments. The precondition of UJ-001, UJ-002, and UJ-009 is stated once (`addr::kernel_access_ok`, `kernel_byte_access_ok`: a Kernel register of the PPB, an NVIC array word for a line below 496, or a word of the peripheral region) and the stub asserts it on every access, so a harness that reaches an address the Kernel may not touch fails. Found by the restructuring: `nvic_*` took `n.saturating_sub(16)` as the line, so an exception number beyond the arrays would have reached the SCB (`irq_of` now refuses it: nothing is written); `dma_program` admitted any word of the PPB (`plausible`), where no EasyDMA register is (`peripheral` now). Both replayed as mutants against the new harnesses (9.2) | The volatile instruction itself and the assembly stubs rest on review (TBR-06); the frame write (UJ-007) is checked through the stub's record of the words. Closed (task 6.3's first open half). |
| Aliasing-model violations, mutation of immutable data, and invalid values not detected unless enabled | `KernelCell`, `ResourceCell`, frame writes through raw pointers | Review (UJ-003 to UJ-006, UJ-050 to UJ-054, UJ-102 to UJ-104). Kani's optional checks evaluated 2026-10-23 (Kani 0.68.0) and enabled where they run (`proofs::kani_extra_checks`): rsk-logic passes all 7 harnesses with `-Z valid-value-checks`, `-Z uninit-checks`, and `--extra-pointer-checks`; rsk-kernel passes all 6 with `--extra-pointer-checks`, but `-Z valid-value-checks` crashes Kani's compiler on a `NonNull` pattern type (an internal compiler error in `check_values.rs`: upstream issue #4829, fixed by PR #4856 on 2026-09-30, after the pinned 0.68.0 release and in no release yet; `docs/upstream/kani-valid-value-checks-ice.md` holds the reproduction; the check returns to rsk-kernel with the next Kani pin, when the open issue #5056 on spurious invalid-value reports also needs watching) and `-Z uninit-checks` cannot check `[MaybeUninit<u32>; 12]` and `UnsafeCell<Option<u32>>` ("not supported": a tool limitation, so those two checks stay off for rsk-kernel and the review identifiers remain the evidence for its cells). Kani has no aliasing-model check; that remains review |
| Bounded verification | Logic harnesses at the demo's sizes (NT = 7, NR = 3, NP = 3, NL = 6, NS = 23, NE = 32) on the demo's Generated_Config, since 2026-11-06 | Verus covers every size; 6.3 |
| Results hold only if every unwinding assertion passes | none | R45.5 verdict rule; fixture `xtask/fixtures/kani-unwind` |
| Stubbing is unstable (`-Z stubbing`) | Not used: no `#[kani::stub]`; the `sim` double is selected by cfg | TBR-07; revisit if stubs are adopted |
| `--restrict-vtable` soundness issue | Not used; the Kernel has no trait objects | Note only; the job does not pass the flag |
| Harnesses restate the Verus postconditions, written by the same author | Not an independent specification | Independence gap (8.5, GAP-08) |

The run costs 23 s for rsk-logic and 120 s for rsk-kernel (the `apply` harness 82 s, the NVIC harness 28 s). The residual risk recorded in TBR-07 is now the behaviour of a larger Application than the demo, which the Verus proofs cover at every size; Kani is used here as a cross-check of the erased code. Both open halves of task 6.3 are closed.

### 6.4 Interleaving tests: Loom and Shuttle (R47; task 17.2, 2026-10-24)

The Host_Test_Harness is the crate `rsk-loom` (host only, never in a Flight_Build). Its model, `Machine`, runs the Kernel's portable logic (`rsk_logic::logic::kernel::Kernel`, the same source the Flight_Build compiles, here for the host; not a reimplementation, R47.2) and represents interrupt preemption by threads constrained to the Hardware_Model's rules: the time base's thread delivers compare matches (`Kernel::timer_event`, as `timer_core` does), a Release_Source's thread delivers intakes of a Sporadic_Task (`Kernel::intake`), and Thread mode's thread runs the executing Job's program (`svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, the return into `job_return`, as `svc_core` does). Each is one Kernel entry, atomic as on the Target (PRIMASK or BASEPRI at the Kernel level while the Kernel runs, A2; non-reentrant, A10), so the explorer interleaves whole entries. After every entry the model does what `apply_core` does (BASEPRI from `basepri_for`, the selected Task's vector pended) and what the NVIC does under HW-05: it takes the pending dispatch vector of the Task whose Priority exceeds the executing Job's and the System_Ceiling (`lemma_nvic_takes_iff_above_job_and_ceiling`) and runs the dispatch stub's entry (`eligible`, then `Kernel::dispatch`), repeating while a vector is takeable. Since the Kernel pends only the Task it selects (revised DD-01), the NVIC rule is a cross-check of `Kernel::select`, not an independent source of dispatches: the model asserts the two name the same Task.

Every explored execution is checked after every entry against the Verus postconditions restated in Rust (R47.2): `Sched::inv` (Invariants 1 to 4 of design.md: the stack of Jobs in progress strictly increasing in Priority, each started above the System_Ceiling of its instant, the lock stack held by Jobs in progress that may access its Resources and ordered by holder, Resources held beneath a Job with Ceilings at most its start Ceiling, the System_Ceiling the maximum Ceiling held), and per entry the observable clauses of each operation's `ensures`: a dispatched Task was eligible and is the SRP selection (R7.1 to R7.3), may access no Resource a preempted Job holds (R8.3), and is pushed as executing; a lock is taken exactly when the executing Job may access the Resource, does not hold it, and the lock stack has room, and raises the Ceiling to the Resource's (R8.1, R8.7, R8.10); an unlock restores the previous Ceiling (R8.2); a return ends the executing Job holding nothing (R7.5, R8.9); a killed Job is unwound at once as `end_plan_core` does; a timer event or an intake starts no Job except through a dispatch; a second intake within the MIT never duplicates a pending Release (R10.3); and once the arbitration settles no eligible Release is left pending (Property 1).

Configurations and runs (`cargo test -p rsk-loom`, part of the job's workspace tests): the small configuration (three Tasks, two Resources of Ceiling 5, two Partitions, one Sporadic_Task of Priority 7; Task programs that nest Critical_Sections and one that attempts a Resource it may not access) under Loom, exhaustively for the time base against Thread mode (two compare matches: the first Releases, then the second period falling anywhere in the Jobs' steps, Critical_Sections included; 1,386 executions, 0.2 s) and for a Release_Source against Thread mode (two intakes within one MIT; 5,373 executions, 0.7 s), and for the three sources together under Loom's preemption bound of 3 (65,339 executions, 13 s); the large configuration (five Tasks, three Resources of Ceilings 3, 6, 7, two Sporadic_Tasks, a Task accessing Resources of different Ceilings) under Shuttle, 20,000 random schedules of four compare matches and three intakes per Sporadic_Task (4 s). Each run asserts that its executions include preemptions (R7.1), Releases held back by a Ceiling (R8.3), and refused locks (R8.7), so a run that explored nothing of interest fails.

Time limit (R47.1): a Loom run must complete within 120 s on the development machine; the unbounded space of the three sources together does not (the first attempt, before the drivers were corrected to lock once per Kernel entry, ran 3.6 million executions in 10 minutes for one tick and two intakes), so the three-source run is bounded and the large configuration goes to Shuttle. Validation of the harness (as section 9.2 does for the proofs): two mutations of the Kernel logic replayed under it, `Sched::lock` leaving the System_Ceiling unchanged (caught by every run at the lock postcondition) and `Sched::select` ignoring the System_Ceiling (caught by the exhaustive time-base run and the bounded run at the selection check, "select chose an ineligible Task"); two mutations of the model's own NVIC rule (ignoring the executing Job's level; skipping the eligibility guard) were not caught, which records that under DD-01 the model's arbitration is downstream of `Kernel::select` and not load-bearing, as the first paragraph says. A finding of the same session: a driver that held the model's lock across a Job's whole program explored almost nothing (the events could land only while Thread mode was idle) and let the `select` mutation through; the drivers lock once per entry.

Limitations recorded (R47.3): Loom explores the interleavings of the model's synchronisation operations under the sequentially consistent semantics of its primitives, not every C11 behaviour, and here only the orderings of whole Kernel entries, which is the Target's granularity by A2 and A10; Loom's preemption bound makes the three-source run incomplete by construction; Shuttle is randomized and not sound (RN-37). The model abstracts the Kernel_Arch sequence inside an entry (BASEPRI written before the vector is pended, the exception frame, the MPU view), which Verus covers (`apply_core`, A1 to A11), and time: the time base advances to the next armed instant only. Every result is testing evidence. Versions: Loom 0.7.2 and Shuttle 0.9.8, vendored and pinned (Cargo.lock, R58.1) and recorded in the Build_Manifest's `toolchain.loom` and `toolchain.shuttle` since 2026-10-26.

## 7. Application prover (R46.1, R46.2)

| Criterion | Prusti | Creusot | Aeneas |
|---|---|---|---|
| Maintenance, release recency | Latest release 2024-02-28 on nightly-2023-09-15; describes itself as a prototype (RN-19) | Releases v0.7.0 (November 2025) to v0.13.0 (30 July 2026) (RN-20) | Active; a September 2026 report verifies 16.7 KLOC of Rust (RN-21) |
| Rust subset | Not re-evaluated (inactive) | Mutable borrows through prophecies, loops with invariants, closures, traits (RN-20); interior mutability and `unsafe` stay behind the `rsk` API with contracts | Safe subset; no `unsafe` or concurrency; some loop forms unsupported (RN-21) |
| `no_std` | to confirm | confirmed 2026-10-23: a `#![no_std]` crate with contracts and a loop invariant proves (toolchain/decision.toml `[creusot]`) | to confirm |
| Soundness argument, trusted base | Viper and its SMT back end | Translation to Coma and Why3, then SMT solvers (TBR-08) | Translation through Charon's LLBC, then the Lean or HOL4 kernel |
| Toolchain compatibility | Its 2023 nightly predates rustc 1.95.0, so rsk crates may not compile | Drives its own rustc: nightly-2026-08-03 as pinned (toolchain/decision.toml), newer than the Flight_Build's 1.95.0 and Verus's 1.98.1, so the proofs speak about the source under a third compiler (ORQ-08 applies, 9.3) | as Creusot |
| Panic and overflow freedom | yes | yes; overflow is checked by default (confirmed 2026-10-23: an unguarded `u32` addition fails its goal) | yes, through the translation |
| Loop bounds (R35.2) | limited | termination checking with variants (RN-20); in use since 2026-11-06, the variant's bound checked against the Loop_Bound_Annotation by the Profile_Lint | termination in Lean |
| Release conditions (R5.5), validation totality (R42.4) | not evaluated | pure functions with contracts and termination | possible for small pure functions |
| DO-330 and DO-333 prospects | none known | none known (ORQ-10); several Why3 solvers allow cross-solver confirmation | none known; kernel-checked proofs give the strongest soundness argument |

Designation: Creusot (ORQ-10, design.md). It is the only active candidate that covers the constructs Task bodies use, closures in particular. Aeneas was evaluated on one pure control law on 2026-10-26 (task 17.3, below): it stays an option for small pure computations that need a Lean-level proof, as substitute evidence where the App_Prover cannot express a construct (R46.5), not a second App_Prover. Pinned 2026-10-23 in toolchain/decision.toml `[creusot]`: Creusot 0.15.0-dev at commit 18f2d371e (a development build 131 commits after v0.14.0, not a release; the Build_Manifest's `toolchain.creusot` (version, pinned commit, rustc, Why3, why3find, solvers; since 2026-10-24) and each `proof-app-creusot` Evidence_Item record it, and the job refuses a Creusot whose version or rustc differs from the pin, R46.2), rustc nightly-2026-08-03, Why3 1.8.2+git, why3find 1.3.0+dev, alt-ergo 2.6.4, z3 5.1.0, cvc4 1.8, cvc5 1.4.1. The install was not reproduced from a release, which TBR-08 says. R46.5 entries (2026-10-23, `proofs/creusot`, verify step 10b'): `part0.control`, `part0.echo`, `part0.sensor` (level A) and `part2.sampler` (level B) are proven against the trusted contracts of the `rsk` API; `part2.filter` is `trusted` because Creusot's floating-point support is partial (RN-10); `part1` is level C and outside R46.3 (proven nonetheless since 2026-11-09, below). How a Partition reaches the prover is design.md "Application prover": the Generator's glue is excluded under `cfg(creusot)`, the API's contracts are trusted, and the proof workspace compiles the real source files. The probe that led there (the same day, on a copy of Partition 2): Creusot's nightly compiles the whole dependency chain (`rsk`, `rsk-kernel`, `rsk-logic`, `vstd`, the Generator's proc macro), and what stopped the translation was `static` items, rejected as an unsupported definition kind and, with `#[creusot::no_translate]`, failing const-evaluation of `ResourceCell::new`; so the glue is now emitted under `cfg(not(creusot))`, the demo's counters became declared per-Task state (PR-25), and the Profile_Lint flags a `ResourceCell` static in Application code (PR-05). Findings the first proofs forced on the Application code, each a real tightening: counters saturate (an unbounded `+= 1` is an overflow obligation nothing discharges), the counter's byte is `% 256` before `as u8` (Creusot checks every `as` for loss), `b"A"` literals became byte arrays (Creusot's backend has no byte strings yet), `&buf[..]` became an unsizing coercion (no contract for `Index<RangeFull>` on arrays), `now() - start` became `wrapping_sub` (the prover knows nothing of the time base's monotonicity), and the busy loop carries an invariant. One contract subtlety: the closure precondition is required only for values satisfying their type invariant (`inv(v) ==> f.precondition((v,))`), without which the array's length is unknown to the closure's index check. Logical models (the same day, later): `PerTask<T>` is viewed as its value and `Shared<T>` as the Resource's invariant, a predicate (design.md "Application prover"); the Resource invariant is declared in the App_Declaration (`invariant |v: u32| v@ <= 1` on the demo's `in_cs`), and the Generator emits, under `cfg(creusot)`, the logic functions, the initial-value check, and each Task's precondition. With them the proofs state functional properties: control, echo, and sampler advance their counters by one (saturating), control reads `in_cs` as 0 or 1, and sensor's two writes to `in_cs` preserve its invariant at each Critical_Section exit (R46.4). A negative check was run: with the invariant tightened to `v@ <= 0`, sensor's write of 1 fails exactly one goal and the other bodies still prove, so the obligation is real and local. Two findings: Creusot's `#[logic]` functions are opaque outside their module unless `#[logic(open)]`, which the generated ones are; and a closure without `#[ensures]` has the postcondition `true`, so the Critical_Sections whose effect the invariant depends on carry their own postconditions in the body. 2026-10-24: a Resource of a Partition-defined type, `part2.stats: crate::Stats` (`total`, `ok` transfers) with the relational invariant `s.ok@ <= s.total@`: `Stats::new` carries the postcondition that gives the initial-value check its facts, `Stats::record` (saturating) its contract, `sampler`'s Critical_Section preserves the invariant and `filter`'s read leaves the value unchanged (`^s == *s`); `filter`'s body is now proven with only the f32 step `lowpass` trusted. Negative check: with the invariant `s.ok@ == s.total@`, the initial value still satisfies it and exactly sampler's write fails. Finding: `#[derive(PartialEq)]` on a Resource type needs Creusot's `DeepModel`; the type does without it (logical equality is structural). The Build_Manifest records the App_Prover since the same day (`toolchain.creusot`), and the job refuses an installation whose version or rustc differs from the pin (R46.2). 2026-11-08 (task 21.6, A-P8; 5.15): the trusted contracts gain `rsk::Gate` (`closed` gives a closed gate, `open` an open one, `is_open` reads it; the model is opaque and the field private, so that a body cannot forge an open gate), the gating Endpoint's receive outcomes (`RecvS*`) implementing `rsk::Received`; and the job runs a second proof workspace, `proofs/creusot-negative`, whose members must FAIL (a fixture body operating on a gated role without the opening receive), the step erroring if one of them proves.

**Loop bounds (R35.2; 2026-11-06).** Every loop of the level A and B bodies carries, beside its invariant, a Creusot `variant` of the form `<bound> - <count>`, where `<bound>` is the loop's Loop_Bound_Annotation as the Profile_Lint derives or reads it (`10_000_000 - i@` on `sensor`'s Critical_Section loop, the same shape on the spin loops of the fault-injection and timing features): the invariant `count <= bound` keeps the variant non-negative, the body's single increment makes it decrease, and the prover's "loop variant" obligation then states that the loop runs at most `bound` times. The Profile_Lint ties the two: for every loop that carries a variant it compares the variant's bound with the loop's annotation, textually (underscores and spaces aside), and reports the loop naming PR-16 when they differ or when the variant is not of that form; the loop-bounds export (`rsk-loop-bounds/1`, R35.3) gains a `proof` field with the variant's text, and the lint's summary counts the loops so proven (5 of the 7 Application loops on 2026-11-06; part1 is level C, and its two loops under features are proven by its HIL members since 2026-11-09). Negative check: with `sensor`'s variant replaced by `i@` (increasing), `cargo creusot` fails exactly `vc_sensor` (14 of 15 goals) and every other file proves. `sensor`'s loop, the only one outside a feature, was proven first; the four loops under the `response`, `timing`, and `stress` features carry their variants, are checked by the lint, and are proven by the HIL-configuration members below (the same day). Fixture `loop_variants.rs` and test `r35_2_loop_variants_match_the_bound`.

**The HIL configurations (R46.3, R46.5; 2026-11-06).** Until this date the proof workspace compiled the two Partitions with no feature, so the bodies the rig runs under the `timing`, `response`, `stress`, `isolation`, and `cycles` features (the injections, the spins, the access matrix, the EasyDMA cases, the register probes) were never seen by the prover, although their binaries are the ones the measured records and the fault-injection evidence come from. The workspace now has one member per (declaration variant, Partition features) pair the rig builds, nine beside the QEMU pair at first and eleven since the measurement instrumentation of task 19.2 (the same day; 11.13): `part0-nrf52840` (the plain configuration), `part0-timing` and `part0-stress` (the measurement configurations, `measure` with `timing` or alone), `part0-response`, `part0-isolation`, `part0-cycles`, `part2-nrf52840` (plain, cycles), `part2-timing` and `part2-stress` (`measure`, with `stress` for the latter: the loop report and the EasyDMA traffic scenario), `part2-response`, `part2-isolation`; each compiles the real source with its features as its own crate and the declaration's variant feature. The declaration's variant features are exclusive, so the job proves the QEMU pair in one run (`[workspace.metadata.creusot] default-members`) and each member by name (`cargo creusot -p <package> -- --offline`), thirteen runs, the Evidence_Item `proof-app-creusot` passing only when every run proves (`creusot_hil_members` reads the members from the manifest; a member added to the workspace is proven without a change to the job). Every member proves: 11 to 16 files each, the whole in about two minutes beside the build. What the first proofs of these bodies forced, each a real tightening or a frame condition: the injection window's `from + len` saturates (an unguarded `u64` addition); the access-matrix step's attempt index saturates and is bounded by 32 before the bit it sets; `rsk::matrix::count`, `target`, and `perform` carry contracts (a well-formed map, `wf`: one entry per Partition in every table and `this` among them, and the attempt below `count`, which now has the postcondition the callers' guard needs); the `Outcome` of an attempt lost its derived `PartialEq` (Creusot's `DeepModel`, as for `Stats`) and callers match on the variant; `isolation_step` states that it leaves `stats` as it was, without which `sampler`'s Critical_Section after the call could not find the Resource invariant (the `&mut` of the whole Context forgets every field the callee says nothing about). Trusted, with reasons (R46.5): the `hw_dt` probes (`register_patterns_fp`, `fpscr`, `fetch`, `dma_request_raw`: assembly and raw accesses of the Hardware_Model differential tests, validated on the rig, 11.8) and `address_of`, a pointer-to-integer cast the language of the prover has no model for, whose value is only reported to the rig. Proven with them: every loop variant of the R35.2 paragraph above, the four under features included.

**Partition 1 under the App_Prover (2026-11-09, night).** `part1` is level C, outside R46.3's requirement, and until this date it had a proof member for the QEMU configuration alone (added the same day for A-P8's `log`). Its feature-gated bodies are what the rig runs too: the board's `pulse` source over GPIOTE (`nrf52840`), the injections of the response and stress configurations, the access-matrix step of the isolation configuration, the cycle spin, and the measurement instrumentation. The workspace now has eight members for it, one per (declaration variant, Partition features) pair the system crate's features build, `part1-nrf52840`, `part1-timing` (`measure`), `part1-stress` (`stress`, `measure`), `part1-measure` (`measure`, `telemetry`, `gating`), `part1-response` (`response`, `telemetry`), `part1-isolation` (`hw-dt`), `part1-cycles`, and `part1-cycles-fpu`, every one with `nrf52840`; twenty-three HIL members in all, twenty-six runs with the QEMU trio (`creusot_hil_members` reads them from the manifest; the xtask test pins the list). Each proves: 11 files for the plain, timing, cycle, and floating-point cycle members, 12 for isolation, 25 for response, 47 for the plain measurement configuration (the role modules of `telemetry`, `pingpong`, `relay`, and `triangle` with the body), and 12 for stress. What the first proofs forced, two tightenings of the level C body of the same kind the level A and B bodies had on 2026-11-06: the injection window's `from + len` saturates (part1 still added), and the stress configuration's period choice `(now / ms(40)) % 4` divides by a value the prover cannot bound (`rsk_target::ms` was a generated `const fn` with no contract, so its result was unconstrained and two calls unrelated); the Generator now gives `ms` its contract, the saturating product `TICK_HZ / 1000 * ms` in the logic, and the body divides by the value bound once, the obligation discharged from the contract (the period is the Target's ticks per 40 ms, never zero). Mutation `gen-ms-contract-dropped` (the App_Prover kind of 9.2) removes the contract's first clause from the Generator and is killed at `vc_log` by the `part1-stress` member. Nothing of part1 is trusted beyond the R46.5 record's rows (its isolation member uses `rsk::matrix` through the contracts of 2026-11-06 and no `hw_dt` probe).

**R46.5 record: constructs the App_Prover cannot express, and the substitute evidence.**

| Construct | Where | Tasks affected | Substitute evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Floating-point arithmetic (`f32`; RN-10: Creusot's float support is partial, Aeneas has no float type, 7.1) | `lowpass` of part2, the one-line first-order filter `y += (x - y) * 0.125` | `filter` (level B); the body around it is proven | The function is `trusted` and reviewed: no panic is possible (float arithmetic never panics in Rust), no overflow obligation exists for floats, and no index or cast is involved; the safe state checks the filter's convergence to its constant input on every QEMU and HIL run (`report_and_exit`), so a wrong step would show as a failed run. Open until Creusot's floats allow (task 17.1 keeps the item). |
| Pointer-to-integer cast (`as usize as u32` of a buffer address: `PointerExposeProvenance`, unsupported by the translation) | `address_of` of part2, the isolation configuration only | `sampler` under `isolation` | Trusted: the value is reported to the rig for its register check and influences no control flow or access; the rig's check of the EasyDMA pointer register against it is the evidence that it is the buffer's address (11.7). |
| Assembly probes and raw accesses of the differential tests (`rsk::hw_dt`) | `register_patterns_fp`, `fpscr`, `fetch`, `dma_request_raw` | `filter` and `sampler` under `isolation` | Trusted glue over the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's `dt` module (HIL builds only, never a Flight_Build); validated by the HIL differential tests of 11.8 (HW-06, HW-10). |
| The `rsk` API's contracts (`Shared::lock`, `PerTask`, `Signal::raise`, `now`, `spin`, `DmaBuf`, `DmaTransfer`, `DmaHeld`, `debug`, `mmio`) | `crates/rsk` under the `creusot` feature | every Task | Trusted specifications of the API over the Kernel's SVCs (TBR-08); the Kernel side is Verus-proven (P-02, P-08, P-16) and the API's behaviour tested on QEMU and the rig. |
| Release conditions (R5.5) and receiver-side validation functions (R42.4) | none exist in the Ravenscar Profile_Variant of Phase P1 | none | Vacuous at this revision: release conditions belong to the Jorvik variant (task 26.1), validation functions to the Endpoints of Phase P4 (task 21.4); each task proves its functions when it adds them. |

**Cross-solver confirmation (9.1; 2026-11-06).** why3find proves a goal with the first solver that succeeds, so until this date a goal that only one solver discharged rested on that solver alone. The job now runs each configuration twice: the first run compiles and proves with alt-ergo and z3 only, the session is cleared with the translation kept, and the second run proves the same files with cvc5 and cvc4 only (`--only prove --no-cache`); `proof-app-creusot` passes only when both runs of every configuration prove, and its note counts the goals per solver. The job's run of 2026-11-06: 531 goals over the eleven configurations, each discharged in both sets (alt-ergo 529 and z3 2 in the first, cvc5 530 and cvc4 1 in the second); later the same day, with the two measurement members of task 19.2 (11.13), 648 goals over thirteen (alt-ergo 643 and z3 5; cvc5 647 and cvc4 1); the second run of a configuration takes seconds from the kept translation. Task 17.1 is closed with this: its four items (the API's contracts, the level A and B bodies with their Resource invariants, the loop bounds, the substitute-evidence record) are above, and the remaining trusted items are the R46.5 record.

### 7.1 Aeneas evaluation on one pure control law (task 17.3, R46.5; 2026-10-26)

**What was done.** `proofs/aeneas/controllaw` is a dependency-free `no_std` crate with one pure control law of the shape a level A Task body computes between its Critical_Sections: a fixed-point PI step (`pi_step`: the error clamped to 2^16, the integral accumulated with an anti-windup clamp at 2^22, the proportional term `KP * e >> 15` in `i32`, the integral term `KI * integral >> 15` in `i64` then cast back, the output saturated at 2^17; Q15 gains). Charon (Aeneas's rustc driver, pinned nightly-2026-09-17) translates the crate to LLBC and Aeneas to Lean (`proofs/aeneas/lean/ControlLaw/Controllaw.lean`, 115 lines, generated and committed): a pure functional model in the `Result` monad in which every `+`, `*`, shift, and `as` is a possible `fail`. The handwritten proofs (`Proofs.lean`, 97 lines) establish for every state satisfying the invariant `|integral| ≤ 2^22` and every error that `pi_step` returns `ok` (no panic, no arithmetic overflow, every cast in bounds), that the output lies in `[-2^17, 2^17]`, and that the new state satisfies the invariant; `clamp` is specified once and reused; the initial state satisfies the invariant. `lake build` checks them in the job's step "Aeneas evaluation" (`proof-app-aeneas`, skipped with a warning where Lean is absent); the translation is committed, so the job needs Lean and the pinned checkout's Lean library, not Charon or Aeneas.

**Toolchain and pins** (toolchain/decision.toml `[aeneas]`): Aeneas commit 472b3d5 and Charon commit 40ca4ab6c (neither is a release), built from a checkout next to the repository as Creusot is (OCaml 5.4.1 through opam, Charon in Rust on nightly-2026-09-17), Lean v4.35.0 through elan, Mathlib at the revision Aeneas's manifest pins (7.6 GB of build artifacts fetched from Mathlib's cache). The install took about an hour of machine time against Creusot's comparable effort; the dependency chain (two translators, two language toolchains, Mathlib) is the heaviest of the tools evaluated.

**Findings.** (1) The translation is immediate and readable; the Rust had to avoid a type named `Pi` (Aeneas refuses the clash with Mathlib's `Pi`) and the `Debug`, `PartialEq`, `Eq` derives (they translate, but pull `core::fmt` models into the file), keeping `Clone, Copy`. (2) The `step` tactic (the renamed `progress`) discharges most obligations with the library's `@[step]` lemmas and `scalar_tac`; three things needed a hand: the constants defined by shifts (`1 << 16`) are `Result` values proven by `rfl`, the shifts' postconditions are `Int` shifts rewritten to divisions by `Int.shiftRight_eq_div_pow` before `omega` can bound them, and the narrowing `i64 → i32` cast needs the in-bounds lemma `IScalar.cast_inBounds_spec` named explicitly with its bound proven first, because the default step leaves only `i10 = IScalar.cast ...` without a value. The proof of a 7-line function is some 45 tactic lines, written in about an hour with the library's source as the reference. (3) Aeneas does not translate floating point (no `f32` in its scalar types), so the demo's `lowpass` is out of reach for it as it is for Creusot (RN-10). (4) Closures, `&mut` through the `rsk` API, and loops were not exercised; the design's assessment (limited loop and closure support, RN-21) stands. (5) Trust: Charon as a rustc driver on a nightly later than the Flight_Build's 1.95.0 (ORQ-08 as for Creusot), Aeneas's translation (OCaml, unverified), and the Lean kernel with Mathlib; no DO-330 qualification exists for any of them; no credit is claimed.

**Conclusion.** Aeneas fits the role the design gives it: small pure computations (control laws, validation functions of R42.4) for which a Lean-level functional proof, possibly against Mathlib, is wanted, as substitute evidence under R46.5 where the App_Prover cannot express a construct. It does not replace Creusot for Task bodies: Creusot proves the demo's bodies against the `rsk` API's contracts with no manual proof text, where Aeneas needs the proof written by hand and its translation needs the code shaped for it. The designation of section 7 stands.

### 7.2 The Analyzer's computation on the code as compiled: Aeneas over the Verus-erased build (2026-11-14)

**What this section proves, and what it does not (stated 2026-11-14 after review).** It proves Property 16 a second time, on the code as compiled: the Analyzer's *computation*, from the Task set's numbers to each Task's verdict, returns the least fixed point of the R29.1 recurrence with the terms of R29.2 to R29.4 (or a not-schedulable verdict, or an overflow). It does **not** prove Property 18, "RTA is sound against execution": nothing here says that a Job of a Task reported schedulable completes within the computed `R_i` in an execution of the Kernel. A theorem about the recurrence is a theorem about arithmetic; that the recurrence bounds response times is a theorem about the scheduler, with a model of releases, execution and the SRP. Property 18 is the subject of section 7.3: it is proven there (2026-11-15) over a run of the Kernel's own functions as extracted, with four kinds of transition still modelled by hand and one condition of the run found not to hold of the Kernel (open); the PBT against the discrete-event SRP simulator and the UPPAAL corpus (P-19, 5.10, 8.7) stay as evidence beside it.


**Question.** The Verus proof of `rta::fixpoint` (P-19, Property 16) is a proof about the source under Verus's reading of it; what runs is the same source compiled by rustc with the ghost code erased by the `verus!` macro. Can the erased build be given to Aeneas, and the result proven there, so that the Analyzer's computation is also proven against an independent reading of the code that runs (the execution of the Analyzer, not of the Tasks it analyses)?

**What was done.** Charon was run on `crates/rsk-analyzer` itself, not on a copy: `charon cargo --preset=aeneas --start-from 'crate::rta::fixpoint' -- --lib` compiles the crate and its dependencies (`vstd` and the Verus macro crates among them) with Charon's pinned nightly and no `cfg(verus_keep_ghost)`, so the macro emits the erased code, and extracts `fixpoint`, `interference_exec` and `ceil_div_exec` from the compiler's MIR. Aeneas renders them as three Lean functions (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Rta/Rta.lean`, generated, committed, 120 lines): the recursion as a `partial_fixpoint` definition, the loop as the library's `loop` over a body function, every arithmetic operation and the vector index as a fallible step. No source change was needed and nothing of Verus appears in the translation.

**What is proven** (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Rta/Proofs.lean`, handwritten, about 200 lines; `lake build` checks it; no `sorry`; the axioms are Lean's `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`):

| Theorem | Statement |
|---|---|
| `ceil_div_exec_spec` | For `t > 0` the function returns, and a returned value is `⌈a / t⌉` |
| `interference_exec_spec` | For positive periods and `from ≤ len` the function returns (the recursion ends, the index is in bounds, no operation overflows, the 128-bit product included), and a returned value is the interference sum of the terms from `from` |
| `fixpoint_spec` | For positive periods `fixpoint` returns for every `base`, `jitter` and `deadline` (termination by `deadline - w`), and: `Fixpoint w` is a fixed point of `w = base + Σ ⌈(w + J_j) / T_j⌉ · E_j`, at most every fixed point, with `jitter + w ≤ deadline`; `NotSchedulable w` has `jitter + w > deadline` and `w` at most every fixed point; `Overflow` claims nothing |

These are the clauses of the Verus contract, with termination and the absence of panics stated outright (in Verus they are side conditions of the function's verification). Two changes to the generated file were tried and both fail the build, as they must: the division's dividend replaced, and the returned fixed point replaced by `base`.

**What it adds, and what it does not.** The two proofs share the Rust source and nothing else: Verus reasons about its own encoding of the source to an SMT solver; this proof reasons about rustc's MIR as Charon reads it and Aeneas's functional model of it, checked by Lean's kernel. An error in Verus's erasure, in its treatment of the `u64` and `u128` operations, or in its loop and recursion reasoning for this function would have to be matched by an error on this path to go unseen. It does not reach the machine code (rustc's back end and LLVM stay trusted, 9.3), it covers the three functions only (`terms::derive_all`, which builds their inputs, and `response_times`, which composes them, are not translated), and the recurrence is stated twice, in `rta.rs` and in `Proofs.lean` (`ceilDiv`, `interf`, `stepF`), their agreement by reading them. Aeneas and Charon are unreleased commits (7.1); no credit is claimed beyond that of a cross-check.

**Findings.** (1) The erased build goes through Charon unchanged; the one obstacle was the crate's binary target, which has no `rta` module (`-- --lib`). (2) The proof effort was an afternoon's, most of it the shapes of the library's facts: `u64::MAX` appears in three spellings (`core.num.U64.MAX`, `U64.rMax`, `U64.max`) that `scalar_tac` does not connect by itself, and a specification stated with a `match` over the loop's control flow needs `WP.spec_mono` to meet `loop.spec_decr_nat`'s own `match`. (3) The mathematics is short over the naturals (monotonicity of the ceiling division and of the sum; an iterate below a fixed point stays below it), shorter than the Verus lemmas over the integers. (4) The step is not in the verification job beyond `lake build` of the committed translation: regenerating it needs Charon and its nightly, as for 7.1.

**Extended the same day to the whole numeric chain.** Three further steps, each on the erased build as above (the translation is now 1,087 lines of Lean from `--start-from 'crate::terms::response_of' --start-from 'crate::terms::derive_all'`).

*The composition, first in Verus.* One Task's analysis from its terms through the fixed point to the verdict was plain Rust in `response_times` (the plan's oldest open proof item, R29.5). It is now `terms::response_of`, inside `verus!` (rsk-analyzer: 71 items verified, 0 errors): a Task reported schedulable has `R = J + w` for the least fixed point `w` of its recurrence from `C + B + O`, with `R <= D`; a Task reported not schedulable without an overflow has no fixed point within its deadline (`meets_deadline` is false); an overflow is never reported schedulable. `response_times` calls it and keeps only the report's breakdown; the Analyzer's tests pass unchanged, the comparison with the reference implementation among them.

*The same chain in Lean* (`Rta/Chain.lean`, about 290 lines):

| Theorem | Statement |
|---|---|
