| `terms_for_spec` | For positive periods `terms_for` returns, and the plain terms are those of Task `i`'s recurrence (`specTerms`: every other Task of equal or higher Priority by its Jobs, R29.1; every lower one with an intake cost by its intake, R19.2), each with a positive period |
| `response_of_spec` | For positive periods and every Task `i`, `response_of` returns; the terms it used are `specTerms`; schedulable implies `w` is a fixed point of `w = (C + B + O) + Σ ⌈(w + J_j) / T_j⌉ · E_j`, at most every fixed point, `R = J + w` and `R ≤ D`; not schedulable without overflow implies every fixed point `x` has `J + x > D`; overflow implies not schedulable |

`response_of_spec` is the end-to-end statement for one Task: from the Task set's numbers to the verdict, on the code as compiled, with no hypothesis left about the terms (the "positive periods" premise of `fixpoint_spec` is discharged by `terms_for_spec`).

*The term derivation in Lean* (`Rta/Derive.lean`, about 420 lines): `derive_all_spec`, with `site_stats_spec`, `overhead_spec`, `interference_bound_spec`, `blocking_spec`. For every input in which each site names a Task, `derive_all` returns; a returned list has one entry per Task, and each entry holds that Task's Resource-site count, longest Critical_Section, `O_i` (R29.3), `E_i` (R29.4), `B_i` (R29.2: the largest candidate over the sites that can block the Task, at least the Kernel-level term), and `J_i = J_rel`. Two parts of the Verus contract are not restated: that `None` is returned only when a term does not fit 64 bits, and which site attains `B_i`.

Three more changes to the generated file were tried and each fails the build: the Jobs' term given the intake cost as its bound, `O_i` stored from `E_i`, and the Ceiling test of a blocking site made strict.

**The link between the two halves, and the whole (later the same day).** `derive_all` produces `B_i`, `O_i`, `E_i`; `response_of` consumes `C`, `B`, `O` and the Tasks' `E_j`. Between them `inputs::prepare` copied the derived numbers into the analysed Tasks and `response_times` built the interferer list from those, in plain Rust. That is now one function inside `verus!`, `terms::analyse_all` (rsk-analyzer: 75 items verified, 0 errors): the derivation, the Tasks as each other's interferers (Priority, `J_rel`, the period or 1 for a period of 0, `E_j`, the intake cost of a Sporadic_Task), and each Task's recurrence and verdict. Its contract (`verdict_ok`) states every verdict against the recurrence from `C_i + B_i + O_i` over the terms of the derived `E_j`, each defined from the Task set and its sites. `prepare` calls it and the report states its verdicts (`prepared_response_times`); a test requires that they are the ones `response_times` computes from the prepared Tasks.

On the code as compiled (`Rta/Whole.lean`, about 300 lines; the translation is taken from `--start-from 'crate::terms::analyse_all'`): `analyse_all_spec`. For every Task set whose sites name Tasks of the set, with a period and a deadline per Task, `analyse_all` returns, and a returned result has one derived entry per Task holding its terms (R29.2 to R29.4); a list of interferers, one per Task, each with that Task's Priority, `J_rel`, period, `E_j` and intake cost; and for every Task the terms of its recurrence over those interferers and the verdict of the recurrence from `C_i + B_i + O_i`: schedulable with `R = J_rel + w` for the least fixed point `w` and `R ≤ D_i`, or not schedulable with no fixed point within the deadline, or an overflow that is never reported schedulable. No hypothesis is left between the Task set's numbers and the verdicts. Three more changes to the generated file were tried and each fails the build: an interferer's bound taken from `O_i`, `B_i` passed twice in place of `B_i` and `O_i`, and a Sporadic_Task's intake cost dropped.

**What remains outside.** `inputs::prepare`'s own work before the numbers exist: reading the Task_Model, the WCET_Records and the Kernel_Timing_Parameters, choosing the records, resolving each site to its Resource and Ceiling, and the conversion of ticks to cycles (`ticks_to_cycles_down`, verified in Verus, not translated); the UPPAAL export; the report (the interference breakdown per term is recomputed for display); and R29.9's utilization test, which precedes the iteration. That the Task set handed to `analyse_all` is the one the Task_Model declares is by the Analyzer's tests and the cross-checks of section 8.

**Findings of the extension.** (1) Aeneas's Lean for a function with a local variable named like a module does not compile: `let terms = ...` shadowed the `terms` module in every later reference; the local is renamed in the Rust source. (2) A function with early returns under several `if`s is translated with its tail duplicated per branch (`overhead`: 390 lines of Lean for 45 of Rust); splitting on the flags first and then `step*` proves all sixteen copies without a line per case, at about a minute of checking and a raised heartbeat limit for that theorem and for `blocking`'s loop body (thirty-two cases, the same way). (3) `usize` against `u64`: the library knows a `usize` is 32 or 64 bits wide (`Usize.bounds_eq`), which a counter bounded by a vector's length needs spelled out.

### 7.3 Property 18 against the Kernel's own scheduler (2026-11-14 and 15; proven over a run of the Kernel's functions as extracted; the transitions still modelled by hand are listed here)

**Status.** For every Task that the Analyzer's `analyse_all` (as compiled, 7.2) reports schedulable, the Lean theorem `property18_kernel` (`Sched/KRun.lean`) bounds the response of every Job by the reported `R_i`, in every run of a model whose Kernel state changes through the Kernel's own functions as Aeneas extracts them: `Kernel::dispatch` of the Task `Sched::select` returns, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `periodic_release`, `deferred_release`, `deadline_miss`, `intake_release` and `apply_response`, and, since the third pass, `budget_event` when a timer entry in progress serves it, and `settle`, the last step of every entry (fourth pass). Four kinds of transition are still modelled by hand ("Modelled by hand"). The theorem of the first pass, `property18` (`Sched/Link.lean`), is the same statement over a specification-level run of the scheduler state alone (`Step`); `ksim` shows that every run over the Kernel's functions is one of those.

The work was done in two passes. The first (2026-11-14 and the morning of 2026-11-15) closed the three gaps of the table below over a run that composed the extracted `Sched` functions by hand. The second (2026-11-15) made the Kernel's own functions extractable and put them in the run; what it changed is marked "second pass" in this section. A third step the same day put the order the Kernel takes for a Budget event with a dispatch pending into the run where the event is served inside another entry, and found what the Analyzer's `B_i` did not cover where the event had a timer entry of its own; it is marked "third pass". The fourth pass (2026-11-15, on the decision to change the Kernel and not the Analyzer) made that second case impossible in the Kernel: the entry that pends a dispatch disarms the executing Job's Budget event and cancels a compare pended for it. The run's condition on a Budget event's own entry is now a consequence of the Kernel's code ("A Budget event ahead of a pending dispatch: closed").

| Gap | Now |
|---|---|
| The run's bounds were hypotheses | The run's parameters are functions of the Analyzer's inputs (`runParams`: the Task set, its Critical_Section sites, the Kernel_Timing_Parameters, each period or MIT and deadline), and the bound is the `r` of the verdict `analyse_all` returns. The theorem uses `analyse_all_spec` (7.2), so `derive_all` is joined to it. What stays a hypothesis is under "The hypotheses of `property18`" |
| One amount of Kernel work per arrival | Each Kernel section is at the transition where the Kernel executes it and is bounded by its Kernel_Timing_Parameter: a timed event's entry, an intake, a dispatch, a lock, an unlock, a Job's Endpoint operations, a Budget event, a Job end, the entry that ends an overrunning Job. Sections do not nest, and no Job executes during one |
| The Kernel transitions were not in the run | In the run: the timer entry (release, deferred release, deadline, unmask, housekeeping), the MIT intake (accept, defer, discard), the Budget event with each Overrun_Response, Endpoint send and receive, and the Health_Monitor's responses. Since the second pass the Kernel's own functions for the dispatch, the Job end, the lock services, the three release and deadline handlers, the intake and the responses are in the run as extracted; the rest is under "Modelled by hand" |

It is a proof about runs of the extracted `rsk-logic` functions, with the model's accounts of time beside them. It is not a proof about Kernel_Arch's entry code, which calls those functions (the cores and stubs of section 5), nor about time on the Target. Every condition of the run is stated below, and each has a mutant that removes it and breaks the proof. The PBT against the discrete-event simulator and the UPPAAL corpus stay as Property 18's evidence beside it.

The proof changed the Analyzer twice (both under "Findings"): the Kernel-level terms of lower-Priority Tasks and of housekeeping now carry the release jitter, and `O_i` now counts the entry that ends the Task's own overrunning Job. Neither changes a response time of the QEMU demo or of the measurement configuration; the second raises `O_i` by 1,613 cycles for the Tasks of the two stop-on-overrun Partitions in the short stress configuration, whose verdicts stay as they were.

**Extraction, as for 7.2 (Charon and Aeneas on the Verus-erased build; second pass).**

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| One translation, rooted at the entry points `Bound` gives Kernel_Arch (`KernelOps` for `Bound` in rsk-kernel `arch::bound`: `init`, `start`, `eligible`, `select`, `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `intake`, `timer_event`), with `rsk_logic::logic` included | `Sched/Kernel.lean`: generated, 3,603 lines, 179 definitions, no `sorry`. It holds every function those entry points reach: the `Sched` state functions and `select`; `Kernel::new`, `start`, `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `intake`, `intake_release`, `timer_event`, `serve_slot`, `periodic_release`, `deferred_release`, `deadline_miss`, `budget_event`, `apply_response` and what it calls; `Timing`, `Slots`, `Budget`, `Log`. The two translations of the first pass (`Sched.lean` from rsk-logic, `Eligible.lean` from rsk-kernel) and the correspondence between their copies of the Kernel's types are gone |
| What made the Kernel's functions translate | `record`, which appended a step to the ghost history, took the step's Task and argument as `Ghost` arguments; the erased build turns a `Ghost(e)` into a closure, which Aeneas rejects (`fn_def_to_fn_ptr`). It is now a lemma (`lemma_record`; `record_intake` likewise `lemma_record_intake`), and each of the 15 sites appends the step in a `proof` block and calls the lemma. No contract changed; `timer_event` and `periodic_release` got their own prover instance (`spinoff_prover`). rsk-logic: 344 items verified, 0 errors; rsk-kernel: 272 |
| `Sched::select` | Translates after two changes to its body, its contract unchanged: the best Task so far is carried as an index (`NT` while there is none) instead of an `Option` (with an `Option` across the iterations Aeneas finds no loop fixed point), and `above_top` has a literal in each branch (a branch that is a comparison is given the type of a proposition) |
| `Kernel::new` | Translates. The empty ghost history comes from `no_history`, a function of its own (verified by Verus) that Charon is told to keep opaque: `Sched/Kernel.lean` declares it as an axiom. The run does not start from `Kernel::new` but from any Kernel whose scheduler state is what `Sched::new` returns, and `new_eff` shows that `Kernel::new` returns such a Kernel. `#print axioms property18_kernel` gives Lean's three; `new_eff` depends on `no_history` |
| Not translated | Kernel_Arch's entry cores (rsk-kernel `arch::cm4::entry`): not attempted. `Config::validate`, `Layout::validate` and their helpers are named by the include pattern and fail in Aeneas (breaks and continues to outer loops); nothing extracted calls them and they are not in the file |

The commands are in `proofs/aeneas/README.md`.

**The run over the Kernel's functions (`Sched/KRun.lean`, second pass).** A state is the Kernel's state (`Kernel`, as extracted) and the model's accounts of the specification-level run below. A run starts from a Kernel whose scheduler state is what `Sched::new` returns, at instant 0. `cfg` is the Kernel's configuration, fixed over the run.

| Transition | Function, as extracted | In the specification-level run (`ksim`) |
|---|---|---|
| `periodic`: the entry of a Periodic_Task's release event | `Kernel::periodic_release` | `erel` when it made the Release (the Task had none held and has one pending after): the Release is one that awaited its entry, for a Job that will execute for `d`, `1 ≤ d ≤ C_t`. Otherwise (an overlap and its MIT_Violation's response, a stopped Partition, the end of the operating duration) `eplain` and the response's steps |
| `deferred`: the entry of a deferral event | `Kernel::deferred_release` | `erel` when it made the held Release; otherwise `eplain` and a dropped Release |
| `intake`: the entry of a Sporadic_Task's Release_Source event | `Kernel::intake_release` | `erel` (accepted), `edefer` and the MIT_Violation's response (held to the end of the MIT), or `eplain` and the response (discarded; a stopped Partition, the safe state: nothing) |
| `deadline`: the entry of a deadline event | `Kernel::deadline_miss` | `eplain` and the Deadline_Miss response's steps |
| `dispatch` of Task `t` | `Sched::select` returns `t`; `Kernel::dispatch` | `dispatch`: R7.1 admits `t` and no Task of higher Priority (`select_some`) |
| `lock`, `unlock` | `Kernel::svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, when they grant | `lock`, `unlock` |
| `finish`: the Job on top executes its last unit and returns | `Kernel::job_return` | `finish` |
| `unwind` | `Kernel::job_return`, with no Kernel section of its own: the unwinding of a killed Job, part of the entry whose response killed it | `kend` |
| `resp`: a Health_Monitor response, at any instant, with no Kernel section of its own | `Kernel::apply_response` | `kend`, `kkill`, `kclear`, as many as the response takes (`apply_response_r`) |
| `bserved` (third pass): a Budget event served inside a timer entry in progress, with its Overrun_Response, at any instant, with a dispatch pending or not, and with no Kernel section of its own | `Kernel::budget_event` | nothing, or the response's `kend`, `kkill`, `kclear` (`budget_event_eff`) |
| `settle` (fourth pass): the last step of every Kernel entry, with no Kernel section of its own | `Kernel::settle` | nothing: it leaves the scheduler state (`settle_sched`) |
| `plain_h`, `svc_h`, `bevent_h`, `overrun_h` | by hand, below | `eplain`, `svc`, `bevent`, `finish` with the overrun flag. For `bevent_h` and `overrun_h` the specification-level run's condition that R7.1 admits no Task is derived (`settled_armed_none`) from the Kernel state they find: one that `settle` left, with the Budget slot of the Job on top armed |
| `jarrive`, `earrive`, `ktick`, `idle`, `exec` | none | the same |

The model-side conditions of a transition (which event awaits its entry, the Kernel section's bound, the allowances of the Critical_Sections, the Job's execution) are those of the specification-level run's transition it maps to. A Job executes, locks, unlocks and calls an Endpoint operation only when `Sched::select` returns no Task. After a transition that may carry a response, the execution accounts follow the scheduler state the Kernel left: a Task keeps the execution its Job in progress still needs only while that Job is in progress, and what its pending Job will ask for only while its Release is pending (`normRun`, `normPend`). At a transition modelled by hand, the scheduler state is as the transition states and the rest of the Kernel's state may change in any way; at a transition that is an instant or a unit of time, the Kernel's state does not change.

**The specification-level run (`Sched/Run.lean`).** A state is the Kernel's `Sched`, the instant, and model accounts: per Task the nominal instant of its last Release and whether that Release still awaits the Kernel entry that makes it, the execution its pending Job will ask for, the execution its Job in progress still needs, and how many locks, unlocks and Budget events and how much Endpoint time that Job may still take; per event source the instant of its last event and whether it awaits its entry; per entry of the lock stack the time its Critical_Section may still take; and the Kernel section in progress, with the account it runs on. An event source is one kind of event of one Task (its arrival: the release event of a Periodic_Task or the intake of a Sporadic_Task; its deadline event; its MIT unmask; its MIT deferral) or the Kernel's housekeeping event. A run starts from `Sched::new` at instant 0. The scheduler state changes only through the extracted functions, and a transition exists only where the extracted function returns.

| Transition | Extracted function | Kernel section, at most | Conditions |
|---|---|---|---|
| `jarrive`: the nominal instant of a Release of Task `t` | none | none | At least `T_t` after `t`'s last one |
| `earrive`: the instant of an event of source `q` | none | none | At least the source's period after its last one |
| `erel`: the entry of a release event, an intake or a deferral makes the Release that awaits it; the Job will execute for `d` | `make_pending` | Δ_timer (Δ_irq for an intake) | `1 ≤ d ≤ C_t`; the source is one that releases `t`; no Kernel section in progress |
| `eplain`: an entry that leaves the scheduler state as it is (unmask, housekeeping, a discarded intake, a deadline event) | none | Δ_timer (Δ_irq) | A deadline event of `t` has an entry only when `t`'s Job is not complete at its deadline; no Kernel section in progress |
| `edefer`: an intake held to the end of the MIT | `defer` | Δ_irq | No Release of the Task held; no Kernel section in progress |
| `dispatch` of Task `t` | `start` | Δ_dispatch | R7.1 admits `t` (`Elig`), and `t` has the highest Priority among the Tasks R7.1 admits; no Kernel section in progress |
| `lock`, `unlock` of a Resource by the Job on top | `lock`, `unlock`, when they grant | Δ_lock_in, Δ_lock_out | At most as many of each per Job as the Task has Critical_Section sites; the Critical_Section takes at most `L(task, resource)` from the lock call on |
| `svc`: an Endpoint send or receive of the Job on top | none | what is left of the Task's Endpoint cost for this Job | |
| `bevent`: the Budget event of the Job on top, found without an overrun or recorded only (RECORD_AND_CONTINUE) | none | Δ_timer | Once per Job |
| `finish`: the Job on top executes its last unit and ends: it returns, or its Partition stops overrunning Jobs and its Budget event ends it (END_JOB, RESTART_PARTITION, STOP_PARTITION) | `finish_top` | Δ_complete; Δ_detect + Δ_enforce for the overrun | The overrun end only in a Partition that stops overrunning Jobs, and as the Job's one Budget event |
| `kend`, `kkill`, `kclear`: a response ends or unwinds the Job on top, kills a Job on the stack, drops a Task's Release | `finish_top`, `kill`, `clear_release` | none of their own | At any instant |
| `ktick`: one unit of the Kernel section in progress | none | | |
| `idle`, `exec`: one unit of time with no Job in progress, or of the Job on top | none | | No Kernel section in progress; none of the Critical_Sections the Job is in is exhausted, and each loses one unit |

A Job executes, locks, unlocks, calls an Endpoint operation and takes its Budget event only when it is not killed, no Kernel section is in progress, and R7.1 admits no Task: a pending eligible Task is dispatched at once, the Hardware_Model's rule. (Until the second pass these two conditions were stated on the extracted `Bound::eligible`; `eligible_ok` still shows that the stub's test returns true exactly when R7.1 holds.) A Kernel section the Job on top begins itself (`lock`, `unlock`, `svc`, `finish`) is inside every Critical_Section the Job is in: its time is taken from their allowances. No unit of time passes that would leave a Release or an event waiting for its entry beyond its jitter: this is how the run states that an entry comes within `J_rel` of its instant.

**Modelled by hand (second pass: what is left).** The left column is the code that was read. The rows of the first pass that are gone are now consequences of the code: `periodic_release`, `deferred_release` and `deadline_miss` with their calls into `Sched` and into the responses; `intake_release`'s accept, defer and discard with the MIT_Violation's response; `apply_response` for each response (`end_top`, `kill_partition_jobs`, `clear_partition_releases`, `enter_safe_state`); `dispatch` after `select`; `job_return`; `svc_lock` and `svc_unlock`.

| Kernel code | In the run |
|---|---|
| `timer_event` → `serve_slot`: that each served slot is an entry with a Kernel section of its own (which handler an armed, due slot gets is read from the code since 2026-11-16, `Sched/Timer.lean`, below) | one `periodic`, `deferred`, `deadline` or `plain_h` transition per event |
| `serve_slot`: unmask, housekeeping, a free slot, the end of the operating duration | `plain_h` and `opend`. Since 2026-11-17 neither is by hand: each carries the Kernel state `serve_slot` returns, and what it did to the scheduler state is read from the code (`KStep.served_kept`, `KStep.served_end`) |
| `budget_event` in a timer entry of its own: no overrun, or `apply_response` with RECORD_AND_CONTINUE | `bevent_h` (the scheduler state kept; the Kernel state is `budget_event`'s since 2026-11-17) |
| `budget_event` in a timer entry of its own → `apply_response` → `end_top` for the overrunning Job on top | `overrun_h` (`Sched::finish_top`, as the Job's last unit); `overrun_p` when a Partition's stop or restart follows; `bevent_r` for any response read only as a response on the scheduler state, which is the transition for the safe state (all three on `budget_event`'s result since 2026-11-17) |
| `Kernel::intake`: the reading of the time base, then the due deadline event served before `intake_release` (KD-R9.6) | `observed`, `deadline`, then `intake` (the decomposition read from the code since 2026-11-17, `kernel_intake_cases`) |
| Endpoint send and receive (rsk-kernel entry cores; no call into `Sched`; a send's declared arrival release is an intake) | `svc_h`, then `faulted` or the calls of `Kernel::intake` (read from the entry cores and from `Bound` since 2026-11-17, `Sched/Endpoint.lean`) |
| `fault`, `fault_of` → `apply_response` | `faulted`, `faulted_of` (on the code since 2026-11-17) |
| Kernel_Arch: which entry calls which function; `job_return` at the unwinding of a killed Job | the transitions as named; `unwind` |

`budget_event`, `serve_slot` and `timer_event` are in the translation. Since the third pass `budget_event` is a transition of the run where another entry serves it (`bserved`); as an entry of its own it is still the two rows above, because its Kernel section and the Job's last unit of execution are accounts of the model that the function does not carry. Putting `serve_slot` and `timer_event` in the run needs the timer slots and the time base in the run's argument (which slot is armed and due, and when): the slots' half is done (next paragraph), the time base's is not.

**The timer entry on the code (2026-11-16, `Sched/Timer.lean`; 426 lines, 16 theorems, no `sorry`, axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).** Three facts about the extracted functions, each of the form "if the function returns". (1) `serve_slot_cases`: what `serve_slot` does with slot `i` is fixed by the slot's kind and is for the slot's own Task: a release slot gets `periodic_release`, a deferral slot `deferred_release`, a deadline slot `deadline_miss`, a Budget slot `budget_event`, each called with the slot's Task and index; any other kind but the end of the operating duration leaves the scheduler state as it was (`serve_slot_kept`). (2) `timer_event_served`: a returning `timer_event` is the reading of the time base (the scheduler state kept) followed by a chain of `serve_slot` calls (`ServedChain`), and every slot of the chain is armed and due (`at ≤ now`) in the state it is served in (`SlotDue`); the two loops of the function, the deadline slots first (KD-R9.6) and then the others, are each proven by induction on the slots left. (3) `KStep.served_release`, `served_defer`, `served_deadline`, `served_budget`, `served_kept`: a served slot of each kind is the run's existing transition of that kind (`periodic`, `deferred`, `deadline`, `bserved`, `plain_h`), with the handler call no longer a hypothesis of the transition but derived from `serve_slot` and the slot's kind. Five mutants (`lean-code-timer-serves-a-slot-not-due`, `lean-code-timer-serves-a-disarmed-deadline-slot`, `lean-code-release-slot-served-as-a-deferral`, `lean-code-slot-served-for-another-task`, `lean-timer-served-slot-need-not-be-due`; the first four change the generated translation, the fifth the definition of a due slot), each run on 2026-11-16 and killed by a failure of `Sched/Timer.lean`; the first two are killed because the proof's case split no longer matches the changed code, the other three by a type mismatch at the statement they name. **Added the same day (the file is now 750 lines, 29 theorems; the whole Lean project builds, `property18_kernel` included).** (4) The slot of the end of the operating duration is a transition of the run: `KStep.opend` (`Sched/KRun.lean`) takes `Kernel::enter_safe_state` from a Kernel state with the scheduler state unchanged, the simulation absorbs it as a response (`enter_safe_state_r`), and `serve_slot_end` with `KStep.served_end` read it from `serve_slot`. (5) A Budget event as an entry of its own is read from `Kernel::budget_event` as extracted: `budget_event_no_overrun` (the account shows no Overrun: the scheduler state is kept) and `budget_event_cases` with `apply_response_record` (an Overrun_Response that only records returns the state it was given) give `KStep.served_bevent` and `served_bevent_recorded`, the run's `bevent_h` with its hypothesis "the scheduler state is kept" derived; `budget_event_overrun`, `apply_response_end_job` and `end_top_finish` (an Overrun of the Job on top, not yet reported, under the response that ends the Job, the Job holding no Resource: `Sched::finish_top` of the scheduler state) give `KStep.served_overrun`, the run's `overrun_h` with its hypothesis derived. `bevent_h` and `overrun_h` stay in the relation: the theorems say the code's Budget event is one of them under the stated conditions on the Kernel state, not that the relation admits nothing else. An Overrun under a response that restarts or stops the Partition, or with a Resource held, is the existing response transition and is not restated here. Four mutants (`lean-kernel-end-slot-is-any-change`, `lean-code-end-slot-skips-the-safe-state`, `lean-code-budget-event-responds-without-overrun`, `lean-code-end-job-response-restarts-the-partition`), each run alone on 2026-11-16 and killed (the first in `Sched/KRun.lean`, the others in `Sched/Timer.lean`). **Not covered, and still as before:** that each served slot of one `timer_event` is an entry with its own Kernel section in the model (the chain is of one call, the run charges one section per served slot: the association is by hand); the time base (the run's instants against the Kernel's `now`), so "due" here is against the `now` the function was given and says nothing of the run's clock; and the deadline slot's disarm, which is still not joined.

Not in the run's argument: the Budget account, the timer slots, the timing state of the MIT, the event log, the system safe state and the counting of MIT_Violations. They are in the run's state (the Kernel's functions read and write them), and the proof uses of them only what the extracted functions do to the scheduler state: a function that returns leaves a scheduler state the specification-level run can reach.

**What is proven** (`Sched/Model.lean`, `Locks.lean`, `Views.lean`, `Run.lean`, `Window.lean`, `Stage.lean`, `Link.lean`, and since the second pass `Entry.lean`, `Calls.lean`, `Select.lean`, `KRun.lean`; handwritten; `lake build` checks them; no `sorry`; `#print axioms` of `property18_kernel`, `ksim` and `property18` gives Lean's `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

| Theorem | Statement |
|---|---|
| `make_pending_ok`, `defer_ok`, `clear_release_ok`, `kill_ok`, `start_ok`, `lock_ok`, `unlock_ok`, `finish_top_ok` | What the extracted function's result is whenever it returns, with no assumption on the state; the loops of `end_job` and `recompute_ceiling` by induction |
| `eligible_ok` | Whenever the extracted `Bound::eligible` returns, it returns true exactly when R7.1 holds |
| `Wf.step` | In a run: the stack's Priorities rise strictly from the bottom; a Task has a Job in progress or killed exactly when it is on the stack; the System_Ceiling is the greatest Ceiling on the lock stack; the entries of a Job higher on the stack are higher on the lock stack; a Job above an entry's holder has a Priority above the entry's Ceiling (the Stack Resource Policy, proven here on the extracted code; section 5.1 has it in Verus) |
| `busy_block` | While Tasks of Priority at least `P_i` have released work, the test accepts none, and the Job on top is of lower Priority, a lock entry of Ceiling at least `P_i` is held, and every such entry is held by the Job on top |
| `low_disp` | A Task of lower Priority than `P_i` is dispatched only when no Task of Priority at least `P_i` has released work |
| `stage4` | For a Task `i`, `B` a bound on every Critical_Section of a Task of lower Priority on a Resource of Ceiling at least `P_i`, and any `R > 0` with `B + Δ_timer + Σ_{k : P_k ≥ P_i} (C_k + O'_k) · ⌈(R + J_k) / T_k⌉ + Σ_q EC_q · ⌈(R + J_q) / T_q⌉ ≤ R` (the first sum includes `i`; `O'_k` is the Kernel time one Job of `k` may take from its dispatch on; the second sum is over the event sources, a deadline source left out when its Task's Jobs are shown never to be incomplete at their deadline): in every state a run reaches in which a released Job of `i` has execution left, the nominal instant `a` of its Release has `now < a + J_i + R` |
| `property18` | If `analyse_all`, as compiled, returns results and reports Task `i` schedulable with the response time `v.r`, then in every specification-level run with the parameters `runParams` of the Analyzer's inputs, in every state in which a released Job of `i` has execution left, `now < a + v.r` |
| `dispatch_eff`, `job_return_eff`, `svc_lock_eff`, `svc_unlock_eff` (second pass) | Whenever the extracted `Kernel::dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock` returns, the scheduler state it leaves is what `Sched::start`, `finish_top`, `lock`, `unlock` returns on the one it found, with no assumption on the Kernel state |
| `periodic_release_eff`, `deferred_release_eff`, `intake_release_eff`, `deadline_miss_eff` | The same for the release and deadline handlers: `make_pending` (only with no Release held; for a deferral only with one held), `defer` (only with none held), or a response |
| `apply_response_r`, `mit_violation_r` | A response, whatever it is, changes the scheduler state by ending the Job on top (`finish_top`), killing Jobs on the stack (`kill`) and dropping Releases (`clear_release`) and by nothing else; the loops of `kill_partition_jobs`, `clear_partition_releases` and `enter_safe_state` by induction |
| `select_some`, `select_none` | When the extracted `Sched::select` returns a Task, R7.1 admits it and admits none of higher Priority; when it returns none in a run, R7.1 admits no Task (`select_cases` and the loop's invariant `select_loop_ok`) |
| `budget_event_eff` (third pass) | Whenever the extracted `Kernel::budget_event` returns, it changed the scheduler state only through its Overrun_Response: not at all when the Task's Job is not on top, when the Overrun is already reported, or when there is none (the slot is armed again) |
| `settle_sched`, `settle_admitted_quiet`, `settled_armed_none` (fourth pass) | Whenever the extracted `Kernel::settle` returns: it left the scheduler state as it was; if `Sched::select` returns a Task, the Budget slot of the Job on top is not armed after it; so a Kernel state that `settle` left with that slot armed is one in which `Sched::select` returns none |
| `unlock_entry_leaves_no_budget_event`, `job_end_entry_leaves_no_budget_event`, `budget_event_entry_leaves_no_budget_event` (fourth pass) | The two cases `B_i` did not cover, on the extracted code: after `svc_unlock`, `job_return` or `budget_event` followed by `settle`, with a Task admitted, the Budget slot of the Job on top (for a Job end: the resumed Job, whatever was armed for it) is not armed |
| `intake_decision_accept`, `budget_event_reported` | On the code as extracted: an event is accepted only with no Release of the Task held and, if the Task has had an accepted Release, one MIT after it became due; a Budget event of a Task whose Overrun is reported leaves the scheduler state, the Budget account and the event log as they were |
| `ksim` | Every state a run over the Kernel's functions reaches is, read through its scheduler state and its accounts, a state a specification-level run reaches |
| `property18_kernel` | `property18` for every run over the Kernel's functions |

`property18` is by induction on the Priority, lowest first. The Analyzer leaves the deadline event of a lower Task out of a Task's recurrence when the same result shows the lower Task schedulable (7.2, `quiet`); in the run a deadline event has an entry only when the Job is incomplete at its deadline; and the bound for the lower Task, which is the induction hypothesis, says it never is.

The argument is the busy period of the Priority level, which needs no distinct Priorities. The invariant is a potential: for some window start `s` and blocking allowance `β`, `(now - s) + β + Σ_x (the work now owed on account x + cost_x · ⌈(s + R - e_x)⁺ / T_x⌉) ≤ R`. The accounts are the Jobs of each Task of the level and every event source; `e_x` is the earliest instant of the account's next arrival and `cost_x` what one arrival can add. A Task's account holds the execution its released Jobs still need, the Kernel time those Jobs may still take (a dispatch if not started, a lock and an unlock per site not yet used, the Endpoint time left, a Budget event if not taken, a Job end), and the Kernel section in progress if it runs on this account. `β` covers what the outermost blocking Critical_Section may still take, its holder's Budget event, and a Kernel section the holder began. A unit of time in which the level is not quiet is spent on an account or inside the blocking; an arrival turns one counted future arrival into at most `cost_x` of owed work; when the level is quiet and the Kernel executes nothing the window restarts, an arrival that still awaits its entry accounting for the jitter in the ceiling.

**The hypotheses of `property18`**, beside `analyse_all`'s result.

| Hypothesis | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `hlen`, `hprio`, `hNT` | The Kernel's configuration and the Analyzer's inputs describe the same Tasks with the same Priorities, each above the idle System_Ceiling; at most 256 Tasks. Not derived from the Generator |
| `hsok`, `hwl` | The Analyzer's own preconditions: every site names a Task, one period and deadline per Task |
| `hbud` | Every Budget is positive |
| `hDT` | Every deadline is at most the period or MIT |
| `hL` | A Task has no Critical_Section on a Resource (its bound is 0 there), or every such Critical_Section that can block a Task is one of the sites R29.2 counts for that Task and takes at most what R29.2 takes for the site: its WCET, or Budget + Δ_detect + Δ_enforce for a site of another Partition that stops overrunning Jobs |

**The conditions of the run**, each a hypothesis of a transition in `Step` or `KStep` and each with a mutant below.

| Condition | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Releases of a Task, and events of one source, are at least the period or MIT apart | R9.1 for periodic releases; for a Sporadic_Task the Kernel's MIT handling (R10.2 and Property 9, proven in Verus over the Kernel's history, 5.1). Joined on the code at the Kernel's time readings only ("The Verus facts and the run") |
| A Release the Kernel makes is one that awaited its entry, of the Task its event's source releases (second pass) | The link between the Kernel's Releases and the run's nominal instants; with the spacing above it says that the Kernel makes a Task's Releases at least `T` apart |
| Every Kernel entry begins within `J_rel` of its event's instant | Measured (11.3, 11.13) |
| A Job executes for at most its Budget; in a Partition that stops overrunning Jobs the Budget event ends an overrunning Job at its Budget | R28.4 (WCET at most Budget) for the Partitions that do not stop overruns; R18 for those that do. The Budget account is not in the run |
| Each Kernel section is within its Kernel_Timing_Parameter, a response's work included in the section that applies it | Measured (11.13); the parameter definitions are the rig's (`rsk-hil`, `kernel_timing.rs`) |
| Kernel sections do not nest and no Job executes during one | The Kernel level (DD-02) |
| The Task dispatched is the one `Sched::select` returns, and a Job executes only when it returns none | The first is A4 (5.8: only the vector `select` returns is pended), the second the Hardware_Model's rule (a pended eligible vector is taken at once). That `select`'s Task is one R7.1 admits and of the highest Priority among those, and that none is admitted when it returns none, are consequences of the code since the second pass (`select_some`, `select_none`) |
| A Job takes at most as many locks and unlocks as its Task has sites, one Budget event, and Endpoint operations within its declared cost | R29.3's counts; the lock count is PR-07's (no re-entry), the Budget event's is `Budget::reported` (Verus) |
| A deadline event has an entry only on a miss | `job_return` disarms the deadline slot at the Job's end (Verus postcondition); the slots are not in the run |
| A Kernel call a Job makes inside a Critical_Section, the unlock that ends it, and an overrun's entry there are within the section's allowance | The site's WCET of R29.2 runs from the lock call to the unlock's return; for a site bounded by Budget + Δ_detect + Δ_enforce the Job's own Kernel calls inside the section must be within that amount: evidence under "The two timing assumptions" |
| A Budget event's own timer entry finds a quieted Kernel state (`Quieted`: with the Budget slot of the Job on top armed, R7.1 admits no Task). Restated 2026-11-18: the premise of the fourth pass, that the state is what `settle` returned, is not what an entry leaves (the paragraph on `Sched/Settle.lean` at the end of this document) | Proven in Lean on the extracted code for every state `apply_core` returns and through `observe`; **not yet proven** for the state the dispatch entry leaves and for the lock, DMA-request, DMA-write and Endpoint services, which return without `apply_core`. Before 2026-11-18 this row read: every entry core ends with `settle` and nothing else changes the Kernel's state (Verus: the entry cores' postcondition `spec_dispatch_guard`, rsk-kernel); a timer entry serves armed, due slots (`timer_event`, by hand) and an armed Budget slot is the executing Job's (`budget_slots_owned`, Verus). That no dispatch is pending there is proven in Lean on the extracted `settle` (`settled_armed_none`) |

**The Verus facts and the run (second pass).** The five facts the first pass took from the Kernel's Verus proofs without joining them:

| Fact | In Verus | In Lean |
|---|---|---|
| `select`'s choice | `Sched::select`'s contract (P-04) | Proven on the extracted code (`select_some`, `select_none`) and used: the specification-level dispatch's two conditions are derived in `ksim` |
| The MIT spacing | Properties 8 and 9 over the history (`lemma_mit_spacing_over_run`, `intakes_spaced`) | Proven on the extracted code at the Kernel's time readings (`intake_decision_accept`: an event is accepted only with no Release held, and only when `last_due + MIT ≤ now`). The run uses the first half. The second half is not joined to the run's condition on nominal instants: the run has no time base (the `now` an entry passes to the Kernel is not tied to the run's instant), and a sporadic Release's nominal instant is its event's, up to `J_rel` before the entry whose reading the Kernel spaces |
| One Overrun report per Job | `Budget::reported`, `Budget::inv` | Proven on the extracted code (`budget_event_reported`). Not used: `budget_event` is modelled by hand. The flag bounds the reports, not the timer entries: `budget_event` re-arms the slot when it finds no Overrun, so "one Budget event per Job" stays a condition of the run (on the rig the stress campaign of 2026-11-15 shows 1,162,162 timer entries for 823,495 Releases and the other timed events: no sign of repeated entries) |
| The deadline slot's disarm | `job_return`'s and `end_top_release`'s postconditions | Not joined. It needs the timer slots in the run's argument (`serve_slot` as a transition, with the rule that an entry is of an armed, due slot) |
| An armed Budget slot is the executing Job's | **New invariant** `budget_slots_owned` in `Kernel::inv` (2026-11-15; until then only `dispatch`'s postcondition stated the disarm). To hold at every recorded step, `job_return` and `end_top_release` now disarm the ended Job's Budget slot before the Job leaves the stack (the two statements moved up; the contract is the same), and `arm_budget` requires its Task on top. Mutant `budget-event-armed-for-an-ended-job` | Not joined, for the same reason as the deadline slot |

No Lean axiom was added. An axiom that states one of these facts over the run would be an assumption about the model's own transitions, which is what a condition of the run already is. The sound form is an opaque predicate for `Kernel::inv` with each extracted function's Verus contract as an axiom about that function, and each call's precondition as a condition of the run; with the slots in the run it would give the last two rows. Not done.

**The two timing assumptions (second pass).**

*A Job's Kernel calls inside a Critical_Section bounded by Budget + Δ_detect + Δ_enforce.* The bound holds if the Kernel's time in the Job's own calls is charged to the Job's Budget, and the Budget event ends the Job and the section whatever calls it made.

| Evidence | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Verus, rsk-kernel `svc_core`: `number != SVC_JOB_END ==> final(state).last_switch == old(state).last_switch` (and the same on `svc_service_core`, `apply_core`, the Endpoint cores and what they call; 272 items verified; mutant `svc-lock-takes-a-charge-point`) | A Job's lock, unlock, time-base, Endpoint, Release_Signal and EasyDMA calls take no charge point and do not move the start of the Job's cycle segment (HW-13): the Kernel's time in the call is charged to the Job at its next charge point. A proof, not a test |
| Kani, rsk-logic `a_critical_section_ends_at_the_budget_event_whatever_kernel_calls_the_job_makes` (the demo's configuration; 0 of 1,877 checks failed) | `sampler` in its Critical_Section on `stats`: any two lock or unlock calls it makes but the unlock that ends the section leave the section open and its Budget event armed for the instant set at its dispatch; at that event, with the account above the Budget, the Job is ended and the Resource released within the entry |

Not shown: the durations on the Target (the Budget event is armed in ticks of 4 cycles; Δ_detect and Δ_enforce are measured, 11.13), and that the cycle counter itself runs through the handler (HW-13 is the Hardware_Model's statement of DWT CYCCNT).

*A Budget event ahead of a pending dispatch: closed (found in the second pass, analysed in the third, closed in the fourth by a Kernel change; 2026-11-15).* The run's condition was that a Job takes its Budget event only when R7.1 admits no Task. It did not hold of the Kernel: the timer entry is at the Kernel level, above every dispatch vector (DD-02), and the Kernel took a Budget event with a dispatch pending in two ways.

| Way | Before the fourth pass | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Inside a timer entry that is in progress (`timer_event` serves every due slot in one entry, in slot order: a release slot of lower index makes the Release, then the executing Job's Budget slot is served with the dispatch pending) | In the run since the third pass: `bserved`, `Kernel::budget_event` as extracted, at any instant; its time is that of the entry that serves it | The same. Δ_timer is the rig's bound on a whole time-base entry, "every due timed event served" (the record's own condition), and the run charges every event source a full entry for each of its events, so nothing is charged as blocking |
| In a timer entry of its own, ahead of the dispatch (the Budget slot's compare fired while another Kernel section ran: the entry that made the Release, the unlock that ended a blocking Critical_Section, a Job end) | Excluded by the run, done by the Kernel, and not covered by `B_i + Δ_timer` in two cases: after a blocking Critical_Section of the Task's own Partition (missing Δ_detect + Δ_enforce − Δ_timer, 5,854 cycles on the short stress record of that pass), and the Job resumed underneath having an entry of its own too (`end_top` armed its slot for now plus its remaining Budget) | **Does not arise.** The Kernel change below |

**The Kernel change (fourth pass; no Analyzer term changed).**

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| `Kernel::settle` (rsk-logic), the last step of every Kernel entry | Takes R7.1's selection (`Sched::select`). When a Task is admitted, the Budget slot of the Job on top is disarmed in the same entry (its re-arm at resume, `job_return` and `end_top`, is as before). When none is admitted and the Job on top is in progress with no Overrun reported and no Budget event armed, the event is armed for the remaining Budget: a dispatch that an earlier entry pended was withdrawn (its Task's Release dropped by a response before the vector was taken, 5.8) and the Job executes on. Returns `select`'s selection for Kernel_Arch to pend, and whether a slot was disarmed |
| `entry::apply_core` (rsk-kernel), which every entry core ends with | Calls `settle` in place of `select`. When a slot was disarmed, a compare armed or already pended for it is cancelled and the compare armed for the earliest slot that is left (`rearm_compare`, `Action::RearmCompare`; `Action::CancelCompare` when none is left). An event already due pends the time base at once: it is served before the dispatch, in an entry of its own source, which the recurrence counts |
| The time-base driver (`hw::timebase_rearm`, `timebase_cancel`) | nRF52840 TIMER0: the compare interrupt is disabled, the new compare value written, the compare event cleared and read back, TIMER0's pending bit cleared in the NVIC, the interrupt enabled, and the line pended if the counter has reached the instant. The new value is written before the old event is cleared, so no match of the old value survives, and a match of the new one that the clearing erased is caught by the last test. QEMU's one-shot timer: stopped and cleared at the timer and in the NVIC, then started for the new distance |
| `Bound::dispatch` | Ends with `settle` as well (the dispatch's entry does not go through `apply_core`). The vector the NVIC took is the selection (Hardware_Model), so no Task is admitted there and the dispatched Job's Budget event stays armed |

| Evidence | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Verus, rsk-logic (345 items verified, 0 errors): `Kernel::settle` ensures `dispatch_guard`: if R7.1 admits a Task, no Budget slot is armed (with `budget_slots_owned`: the only slot that could be is the executing Job's). Mutant `settle-skips-the-early-disarm`, killed by a proof failure | The invariant at the end of the entry's last step |
| Verus, rsk-kernel (274 items verified, 0 errors): `KernelOps::settle` and `dispatch` ensure `spec_dispatch_guard`; `apply_core` ensures it; `start_core`, `timer_core`, `intake_core`, `unbound_core`, `fault_core`, `stack_violation_core` and `dispatch_job_core` ensure it; `svc_core`, `svc_service_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `dispatch_vector_core` and `epilogue_core`, which have paths that leave the Kernel's state as it was (a stale vector, a clock call, an accepted EasyDMA request or receive) or only raise the System_Ceiling (a lock: `svc_lock` keeps the guard, no Task becomes admitted), ensure that it is kept. Mutant `entry-selects-without-settling`, killed by a proof failure | No Budget event is armed while a dispatch is pending, at the end of every Kernel entry, by induction over the entries from `start_core` |
| Kani, rsk-kernel `a_pended_dispatch_cancels_the_budget_compare_and_keeps_another_due_event` (the demo's configuration) | `sampler` executes and its Budget instant is reached with the time base pended; an event of `pulse`'s Release_Source is taken first and pends `pulse`'s dispatch. With no other event due: the Budget slot is disarmed, the time base not pended, the compare armed for a later instant. With `log`'s release event due at the same instant: the time base is pended again, and its entry makes `log`'s Release, ends no Job, reports no Overrun and leaves the Budget slot disarmed |
| Kani, rsk-kernel `timebase_rearm_cancels_the_compare_and_pends_a_due_instant` (0 of 240 checks failed) and `apply_every_action_is_total` (0 of 484) | The driver's register writes on the simulated TIMER0 for any instant and counter reading: compare value, event clear, interrupt enabled, the NVIC's clear-pending written, and set-pending written exactly when the counter has reached the instant; the cancel alone leaves the interrupt disabled and writes no set-pending |
| Loom, `rsk-loom` (its model's `apply` ends with `settle`) | Over the model's interleavings, no Budget slot is armed after an entry that pends a dispatch |
| Lean, `settled_armed_none` and `ksim` (`Sched/Calls.lean`, `Sched/KRun.lean`) | In the run, `bevent_h` and `overrun_h` no longer assume that `Sched::select` returns no Task: it follows from the extracted `settle`. Mutants `lean-kernel-budget-entry-in-an-unsettled-state`, `lean-kernel-budget-entry-of-an-unarmed-slot`, `lean-code-settle-leaves-the-budget-event-armed` |
| Lean, `unlock_entry_leaves_no_budget_event`, `job_end_entry_leaves_no_budget_event`, `budget_event_entry_leaves_no_budget_event` | The two uncovered cases on the extracted code: after the unlock that makes a dispatch pending, and after an entry that ends the Job on top with a dispatch pending, the Budget slot of the Job on top (the resumed Job's, whatever `job_return` or `end_top` armed) is disarmed |
| Kani, rsk-logic `a_budget_event_served_with_a_dispatch_pending_leaves_the_state_of_the_event_served_first` (second pass) | The first way: served in the release's entry, the Kernel's state is that of the event served first |

What the Lean proof takes from elsewhere here, both stated as the condition in "The conditions of the run": that the Kernel state a Budget event's own entry finds is one an entry's last step left (Verus, the entry cores), and that such an entry is of an armed Budget slot of the Job on top (`timer_event` and `serve_slot` by hand; `budget_slots_owned` in Verus). A compare that matches after the entry's last read of the pending state but before its cancel is cancelled with the rest; one that matches for a slot still armed pends the time base as before.

A consequence for R18.2, the same as that of the disarm at the dispatch's own entry (2026-11-14): a Job whose Budget instant falls in the entry that pends a dispatch, or after it and before the dispatch, has its Overrun found when it resumes (`arm_budget` then arms the event for the present instant), not ahead of the dispatch. The Job does not execute in between, so it consumes nothing beyond what it had consumed at that entry; the report comes later by the time the preempting Jobs take. Property 10 as proven (an Overrun found at the Job's Budget event is reported and answered) is unchanged. R18.2 as written bounds the detection by Δ_detect from the instant the consumption exceeds the Budget: for such a Job that holds in the Job's own execution (none follows before the report) and not in elapsed time. Whether R18.2 is to say so is open ("What remains").

Not changed by this: "one Budget event per Job" stays a condition of the run (`budget_event` arms the slot again when it finds no Overrun; with the account equal to the Budget exactly, `overrun` is strict and the slot is armed for the present instant, which is one more entry of the executing Job with no dispatch pending).

**What the model does not use.** Δ_release, Δ_wake, Δ_hm, Δ_entry, Δ_stamp, Δ_fp_save and Δ_fp_restore are in `O_i` and no transition of the run spends them: they are margin here, not shown necessary or sufficient. The same holds for the `L_kernel` and stop and restart floor of `B_i`: in the run a Kernel section in progress at a Release's nominal instant delays the entry, which the jitter `J_i` covers, and a response's work is inside the section that applies it.

**Time.** Discrete units; a Job that executes its last unit in the unit starting at `now` has `now + 1` at most `a + R`.

**Findings of 2026-11-15 (both from the link to `analyse_all`; both changed the Analyzer).**

| Finding | Detail | Change |
|---|---|---|
| The Kernel-level terms of lower-Priority Tasks and of housekeeping had no jitter | The recurrence counted `⌈w / T_l⌉` entries of each event of a lower Task `l` in a window of length `w`. The Kernel entry of an event comes up to `J_rel` after the event's instant when a Kernel section is in progress at that instant, so an event from just before the window can have its entry inside it, and the entries in the window number up to `⌈(w + J_rel) / T_l⌉`. The two counts differ when `w` is within `J_rel` below a multiple of `T_l`. In the model, a run with entries at their events' instants is not one in which Kernel sections exclude each other | `terms::term_of_intake`, `term_of_events` and the housekeeping term carry `J_rel` (R19.2 and R29.1 amended). Verus re-verifies `rsk-analyzer`; the Lean proofs of 7.2 rebuild on the regenerated translation |
| `O_i` had no term for the entry that ends the Task's own overrunning Job | In a Partition that stops overrunning Jobs, a Job that overruns has no Job end of its own: its Budget event's entry ends it, in up to Δ_detect + Δ_enforce (R29.4 has this in `E_j`, for the Tasks the Job interferes with). `O_i` counted Δ_timer for the Budget event, Δ_hm and Δ_complete. Where Δ_detect + Δ_enforce is larger than those three, the window of the Task's level is longer than the recurrence allows by the difference | `terms::overrun_extra`: `O_i` adds the excess of Δ_detect + Δ_enforce over Δ_timer + Δ_complete + Δ_hm for a Task of such a Partition (R29.3 amended). Verus: 88 items verified in `rsk-analyzer` (86 before). On the short stress records the excess is 1,613 cycles; on the measurement records and the placeholders it is 0 |

Effect on the verdicts, each analysis run before and after on 2026-11-15 with the records of the tree: the QEMU demo with placeholder timing and the measurement configuration give the same response time for every Task; the short stress configuration gives the same verdicts (the protected Partition and `filter` schedulable; `log`, `pulse` and `sampler` not), with `filter`'s slack 436,722 cycles (441,561 before).

All 68 were run on the fourth pass's files on 2026-11-15 through `cargo xtask mutations --only`, one after the other (20:02 to 21:04): each was killed, `lake build` failing in the file named (the 65 of the third pass had been run on its files the same day, 16:56 to 17:53, each killed). Two conditions of an earlier draft of the run had mutants that survived (no second Release, and no second event of a source, while one still awaits its entry); the proof does not use them, and they were removed from the run rather than kept. The two eligibility mutants change the generated translation and break the lemma that ties it to R7.1 (`eligible_ok`, which the run no longer uses since the second pass: the dispatch is of the Task `select` returns). The eight `lean-code-*` mutants change the generated translation of a Kernel function and break the lemma that reads it; that the bound itself fails with the changed function is not shown separately. `unwind` and `resp` have no condition beside the function's result, and the proof needs none: a response, or the end of a Job with no Kernel section, at any instant only removes work.

**Also found.** The verification job counted `sorry` in the Lean output by the text Lean printed before version 4.2x (straight quotes); the installed Lean prints backticks, so the count was always 0. `lake build` itself does not fail on `sorry`. Fixed to count both forms (`xtask/src/verify.rs`). The mutation runner's tree copy included the Lean project's `.lake` directory (7.2 GB of fetched packages); it now skips it.

**Finding (2026-11-14), closed the same day: the recurrence had no term for the timer work of lower-Priority Tasks.** Found while preparing to charge overheads where the Kernel spends them. The timer entry runs at the Kernel level, above every Task (DD-02; `lemma_kernel_level_never_masked`), so the entry for a lower-Priority Task's timed event takes processor time from a higher-Priority Job that is executing. `terms::interferers` gave a lower-Priority Task a term only for a Sporadic_Task's intake (R19.2, the correction of 2026-11-06); a lower Periodic_Task contributed nothing, and `B_i`'s floor `L_kernel` covers one entry per busy period. Reading the Kernel's slots for the count turned up three more facts:

| Fact (kernel.rs) | Consequence |
|---|---|
| A preempted Job's Budget slot stayed armed and fired once, stale, while the preempting Job executed (`budget_event` disarmed it and returned) | A timer entry per preemption that no term counted. **Kernel change:** `dispatch` disarms the preempted Job's Budget event (`job_return` and `end_top` already re-arm at resume) and states it in its postcondition; mutant `preempted-budget-slot-left-armed` |
| A Sporadic_Task's own `O_i` counted two timed events (three with `defer`); its deadline and its MIT unmask events were not among them | `timed_events` is now 4 for a Periodic_Task (release, deadline, Budget, a blocking Job's Budget event) and 4 or 5 for a Sporadic_Task (deadline, Budget, unmask, a blocking Job's Budget event, deferral) |
| The time base's 32-bit wrap has no interrupt of its own (the counter is extended at each read, and housekeeping reads it every 125 ms) | Nothing to charge. The housekeeping event itself fires every 8,000,000 cycles and is now a term of every Task |

**The terms after that finding** (R19.2 and R29.1 as amended on 2026-11-14; `terms.rs`, Verus, 86 items then; six mutants, each killed by a proof failure; the counts below became `⌈(w + J_rel) / T⌉` on 2026-11-15, "Findings of 2026-11-15" above). For a Task `i` and every Task `l` of lower Priority: `⌈w / T_l⌉ · K_l`, where `K_l` is Δ_irq for a Sporadic_Task's intake or Δ_timer for a Periodic_Task's release event, plus Δ_timer for each other timed event of `l` that can fire while a higher Job executes: the unmask of a Sporadic_Task, its deferral under `defer`, and the deadline event unless the analysis reports `l` schedulable. And `⌈w / T_hk⌉ · Δ_timer` for housekeeping. No carry-in Job is needed: each kind of event comes at a fixed distance from the arrival, so at most `⌈w / T_l⌉` of each fall in a window of length `w`; and a lower Task's Budget event cannot fire while its Job is preempted. `analyse_all` runs in passes: the first charges every deadline event; each further pass leaves out those of the Tasks the pass before found schedulable and is kept only if every Task it so marks is still schedulable in it. The result is sound by induction on the Priority from the lowest, because a Task's recurrence takes only lower Tasks' events. The Lean proofs of 7.2 are redone on the regenerated translation (`terms_for_spec` with `specAll`, `analyse_all_spec` with `PassOk` and `quietOk`). The argument "each kind of event comes at a fixed distance from the arrival" held for the events' instants and not for their Kernel entries, which is the finding of 2026-11-15.

**Not proven in the Kernel** (a condition of the run of 7.3): that the events of one kind of one Task are at least the period or MIT apart. (That an armed Budget slot always belongs to the executing Job was the other item here until 2026-11-15; it is now the invariant `budget_slots_owned`, 7.3.) The spacing is read from `periodic_release`, `intake` and `end_mit_window`; for a Sporadic_Task's Releases and intakes it is R10.2 and Property 9, proven in Verus over the history, and joined to the Lean proof only at the Kernel's time readings (7.3, "The Verus facts and the run").

**Effect on the verdicts.**

| Configuration | Before | After | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| QEMU demo, placeholder timing (the job's `analysis-rta-p1-demo`) | PASS, `control` slack 400 | FAIL by 2,600 cycles, then PASS with slack 2,400 after the one demo change: `sensor`'s Budget 8.7 ms to 8.5 ms in the QEMU declaration | Golden report regenerated |
| `nrf52840_measure`, re-measured 2026-11-14 on the new Kernel | PASS, `control` slack 33,739 (v3 records) | First re-measurement: inputs rejected (`sensor`'s measured WCET with the margin 512,058 cycles against its 512,000-cycle Budget, R28.4) and utilization 101 %. After the second demo change (below): **PASS**, utilization 96 %; WCRT against deadline in ms: `control` 9.025 / 10, `echo` 9.833 / 20, `sensor` 13.129 / 20, `log` 39.612 / 40, `pulse` 14.137 / 15, `filter` 16.961 / 25, `sampler` 79.209 / 100; every observed response within its WCRT | Measured (180 s run) |
| `nrf52840_stress_short`, re-measured | FAIL (`log`, `sampler`) | First re-measurement: utilization 102 %, every Task fails. After the second demo change: utilization 97 %; the protected Partition's Tasks schedulable (`control` 9.447 / 10 ms, `echo` 11.921 / 20, `sensor` 14.332 / 20), `log`, `pulse` and `sampler` not, which is that configuration's claim | Measured (short run). The hour-long `stress` and `timing` records were re-measured on 2026-11-15 (below) |

**The hour-long records, re-measured (2026-11-15, on the Analyzer and Kernel of the Property 18 work; `cargo xtask hil --release-timing` then `--stress`, 3,660 s each, both passed).** Release timing (`docs/hil/release_timing_nrf52840_timing.md`): 823,317 periodic Releases at their nominal instants, lag at most 9,501 cycles (148.5 µs) against J_rel 12,500; 203,332 stimulus pulses answered, none unanswered and none later than 15 ms; the masked span at most 19,799 cycles (309.4 µs) against L_kernel 35,000; two entry-window deferrals handled; the configuration's measured analysis is not judged (R28.4, the injected overruns). Stress (`docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress.md`): 823,495 Releases, lag at most 9,098 cycles; the storm's 242,949 intakes within 244,001 MIT windows, intake at most 2,233 cycles against Δ_irq 2,600; the masked span at most 25,236 cycles (394.3 µs). The Analyzer on that run's records: FAIL at utilization 97 %, which is the configuration's claim; WCRT against deadline in ms, with the observed maximum: `control` 9.507 / 10 (0.654), `echo` 11.966 / 20 (0.329), `sensor` 14.332 / 20 (5.852), `filter` 18.154 / 25 (6.469); `log` (40.641 / 40), `pulse` (15.229 / 15) and `sampler` (100.404 / 100) not schedulable. Every observed response is within its WCRT where there is one.

**The job on this tree (2026-11-15, after both campaigns, boards attached).** One `cargo xtask verify`: passed, 0 violations, 148 Evidence_Items. Verus: `rsk-logic` 341 items, `rsk-kernel` 272, `rsk-analyzer` 88, `rsk-confcheck` 19, `rsk-protocol` 550; the Lean step builds the proofs of 7.2 and 7.3; the traceability matrix is regenerated from that run's evidence store.

**The second demo change (2026-11-14, on the user's decision after the re-measurement).** `sensor`'s Critical_Section is 5 ms (6 before) in every build but the timing configurations (12 ms on purpose) and the cycle-attribution configurations (6 ms: with 5 ms their comparison of `control`'s and `log`'s executions came out at 10.0 to 10.7 % against the rig's 10 % in four runs; they are probes and are not analysed). Its Budget is 7.8 ms (8.8 before) in the HIL configurations that are analysed or injected, and 7 ms (8 before) in the measurement configuration.

**The lead that was checked.** The unexpected deadline miss of `control` in the hour-long release-timing run of 2026-11-13 (3,444,040.015 ms) is not evidence of this finding. That configuration gives `sensor` a 12 ms Critical_Section against `control`'s 10 ms deadline on purpose; the injected 38 ms late `sensor` Job shifted the section so that it began 2.35 ms before a `control` Release and blocked it for 9.68 ms; two timer entries of about 55 µs each then fell inside the 237 µs `control` had left. It is the deliberate overload case, and the rig's judgement now says so (`Events::overload_misses`, `rsk-hil`; the trace and the mirror still stop at the first such miss, which the report states). The report of that run on `eval-v3-campaign` predates this and still shows the step as failed.

**The job on the second pass's tree (2026-11-15).** One `cargo xtask verify` with the boards attached: passed, 0 violations, 143 Evidence_Items. Verus: rsk-logic 344 items, rsk-kernel 272.
