**The campaign on the fourth pass's Kernel (2026-11-15 22:48 to 2026-11-16 03:54, `cargo xtask nightly` on the committed tree `79485112`, boards attached; logs `target/xtask-nightly/2026-11-16/`).** Fifteen steps, fourteen passed: the job (76 minutes, 0 violations), the two cycle configurations, the short timing and stress runs, the plain measurement run, the hour-long release-timing run, the six coverage runs, and the job again on the refreshed records (76 minutes, 0 violations, 148 Evidence_Items; Verus: rsk-logic 345 items, rsk-kernel 274, rsk-analyzer 97, rsk-confcheck 19, rsk-protocol 552; Kani: rsk-kernel 16 harnesses, rsk-logic 15). This is the first full job and the first hour-long campaign on the Kernel with `settle`. Hour-long release timing (`docs/hil/release_timing_nrf52840_timing.md`): 823,317 periodic Releases, every one at its nominal instant, lag at most 8,742 cycles (136.6 µs) against J_rel 12,500; 203,333 stimulus pulses answered of 203,333 counted, none unanswered, none later than 15 ms (latency at most 12.64 ms); the longest masked span 20,344 cycles (317.9 µs); no entry-window occurrence. The measurement configuration: PASS at 96 % utilization, every observed response within its WCRT (`control` 0.721 of 8.966 ms, `pulse` 5.578 of 14.088, `log` 4.244 of 39.517, `sampler` 4.611 of 79.052). **`sampler`'s WCRT there is 79.052 ms again; the 39.873 ms of the fourth pass's short run was not traced and is not explained: three records of the same configuration gave 78.502, 39.873 and 79.052.**

**The step that failed, and the Budget it moved (2026-11-16).** The hour-long stress run passed every rig step and failed the last one: the Analyzer rejected its records under R28.4, `sampler`'s measured entry 41,133 cycles, 51,417 with the 25 % margin, against a Budget of 51,200 (800 µs). The record before (2026-11-15, the Kernel before `settle`) was 37,506 with the same mean (28,268 against 28,274): the tail moved, the body did not. The repeat below measured 41,221, so the figure is the Kernel's and not one Job's; the Kernel's entries are charged to the Job they interrupt, and the entries of this Kernel are longer by what `settle` adds, which is the likely cause and is not separated from the run's own variation. As with `log` and `echo` on 2026-11-07 (11.14), the stress configurations' Budget follows the margin's demand: `sampler` 900 µs in `nrf52840_stress` and `nrf52840_stress_short` (800 before), the Generated_Config regenerated. On the changed configurations: the short stress run passed (04:00), and **the hour-long stress run passed (04:07 to 05:09; `docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress.md`)**: 823,495 Releases at their nominal instants, lag at most 9,817 cycles (153.4 µs); the masked span 24,158 cycles (377.5 µs); no entry-window occurrence; the protected Partition within its WCRTs (`control` 0.653 of 9.508 ms over 366,000 Jobs, `echo` 0.355 of 11.996, `sensor` 5.881 of 14.413); the analysis at 98 % utilization with `log` (41.033 of 40 ms), `pulse` (15.320 of 15) and `sampler` (101.168 of 100) not schedulable, as the configuration's claim allows (11.14). **`pulse` not schedulable under the stress configurations stands since the fourth pass (15.049 ms in its short run, 15.229 in the hour-long record of 2026-11-15, 15.320 now); the claim of 2026-11-07 named `log` and `sampler` only.** The job's final step ran before the Budget change and recorded the rejected stress record as a finding with an inconclusive item, in words written for the timing configuration ("the configuration's injected overruns"), which do not describe this case: none of the stress configuration's Jobs completed above its Budget. The job was then run on the tree with the changes of 2026-11-16 (05:11 to 06:26): it passed with no violation, all steps in 75 min 29 s.

**The register run whole (2026-11-16, 06:26 to 08:07).** `cargo xtask mutations` over all 192 entries on that tree, the first run of the whole register in one pass: 191 mutants were killed with the text their entries expect, and one, `preempted-budget-slot-left-armed`, was refused by the prover with another text than its entry's. Its entry dated from 2026-11-14 and expected the assertion at the arming of the new Job's Budget event; since 2026-11-15 the invariant's clause `budget_slots_owned` refuses the mutant's state earlier, at the step record (`lemma_record`'s precondition `inv_unrecorded`). The mutant was never accepted; the entry's expectation was stale since the commit of `Kernel::settle`, which was merged without a run of the register. The entry now names the step record, and its run alone kills it. So every mutant of the register is killed on this tree, 191 in the one run and the one after its entry's correction.

**Two readings of the day's figures (2026-11-16; from the committed records and the code, no further run).** (1) Why `sampler`'s maximum moved and its mean did not. A Job's account is the wall-clock cycles between switch points (`charge_point`, `arch/cm4/entry.rs`), so a Kernel entry that switches no Job, a timer entry for a lower Task's Release for one, is charged to the Job it interrupts. The differences between the last committed records and this day's, margin removed, are of one size and of both signs: `sampler` +3,715 cycles (hour-long stress), `pulse` +3,580 and `control`'s section `in_cs` +3,544 (measurement), `sampler`'s section `stats` -3,492 (short stress); the timer entry's measured maximum in the stress record is 4,885. The other items of the hour-long stress record moved by -0.9 % to +4.4 %. So the step is one more such entry inside one Job, not a slower body and not a general rise of the Kernel's cost. Not established: which entry it is in `sampler`'s worst Job, and whether `Kernel::settle` caused the shift or the new binary only moved the phasing; either needs a per-Job trace on the rig. The consequence stands whatever the cause: an item near its Budget can cross R28.4's margin from one build to the next, and 900 µs leaves `sampler` 6,073 cycles beyond the margin. (2) Why `sampler`'s WCRT in the measurement configuration reads about 39.8 ms in some records and about 79 ms in others (39.670 on 2026-11-08; 79.209, 78.502, 39.873 on 2026-11-15; 79.052 on 2026-11-16). `sampler` has the lowest Priority at a utilization of 95 to 96 %, and its WCRT follows `log`'s by about 0.81 ms while it stays below 40 ms, the period of `log`: `log` 38.859 and 39.067 give 39.670 and 39.873. With `log` at 39.258 or above the sum passes 40 ms, the Releases of `control`, `sensor`, `echo` and `log` at that instant are inside the busy window, and the fixed point is found a round later (78.502, 79.052, 79.209 for `log` 39.258, 39.517, 39.612). The five records fit this without exception; the analysis was not re-run with altered inputs to confirm it. Both values are within the deadline of 100 ms. The jump is a property of the configuration's load and not of a change in the Kernel: a difference of 0.2 ms in `log`'s terms moves `sampler` by 39 ms.

**The entries inside the longest Job, recorded (2026-11-16, later; one short stress run, `cargo xtask hil --stress --short`, passed).** The HIL build now keeps, for each Task, the Kernel entries that interrupted the Job its execution maximum is of: how many, their kinds, and their Rust halves' summed cycles (`stats_interrupted`, `stats_exec`, `stats_job_start` in `arch/cm4/unsafe_module/mod.rs`, feature `trace-ram`; the account starts empty at the Job's dispatch). The rig prints the line in the run's report and writes it among the conditions of each Task-entry WCET_Record. The run on this build (`docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress_short.md`, `wcet_nrf52840_stress_short.json`): `sampler` 37,815 cycles over 900 Jobs (mean 28,343, minimum 20,066), its longest Job interrupted by 4 entries (dispatch, timer, intake) of 11,273 cycles together; `control` 20,931 with 3 entries of 8,312; `echo` 10,427 with 2 of 5,213; `sensor` 346,849 with 5 of 14,348; `log` 206,681 with 3 of 8,482; `pulse` 14,275 with 3 of 9,727; `filter` 39,367 with 4 of 11,145; the timer entry at most 4,814, the dispatch entry 3,290, the intake 2,421. So about 30 % of `sampler`'s maximum is Kernel entries, and its spread from 20,066 to 37,815 is of the size of those entries, which is the reading (1) above measured on one Job. A dispatch entry that preempts the Job is counted whole, though the Job is charged it only up to the charge point, so the cycles are an upper figure for what the entries put in the Job's account. Not shown by this run: the Job of 41,221 cycles, which belongs to the hour-long configuration and was not re-run; its record will carry the same line when that run is next made. **R28.4 keeps the accounting as it is.** The Budget slot is armed in time while the Job executes, so an entry that switches nothing consumes the Job's Budget on the board as it consumes the measured execution: the record measures what the Kernel enforces, and a record without the entries would pass a Budget the Kernel would then report overrun. A Budget is therefore sized for the body and the entries that can fall inside it, and the record now says how many the longest Job had. The verify job's note for an R28.4 rejection no longer names the injected overruns as the cause, which it never checked: it says the rejection is expected of a Task whose overruns the configuration injects and is a finding to examine for any other.

**The Kernel-coverage configuration's intermittent Overrun, explained (2026-11-15).** `cargo xtask hil --coverage --kernel` failed in one of two runs on the third pass's Kernel and in the first run on the fourth pass's, each time on an unexpected Overrun of `echo` at about 1.06 ms against its 1 ms Budget in that configuration. The trace of the last failure shows the Job dispatched at 99,952.513 ms and three Kernel entries inside it before the Budget event that found the Overrun at 99,953.421 ms (timer entries at 952.601, 953.115 and 953.402 ms and an intake at 952.882 ms), each of about 250 µs in the instrumented Kernel. A Kernel entry that interrupts a Job is charged to that Job (R18.1's note, Δ_entry), so a Job of `echo` that three such entries interrupt consumes its Budget in Kernel time. The configuration's Budgets were set on 2026-11-06 at two to three times the plain demo's for the instrumented Kernel of that day; the Kernel has grown since (its record before today was of 2026-11-08). It is a property of the instrumented build's configuration, not of the Kernel: the plain configurations ran without an Overrun in every run of the day. Change: in `nrf52840_coverage` only (not analysed), `echo` 1.5 ms (1 ms), `pulse` 2 ms (1.5 ms), `filter` and `sampler` 3 ms (2 ms); with `echo` alone raised, the next run failed on `sampler` at 2.23 ms against 2 ms. With the four raised: three runs, three passes (RSK-OK, no Kernel event but the end of the operating duration). The stress configuration's `log` Overrun (11.6, deterministic per binary) is a different case and stays open.

**What remains (after the fourth pass; revised 2026-11-17, when the last transition modelled by hand went).** This is the list of what Property 18 over the Kernel's code is conditional on, with where each condition is discharged. Three kinds: proven elsewhere and not imported (the Verus facts, the configuration), measured (the Kernel sections, `J_rel`, the reading's distance from the event), and choices of the model (granularity, a section per served slot).

| Item | Detail |
|---|---|
| R18.2's wording for a Job preempted at its Budget instant | Closed 2026-11-15: R18.2 now states that Δ_detect is counted while the Job is the executing Job, and that an Overrun whose instant falls in the entry that pends a dispatch, or while the dispatch is pending, is reported within Δ_detect of the Job's resumption, the Job executing nothing in between (requirements.md, R18.2 with its note; Table 13-1's Δ_detect row; Property 10's statement in the design) |
| Transitions modelled by hand | None since 2026-11-17: every transition of the run that changes the Kernel's state carries the result of the extracted function (`periodic_release`, `deferred_release`, `deadline_miss`, `intake_release`, `dispatch`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `job_return`, `apply_response`, `fault`, `fault_of`, `record_event`, `budget_event`, `serve_slot`, `settle`, `observe`). What stays a choice of the model and is not derived: that each slot `timer_event` serves is an entry with a Kernel section of its own (the chain of served slots is from the code, `timer_event_served`; that the run gives each one a section and its conditions is the model's), the model-side conditions of each transition (which event awaited its entry, the section's bound, the Job's execution), and which entry of Kernel_Arch calls which function, read from the entry cores for an Endpoint operation and its intake only (`Sched/Endpoint.lean`) |
| The time base | The run's instants and the `now` the Kernel is given are not tied. This is what keeps the MIT spacing (and the nominal instants `O + k·T` the Kernel computes for periodic Releases) from being consequences of the code: "Releases at least `T` apart" and "an entry within `J_rel` of its instant" stay conditions **The code's half (2026-11-16, `Sched/Time.lean`; 34 theorems, no `sorry`, axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`):** `Kernel::now` is written by `Kernel::observe` alone and never moves back (`observe_now`); every function a served slot reaches leaves it, and leaves every nominal instant but for the one call of `Timing::advance_periodic` (`TimeKept`, through the responses and their loops); a timer entry therefore serves all its slots under one reading, the larger of the value it was given and the reading before, and each slot it serves has its instant at or before that reading (`timer_event_reading`); `advance_periodic` moves a Task's nominal instant by exactly its period or leaves everything (`advance_periodic_cases`), and `periodic_release` arms the release slot for that new instant, for this Task, or disarms it (`periodic_release_nominal`). So on the code a Periodic_Task's Releases are made at readings at or after instants exactly `T` apart. Still not joined to the run: the run's own instants are not these readings, the release slot's instant is tied to the nominal instant across the other entries only in Verus (`release_slots_consistent`), and the reading an entry is given is the hardware's (by inspection, and the rig's J_rel). Four mutants of the extracted code registered, each run alone and killed. **Joined for the Periodic_Tasks' arrivals (2026-11-16, later; `Sched/Arrive.lean`, 349 lines, 14 theorems, no `sorry`, the same three axioms).** `TStep` is a run in which the arrival of a Periodic_Task's Job carries no spacing condition: it is at the Task's nominal instant in the Kernel's state (in the run's units, `tk` per tick), and that instant has not arrived before. `treach_kreach` shows every such run is a run of `KRun.lean`, the spacing `KStep.jarrive` asks being a consequence: the last arrival lies a whole number of periods before the present nominal instant (`ArrInv`), because every transition keeps each nominal instant or moves it by its Task's period (`kstep_nomrel`). That holds from the code for every transition that is one of the Kernel's functions (`TimeKept` extended to `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `settle`; `intake_release` keeps the nominal instants and moves the reading; `periodic_release` of any Task) and is a condition for the four transitions modelled by hand and the safe state's entry from a free Kernel state (`HandShape`), which fix the scheduler state only. `property18_timed` is Property 18 over the timed run. What it still assumes of time: that an arrival is the nominal instant (a definition of the model, with J_rel covering the distance to the entry), that the analysis's period is not longer than the configured one (`TimeBase.hT`; R29.8 rounds down), the hand-modelled transitions' condition, and for Sporadic_Tasks the spacing as before (their arrivals are the environment's). Three mutants registered, each run alone and killed (an arrival allowed to repeat an instant; the hand-modelled transitions' condition dropped; the analysis's period allowed to exceed the configured one) |
| Verus facts not joined | Of the two: the Budget slot's ownership is proven on the extracted code since 2026-11-17 (`Sched/Slots.lean`, `kreachb_bo`, `budget_slot_top`), so "a Budget event's own entry is of the armed slot of the Job on top" is a consequence of the code; "in a state an entry's last step left" stays the entry cores' Verus postcondition. The deadline slot's disarm is not joined, and "a deadline event has an entry only on a miss" did not hold of the Kernel when a deferred Release was held at a Job's end (the finding of 2026-11-17, closed by a Kernel change the same night); the invariant that makes the Job's incompleteness a consequence of the code (outside the safe state an armed deadline slot belongs to a Task with a Release pending or a Job in progress) is proven in Lean since the same night (`Sched/Deadline.lean`, `kreachs_di`, `deadline_slot_incomplete`); that the deadline has passed in the run's time stays the model's. "One Budget event per Job" is not a Verus fact and stays a condition |
| The measured conditions | Each Kernel section within its Kernel_Timing_Parameter and each entry within `J_rel` stay measurements (11.13) |
| Kernel_Arch | The stubs and the hardware are not in the run: the run is of the `rsk-logic` functions the entry cores call. The entry cores are extracted since 2026-11-17 and two of them are read (`endpoint_send_core`, `endpoint_recv_core`: what they do to the Kernel); the others (`timer_core`, `intake_core`, `dispatch_job_core`, the fault cores) are joined to the run by their Verus contracts and by inspection. Since 2026-11-27 every entry core's writes to the Kernel are proven a sequence of the functions the run's transitions carry (`Sched/Trace.lean`, `coreret_trace`); which ones and in what order is `Sched/Script.lean` and `Sched/EntryRun.lean` (`coreret_ksh`); the model's side is one assumption for each call (`Follows`, `exec_follows`). The Task of a dispatch is the NVIC's choice and is joined to the hardware model's statement (`Sched/NvicRun.lean`, `execn_kreach`). The stubs have their own machine model in Verus |
| The configuration | That the Kernel's configuration and the Analyzer's inputs name the same Tasks with the same Priorities is a hypothesis, not derived from the Generator (the Config_Checker checks the values of R26.2 on the binary, 8.2) |
| Granularity | Time in whole units; Kernel sections change the Kernel's state at their beginning |

## 8. Tool qualification (R51)

### 8.1 Classification (R51.1, R51.2)

DO-178C Table 12-1 (RN-38): criterion 1 gives TQL-1 at DAL A and TQL-2 at DAL B; criterion 2 gives TQL-4 at both; criterion 3 gives TQL-5 at both.

| Tool | Criterion | TQL at DAL A / DAL B | Justification | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qualified_Toolchain (Ferrocene, LLVM, linker) | 1 | TQL-1 / TQL-2 | Its output is the airborne code | Upstream rustc 1.95.0 until P5; Ferrocene supports up to DAL C (RN-39), ORQ-04, GAP-01 |
| Generator (rsk-macros, rsk-gen) | none, if its output is verified (8.2); otherwise 1 | none (otherwise TQL-1 / TQL-2) | Config_Checker, Kernel boot validation, and review verify its output | Config_Checker built (task 13.1) and run on every Flight_Build; the argument holds for the values of R26.2 (8.2), the mutation suite (13.2) having shown its detection ability (16 of 16); the outputs listed in 8.2 stay on review |
| Verus ghost-erasure macro (verus_builtin_macros) | 1, unless the erased source is shown equal to the verified code (R55.3) | TQL-1 / TQL-2 | It produces the source that becomes airborne code | No comparison yet (task 23.1) |
| Config_Checker | 2 | TQL-4 / TQL-4 | Its verdict removes the need to qualify the Generator (R26.6) | Built (task 13.1) and run on every Flight_Build (R26.5) with the mutation suite of R26.7 (task 13.2), 2026-10-23 |
| Link_Checker | 2 for stack bounds; 3 for the defence-in-depth scans | TQL-4 / TQL-4 (stack bounds); TQL-5 / TQL-5 (scans) | Stack bounds replace stack-usage testing | Stack bounds, the instruction-class and symbol scans, the Core_Subset mapping, and the no-panic-path check built (tasks 12.1 to 12.3, 2026-10-23) and run on every Flight_Build; each check has a fixture that produces its violation (`scan::tests`) |
| Profile_Lint | 3 | TQL-5 / TQL-5 | Backed by LK checks and compile errors | Built (task 11.1, 2026-10-23): `rsk-lint` (source lint over `syn`) and the Clippy deny-lists, run on every Flight_Build (verify step 8c); one UI fixture per lint and the configuration-bypass test |

| UPPAAL | 3 | TQL-5 / TQL-5 | Cross-check only; no credit claimed; licence ASM-13 | Exporter and corpus exist (task 16, section 8.7); the SRP ceiling is approximated by the blocking interval; first verifier run 2026-11-14 (section 8.7) |

| Verus | 2 where proofs replace tests | TQL-4 / TQL-4 | 8.3 | In use, unqualified |
| Kani | 2 where credit is claimed | TQL-4 / TQL-4 | 8.3 | In use, unqualified |
| App_Prover (Creusot) | 2 | TQL-4 / TQL-4 | Replaces robustness and requirements-based testing of Task bodies | P2 |
| Coverage tool | 3 | TQL-5 / TQL-5 | | P3, ORQ-05 |
| HIL_Rig test automation (`rsk-hil`) | 3 | TQL-5 / TQL-5 | Its verdicts rest on the Kernel's own records and the analyzer capture, both read back and checked against each other (11.2) | Built (task 7.1, 2026-11-01): the self-test, the suites of section 11, the record writers; each suite's pass criteria are in its report |
| Flashing tool (probe-rs 0.32.0) | none: output verified | none | Read-back hash check (R48.4) | In use since 2026-10-29; every flash read back and hashed against the Build_Manifest (11.2) |
| Build_System (xtask) | 3 | TQL-5 / TQL-5 | It aggregates verdicts and could fail to detect a failure; rebuild comparison (R59.3) and archived logs back it | In use |

Qualification is per project; rsk tools can at most be qualifiable, and qualification itself is an applicant activity (R51.6, R54.4).

### 8.2 The Generator needs no TQL-1 or TQL-2 (R51.3)

The argument: every Generator output that reaches the Flight_Build is either recomputed and compared by the Config_Checker from the Task_Model and the linked binary (R26.2, R26.4), checked at boot by the proven `Config::validate` and `Layout::validate` (well-formedness, not equality to the declaration), or covered by review. The mutation suite of R26.7 (task 13.2) is the planned evidence of the Config_Checker's detection ability. Since 2026-10-23 the Config_Checker exists (`rsk-confcheck`, task 13.1) and runs on every Flight_Build (verify step 8b): on the P1 demo it compares 197 values of every R26.2 class with the Task_Model and the `CONFIG` and `LAYOUT` images and their checksums with the binary. The mutation suite (task 13.2, `docs/config_mutations.toml`) alters a Task_Model or a binary in each class and every one of its 16 mutants is reported, so the detection ability the argument needs is demonstrated on the P1 demo; the Profile_Conformance_Suite Application (task 14) is to replace the demo as its subject. Outside the argument still: the stack region sizes (Link_Checker, task 12) and the outputs listed below.

Generator outputs that R26.2 as written does not cover, which need an extended Config_Checker or review:

- the dispatch-vector assignment and its order (DD-03; the ascending order of the built-in Target tables is only unit-tested);
- the intake IRQ table and the `binding` function from exception numbers to Tasks;
- the `task_entry` table from Task index to Job entry function (a wrong entry runs the wrong code);
- periods, offsets, and deadlines (R26.2 names the Budget and MIT tables only);
- `System` constants: `TIMER_VECTOR`, `IRQ_COUNT`, `PRIORITY_BITS` (the last is also boot-checked), stack bounds and the safe-state path;
- linker-script archive placement, beyond what the memory-map comparison of R16.7 checks;
- the Partition Context types (type-level; wrong types fail compilation or show in review);
- `summary.txt` (informational, no verification needed).

### 8.3 Verus and Kani (R51.4)

- Verus: credit claimed for the Kernel logic properties of section 3 (compliance of the logic source with its formalized low-level requirements, freedom from overflow, bounds errors, and panics) and for the Analyzer fixed-point core. That credit replaces verification activities, so Verus is criterion 2, TQL-4. No DO-330 qualification of Verus is known (ORQ-06). The Vermilion Lean 4 back end (RN-15) is followed as a route to independent proof checking.
- Kani: credit claimed only for bounded absence of UB in the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's register path down to the volatile instruction (6.1) and as a cross-check of the Verus postconditions on erased code. Criterion 2, TQL-4 where that credit removes review; no qualification known.

### 8.4 Analyzer (R51.5)

Criterion 3, TQL-5, while its result serves only schedulability verification and the HIL response-time tests stay. If those tests are dropped on the strength of its result, criterion 2, TQL-4.

### 8.5 Independence (R51.7)

| Verifying tool | Verified item | Tool independence | People independence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Config_Checker | Generator | Required by R26.1 and met by construction (task 13.1): `rsk-confcheck` depends on serde_json and the shared ELF reader only, never on rsk-gen, rsk-model, rsk-logic, or rsk-kernel; it reads the Task_Model with its own parser and restates the Kernel's table encoding | Not achieved: one author. Applicant activity |
| Kani cross-checks | Verus proofs | Different technique (bounded model checking on erased code) | Same author writes the specification and the harness |
| Creusot proofs | Partition Task bodies against the `rsk` API contracts | Different prover and solvers from the Kernel's (Why3 with alt-ergo, z3, cvc4, cvc5) | Same author writes the API contracts, the bodies, and the Kernel they trust (task 17.1) |
| HIL differential tests | Hardware_Model | Silicon against model (run 2026-11-02, 11.8: one test per rule, HW-12 refuted and revised) | The rules and the tests have one author; the silicon is the independent party |
| Analyzer reference implementation, simulator | Analyzer | A reference implementation of the R29 equations and a unit-time SRP simulator, both in `crates/rsk-analyzer/tests/properties.rs` (task 15.4); written by the same author as the Analyzer, in a different style, so the independence is of code, not of person | Partly achieved |
| Link_Checker | Stack and call graph | Differential run against `cargo-call-stack` (task 12.1): not run, the tool is not vendored (R58.1) and needs a nightly compiler; the decoder's encodings are unit-tested against hand-assembled instructions, and the bounds are checked against stack painting on the HIL_Rig since 2026-11-16 (11.15: every region's deepest use within its bound on the short stress configuration; one-sided, a use above a bound refutes it, a use below does not prove it) | Not achieved for the call graph (no second tool); the bounds have the silicon as the independent party |

Every DAL A and DAL B verification objective that requires independence needs a reviewer other than the author. The prototype cannot supply that; Gap_Register entry GAP-08 records it as an applicant activity (R54.4). Which Annex A objectives require independence at each level is to be confirmed against the standard (Gap_Register OPEN-01).

### 8.7 UPPAAL cross-check (R31, R32.6, ORQ-22; task 16, 2026-10-26)

The UPPAAL_Exporter (`rsk-analyzer::uppaal`) writes, for every analysis, the model (`model.xml`, UPPAAL's XML) and the query file (`model.q`; R31.1). The model (`rsk-uppaal/3`; `rsk-uppaal/2` had no urgent dispatch, see the corpus result below): one release generator per Task, periodic (exactly every period) or sporadic (at least the MIT after the previous release, any later instant), each followed by a jitter of up to J_rel; one Task automaton per Task with the SRP start condition (no Job of higher or equal Priority and lower identifier executing, Priority above the System_Ceiling; ties by R7.3), a blocking interval of up to B_i before the Job starts, a stopwatch execution bounded by E_i (Budget plus the overheads of R29.1) that progresses only while the Task executes, resumption of the highest-Priority preempted Task on completion, and a deadline-miss location (R31.2); the query `A[] not (T0.Missed || ...)` is satisfied exactly when no deadline-miss location is reachable (R31.3); every automaton's Task_Model identifier is recorded in the declaration (R31.7); the reader `read_export` recovers each Task's Priority, period or MIT, deadline, and execution-time bound (R31.8; Property 15, tested). Two approximations are recorded in every report's omitted terms (R31.2), and a third since 2026-11-06 where a Sporadic_Task exists (the intake term of R19.2 on the Tasks above the source's Task is in the RTA only): Critical_Sections and the System_Ceiling are represented by the blocking interval, not by locations that raise the ceiling, and the Kernel overheads of R29.3 are folded into E_i rather than modelled as locations. Where `verifyta` is installed the Analyzer runs the query under a time limit and an address-space limit (`--uppaal-timeout`, `--uppaal-memory`; R31.9), records the verifier's verdict and version in the report, and classifies the result against the RTA verdict (R31.4): agreement; pessimism (RTA FAIL, no miss reachable; R31.6); discrepancy (a reachable miss while RTA PASS; the trace is attached and marked as requiring confirmation, because stopwatch models are verified by over-approximation; R31.5); inconclusive (limit exceeded or no verdict; R31.9). A verifier that is absent leaves the status `not_run`, which is never counted as agreement.

The corpus (R32.6, PAR-03): `cargo xtask uppaal-corpus` generates 50 Task_Models deterministically from synthetic App_Declarations through the Generator itself (2 to 7 Tasks; PAR-03 proposes up to 8, but the Target tables offer 7 free dispatch vectors, DD-01; two Partitions; Priorities 1 to 7 with ties; periods 5 to 200 ms; deadlines at or below the period; up to two Resources per Partition with random accessors; about a third of the Tasks sporadic, released by Release_Signals), analyses each with placeholder timing, runs the cross-check where the verifier exists, and writes `docs/uppaal_corpus_report.md`; the verification job runs the same corpus (step "UPPAAL cross-check corpus", Evidence_Item `check-uppaal-corpus`), requires the committed report to equal the regenerated one, and gives the item `pass` only when no cross-check is a discrepancy without a disposition, `fail` on an undisposed discrepancy, and `inconclusive` when the verifier is absent or a run hit its limits. Dispositions are recorded in `docs/uppaal_dispositions.toml`, one per model, and the report prints them beside the discrepancy; this is the record R32.6 asks for. Status at 2026-10-26: UPPAAL is not installed on the development machine (a licensed download the project has not made), so the 50 exports exist, the RTA verdicts are 11 PASS and 39 FAIL, every cross-check is `not_run`, the item is `inconclusive`, and no discrepancy has been seen or disposed; the model's semantics have been checked by its reader and by inspection only, which is a limitation to clear on the first machine with UPPAAL (ORQ-22, GAP-19). The `-t0` option asks the verifier for the shortest trace on a reachable miss.

**First run with a verifier (2026-11-14).** UPPAAL 5.0.0 (`verifyta`, x86-64 under Rosetta, academic licence) was installed and the corpus run. The model as exported until then (`rsk-uppaal/2`) had never been checked and was wrong in two ways:

| Defect of `rsk-uppaal/2` | Effect | Change in `rsk-uppaal/3` |
|---|---|---|
| Nothing forced a released, eligible Task to start: it could wait in `Pending` with the processor idle until its deadline | A deadline miss was reachable in all 50 models, the 11 the Analyzer passes included; the cross-check distinguished nothing | The steps out of `Pending` and `Ready` synchronise on an urgent broadcast channel (the Hardware_Model's rule: the dispatch exception is taken before the preempted code continues) |
| The stopwatches were declared `hybrid clock`, which is for UPPAAL's statistical checker | With the first defect fixed, `verifyta` 5.0.0 and 5.1.0-beta5 crash (exit 139, 134) as soon as they explore past the first instant | Ordinary clocks with a rate of 0 or 1 in the invariant |

Three further changes came with them: a `Ready` location (a Task whose blocking interval ends while another Job holds the processor would otherwise stop time); the deadline clock starts at the nominal release instant, before the jitter, as `R_i = J_i + w_i` is measured; a Job that has executed for `EXEC` at its deadline completes and has not missed. With stopwatches `verifyta` checks an over-approximation: "satisfied" is a proof, and a violation found only in the over-approximation is reported as "may not be satisfied" without a trace; the cross-check files that as `discrepancy` (RTA PASS) or `agree` with the word "may" (RTA FAIL).

Result of `cargo xtask uppaal-corpus` (about 14 minutes; 120 s and 4096 MiB per model):

| RTA | Models | `verifyta` | Status |
|---|---:|---|---|
| PASS | 7 | No deadline miss reachable | `agree` |
| PASS | 2 | A miss may be reachable (m01, m49) | `discrepancy`, unconfirmed (`docs/uppaal_dispositions.toml`) |
| PASS | 2 | No answer in 120 s (m12, m18; six Tasks) | `inconclusive` |
| FAIL | 7 | A miss is reachable | `agree` |
| FAIL | 26 | A miss may be reachable | `agree` |
| FAIL | 2 | No miss reachable | `pessimism` |
| FAIL | 4 | No answer in 120 s | `inconclusive` |

What this is evidence of: for seven Task sets the Analyzer's PASS is confirmed by a second method on a model with interference, jitter and a blocking delay. What it is not: the two possible misses are neither confirmed nor refuted. The model gives every Job its own blocking delay of up to `B_i` before it starts, where the Analyzer charges blocking once per busy period, so the model can be more pessimistic than the Analyzer; that, the over-approximation, or an optimism of the Analyzer are the three explanations, and none has been established.

## 9. Formal-methods credit (R52)

### 9.1 Soundness justification (R52.1)

| Method | Semantics and trusted base | Known issues and how rsk handles them |
|---|---|---|
| Verus (Kernel, Hardware_Model, Analyzer core) | SMT encoding of a Rust subset; trusts Verus, Z3 4.16.0, and `vstd` with its `assume_specification` items (TBR-04) | No `assume` or `admit` in the Kernel crates or rsk-analyzer, and eight `external_body` items, every one in the assumption annex (R50.2 gate; "no assumption construct" until 2026-11-13, audit finding 16); ghost-erasure cfg flags can introduce unsoundness (RN-12): rsk never sets `verus_keep_ghost` itself (only the Verus toolchain does), and Flight_Builds compile the erased code; no abort specification (RN-13, 5.6) |
| Kani | CBMC bounded model checking of MIR for the host; trusts Kani, CBMC 6.11.0, the SAT solver, and the `sim` double (TBR-07) | RN-17 issues in 6.1; `--restrict-vtable` unused; the host build replaces the volatile leaf by the stub, which asserts the access preconditions (6.1) |
| Creusot | Translation to Coma and Why3, SMT solvers (TBR-08) | In use since 2026-10-23 (`proof-app-creusot`, section 7); soundness of the Why3 translation is the residual risk; since 2026-11-06 the job proves every configuration twice, with alt-ergo and z3 only and then, on the same translation, with cvc5 and cvc4 only (`-P` of why3find; `--only prove --no-cache` for the second run), and the Evidence_Item passes only when both runs prove, so every goal is discharged by two solvers of disjoint sets and a defect of one solver cannot pass a goal alone; the Evidence_Item's note counts the goals per solver |
| Protocol_Checker | Projection with full merge and the universal buffer bound over every interleaving (RN-22's theory; the bound deviates from k-MC on purpose, design Component F; task 21.1) | P4 |
| UPPAAL | Timed automata model checking | No credit claimed; the exported model approximates the SRP ceiling by the blocking interval (recorded in every report, R31.2); first verifier run 2026-11-14, two possible misses on passing models unconfirmed (section 8.7) |
| Static_WCET_Analyzer | Binary-level analysis | P5 |

### 9.2 Validating models and formal specifications (R52.2)

- **Hardware_Model.** Every rule cites its manual section (R49.2); the not-formalized list states why no proof depends on each omission (R49.6); the HIL differential test per rule (R49.3) ran on 2026-11-02 (11.8): thirteen rules as stated, HW-12's constant refuted by the silicon and revised, and the untested parts of HW-04, HW-09, and HW-14 dispositioned. QEMU runs are pre-hardware checks only.
- **Specifications against requirements.** Every Verus specification names the criteria it formalizes in its doc comment. A review of each specification against the natural-language criterion is open (section 13).
- **Vacuity checks.** During 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-14 each new specification was checked by breaking the code or model and confirming that Verus fails:

| Change | Failed specification |
|---|---|
| Reverse the dispatch-vector order | `lemma_dispatch_selects_srp_choice` (tie step) |
| `encode_level(0)` = 256 | `lemma_encoding_order`, `lemma_kernel_level_never_masked` |
| Count the shared code as Partition-owned | `lemma_hw_partition_isolation` |
| Let the MPU grant every write | `lemma_mpu_grant_covered` |
| Kill only one Job of a restarted Partition; drop a deadline disarm | `end_partition_jobs`, `job_return` postconditions |
| Kill other Partitions' Jobs; reset a neighbouring Budget | `others_unchanged` (R14.9) |
| Clear a pending Release on discard; make a deferred Release due now; defer a periodic Release | `intake`, `deferred_release`, `Kernel::inv` |

Since 2026-10-22 these checks are fixtures: `docs/proof_mutations.toml` registers nine mutations (the seven above, two of them in equivalent form: `shared-code-writable` for the shared-code row and `restart-kills-one-job` for the kill row, plus two for `arch::bound`), and `cargo xtask mutations` applies each to a copy of the tree, runs Verus, and fails unless the mutant is rejected with the registered diagnostic; `cargo xtask mutations --check` (part of the repository checks) keeps the register applicable to the current sources. The run is not part of `cargo xtask verify` (nine Verus runs); it is run before a Verification_Plan revision and recorded here. Run of 2026-10-22 (Verus 0.2026.10.04.426d8b0): all nine mutants killed, each by the registered diagnostic (`dispatch-vector-order`, `kernel-level-encoding`, `shared-code-writable`, `mpu-grants-every-write`, `restart-kills-one-job`, `kill-other-partitions`, `discard-clears-release`, `bound-dispatch-unguarded`, `basepri-lower-level`). Not reproduced from the manual list: the dropped deadline disarm, the deferred Release made due now, and the deferred periodic Release; they are to be added to the register. **2026-11-05:** the register holds 24 mutations; `periodic-release-not-recorded` (the Release step of a due nominal instant dropped) was added with the Property 7 contract of `timer_event` and run with `cargo xtask mutations --only periodic-release-not-recorded`: killed, by the `release_step` assertion of `periodic_release` that `periodic_release_served` needs. The full run of all 24 is due before the next revision.

Since 2026-11-09 the register also takes App_Prover mutations (`prover = "creusot"`, the package a proof member of `proofs/creusot`; the mutant is killed when `cargo creusot -p <member>` fails with the registered text): the first is `roles-send-label-word-dropped`, the generated send without its label word, killed by the role module's `sent` contract (5.15); the second, `gen-ms-contract-dropped`, removes the generated `rsk_target::ms`'s contract in the Generator and is killed at `vc_log` by the `part1-stress` member, whose body divides by `ms(40)` (7). The Kani harnesses were validated the same way on 2026-11-06, by hand (the register's Verus and App_Prover kinds do not reach Kani): the two defects the restructuring of 6.1 found were replayed as mutants of `hw.rs`, the NVIC line as `n.saturating_sub(16)` (caught by `uj_001_mmio_addresses_valid` at `irq_of(n).is_none()` and by the stub's own assertion in `write_u8` and `write`) and `dma_program` guarded by `plausible` (caught by `apply_every_action_is_total` at its write count and by the stub's assertion). Both runs under the job's flags; neither mutant survives.

### 9.3 Property preservation to object code (R52.3, ORQ-08)

1. Verus checks the `verus!` source, ghost code included, on rustc 1.98.1.
2. Ghost erasure removes spec and proof code; the same source files then build for the Target with upstream rustc 1.95.0 in every verification job (Ferrocene 26.05 in P5). Equality of the erased executable code to the verified code rests on `verus_builtin_macros` (criterion 1, 8.1). Mitigations: the Kani cross-check harnesses run the erased code against restated postconditions (bounded); archiving and comparing the erased source (R55.3) is planned for task 23.1.
3. rustc 1.98.1 and 1.95.0 differ. The Kernel uses no feature stabilized after 1.95.0, which the 1.95.0 build enforces; the remaining assumption is that both compile the subset with the same semantics (ASM-09, TBR-01).
4. LLVM optimization: no translation validation (ORQ-08 open). Mitigations: requirements-based HIL tests on the binary (section 11; the HIL builds carry the `debug-port`, `quiet`, `trace-ram`, and `trace-pins` features, so they are the Flight_Build's source with the instrumentation of 11.12 added, not the Flight_Build binary itself, which is a residual) and source-to-object traceability (R53.2, P3).
5. The Build_Manifest binary hash (R33.3) ties every Evidence_Item to the binary tested.

### 9.4 Objectives claimed and testing that remains (R52.4)

The project does not hold the DO-178C or DO-333 texts. The Gap_Register (`docs/gap_register.toml`) lists every DO-178C Annex A objective by table and number, as public summaries give them, with its applicability, status, owner, and gaps; the DO-333 items are described, not numbered. Both are to be confirmed against the standards' texts (Gap_Register OPEN-01, OPEN-02). The Annex A column below uses the Gap_Register's numbering.

| Objective (description) | Annex A | Formal credit claimed | Testing or review that remains |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source code complies with the low-level requirements | A-5.1 | Kernel logic and Analyzer core: the Verus specifications are the formalized low-level requirements | Kernel_Arch, unsafe module, and `rsk-entry`: review and HIL (GAP-10) |
| Source code is accurate and consistent (overflow, exception handling, stack, timing) | A-5.6 | Overflow, bounds, and panic freedom of the logic | Stack (Link_Checker), timing (WCET), Kernel_Arch panics (5.6; GAP-12, GAP-13, GAP-04) |
| Partitioning integrity is confirmed | A-4.13 | MPU decision (P-16), R14.9 (P-18), Kernel never masked (P-17) | HIL access matrix, DMA, fault injection (GAP-05, GAP-06, GAP-09) |
| Low-level requirements are accurate and consistent | A-4.2 | Verus checks that specifications are well formed | Review against natural-language criteria; vacuity fixtures (GAP-18) |
| Executable object code complies with the low-level requirements, including robustness | A-6.3, A-6.4 | Logic properties carried to object code through 9.3 | HIL requirements-based and robustness tests on the Target (GAP-09, GAP-11) |
| Executable object code is compatible with the target computer | A-6.5 | none | HIL testing in full (GAP-09) |
| Verification coverage of requirements and of structure | A-7.3 to A-7.8 | Formal coverage of criteria (9.5) | Structural coverage: no objective replaced in v1 (R53.4); MC/DC tooling open (ORQ-05, GAP-02) |

"Complete" means the criterion is stated as a proven specification with no gap at the logic level; the hardware realisation still rests on TBR-02, TBR-05, and section 4.

all five the contract of `timer_event`, 2026-11-05); R10.1, R10.2 (per event, and spacing over a run by `lemma_mit_spacing_over_run`, task 4.14), R10.3 (within the threshold), R10.4 (the count since instant 0 or the Partition's last restart over the run, `violations_linked` and the violation and restart steps of the history; the response exactly when it exceeds the threshold, `lemma_r10_4_count`; 2026-11-05), R10.7 (attribution to the given Partition); R12.3 (tables); R14.5 (effects), R14.6, R14.7, R14.9 (no Endpoints yet); R44.8 |

## 10. HAL and PAC strategy for Partitions (R58.3, ORQ-21)

- Partition crates keep `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` (PR-20); the Build_System checks every crate root.
- Peripheral access goes through rsk drivers over the PAC. Third-party crates with `unsafe` (PAC, HAL) are allowed as dependencies of a Partition only with a Trust_Base_Register entry per crate and version (R58.2, R58.5, TBR-13); the dependency gate enforces the entries.
- The MPU confines their effects to the Partition's own peripheral windows (P-16). The Generator places only declared, Partition-owned peripherals in windows and rejects Kernel-owned ones (PR-29); since 2026-11-05 the layout also carries the PPB and every Kernel-owned peripheral of the Target table as Kernel peripheral intervals, which the boot validation keeps apart from every Partition interval and the proof keeps outside every view (R16.2), and the Config_Checker compares with its own table and with the linked binary's `LAYOUT` image (R16.7).
- EasyDMA pointer and length registers are written only through the Kernel's mediation service (DD-08, task 5.5, built 2026-10-23: `rsk::DmaBuf::lend` over SVC 6 and `dma_request_core`), because a DMA master bypasses the MPU (ORQ-03). The service accepts a channel of the executing Job's own Partition and a buffer in that Partition's RAM above its stack region and within enabled subregions (R17.1, `PartitionLayout::dma_permitted`); anything else is refused before any register is written and applies the Partition's fault response (R17.2, `EV_DMA_REJECTED`). List-mode registers are cleared at boot and never written; PPI channels are cleared at boot (true since 2026-11-13: until then the boot write went to CHENSET and enabled all 32, audit finding 3; boot check bit 14 reads PPI.CHEN back); the windows of EasyDMA-capable peripherals are mapped read-only to their owner (true since the same date: until then the Kernel's layout did not carry the flag and the owner could write the pointer registers itself, audit finding 1; `Layout::lemma_ro_window_not_writable`, boot check bit 15); only the SPIM class is mediated (finding 10); a stop or restart triggers every stop task of the Partition's channels before its memory is re-initialized (R17.6). The channel table is Generated_Config's `DMA` (one entry per channel of every EasyDMA-capable peripheral a Partition owns, from the Target table's register offsets).
- The p1-demo uses no peripherals, so no HAL or PAC crate is in the Flight_Build graph yet.

## 11. Hardware evidence

Written on 2026-10-14 as the list of hardware-pending items, when no nRF52840-DK was available; the first board was connected on 2026-10-29 and the rig built on 2026-11-01 (11.2), and every item of the table has since run (the bold notes in each row and sections 11.1 to 11.12). The table is kept as the index of the HIL evidence, each row naming the claims that rest on it and the pre-hardware evidence that stood in. QEMU results are recorded as pre-hardware evidence and never as their substitute; QEMU mps2-an386 differs from the Target in its timer (CMSDK, not the nRF TIMER), priority bits (8, not 3), cycle counting (none), EasyDMA (absent), and errata (not modelled). What QEMU is trusted for is Trust_Base_Register entry TBR-23; the open gap is Gap_Register entry GAP-09.

How the hardware evidence came about (the three preparatory items of the first draft, kept for the record): each test's procedure and the outcome the Hardware_Model predicts were written before the rig existed; the `rsk-hil` orchestration was dry-run against QEMU where QEMU models the behaviour; and the dates boards became available are recorded here. **2026-10-29: the first nRF52840-DK is connected** (one board; the stimulus board is missing). **2026-11-01: the HIL_Rig is built** (section 11.2): three boards, the target and the stimulus wired to each other and to the logic analyzer, the spare unwired. probe-rs 0.32.0 flashes and runs the demo's `nrf52840` declaration variant (`cargo xtask hil`; job step "HIL: dispatch spike", item `hil-spike-p1-demo`). Status: **`RSK-OK` on the board since 2026-10-29** (section 11.1): the demo's seven Tasks run on silicon with the counts, the blocking, the FP state, and the EasyDMA requests the report requires, and no fault or deadline miss; the job's step "HIL: dispatch spike" passes when the board is attached.

### 11.1 First results on the nRF52840-DK (2026-10-29)

- **HFCLK.** The Kernel starts the crystal oscillator at boot (DD-05: the time base runs from it); the RC oscillator's HFCLKSTAT.STATE already reads running, so the boot waits on EVENTS_HFCLKSTARTED. Before the fix, boot check bit 10 (R12.1, ASM-05) failed and the safe state reported it: the check does its job.
- **Access port protection.** This silicon enables APPROTECT at every reset. A HIL build (feature `debug-port`, never a Flight_Build feature) writes APPROTECT.DISABLE first thing at boot; the persistent UICR.APPROTECT = HwDisabled is the operator's one-time setting. A Flight_Build leaves the port protected, which is the production setting.
- **Watchdog path.** The first full run locked up and the Kernel-owned watchdog reset the chip; the next boot logged EV_WATCHDOG_RESET, boot check bit 7, EV_SAFE_STATE, and reported through the semihosting console as ORQ-31 specifies. `hil-watchdog`'s claim is observed, not yet as a designed test.
- **The "lockup" was the time base.** The nRF TIMER0 compare is absolute: a compare instant the counter has already passed matches only after the 32-bit wrap, and the Kernel's minimum compare distance of 2 ticks (125 ns) is below the time the write itself takes, so the first compare (instant 0) never fired, no tick fed the watchdog, and the watchdog reset the chip. The nRF driver now pends the time-base interrupt when the instant has passed by the time the compare is written; the QEMU timer is armed with a relative distance and never showed it (HW-12 holds for both, by different means).
- **Semihosting halts the core.** Every semihosting call stops the CPU while the probe services it, with the time base running; three boot trace lines consumed the 200 ms operating duration, and the Tasks' one-byte prints would have cost Budgets and deadlines. HIL builds carry the `quiet` feature (no prints during the run; the report after the operating end is unaffected), and the Kernel's `trace` feature is unusable on a board under a watchdog.
- **A real MPU caught a real layout defect.** The Job wrappers of `echo` and `pulse`, small once quiet, were copied by cross-crate inlining into the system crate's object and placed in `.kernel.text`; the Jobs fetched Kernel code and the MPU raised an instruction access violation (CFSR.IACCVIOL) at each release, each a Partition restart. QEMU never faulted. The Link_Checker's R16.3 check reports exactly this on the board's binary (two violations), which the job had not run on that binary; the Generator now emits `#[inline(never)]` on every Job wrapper, and the check is clean. Diagnosed through a new Kernel record of the first MemManage fault (CFSR and the stacked PC, printed by the demo's report).
- **Durations in ticks are board-specific.** The demo's busy waits were tuned for the QEMU board's 25 MHz time base and 250,000-iteration spin; on the nRF52840 `log` overran its Budget and `sampler` starved. The Generator now emits each Partition's `rsk_target::{TICK_HZ, CPU_HZ, ms}` and the demo states its waits in milliseconds.
- **The debugger-attached event is information**, not a fault, in the demo's pass criterion (ORQ-30); QEMU never raised it.
- **Bench procedure.** After a power cycle or any reset outside a probe session the access port is locked; the operator unlocks it once with probe-rs's `--allow-erase-all` run (the board holds nothing else), after which the HIL build's APPROTECT.DISABLE write at boot and the fed watchdog in the safe state keep the port open between runs, and `cargo xtask hil` attaches without erasing. UICR.APPROTECT = HwDisabled was written as well but did not by itself open the port on this silicon. **2026-11-06:** both are needed on this silicon. A `--allow-erase-all` unlock erases the UICR too, after which every HIL run left the port locked at its reboot although it wrote APPROTECT.DISABLE; restoring UICR.APPROTECT = HwDisabled (0x5A at 0x10001208, programmed through probe-rs's flash loader: `probe-rs download --binary-format bin --base-address 0x10001208` of the four bytes `5A 00 00 00`) made the runs keep the port open again. A probe-rs run killed mid-way leaves the board locked; the rig's runs are left to end.
- **Open until 2026-11-17, closed.** The Link_Checker and the Config_Checker run on every HIL build in the job since that date (the step "HIL builds", section 12's last paragraphs; the Analyzer already ran on the measurement configurations with their records, 11.14). `hil-watchdog` and `hil-boot-checks` are designed tests since 2026-11-04 (11.9: the watchdog started and never fed is a case of the boot-check suite). The Build_Manifest entry for the HIL build was added with the rig (section 11.2).

### 11.2 The HIL_Rig (2026-11-01, task 7.1)

**The rig.** Three PCA10056 v3.0.3 boards (nRF52840 AAF0) and an fx2lafw logic analyzer (Saleae Logic compatible, 8 channels, 24 MHz at most) driven by sigrok-cli 0.7.2, described in `hil-rig.toml` at the workspace root (schema `rsk-hil-rig/1`): the target board A, the stimulus board B, and the spare C (listed, not wired). Every probe-rs command names its board by J-Link serial (`VID:PID:SERIAL`); `probe-rs list` orders probes arbitrarily, and with three attached a positional choice would flash the wrong board. The rig's code is `rsk-hil` (R34.8 orchestration: the rig description, probe selection, flashing with the read-back hash check, the stimulus board, the analyzer capture and its edge analysis, the Kernel trace, the self-test) and the HIL steps of `cargo xtask verify`; `cargo xtask hil` runs the self-test by hand and `cargo xtask hil --spike-only` the single-board run of task 3.1.

**Wiring**, measured rather than assumed: a discovery pass drove each header pin of one board in turn while the other board's inputs and the analyzer were read. Board to board, the same pin on both DKs: P1.01 (Arduino D0), the stimulus line, and P1.02 to P1.04 as spares. Stimulus board to analyzer: P1.05 (D4) to the analyzer's D6, the mirror of the stimulus line, driven in the same register write as P1.01 so that the mirror carries the edge without skew (the analyzer cannot see the board-to-board wire itself); P1.06 (D5) to D7, spare. Target to analyzer: P1.10 (D8) to D0, the response line, and P1.11 to P1.15 (D9 to D13) to D1 to D5, spares for Kernel trace pins later. Common ground across both boards and the analyzer.

**The stimulus board runs rsk.** The stimulus firmware is an rsk Application (`apps/hil-stimulus`: a declaration, one Partition owning the GPIO window, one periodic Task every 18 ms that raises the stimulus line for 100 µs and blinks LED1 every 28 pulses, a system crate whose safe state idles with the watchdog fed, and a binary), so no bench firmware has unsafe code outside the Kernel, and the Kernel itself runs on a second board. The entry crate binds exactly one Application per build, selected by its `app-*` cargo feature (`app-p1-demo` by default, `app-hil-stimulus` for the stimulus); the stimulus binary is `hil-stimulus = true` in the policy, built by the HIL steps in its own cargo invocation and never in the Target build or as a Flight_Build. Its operating duration is 2 hours, which covers PAR-05. The target's `pulse` Task is now released by GPIOTE channel 0 on the rising edge of P1.01 (the nRF52840 declaration variant; TIMER1 stood in before the rig) and toggles the response line in every Job.

**The Build_Manifest records the HIL builds** (`hil_binaries`: package, features, hash over the loadable image, loadable bytes, Generated_Config checksum), built in the manifest step whether or not a board is attached; the rig reads back every loadable part of each image from the board after flashing (the target's after its run, because probe-rs `run` is the flashing step and `attach` does not reset) and hashes the read-back exactly as the manifest hashes the ELF, so the two agree only when every byte agrees (R48.4).

**The self-test** (the evidence of task 7.1, item `hil-rig`, artifacts `hil-rig-self-test.md`, `hil-rig-edges.txt`, `hil-rig-trace.txt`): the boards by serial (the target and the stimulus required; a probe not in the rig is reported); the analyzer seen by sigrok; the stimulus image flashed, read back, hashed, and the board reset; the analyzer capturing the stimulus mirror and the response line while the target image is flashed and run with the console, then stopped (SIGINT; sigrok-cli writes what it has); the target image read back and hashed; the Kernel's trace read and checked for an interrupt storm; and the capture against the console: each response edge matched to the latest stimulus rise before it, the run being the last N response edges where N is the `pulse` count the target reported (earlier edges are the line floating while the target is flashed), every stimulus rise inside the run answered before the next one and within the Task's 15 ms deadline, the stimulus period within 2 % of 18 ms. Unattended: every command is bounded, a locked access port or an absent board is a verdict, not a hang, and nothing waits for an operator. First passing run: 10 pulses answered, latencies 66.5 to 7,211.5 µs with a median of 88 µs (the slow ones are pulses arriving while `sensor` holds `state` at Ceiling 7, answered at the unlock), 515 stimulus pulses seen at 18,000 µs with a 107 µs width.

**Findings on the rig** (each recorded where it belongs):

- **An interrupt storm at the end of the operating duration (Kernel defect, fixed).** With the stimulus at a random phase to the schedule, about one run in eight ended with `control`'s Job at 190 ms missing its deadline. The Kernel's RAM trace showed GPIOTE (vector 22) re-entering the Kernel every 27 µs from 188.6 ms to the end, and nothing below it running again. Cause: an event too late for its Job to complete within the operating duration makes no Release (R9.9), and the intake path left the source unmasked; a level-triggered event that no Job will ever clear then pends again at once, every time, until the operating end, up to one deadline's worth of time (R19.1 broken at the end of the duration). TIMER1 never showed it because its fixed phase never put a pulse in the last 15 ms. `Kernel::intake` now masks the source on that refusal (`Timing::mask`; DD-06); the Verus specification of `intake` states it (`unchanged_but_log_and_mask`), rsk-logic and rsk-kernel verify, the Kani harness `intake_late_event_masks_source` checks the late and the in-time cases, and 16 of 16 runs then passed with no storm in the trace.
- **A Kernel trace in RAM.** The `trace` feature's semihosting halts the core; the new `trace-ram` feature (HIL builds only) keeps the same entry-point trace in a 512-entry ring in Kernel RAM (`TRACE_RING`, tag, value, time-base ticks; `now()` calls excepted, which the Tasks' busy loops would flood it with), placed in `.kernel.noinit`, Kernel RAM the reset path does not zero, so the rig reads it over the probe after the run, through the reset below. The self-test fails on a storm (32 or more re-entries of one vector without a dispatch).
- **The board resets when the probe detaches.** After the semihosting exit, probe-rs's detach leaves the core to re-execute the exit breakpoint with no debugger: a HardFault, then a lockup and a watchdog reset (RESETREAS.LOCKUP and DOG), and the Application boots again with boot check bit 7 failed into the safe state. The first run after flashing on 2026-11-01 found the board in that state. Two changes: the safe state and the exit skip semihosting when no debugger is attached (`hw::debugger_attached`, DHCSR.C_DEBUGEN; the QEMU board reports attached, since its semihosting needs no debugger), so a board left running settles in the safe state's loop with the watchdog fed (a busy wait between feeds since 2026-11-13, the audit's contested finding B: the loop was `watchdog_feed(); wfi` inside a Kernel-level handler, which nothing could wake, so the watchdog reset the board half a second later and the next boot reported that reset; the claim here was unsupported for a safe state entered at run time); and the trace ring survives the reset. The breakpoint re-execution itself remains (probe-rs's detach behaviour, noted for `hil-watchdog`).
- **A board running without a debugger must never semihost.** Before the guard above, a target left running after a session restarted every 200 ms (fault, watchdog reset, boot, run), which a capture longer than the run showed as hundreds of response edges after it.
- **Windowing the capture on the run.** The response line floats high while the target is flashed and is driven low when its Partition's first Job configures the pin; the stimulus board runs before and after the target. The analysis therefore takes the run as the last N response edges, N being the count the target reports, and reports the rest apart.
- **Probe selection by position is wrong with more than one board.** `cargo xtask hil` ran whichever probe `probe-rs list` showed first; with three attached that is arbitrary. The rig file names every board by serial, and a run without the file requires exactly one probe.

**Open after 7.1.** The stimulus board's spare lines for storms (7.5; hardware PPI from a TIMER to GPIOTE on board B, no CPU); probe-rs's detach behaviour. The trace pins and the release-timing test followed on 2026-11-01 and 02 (section 11.3).

### 11.3 Release timing over PAR-05 (2026-11-01 and 02, task 7.3, R9.10)

**The configuration.** Two declaration variants of the P1 demo for the rig (`nrf52840_timing`, 61 minutes, the PAR-05 run: the longer of one hour and 1,000 hyperperiods of 200 ms; `nrf52840_timing_short`, 3 minutes, the pipeline check), built with the HIL features plus `timing` or `timing-short`: every periodic offset 0, so that all five Periodic_Tasks' nominal instants coincide every 200 ms; `sensor` holding `state` (Ceiling 7) for 12 ms from each of its 20 ms instants, so that a Critical_Section is in progress at `control`'s nominal instants 10 ms later (control is blocked until the unlock, within its deadline); `sensor` at Priority 1, the lowest, so that its injected late Jobs starve nobody; every 1,024th `sensor` Job running 38 ms more (50 ms in all) under the Partition crate's `timing` feature, past two of its nominal instants and short of the third, in Partition 0 (level A, RECORD_AND_CONTINUE, R18.6) with its MIT threshold raised to 1,000; the stimulus board releasing `pulse` every 18 ms throughout. The target's own report judges the run: every count within two Jobs of the operating duration over the period, `sensor` short by exactly its discarded Releases (one per late Job), no fault and no deadline miss other than the late Jobs' own (its Budget overrun, its deadline miss, its held Release, R9.7, and Partition 0's MIT_Violation for the discarded one, R9.8), the FP state converged.

**The record.** A HIL build keeps the Kernel's release-timing record in no-init RAM (`RELEASE_STATS`, feature `trace-ram`): at every time-base interrupt the arch wrapper notes the instant and the cycle counter, calls the verified handler, and for every periodic Release whose due instant moved records the lag `(now - due) * 4 + handler cycles` in CPU cycles per Task (count, maximum, sum, a log2 histogram), counts due instants that are not `O + k*T`, and keeps the last 256 Releases. The lag includes the handler's own work up to the point the Release is pending (the trace entries and the pins of the HIL build included) and the entry cycles of the stub; it is the J_rel the Analyzer assumes, measured on this board. The rig reads the record after the run, with the trace ring and the console, and `cargo xtask hil --release-timing [--short]` writes the report under `docs/hil/`.

**The event mirror.** The 32-entry event log of R14.7 is overwritten many times over in an hour whose late Jobs each log three or four expected events, so the HIL build also mirrors every event Kernel_Arch sees the verified logic push (its sequence number, kind, Task, Partition, instant, detail; the last 512, `EVENT_MIRROR`) and freezes the trace ring at the first event outside the run's expected set, which the system crate declares (`TRACE_EXPECT`: for the release-timing configuration, `sensor`'s overruns, misses, held Releases, and MIT_Violations) and the entry crate passes to the Kernel before boot. Both records are cleared at instant 0 rather than at boot, so that the reboot the board takes when the probe detaches (a failed boot check into the safe state, section 11.2) appends its own events after the run's instead of erasing them; the rig reads the run's events as those up to its safe-state event. A run thus leaves the event that first went wrong and the 512 Kernel entries leading to it.

**Results of the pipeline check (3 minutes, after the fixes below):** 40,487 periodic Releases recorded over the five Periodic_Tasks, every nominal instant `O + k*T`, lag at most 5,090 cycles (79.5 µs; the tick that releases five Tasks at once), control 18,000 of 18,000 Jobs, sensor 8,992 of 9,000 (eight late Jobs, eight discarded Releases), log 4,500, filter 7,200, sampler 1,800, 10,000 of 10,000 stimulus pulses answered within 70 µs to 12.4 ms (median 2.4 ms: the pulse waits behind sensor's 12 ms Critical_Section; never past its 15 ms deadline), the trace without a storm, the dispatch timeline showing the Tasks executing 71 % of the run. **Results of the PAR-05 run (61 minutes, 2026-11-02, `docs/hil/release_timing_nrf52840_timing.md`):** 823,317 periodic Releases over the five Periodic_Tasks (control 365,999, sensor 182,821, log 91,499, filter 146,399, sampler 36,599; the record misses the first of each because the instant-0 Releases precede the arch wrapper's first diff), every nominal instant `O + k*T`, lag at most 6,789 cycles (106.1 µs) with means of 3,248 to 3,735 cycles (51 to 58 µs) and every lag in the 2^12 or 2^13 bucket (64 to 128 µs), 13 counter wraps with the trace's instants ordered across them (R9.5), no storm, the target's counts exact (control 366,000, sensor 182,822 of 183,000 with 178 late Jobs and 178 discarded Releases, log 91,500, filter 146,400, sampler 36,600, echo 121,999), 1,324 events all of the late Jobs' expected kinds (462 overruns, 139 misses, 279 held Releases, 139 MIT_Violations) and the trace never frozen, 203,333 of 203,333 stimulus pulses answered within 70 µs to 12.5 ms (median 2.4 ms, none past the 15 ms deadline), the Tasks executing 71.5 % of the hour.

**Findings:**

- **A late Job must be the lowest-priority Task's.** The first short run injected the late Job into `sensor` at Priority 5: its 50 ms starved `filter` (deadline 25 ms) and `log` into deadline misses and MIT_Violations until their Partitions restarted (threshold 10), and after Partition 1's restart `pulse` never answered again (its GPIOTE source: whether a restarted Partition's interrupt source resumes is for the fault-injection suite, task 7.5, to settle). R9.10's scenario is only meaningful where the late Job harms its own Task alone.
- **One pulse of 203,333 unanswered (open).** The first hour-long run (2026-11-01, `docs/hil/release_timing_nrf52840_timing_run1.md`) passed every Kernel-side check and left one stimulus pulse unanswered, 581.3 s into the run: the trace pins show `pulse`'s Job dispatched 70 µs after the rise and ending after a normal 40 µs, with neither the response toggle nor the count it cannot skip, right after `sensor`'s Job ended at a nominal instant of `control` and `sampler` (`docs/hil/release_timing_nrf52840_timing_run1_missed_pulse.md`). The event log had been overwritten and the trace ring held only the last 200 ms, which is what the event mirror and the trace freeze now remedy; the second hour (2026-11-02, with them) answered every pulse and froze nothing. One miss in 203,333 is a rate of about one per hour, so a clean hour does not close it: the finding is carried to the fault-injection suite (task 7.5), whose runs keep the mirror and the freeze, and a recurrence will leave the event and the 512 Kernel entries leading to it.
- **One UsageFault of `control` (open, 2026-11-05).** In the short timing run on the binary of the R16.2 change (sha256 7e8fe0fa…), `control`'s Job released at 148,400.000 ms was dispatched at 148,400.134 ms onto an empty Partition stack (frame 0x20008fe0, no live context below) and took a UsageFault 12 µs later, before its first SVC; the Kernel applied part0's declared response (RESTART_PARTITION, the restart logged), the run went on to its operating end with every later pulse answered and `control` counting again from the restart, and the rig's verdict was the fault alone. The part0 trace freeze of 11.6 held the 512 entries leading to it (`docs/hil/findings/timing_short_7e8fe0fa_*`): a normal tick, dispatch, and fault. The CFSR was not read: the Kernel's fault record (`FAULT_RING`) was read by the isolation rig only, and the board had been reflashed before the record was looked for. The timing and stress rigs now read the record after every run and print each fault's CFSR with the UsageFault bits decoded (UNDEFINSTR, INVSTATE, INVPC, NOCP, UNALIGNED, DIVBYZERO), so the next occurrence names its cause. The run repeated on the same binary passed (190 s, no fault). Nothing of the change runs at that point: the Kernel peripheral intervals are validated at boot, and the matrix is the isolation build's. Not reproduced; open with the diagnostics armed.
- **J_rel measured.** 5,090 to 6,789 cycles (80 to 106 µs) at the five-Task tick on this board with the HIL instrumentation; the job's placeholder Kernel_Timing_Parameter `j_rel` of 500 cycles is an order of magnitude below it. Task 19.3 feeds the measured value to the Analyzer; until then the Analyzer's verdicts for this board rest on a placeholder the plan now knows to be optimistic (GAP-09).
- **The DWT boot check depended on the probe.** Boot check bit 3 reads DWT_CTRL.NOCYCCNT, which is readable only with DEMCR.TRCENA set; a probe detach had cleared it and the check failed into the safe state. The check sets TRCENA first (the Kernel sets it a step later in any case).
- **Trace pins.** The HIL build's `trace-pins` feature writes the executing Task's index on P1.11 to P1.13 and a Kernel-active flag on P1.14 at every handler's entry and return (two register writes); the analyzer reads them on D1 to D4 and the rig decodes a dispatch timeline (per Task: executing samples, runs, longest run; Kernel entries), the basis for observed response times in 19.4. The timeline shows the Kernel active 36 to 50 % of the demo's run: the busy loops of `sensor` and `log` call `now()` continuously, each an SVC.

### 11.4 Fault injection (2026-11-02, task 7.5, R14.11, R18.8, R19.3)

**The response suite (R14.11).** The demo's response configuration (`nrf52840_response`, 2 seconds; features `response` of the three Partition crates) declares one Partition for each response of the closed sets of R14.5 and injects, at fixed instants of the time base (never by Job count, so that a restarted Partition does not repeat them), the fault that triggers each: part0 (level A) RECORD_AND_CONTINUE for `control`'s Budget overrun at 200 ms, RECORD_ONLY for `sensor`'s own deadline miss during its 120 ms Job of 1,305 ms, and SAFE_STATE for `control`'s MemManage fault at 1,900 ms; part1 (level C) END_JOB for `log`'s overrun at 400 ms and RESTART_PARTITION for its fault at 800 ms; part2 (level B) END_JOB for `filter`'s fault at 250 ms, END_JOB escalated to RESTART_PARTITION (R18.4) for its overrun inside its Critical_Section at 500 ms, and STOP_PARTITION for `filter`'s deadline missed while pending behind `sensor`'s 120 ms Job (1,350 ms). The rig (`rsk-hil::response`) passes the run only when the Kernel's event mirror holds exactly the expected events in order and in their windows (the consequences of the 120 ms Job on the delayed Tasks tolerated), the counts show the declared effects (part1's and part2's state back at zero after their restarts, part2's Tasks silent after its stop), and every other Partition ran unchanged (R14.9: part0's counts exact to its own safe state; `pulse` answering before part1's restart, after it, and after part2's stop, seen by the analyzer). The suite is the job's step `hil-response` (Evidence_Item `hil-response`, about a minute) and `cargo xtask hil --response`.

**Results (2026-11-02, `docs/hil/response_nrf52840_response.md`):** pass, 45 events, every response in the expected order: control 190, echo 63, sensor 90 Jobs at the safe state; log 24 and pulse 54 since part1's restart (47 and 101 had they kept their state); filter 32 and sampler 8 between part2's restart and its stop; 44, 24, and 24 pulses answered in the three phases.

**Found by the response suite:**

- **A Partition restart did not re-initialize the Partition** (R14.5). `apply_core` acknowledged the Kernel's re-initialization request and did nothing: a restarted Partition kept its state, its `log` count never returned to 0, so its first Job never restarted the `pulse` source, which is the observation of 11.3. `Action::ReinitPartition` now zeroes the Partition's range of the linker's zero table (every Partition static is zero-initialized by construction: the Generator places Partition `.data` in a no-load section, so a static with another initial value would have been wrong since reset, which the design now states); the Partition Init of PR-34 is still not built.
- **KD-R9.6 (fixed): the running Job's own deadline miss was not reported when a Release of the same Task became due at the same instant.** `timer_event` served the due slots in slot order, and a Task's release slot precedes its deadline slot: the Release that became due at the instant of the running Job's deadline (deadline = period, as the demo declares) re-armed the Task's single deadline slot for its own deadline before the miss was served, so every late Job's own miss was lost and the held Release's (one period later) reported instead. The suite recorded the Kernel as it was (the miss at 1,345 ms) while the item stayed open. Fixed on 2026-11-02 after the hour-long stress run: `timer_event` now serves the due deadline events of an instant before its other events; with the Config_Checker bounding every deadline by the period or MIT, the running Job's deadline is at or before its Task's next Release, so one slot holds every unserved deadline. `intake` likewise serves an overdue deadline of the Task before taking an event in (a deadline event pending behind the intake entry); its contract states the outcome from the state after the miss (`overdue_deadline_served`, `intake_outcome`). The Kani harness `deadline_miss_of_the_running_job_is_reported_at_its_next_nominal_instant` checks the Job's own miss at its next nominal instant and the held Release's at the one after, and the response suite now requires sensor's own miss at 1,325 ms before the held Release's at 1,345 ms.
- **RECORD_AND_CONTINUE recorded one Overrun a dozen times.** The Budget compare of a Job that runs on past its Budget is re-armed with no remainder at every resume after a preemption, and fired at once; each firing logged the Overrun again (12 records of `sensor`'s one 120 ms Job). `budget_event` now records an Overrun once per Job.
- **Preempted busy waits do not reach their Budgets.** An injected overrun written as "busy for 9 ms by the time base" under a 6 ms Budget never overran: the Job's execution, which the Budget measures, was below 6 ms once `sensor`'s Critical_Section and `control` took their share of the 9 ms. R18.1 working as specified; the injections now busy-wait for 20 to 25 ms.

**The stress configuration (R18.8, R19.3).** `nrf52840_stress` (61 minutes, PAR-05) and `nrf52840_stress_short` (3 minutes): the plain demo's declaration with the lower-criticality Partitions misbehaving throughout under the Partition crates' `stress` feature (part1's `log` exceeding its Budget outside a Critical_Section every fourth Job; part2's `filter` holding its Resource for 250 µs, its maximal Critical_Section, every Job and exceeding its Budget inside it every eighth, so that the escalated restart churns part2 every 200 ms; `sampler` exceeding its Budget every second Job) and the stimulus board's `storm` feature toggling the stimulus line as fast as its core can write the port for 16 of every 18 ms (several million edges per second into the target's GPIOTE, the analyzer's mirror quiet). The Kernel's record gains, per Task, the Jobs completed and the largest response time (Release due to Job end) and, per interrupt source, the intake handler's runs and cycles. The rig (`cargo xtask hil --stress [--short]`) runs the Analyzer on the configuration's Task_Model with placeholder timing (a copy of the job's Build_Manifest carrying the configuration's own Generated_Config checksum, R28.8) and passes the run only when every Job of part0 (level A) completed within the WCRT the Analyzer computed for its Task (R18.7, R18.8), the stormed source cost at most one intake per MIT window (R19.1: DD-06's masking, whatever the event rate) with the response line toggling once per window, no event of part0's Tasks was recorded (the trace freeze watches them), and the release-timing checks of 11.3 hold.

**Found by the stress configuration:**

- **A killed Job's late-arriving SVC took the Target to the safe state (fixed).** The second short run ended at 36.9 s with a Kernel-attributed panic: the trace freeze left the ring at the event (`docs/hil/stress_short_panic_2026-11-02/`): `log`'s Budget overrun at a tick, END_JOB, the unwind of its level, then an SVC entry numbered 48, which `svc_core` treats as a panic of the executing Job, and with no Job executing, as the Kernel's. 48 is the low byte of 0xBF30, WFI: the SVC handler had decoded the idle activity's return address. The Job had executed `SVC` (its busy wait polls `now()`), the time-base exception arrived during the exception entry and was served first (late arrival, Armv7-M B1.5.11), killed the Job and unwound its level, and the pending SVC then tail-chained onto the resumed idle context. A lower-criticality Partition's overrun thus ended the run, against R18.7. The SVC handler now treats an entry whose frame's return address does not follow an SVC instruction as a stale SVC of a killed Job and ignores it (`arch::cm4::svc`, `SVC_STALE`); the window is a few cycles per SVC and the stress configuration, with its Jobs polling `now()` continuously, hit it within a minute.
