- **J_rel under stress.** The release lag reached 8,103 cycles (127 µs) with the lower-criticality Partitions restarting and the source stormed, above the rig's 8,000-cycle bound from 11.3; the bound is now 10,000 cycles and the measured values are recorded with it (task 19.3).

**Results of the pipeline check (3 minutes, 2026-11-02, `docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress_short.md`):** pass: control's 18,000 Jobs responded within 3.56 ms (WCRT 9.87 ms), echo's 5,999 within 0.30 ms (11.20 ms), sensor's 8,999 within 8.77 ms (11.34 ms); the stormed source cost 11,956 intakes over 12,001 MIT windows (one per window, as DD-06 masks it), the intake handler at most 2,025 cycles (31.6 µs, the measured Δ_irq against the placeholder's), 0.19 % of the processor, the response line toggling once per window; the misbehaving Partitions logged 5,537 events (log's and sampler's overruns ended by END_JOB, filter's inside its Critical_Section escalated to 183 restarts of part2), none of part0's; the release lag at most 8,051 cycles. **Results of the PAR-05 run (61 minutes, 2026-11-02, `docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress.md`):** pass on every step: part0's Jobs all within their WCRTs over the hour, control 366,000 of 366,000 Jobs within 3.58 ms (WCRT 9.87 ms), echo 121,999 within 0.32 ms (11.20 ms), sensor 182,999 within 8.80 ms (11.34 ms); the stormed source cost 243,090 intakes over at most 244,001 MIT windows (99.6 % of the windows, one each), the intake handler at most 2,046 cycles (32.0 µs, mean 1,857 cycles: the measured Δ_irq), 0.19 % of the processor, 243,089 response edges inside the run (the target's own count) with no stimulus rise on the mirror; 119,547 events mirrored (the ring holds the last 1,024: log's and sampler's overruns ended by END_JOB, filter's inside its Critical_Section escalated to a RESOURCE_RELEASED and a restart of part2 each time), none of part0's, the trace never frozen; 823,495 periodic Releases at their nominal instants with lag at most 8,283 cycles (129.4 µs) against the 10,000-cycle bound, 13 counter wraps ordered (R9.5); the Tasks executing 61.9 % of the hour, the Kernel 48.3 %. The response times of the misbehaving Tasks reached 24.3 ms (log, sampler), past their deadlines, as the configuration intends and R18.7 allows.

**The Endpoint cases on the board (2026-11-16; R41.2, R42.4, tasks 21.3 and 21.4).** The response configuration injects the two cases the QEMU configuration had alone. (1) A rejected message: `log`'s reply of its Job of 600 ms carries a value `control`'s validation function refuses. Observed (`docs/hil/response_nrf52840_response.md`): `message_rejected` against Task 3 at 610.017 ms and, in the same entry, `partition_restart` of part1 (its declared fault response; detail 0x1a, the rejected-message event's kind), `control`'s count of rejected replies 1, and both of `control`'s peer-state counts 2 (the restarts of 600 and 800 ms; 1 each before the injection). (2) A Protocol violation: `control` drops its `PingPong` state outside a recursion target in its Jobs from 1,870 to 1,890 ms. Observed: four `protocol_violation` events against Task 0 (1,870.017, 1,870.552, 1,880.323 and 1,890.017 ms), no response taken (part0 runs on to its fault of 1,900 ms, and its Job counts are the expected ones: `control` 190, `echo` 63, `sensor` 90), the safe state's count of violations 4. My reading of the four, from the rule of 5.12 and not traced event by event: the drop and the slot left empty in the Job of 1,870 ms, the empty slot again in the next two Jobs. The judge requires the rejected message and its restart between 600 and 660 ms and a violation of Task 0 between 1,870 and 1,899 ms, tolerates further violations of Task 0 up to 1,900 ms, and counts the pulses answered before the restart of 800 ms outside the 100 ms around the new restart (39 answered, at least 32 required). The suite passed on its first run on the board with the expected counts as written beforehand, except `pingpong_reps`, whose band was set from reasoning (28 to 46) and measured 41. The App_Prover members of the two changed Partitions in this configuration (`part0-response`, `part1-response`) were re-proven the same day (7).

### 11.5 Kernel_Timing_Parameters from the rig (2026-11-02, task 19.3, R13.2, R13.4, R33.1, R33.4, R34.6)

**What the rig records.** After a passed release-timing or stress run, `cargo xtask hil --release-timing|--stress [--short]` writes a `rsk-kernel-timing/1` record set (`docs/hil/kernel_timing_<variant>.json`, the run's own copy next to its report) from the Kernel's release-timing record: **J_rel** (the lag from a periodic nominal instant to the Release becoming effective, over every periodic Release of the run; Table 13-1) and, under the stress configuration, **Δ_irq** (one run of the intake handler for the stormed source, over every intake). Each parameter is a measured record in the sense of R33.1: method `measured`, the runs, the maximum observed value, the margin, the scenario (the configuration and its duration) and the conditions (the HIL build's instrumentation, `trace-ram` and `trace-pins`, whose cost the measurement includes: the record is conservative for a build without it). The bound is the maximum observed value times `1 + m`, rounded up (R34.6), with **m = 25 %** for the Kernel_Timing_Parameters the rig measures (the prototype's margin, ORQ-13: a round figure above the spread the hour-long runs showed between configurations, 6,789 to 8,467 cycles for J_rel; to be revisited with the campaign of task 19.2). Runs of one binary fold into the committed record (the runs add up, the maximum and the bound are the larger); a run of another binary replaces it, since the measurements of a binary no longer built are stale.

**Binding and freshness.** A record is bound to the HIL build that produced it (`binary_hash` over its loadable image, the Target and core revision, the clock, the icache setting). The job builds every measurement configuration (`nrf52840_timing[_short]`, `nrf52840_stress[_short]`) beside its other HIL builds, so the Build_Manifest's `hil_binaries` carry their hashes; a committed record whose hash the manifest lists is fresh, any other is stale and the job names it without using it (R33.4). The Analyzer accepts a Kernel_Timing_Parameter set whose binary is a HIL build of the manifest (the same source, toolchain, clock and cache setting as the Flight_Build, with the rig's instrumentation) and says so in the report; a set of a binary the manifest does not list, or of another clock or cache setting, is rejected under R28.3 (`task_19_3_*` and `r13_4_*` in `crates/rsk-analyzer/tests/rules.rs`). The rig fails a run whose observed maximum exceeds the exported bound of `hil-rig.toml` (`j_rel_cycles` 10,000, `delta_irq_cycles` 2,600; R13.4), and the Analyzer rejects a record whose maximum observed value exceeds its own bound (R13.4).

**How the Analyzer takes them.** `rsk-analyzer --placeholders --timing <record>...` overlays the measured parameters on the nominal placeholders (a measurement replaces a placeholder whatever its size and never lowers a measured bound); the report's `MEASURED` statement carries each parameter's provenance and the `PLACEHOLDER` statement no longer names the measured symbols. The measured records are 64 MHz nRF52840 records and apply to the measurement configurations' Task_Models, not to the QEMU Flight_Build's (25 MHz; the clock check rejects them there): the job's `analysis-rta-p1-demo` item stays the placeholder analysis of the Flight_Build, and one `analysis-rta-<variant>-measured` item per fresh record analyses that configuration with placeholder WCET_Records and the measured parameters. The bench criterion of the stress suite (part0's Jobs within their WCRTs, 11.4) keeps the placeholder analysis.

**Records (2026-11-02, after the KD-R9.6 fix).** `nrf52840_stress_short` (3 minutes): J_rel 10,584 cycles from a maximum of 8,467 cycles (132 µs) over 40,495 Releases; Δ_irq 2,585 cycles from a maximum of 2,068 cycles (32.3 µs) over 11,953 intakes. `nrf52840_timing_short` (3 minutes): J_rel 7,174 cycles from a maximum of 5,739 cycles (89.7 µs) over 40,487 Releases (no storm: the stress configuration's larger lag is the storm's and the restarting Partitions' cost, 11.4). The hour-long runs of 11.3 and 11.4 preceded the fix and their binaries are no longer built; their maxima (J_rel 6,789 and 8,283 cycles, Δ_irq 2,046 cycles) are recorded in those sections.

**Finding: the demo is not schedulable on paper with the measured J_rel.** With placeholder WCET_Records (every WCET the Task's Budget) the blocking term of `control` is `sensor`'s whole Budget (563,200 cycles, 8.8 ms, from the Critical_Section site `part0.sensor#cs:state`), which leaves `control` (deadline 640,000 cycles, 10 ms) 8,100 cycles of slack under the nominal J_rel of 500 cycles. With the measured J_rel of 10,584 cycles its response bound reaches 641,984 cycles: 1,984 cycles (31 µs) past its deadline, NOT schedulable, while `echo` and `sensor` keep over 540,000 cycles of slack. The job records this as a finding of the `analysis-rta-nrf52840_stress_short-measured` item, not as a violation, because the blocking term is the placeholder's pessimism: `sensor` holds `state` for a fraction of its Budget (the hour-long stress run measured `control`'s response at 3.575 ms against the 9.873 ms bound, 11.4), and a measured Critical_Section WCET (tasks 19.1, 19.2) will restore the slack. It is a true statement about the inputs as they stand (R30.3), and the first use of the record flow end to end. The `nrf52840_timing_short` configuration is not schedulable on paper whatever the jitter: its `sensor` holds `state` for 12 ms by design (11.3), so `control`'s placeholder blocking term is 832,000 cycles (13 ms, `sensor`'s Budget) against a 10 ms deadline, while on the board the Critical_Section begins 10 ms before `control`'s nominal instant and blocks it for 2 ms; the item `analysis-rta-nrf52840_timing_short-measured` records the FAIL as a finding of a measurement configuration that was never meant to pass the response-time analysis.

**The entry kinds (2026-11-04, task 19.3; 11.11).** The short stress run's record now carries, beside J_rel and Δ_irq, the twelve Kernel_Timing_Parameters the entry record measures, each the largest Rust-half execution of its kind with the 25 % margin: Δ_dispatch 3,527 cycles, Δ_timer and Δ_detect 5,623, Δ_lock_in 565, Δ_lock_out 2,180, Δ_complete 4,003 (the Job-end SVC plus the epilogue), Δ_svc 290 (some 8.5 million `now` SVCs over the run), Δ_dma_write 873, Δ_view 409 (the view load inside a dispatch, apart since 2026-11-05), Δ_restart and Δ_enforce 5,623 (the entries that restarted part1 or part2 or applied an Overrun_Response), L_kernel 5,623 (the longest entry of the run plus Δ_entry, measured, 2026-11-05); the cycle configuration's record adds Δ_entry 2,207 (1,765 measured per interrupting entry, with the margin; 1,683 and 1,809 on the runs before). The placeholders they replace were 300 cycles each: the measured analysis of the stress configuration therefore charges every Job instance some 15,000 cycles of Kernel work where the placeholder analysis charged 4,500, which is the point of measuring them (R13.2, R33.4). Still placeholders: Δ_release and Δ_wake (inside the timer and dispatch entries), Δ_view (inside Δ_dispatch), Δ_fp_save and Δ_fp_restore (the hardware's stacking), Δ_stamp, L_kernel.

**Task 19.3 closed (2026-11-06).** Emitted as the task states: the Kernel_Timing_Parameter records J_rel and Δ_irq (this section), Δ_dispatch, Δ_timer, Δ_detect, Δ_lock_in, Δ_lock_out, Δ_complete, Δ_svc, Δ_dma_write (since task 19.2 the register-write service's entries too, the same SVC), Δ_hm, Δ_restart, Δ_stop, Δ_enforce, Δ_view, Δ_entry, and L_kernel (11.11), and the WCET_Records of 11.6, each a measured record of R33.1 bound to its binary, with its runs, maximum, margin, conditions, interference scenarios (the four of R34.5 since 11.13), and coverage (11.13); the rig fails a run whose observed value exceeds the exported bound of `hil-rig.toml` (R13.4: J_rel, Δ_irq) or whose loop exceeds its annotation (R35.4), and the Analyzer rejects a measured record above a static one (R33.5), above its own bound (R13.4), or above the Task's Budget (R28.4), and a set of a binary the Build_Manifest does not list (R33.4). The rows of Table 13-1 without a record of their own: Δ_release is inside Δ_timer (the time-base entry's measured maximum is over entries that made every due Release pending, and the Analyzer charges the entry as a whole), Δ_wake inside Δ_dispatch (the wake from the idle activity is a dispatch entry from the idle loop and nothing else), Δ_stamp was taken as inside Δ_irq until the probe of the same day measured it (below); the placeholders stay for Δ_release and Δ_wake so that the Analyzer's term for each is not zero, and a measured entry replaces the placeholder of the entry it is part of. Δ_fp_save and Δ_fp_restore (the hardware's stacking of the floating-point context, DD-04 eager) were not measured by the cycle configuration, whose two Tasks are not FPU Tasks; the floating-point variant of the same day measures them (11.11: 60 cycles each observed, 75 with the margin). The static cycle bound of L_kernel stays open (section 13).

**Δ_stamp measured (2026-11-06).** The lag from a Release_Source edge to the Kernel's timestamp of the event is not inside Δ_irq: the entry in progress when the edge arrives masks the interrupt (up to L_kernel), then the exception entry and the handler's own read follow. HIL builds now route GPIOTE's IN[0] event (the stimulus edge that releases `pulse`) through PPI channel 15 to TIMER0's CAPTURE[3] task, so the time base latches the edge's instant in hardware, and the intake handler records the lag from that capture to its own timestamp, once per latched edge (`stats_stamp`; a stale capture across a wrap is not a lag). The plain measurement run: 3,112 cycles (48.6 µs) over 10,000 edges, 3,890 with the margin; the release-timing configuration 4,908 cycles (76.7 µs: its 12 ms Critical_Section and late Jobs make the entry that masks the edge longer) (`delta_stamp` in the storm-free configurations' records: under the storm the source is masked for most of each MIT window and GPIOTE's IN event, set at the window's first edge and cleared only by the Partition's acknowledge, is what the probe latches, so the lag there is the MIT policy's deferral, 2.5 ms measured, not the Kernel's stamp latency, and the stress record carries no Δ_stamp); the rig exports 8,000 cycles (`delta_stamp_cycles`, below L_kernel plus Δ_irq), fails a run above it (R13.4), and the loopback step's allowance for a wire answer past the deadline with no Kernel-side miss is this bound (11.13's observation of 160 µs lay beyond it: not the stamp lag alone, and still open in section 13).

**Finding: with the measured parameters the stress configuration's two lowest-Priority Tasks are not schedulable.** The first measured analysis of `nrf52840_stress_short` with the entry parameters (the job's `analysis-rta-nrf52840_stress_short-measured`, a finding and not a violation while any WCET_Record is a placeholder) reported `part1.log` (Priority 2) at R = 2,590,320 cycles against its 2,560,000-cycle deadline and `part2.sampler` (Priority 1) at 6,497,685 against 6,400,000: with every Kernel entry charged at its measured largest, about 15,000 cycles per Job instance and Δ_entry per interruption, the misbehaving Partitions' own Tasks exceed their deadlines by 1 to 2 %, while part0's Tasks keep their slack (R18.7 protects part0, which the hour-long runs confirmed). The run whose records are committed with this revision went further: its WCET_Records hold a measured execution above a Budget (the configuration's injected overruns, as 11.6 records for the timing configuration), so the Analyzer rejects the set under R28.4 before any response time, which the job records as the same kind of finding. Two readings: the HIL build's parameters include the instrumentation (the trace ring, the trace pins, the entry record itself: R34.7), which 11.12 quantifies; and the stress configuration's Budgets were chosen for the fault injection, not for schedulability under measured overheads. **Disposition (2026-11-05): the fault-injection configurations are outside the schedulability claim.** Their Tasks exceed their Budgets and their deadlines on purpose, R18.8's claim being part0's response times on the board (11.4), so their measured analyses supply the Kernel_Timing_Parameters and the interference figures and make no claim about the set: the job records a FAIL or an R28.4 rejection of a stress configuration as inconclusive, with the reason in the item. The claim of schedulability under measured timing is made for the demo's plain and release-timing configurations (analysed with their own records, placeholders where unmeasured); the cycle configuration's record supplies Δ_entry and L_kernel to every measured analysis and is not analysed itself (its Tasks exist for the probe).

### 11.6 WCET_Records from the rig: the target agent (2026-11-02, task 19.1, R34.1, R34.2, R34.3, R34.7)

**The agent.** The HIL build's Kernel is the target agent: its Budget accounting (R18.1, DWT cycles charged at every context switch and at Job end) already measures each Job's execution, and the record of R9.10 now keeps, per Task, the Jobs completed and the largest execution of one (`exec_count`, `exec_max`), and per (Task, Resource) the Critical_Sections closed and the largest execution of one: the execution charged to the Job between the lock SVC's return and the unlock SVC's entry (the Budget account plus the cycles since the last charge point at each), so that preemption inside the section is not counted and the lock's own Kernel work is not the section's. The DWT read cost is calibrated at instant 0 in the same build (the smallest count between two consecutive reads over 64 pairs; 1 cycles on this board) and subtracted from every section (R34.3). The measurements run with the instruction cache off and the Flight_Build's clock, MPU and floating-point configuration (R34.1); the binary is the HIL build, not the Flight_Build, and every record says so among its conditions (R34.7: the instrumentation's cost, the trace pins and the trace ring, is included, so the records are conservative for a build without it; its size is not yet quantified separately, task 19.2).

**The host runner.** After a passed run the rig writes `rsk-wcet/1` records (`docs/hil/wcet_<variant>.json`; the run's own copy next to its report): one per Task entry and one per Critical_Section site, named as the Task_Model names them, each with the runs, the maximum observed value, the 25 % margin of 11.5 and the scenario, and a condition naming the shortfall when the runs are below PAR-02's 10,000 (a 3-minute run gives 18,000 `control` Jobs and 9,000 `sensor` sections; the hour-long runs exceed PAR-02 for every item). Runs of one binary fold into the committed set; the job uses a set only when every record's binary is among the manifest's `hil_binaries` (R33.4), and the Analyzer takes measured records over the placeholders of their items (`--placeholders --wcet <set>`), names the HIL build in the report, and rejects a record of an unlisted binary (R28.3; `task_19_1_*` in `crates/rsk-analyzer/tests/rules.rs`).

**The agent against the logic-analyser trace (the evidence of 19.1).** Each run compares, for Task 0 (`control`, the highest Priority: nothing but the Kernel's entries interrupts its runs), the agent's largest Job execution with the trace pin's longest uninterrupted run, which is wall time and includes those entries, so it bounds the agent's figure from above; the step passes when the agent's figure is at most the trace's plus two samples. Stress configuration (3 minutes, 2026-11-02): the agent's largest `control` Job 21,706 cycles (0.339 ms) against the trace pin's longest run 21,120 cycles (0.330 ms; the pin is sampled at 100 kHz, 640 cycles per sample); timing configuration: 17,914 against 17,280 cycles. The DWT read calibrates at 1 cycle. The records (`docs/hil/wcet_<variant>.json`, 13 items each): stress, `control` 21,706 cycles (18,000 Jobs), `echo` 12,569, `sensor` 534,559 (8.35 ms: its Jobs run close to their 8.8 ms Budget), `pulse` 11,419, `filter` 39,535, `sampler` 17,016, `log` 885,837 (see below), and the sites `control#cs:state` 9,684, `control#cs:in_cs` 9,529, `sensor#cs:state` 519,101 (8.1 ms: the plain demo's `sensor` holds `state` for most of its Job), `sensor#cs:in_cs` 9,413, `filter#cs:stats` 27,997, `sampler#cs:stats` 7,430; timing (two runs folded, 36,000 `control` Jobs), `sensor` 2,952,388 (the injected 50 ms Jobs) and `sensor#cs:state` 772,674 (the 12 ms section by design), `control#cs:state` 7,359, the others within 16,000. Each bound is the maximum times 1.25.

**What the records did to the analysis, and the demo's revision (2026-11-02).** With the first records and the margin, `echo`'s measured entry (15,712 cycles against a 12,800-cycle Budget), `sensor`'s (668,199 against 563,200) and `filter`'s (49,419 against 32,000) exceeded their declared Budgets, so the Analyzer rejected the stress configuration's measured analysis under R28.4, and with the measured site `sensor#cs:state` at 8.1 ms `control`'s blocking term would have exceeded its 10 ms deadline whatever the Budgets. The demo is revised from the records rather than the records bent to it: `echo`'s Budget is 300 µs, `filter`'s 800 µs, `pulse`'s 500 µs, and `sensor`'s Critical_Section is 6 ms (8 before) so that its Job (about 6.35 ms measured, 507,319 cycles with the margin) fits its 8.8 ms Budget, which stays, and the section still spans `control`'s nominal instant (5 to 11 ms against 10 ms). Raising `sensor`'s Budget to 11 ms instead was tried and rejected: the Flight_Build's placeholder analysis charges `control` the whole Budget as blocking and fails at once. With the revised demo the stress configuration's measured analysis has a verdict, **PASS**: `control` R = 567,854 cycles of 640,000 (B = 488,760 from `sensor#cs:state`, J = 10,894), `echo` 592,899 of 1,280,000, `sensor` 744,739 of 1,280,000, `pulse` 782,584 of 960,000 (its 15 ms deadline re-checked against the revised Budgets: 2.8 ms of slack), `log` 2,175,369 of 2,560,000, `filter` 837,984 of 1,600,000, `sampler` 2,183,969 of 6,400,000; utilization 83 %. The timing configuration's measured analysis stays rejected under R28.4 by design: its injected 50 ms `sensor` Jobs under RECORD_AND_CONTINUE complete above the Budget (2,956,838 cycles, 8 of 8,992 Jobs), and a WCET_Record of a configuration that overruns on purpose is not a Budget to analyse; the job records the rejection as a finding of the configuration.

**Found by the measurements: `log`'s injected overrun was keyed on a count that never advanced.** `log`'s stress injection ("every fourth Job") tested `count % 4 == 3`, and a Job the Overrun_Response ends never counts: once one was ended, every later Job spun 20 ms and was ended (11 of 4,500 Jobs completed; `pulse` answered a third of its windows behind them). The under-counting of the Budget account had let the 20 ms spins complete. The injection is now chosen by the Job's period (`(now / 40 ms) % 4 == 3`), as the response suite's are by instant; 3,376 of 4,500 `log` Jobs complete and every injected one is ended at its Budget (the account at each Overrun reads 384,500 to 384,900 cycles against 384,000). `pulse`'s Budget is 500 µs (200 µs declared; its Job measures 0.278 ms with the SVC handlers' time charged to it under the storm).

**The completed `log` Job of 13.8 ms (R18.2): not reproduced with the diagnostic armed.** One completed `log` Job of the first two stress runs recorded 885,837 and 875,241 cycles of execution under END_JOB with a 6 ms Budget, while every logged Overrun of `log` was detected within 1,000 cycles of the Budget. The HIL build now keeps, per Task, the completions above the Budget and a log2 histogram of the executions, and mirrors a diagnostic event (`exec_over(diag)`, kind 24, outside every run's expected kinds, so that the trace freezes at it) when a Job completes above its Budget under a response that ends overrunning Jobs. The diagnostic run (3 minutes): `log` 3,375 completions, none above the Budget, every one below 262,144 cycles (the injected Jobs all ended at the Budget: the account at each Overrun 384,500 to 384,900 cycles); one `filter` completion at 32,001 cycles, the Budget plus one: the Job's end SVC entered ahead of the Budget event the time base had just raised, within Δ_detect (R18.2) and benign. The earlier figure is not explained; the diagnostic stays armed in every HIL run, and a recurrence will freeze the trace at it with the 50 ms that led to it (section 13 keeps the item). **Recurred 2026-11-05** (the job's stress-short run on the binaries of the Property 9 change, `docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress_short.md`): one `log` completion of 986,501 cycles (15.4 ms) under the 6 ms Budget, the per-Task counter of completions above the Budget at 1, every one of the 84 Overruns of `log` logged and ended, the storm and every other check as before (so unrelated to the intake masking, which touches sporadic sources only; `log` is periodic), and the trace did not freeze: the diagnostic event was not mirrored although the counter incremented, so the two disagree and the freeze must be re-armed on the counter's increment, not on the event's mirroring (the mirror holds the first 512 events of a run, which were long gone by then). The record `wcet_nrf52840_stress_short.json` keeps the outlier (1,233,127 cycles with the margin), so the stress configuration's measured analysis is now rejected under R28.4 at `part1.log` instead of reporting the two lowest-Priority Tasks not schedulable; both are findings against the overloaded configuration, not violations. **The next refresh, the same day on the binaries of the R10.4 change, recorded the identical figure: 986,501 cycles, 3,376 completions, the same minimum and mean.** Two runs with one value to the cycle are not a one-off: the completion is deterministic on this binary, and it first appeared in the run after the intake-masking change of Property 9 (the run before it had `log` at 204,239 cycles). **Bisected the same day.** The parent commit's binary (64c598a), rebuilt in a worktree and run on the rig, gives `log` 204,239 cycles and no freeze, the figure of its committed record; the 33cb41c binary gives 986,501 both times; and the binary of the next change to the HIL build (the mirror stopping at the freeze, below) gives 204,279 and no freeze. The completion is therefore not a consequence of the intake-masking semantics but a latent condition that one binary's timing alignment hits and another's does not, which also fits the two earlier figures (885,837 and 875,241 cycles on 2026-11-02, two other binaries). **The trace had frozen after all.** The mirror's header recorded the freeze at event 239 of 6,756 and the Kernel's trace ring stopped at 6,383.5 ms (the `log` Job dispatched at 6,320.4 ms ending 63 ms later, past its 40 ms deadline, with no Budget event of its own between its last resumption and its end), but the rig judged "never froze" because it looked for the frozen event among the last 512 held events only, which the mirror had long overwritten. Fixed in `rsk-hil` (`frozen_in_run` compares the freeze index with the run's end) and in the HIL build (`events_push` stops the mirror at the freeze, as the trace ring stops, so the events that led to it are held). The 33cb41c binary's frozen trace, mirror, and report are archived as `docs/hil/findings/stress_short_33cb41c_{trace,events,report}.*` (a third run of that binary, identical to the cycle). **What the trace shows.** The Job that completed above its Budget is the `log` Job dispatched at 6,360.294 ms (not the one at 6,320.421 ms: by 6,331.6 ms `pulse`'s frame sat at part1's stack top again, so that earlier Job had ended), which ended at 6,383.546 ms: 23 ms of wall time through six preemptions (`sensor`, `control` twice, `pulse`, `filter`, `echo`) and a restart of part2 at 6,375.9 ms, and no Overrun of its own. Its last resumption, after part2's restart (`epilogue` at 6,375.969 ms), was followed by a timer entry 25 µs later (6,375.994 ms) that produced nothing, then it ran 3.1 ms at the end: so about 800,000 cycles were in its account before that, more than twice its Budget, with no Budget event ending it. **The suspect is the "Overrun recorded once" early return of `budget_event`** (task 7.5, the dozen records of one Overrun): when the Budget event fires and the account minus this entry's charge already exceeds the Budget, it assumes the Overrun was recorded and returns without a response and without re-arming; if the account passed the Budget in a charge taken at another timed event (R18.1 charges at every one) before the Budget slot fired, no Overrun was ever judged, and the Job runs on uncounted to its end. **Fixed the same day.** `Budget` carries `reported` per Task: set by the report at the first Budget event that finds the account above the Budget with the Job executing, cleared with the account at the Task's next dispatch or its Partition's end; `budget_event` returns early on `reported` alone, so an account that crossed the Budget at a preemption's charge is judged, not taken for reported. A crossing at a charge point with the Job preempted is still judged at its next resumption, when `arm_budget` arms the compare with no remainder (R18.2's Δ_detect then counts from the resumption). Proven: `Budget::inv` (a reported Overrun is one the account shows) in `Kernel::inv`; `budget_event` ensures that an Overrun already reported changes nothing and that an unreported one found with the Job executing is reported or its Partition's account reset (`reported_kept` through every response); mutation `overrun-not-marked-reported` killed. The deterministic reproduction cannot confirm the fix on the same alignment (any change to the Kernel shifts it), so the evidence is the proof and the stress runs, whose completions above the Budget are counted on every run.

**A second one-off: part0's counters at 13 % (not reproduced).** The first run of the revised demo passed every rig check but the target's own report: `control` counted 2,385 Jobs, `sensor` 1,191 and `echo` 794 of 18,000, 9,000 and 6,000 (the record and the trace pins showed every Job run), while part1's counts were complete: part0's state had been zeroed about 24 s before the end, which only a Partition restart does (part0's fault response in this configuration is RESTART_PARTITION), and no check could have seen one: the target counts part0's events by Task over its 32-entry log, the rig's check is by Task over the mirror's last 512 events, and a restart names no Task. The trace freeze now takes a third expectation, the Partitions whose events freeze the trace whatever their kind (`TRACE_EXPECT.2`, bit p; the stress configuration names part0), so that a part0 restart freezes the trace at its cause with the 50 ms before it. The next two runs completed with every counter exact and no freeze; the one-off joins the 13.8 ms Job in section 13 with its diagnostic armed.

**Found by the first runs: the Kernel stack had no room.** The record grew by 1.6 KiB and the first two runs died within a second (a watchdog reset after an escalated BusFault, the trace ring overwritten): the three no-init records (the 512-entry trace ring, the event mirror, the release-timing record) and the Kernel's data and bss had left the MSP 776 bytes of the 32 KiB of Kernel RAM against a 3,564-byte worst case. The Link_Checker had passed the binary because it counted the Kernel's data and bss against the stack but not `.kernel.noinit`; it now counts the no-init records too (R37.2; `rsk-link` flags the failing binary), every HIL build runs it (`xtask::hil::link_check`, with the declaration variant's Task_Model), and the event mirror holds 512 entries instead of 1,024 (8 KiB back to the stack; the hour-long runs overwrite either many times over).

**Found by the first measurements: the Budget accounting dropped the running segment at every timed event that was not the Job's own Budget event (R18.1).** Every Critical_Section of a Task that a timed event interrupts measured as a wrapped negative (sampler's, never interrupted, measured 279 cycles): Kernel_Arch takes a charge point at every time-base event, which moves the account's origin, but the Kernel charged the cycles to the executing Job only when the event served its Budget slot; a Release or deadline of another Task dropped the segment. Overruns were therefore detected late by the dropped cycles: the response suite's 120 ms `sensor` Job, Budget 6 ms from 1,305 ms, logged its Overrun at 1,319 ms and 1,338 ms on successive runs (11.4 took the spread for preemption; it was the dropped segments at `control`'s and `echo`'s instants). `timer_event` now charges the executing Job on entry at every event and the Budget event judges "already recorded" from the account before the event's cycles (284 Verus items; the Kani harness `running_cycles_are_charged_at_every_timed_event` runs a Job across another Task's Release and its own Budget event). The proofs had not caught it because the contracts of `timer_event` say nothing of the Budget account (the entry points' unstated behaviour listed in `logic/kernel.rs`): a property of R18.1 for `timer_event` is open (task 4.x follow-up, section 13).

**Endpoint operations, release conditions, loop bounds.** None exist in the Ravenscar Profile_Variant of Phase P1 (Endpoints are Phase P4, release conditions Jorvik; R34.2); the loop-iteration report of R34.9 and R35.4 and the coverage of R34.4 belong to task 19.2 (11.13).

**Task 19.1 closed (2026-11-06).** The audit of the task's text against what was built: the target agent is the HIL build's Kernel (the Budget accounting, R18.1, charged at every charge point; the Critical_Section figures at the lock's return and the unlock's entry; task-entry maxima from the accounting, as the task states); the host runner is `rsk-hil` through `cargo xtask hil --release-timing|--stress`, writing `rsk-wcet/1` records folded over runs; the cycle-counter read cost is calibrated on the Target in the same build at instant 0 and subtracted from every section (R34.3; 1 cycle on this board); the agent's figure is compared with the logic-analyser trace for one Task on every run (the evidence the task names); the Kernel operations are measured by the entry record of 19.3 (11.5, 11.11). R34.7's two alternatives: the Flight_Build of the demo is the QEMU binary, which the rig cannot measure, and the nRF52840 HIL build differs from a Flight_Build of the same declaration by its instrumentation alone; the harness takes the second alternative, quantifying every instrumentation difference (11.12: the ring and the pins against the lean build, the records' own cost bounded by the calibration) and keeping the records of the instrumented build, whose figures include the effect rather than add it, so a record is conservative for a build without the instrumentation by the shares of 11.12. A Flight_Build measured without any instrumentation stays an item of section 13 (the trace pins alone would be the instrument, and the records' tables without the mirror remain to be built), as does the static cross-check of P5.

### 11.7 Isolation: the MPU access matrix and the EasyDMA cases (2026-11-02, task 7.4, R16.8, R17.7)

**The configuration.** The demo's isolation configuration (`nrf52840_isolation`, 3.6 seconds; feature `isolation` of the three Partition crates, which pulls the rsk-kernel feature `hw-dt`) declares every Partition's fault response END_JOB, so that one Task per Partition walks the access matrix one attempt per Job: a refused access ends that Job with a MemManage or BusFault event, and the next Job makes the next attempt. The walkers are `control` (part0), `log` (part1), and `sampler` (part2, after its EasyDMA cases). The matrix is generated, not written: the Generator emits each Partition's memory map into its `rsk_target::layout` (the Kernel's, the shared code's, and every Partition's code and RAM regions, stack sizes, and peripheral windows, DD-07), and `rsk::matrix` enumerates from it, against every other Partition in index order, a read, a write, and a fetch of its code, a read and a write of its data above its stack, a write into its stack, and a read and a write of its first peripheral window (skipped when it owns none), then against the Kernel a read and a write of its RAM, a read and a fetch of its flash, a read and a write of the Private Peripheral Bus (NVIC_ISER0, which a privileged Kernel reads and writes at every dispatch), since 2026-11-05 a read and a write of the first Kernel-owned peripheral after the PPB in the layout's Kernel peripheral intervals (CLOCK on the nRF52840; R16.2, the class the layout proof gained that day), and a write to the word below the Partition's own stack (R16.5): 25 attempts per Partition, 71 with an address (23 and 65 before the Kernel-owned peripheral class). The attempt index advances before the access and a returning access sets its bit in `survived`. The rig (`rsk-hil::isolation`) mirrors the enumeration from the Task_Model and judges: per walker, the fault events of its Task in order against the attempts, each followed by an END_JOB of the Task; the Kernel's new fault record (`FAULT_RING`, the CFSR and MMFAR, BFAR, or stacked PC of every fault) holding one entry per attempt with the attempted address and class (IACCVIOL for a fetch, DACCVIOL with MMARVALID for data, a precise BusFault with BFAR for the PPB); the walkers' counters (every attempt made, none returned); and the trace never frozen. Endpoint storage has no attempt: no Endpoint exists before Phase P4.

**The EasyDMA cases (R17.7).** `sampler`'s first seven Jobs request, on SPIM0's channel 0, its own 16-byte buffer (accepted), a local array in the Partition's stack (refused, R17.1), another Partition's RAM, the Kernel's RAM, 16 bytes starting 8 bytes before the Partition's RAM ends, the buffer on an absent channel (all refused), and the buffer again (accepted); each refusal is a `dma_rejected` event with its END_JOB. The safe state reads SPIM0's registers for the report (the reset the board takes on probe detach clears them): after the run RXD.PTR holds the buffer's address and RXD.MAXCNT 16, the TXD channel is 0, both LIST registers 0, ENABLE 0, no STARTED or END event, RXD.AMOUNT 0: the accepted request's pointer and count and nothing else, no transfer started (R17.2: a refused request reaches no register). For R17.6 `filter`'s Job of 50 ms exceeds its Budget (part2's Overrun_Response RESTART_PARTITION) and the Kernel's new actions record (`ARCH_ACTIONS`: every EasyDMA stop task and every Partition re-initialization, in order) shows SPIM0's stop task triggered before part2's re-initialization. The suite is the job's step `hil-isolation` (items `hil-mpu-matrix`, `hil-dma`, and the differential tests of 11.8 from the same run) and `cargo xtask hil --isolation`.

**Results (2026-11-02, `docs/hil/isolation_nrf52840_isolation.md`):** pass. 65 attempts, 65 faults (59 MemManage, 6 BusFault), each followed by its END_JOB, each with its address and class in the fault record, no survivor; the word below each Partition's stack refused; 5 refused EasyDMA requests, 2 accepted, the registers as stated; part2 restarted at 72 ms with the stop task (0x4000_3014) before the re-initialization; 145 events, none unexpected; `filter`'s register-pattern probe (11.8) 141 runs without a changed register.

**Found and fixed: a refused EasyDMA request under END_JOB returned into the ended Job (Kernel_Arch, R17.2).** The first refused request ended `sampler`'s Job inside the SVC, but the SVC returned into the Job as if it continued: `svc_core` read the executing Job for the end plan after the refusal had applied the response, found nothing changed, and planned no unwind, so the stub returned to the instruction after the SVC with the Kernel holding no Job for the Task; the Job's next SVC was served with no executing Job and the Kernel entered the safe state at 212 ms. The same defect, read after the response, had been found and fixed in the panic SVC by the fi-PR-19 fault injection (2026-10-23); the EasyDMA and raise branches had it too (the raise one by inspection, never reached: the demo's raises are all accepted). Both now read the executing Job before the request. Neither QEMU (every request accepted) nor the response suite (no refused request) could have found it.

**Found by inspection while preparing the cases: SPIM's list-mode registers were mis-addressed in the Target table.** `SPIM_DMA` named 0x53C and 0x54C as the LIST registers; those are the read-only RXD.AMOUNT and TXD.AMOUNT, and LIST is 0x540 and 0x550 (PS 6.26). The Kernel's clearing at boot (DD-08) wrote a read-only register and left LIST at its reset value, 0, which is the value it writes, so no behaviour differed; the table is corrected and the report reads both LIST registers.

**The static checks and the isolation build.** The Link_Checker's PR-22 and R16.3 checks report the isolation configuration's own attempts (the materialised PPB address, the fetches into Kernel code through the matrix's `blx`), as they should: the configuration exists to perform the accesses the Flight_Build must not contain. The job's HIL link check (R37 bounds) runs on it; the instruction scan is the Flight_Build's.

**The buffer of a transfer is out of the Task's reach (R17.5), and what a transfer touches (HW-15); 2026-11-16.** Until this date nothing addressed R17.5: `rsk::dma_request(channel, &[u8])` borrowed the buffer for the length of the call, the transfer ran on after it, and any Job of the Partition could read or write the bytes meanwhile. The request is now made through a type that owns the bytes:

- `rsk::DmaBuf<N>` (per-Task state) keeps its bytes private. `bytes()` gives them only while the buffer is not lent. `lend(channel, window)` asks the Kernel to program the channel (`dma_request_core`, R17.1, R17.2) and, when accepted, marks the buffer lent and returns a `rsk::DmaTransfer`.
- `DmaTransfer` is neither `Copy` nor `Clone`. `DmaBuf::reclaim(transfer)` requests the stop through the Kernel's register-write service (the shortcuts off, the events cleared, the STOP task), waits for the peripheral's STOPPED event for at most `DMA_STOP_POLLS` reads, and only then makes the bytes reachable; a peripheral that is not enabled runs no transfer and counts as stopped. Without the event the buffer stays lent and the transfer is handed back for a later Job. **Dropping a transfer stops it**; its buffer then stays lent until the Partition restarts.
- `rsk::DmaHeld` keeps a transfer between Jobs. It exists because a Partition's statics start as zeroed memory at reset and at every restart (R14.5), and zeroed memory read as `Option<DmaTransfer>` is not `None`. The first version of this change kept the transfer in an `Option` and passed the QEMU run without ever lending; the board's footprint step showed it (below).

Evidence, each run on its own: the host tests `crates/rsk/tests/dma.rs` (a lent buffer gives no bytes; a second lend is refused; another buffer's transfer does not release it; release waits for STOPPED; a new buffer is free and a new holder empty, both written with every field zero); the compile-fail members `api_dma_private` (the bytes' field is private, and the slice-borrowing request no longer exists) and `api_dma_moved` (a transfer used twice); the Profile_Lint on the Partition crates and `rsk` (no violation); the QEMU demo (RSK-OK, 2 of 2 `sampler` Jobs holding an accepted transfer); Creusot on the eight Partition 2 members, one at a time, on the final source (all proved); and on the rig the plain measurement, isolation and short stress configurations (all passed; the isolation suite's 5 refused and 2 accepted requests as before, with case 5 taking the held transfer back before it offers the buffer on an absent channel).

**HW-15 and the footprint.** The Hardware_Model has a fifteenth rule: an EasyDMA transfer reads and writes the bytes from its pointer register's address for its count register's length and no other (`hw_model::dma_touches`; the vendor's specification, RN-35, ASM-07; SPIM only). Over it, `lemma_dma_footprint_in_partition` (Verus on `rsk-logic` alone: 346 verified, 0 errors) shows that every byte of a DMA-permitted buffer of a Partition lies in that Partition's RAM above its stack and in no memory of the Kernel or of another Partition; and the Kani harness `dma_program_writes_the_pointer_and_the_count_and_nothing_else` shows that the function behind `Action::DmaProgram` writes exactly the pointer and the count register it is given. So the chain from the request to the bytes is: the postcondition of `dma_request_core` (what is programmed), the harness (the two register writes), HW-15 (what the peripheral then touches), the lemma (where those bytes are). HW-15 itself is checked on the board by the step "EasyDMA footprint (HW-15)" of the plain measurement run: the receive buffer of the traffic scenario lies between two 8-byte guards (`rsk::DmaGuarded`); at its second Job `sampler` takes the receive transfer back, which stops the traffic and waits for STOPPED, finds both guards as written and the buffer overwritten by the transfers of the 100 ms before, lends the buffer again and restarts the traffic. Passed (`docs/hil/measure_nrf52840_measure.md`). Its first two runs failed with every guard byte reading zero on both sides, which no overrun produces: the guards had been given their value as the static's initial value, and a Partition's statics are zeroed whatever value they declare. The guards are now written at run time.

**Findings of this change.** (1) A per-Task state or Resource declared with a non-zero initial value is zero at run time, at reset and after every restart, and the Generator accepts the declaration without a diagnostic. The design states that every Partition static is zero-initialized by construction; nothing checked it. Closed the same day (next paragraph). (2) The host doubles of the two EasyDMA supervisor calls wrote their record through the Kernel-side register double, which has asserted the Kernel's address precondition since 2026-11-06, so no host test could make a request; they now use the Partition-side double. (3) `sampler`'s measured maximum: 20,571 cycles in the plain measurement run (25,786 before, when every Job made a request) and 39,127 in the short stress run (37,815 before), within their Budgets with the margin.

Not shown at that point: the library tested and not proven, the peripheral's window address the Task's argument, HW-15 checked at one rate; each is taken up in the next paragraph. The hour-long configurations were not run on this build.

**The same day, later: the zeroed statics checked at compile time, and three gaps of R17 narrowed.**

- **A Partition static's initial value.** The Generator emits every cell (per-Task state, Resource, role state) through `ResourceCell::for_static`, a constant function that asserts every byte of the initial value is zero (`all_bytes_zero`, UJ-063, review). A declaration with a non-zero initial value no longer compiles, with the message naming R14.5; neither does a state type with padding bytes, whose value at those bytes is undefined (the compiler's own diagnostic). Compile-fail member `api_static_nonzero`. The check found one case in the tree at once: the isolation configuration declared `state filter.fpscr: u32 = 0xFFFF_FFFF` as a mark of "the probe has not written this", which was never in memory, so an unwritten record read 0, a value the rig's HW-10 row accepts when the declaration asks no flush-to-zero. The record now has a written flag, and the report omits the key when the probe did not run (the rig then lists the row as untested). On this build the row reads 0x1000000 against 0x1000000.
- **`dma_write_core` (R17.3).** The register-write service now ensures: a write is performed only for a channel of the executing Job's own Partition, at a word of that channel's 4 KiB window, to a register that is none of the pointer, count or list-mode registers of any channel of that window (`dma_reg_unmediated`); the model's record of such writes (`HwModel::dma_writes`) gains exactly that register and value; a refused request writes nothing; and the record of programmed transfers is untouched, as `dma_request_core` leaves the record of writes untouched. Verus on `rsk-kernel` alone: 274 verified, 0 errors. Two mutants registered, each run alone and killed (the mediated-register test dropped; the ownership test dropped).
- **The peripheral's address is not the Task's (R17.5).** The Generator emits each Partition's channels as constants (`rsk_target::DMA: [rsk::DmaChannel; n]`: the index in the system's DMA table and the peripheral's base address, from the table the Kernel's is generated from). `DmaBuf::lend` takes one of those; a Task supplies no index or address.
- **The lending rules by bounded model checking (R17.5).** Kani harness `bytes_are_out_of_reach_while_a_transfer_is_outstanding` (in `crates/rsk`, run alone: successful in 154 s): for every sequence of four operations on one buffer (read its bytes; lend it and keep or drop the transfer; take a kept transfer back; drop a kept transfer), with the peripheral's ENABLE register and STOPPED event set to any values before each, the bytes are handed out exactly when no accepted transfer is outstanding, a lend is accepted exactly when none is, and a transfer stops being outstanding only through a take-back that found the peripheral not enabled or reporting STOPPED. With the release rule broken on purpose (the buffer released without the event) the harness fails; the change was not kept. Bounds: four operations, a wait of two reads under Kani (1,024 on the Target), the host doubles of the supervisor calls (a request always accepted). **Since 2026-11-21 the bound of four operations is lifted** by a second harness, `any_operation_from_any_state_keeps_the_bytes_out_of_reach`: from any state of the buffer (any flag byte, any bytes), with any transfer value (any channel, window, buffer address and stop flag) and any values of that transfer's ENABLE register and STOPPED event, each operation keeps the invariant that the lent flag is set exactly while a transfer is outstanding: `bytes` succeeds exactly when the flag is clear; `lend` succeeds exactly when it is clear, sets it and returns a value naming this buffer and the channel's peripheral; `reclaim` clears it only when it was set, the value names this buffer, and that value's peripheral is not enabled or reports STOPPED, and otherwise hands the value back unchanged; a `DmaHeld` returns the value it was given, once; `end` and a drop leave the flag. `a_new_buffer_is_not_lent` is the base case. Both run alone in 40 s. What the induction takes from outside the harness: Task code makes a transfer value only by `lend` and cannot copy one (private fields, no `Clone`; the fixture `api_dma_private`). The other bounds stand: the two-read wait and the host doubles, under which a refused request is not exercised. Mutants (Kani): `kani-dma-bytes-handed-out-while-lent`, `kani-dma-reclaim-without-the-stop`, `kani-dma-reclaim-with-another-buffers-transfer`, `kani-dma-lend-while-lent`, all killed.
- **HW-15 at three rates.** The footprint step now runs at the three Jobs after the first, for the traffic of the 100 ms before each: at 8 Mbps, 1 Mbps and 125 kbps (the peripheral disabled while its rate is changed), the run going on at 8 Mbps. All three passed on the board: the buffer written, neither guard, the buffer read only after STOPPED. One peripheral (SPIM0); a second instance was not declared.

On the final source, each on its own: Creusot on the eight Partition 2 members (all proved), the Profile_Lint (no violation), the QEMU demo (RSK-OK), the Generator's tests, and on the rig the plain measurement, isolation and short stress configurations (all passed). `sampler`'s measured maximum: 22,255 cycles in the plain measurement run (the three footprint Jobs included), 39,135 in the short stress run. Not run: the full job, the hour-long configurations, the whole mutation register.

**Postconditions the requirements file exposed, and a defect one of them found (2026-11-16, later).** `docs/requirements_evidence.md` recorded three rules that were in function bodies and not in contracts. Each is now a postcondition (Verus on `rsk-kernel` alone: 274 verified, 0 errors; `rsk-logic` 346):

- **A send that is not accepted leaves every channel as it was and writes nothing into the Endpoint storage (R40.4, R40.6, R39.4).** Stating it failed to verify: `endpoint_send_core` took the slot (`channel_push`) and, when the slot's address could not then be formed (`endpoint_slot` returning nothing), returned "refused" with the slot counted and nothing copied into it, a message that was never sent and that the receiver would have taken. The path needs an inconsistent table to be reached, which the boot's validation excludes, but nothing in the function relied on that. The queue is now put back on that path.
- **A full buffer is reported (R40.5).** The log's newest event is the buffer-bound violation against the executing Job's Task and that Task's Partition (`KernelOps::spec_last_event`, from `Kernel::push_event`'s new clause that the pushed event is the log's last).
- **A failed boot has made nothing live (R12.2, R12.4).** `boot_core` returning false has enabled and pended no interrupt, armed no compare and not started the time base.

Three mutants registered, each run alone and killed (the restore removed; the report removed; a failing boot that starts the time base). On the changed Kernel: the response suite on the rig passed, with its Endpoint cases.

**HW-15 on a second instance (2026-11-16, later).** The plain measurement configuration's Partition 2 owns SPIM1 as well; at two further Jobs `sampler` stops SPIM0's traffic, runs SPIM1 on the same pins for 100 ms into a second guarded buffer, takes that transfer back and judges it, and restarts SPIM0. Passed on the board with the three rates of SPIM0 (`sampler`'s maximum 26,664 cycles, within its Budget with the margin). The unanswered stimulus pulse of the first hour-long run (2026-11-01) is as it was: no later run reproduced it, and the hour-long configurations were not run for this revision.

**`docs/requirements_evidence.md` covers the 61 requirements (2026-11-16, later).** Twenty-two rows are written from the text of the contracts and theorems they cite (63 statements, each hashed). Thirty-nine are written from the requirement's text, the plan and the matrix's facts, and say so where the tool's source was not read: their judgment is of the kind of evidence, not of the tool's code. 5 read "no" (R5, R11, R36, R56, R60: R5, R36, R56 and R60 are not implemented, Phase P5; R11 has no formal statement, tests only), 5 "yes" (R1, R2, R4, R8, R47: met as asked), the other 51 "weaker" with the reason.

**The Generator: its arithmetic proven, its output validated at every build (2026-11-16, later).** The Generator (about 6,300 lines) had no proof. Three things were done, in the order of what decides something.

- **The derivation arithmetic is verified** (`crates/rsk-gen/src/derive_v.rs`, inside `verus!`; `cargo verus verify -p rsk-gen`: 17 verified, 0 errors; the crate opts into Verus as `rsk-protocol`, already in its closure, does). `ceiling` returns an upper bound of the accessors' Priorities that one attains (R23.1); `priority_byte` is `(8 - p) * 32` for a level 1 to 8 and 0 otherwise (DD-02); `slot_plan` gives every Task a deadline and a Budget slot and a release slot when asked, no slot twice, all below the count less the Kernel's two, the count at most 253 (R9.4, R23.4); `scale` returns the quotient rounded down or up as asked, within one unit of the exact value on that side, with the inexact flag (R21.2, R29.8, Property 14); `region_log2` returns the smallest power of two, not below the minimum, that holds the bytes (DD-07). `derive.rs`, `convert.rs` and `layout.rs` call these. The specifications are the Generator's own: the Config_Checker's (5.19) are written separately in its crate and nothing is shared (R26.1), so each derived number now has two independent proofs and a comparison. The generated files of the thirteen variants are byte for byte what they were (`cargo xtask gen --check`). Two faults of the code replaced, both outside what any declaration in the tree reaches: `(8 - level) << 5` underflowed for a level above 8, and the slot counter was a `u8` incremented before the check against 253. Five mutants registered, each run alone and killed.
- **A configuration that is not well-formed does not compile.** `Config::validate` (proven: true exactly when `Config::wf`, the premise of every Kernel proof) and `Layout::validate` (proven: true only for `Layout::wf`, the premise of the isolation proofs) are now constant functions, and the Generator emits `const _: () = assert!(CONFIG.validate(), ..)` and the same for `LAYOUT` after the tables. The compiler evaluates the Kernel's own validators on the tables as generated; until now that happened at boot, on the board. No specification is restated for this: it is the Kernel's. Compile-fail member `api_tables_invalid` (a Task Priority of 0) beside `api_tables_valid`, which builds. `rsk-logic` and `rsk-kernel` re-verified with the constant functions: 346 and 274 verified, 0 errors.
- **The printed table is read back** (`crates/rsk-gen/src/readback.rs`): after the system source is written, a reader that shares only the field names with the emitter takes the numbers of the `CONFIG` table out of the text and compares them with the derived values; a difference fails the generation, naming the item. Tested on the demo and on three altered texts. Since 2026-11-17 the `LAYOUT` table is read back the same way against the derived memory plan (`check_layout`: the three Kernel intervals, the Kernel windows and their count, and per Partition the code and RAM intervals with their subregion masks, the stack size, the windows, their count and their read-only flags; the padding entries must be `NO_WINDOW`). `Layout::validate` at build time accepts any well-formed layout; this refuses one that is not the derived one. Tested on the demo and four altered texts (a RAM base moved, a stack halved, a window granted, a Kernel window enlarged); all 14 configurations regenerate unchanged under it.

What this is and is not. It is not a proof that the Generator is correct for every declaration: its validation rules (`validate.rs`), its reading of the declaration, the layout placement and the printer are ordinary code. It is that the numbers are computed by proven arithmetic, that every build's tables are accepted by the Kernel's proven validator before the crate compiles, that what was printed is what was derived, and (as before) that what the binary holds is what an independent recomputation gives. The front end (the `rsk::app!` parser) stays tested.

**The full job and the hour-long records on the Generator's tree (2026-11-17).** `cargo xtask verify` passes with 0 violations on this tree (79 minutes; rsk-gen's 17 Verus items attributed). Its first run found four things, each fixed and checked alone: the API-misuse fixture's lock file was stale after rsk-gen gained its Verus dependencies; the loop of `all_bytes_zero` (the compile-time zero check of Partition statics) had no bound for the Profile_Lint and is now bounded by `STATIC_BYTES_MAX` (256 KiB, a larger static refused); the job counted rsk-gen's `cli`-gated binary as a Verus target although the command never builds it; and the UPPAAL lease had expired, which the job's network sandbox cannot renew (running `cargo xtask uppaal-corpus` outside it renews the lease; the report was unchanged). Hour-long runs, 3,660 s each, both passed: release timing, 823,317 periodic Releases at their nominal instants, lag at most 8,377 cycles against J_rel 12,500, 203,333 stimulus pulses answered and none unanswered, the one deliberate-overload deadline miss of `control` as before; stress, lag at most 9,422 cycles, the protected Partition's responses within their WCRTs (`control` 0.679 ms, `echo` 0.360 ms, `sensor` 5.901 ms), the longest masked span 24,429 cycles against L_kernel 35,000. The stress record now names the Kernel entries inside each Task's longest Job: `sampler`'s 42,719-cycle Job carries 5 entries (dispatch, timer, intake) of 14,156 cycles. The Analyzer on the stress run's measured records still reports FAIL (utilization 97 %; `log`, `pulse`, and `sampler` above their deadlines by the margin-inflated terms), as the record before it did (98 %); every observed response is within its WCRT where one exists.

**The five requirements judged "no", the lint in the static checks, the licence message (2026-11-17, later).** R11 and R60 now cite proven text and read "weaker": for R11 the stub theorem `theorem_nested_irq` with `resumed_as_left` (S16 to S31 on the stub model), `booted` (eager stacking) and a new postcondition of `dispatch_job_core` (the model's floating-point access equals the dispatched Task's declaration, `KernelOps::spec_fpu`; mutant `dispatch-grants-fpu-to-every-job` killed; rsk-kernel 274 verified, 0 errors); the same statement for the return to a preempted Job is not proven. For R60 the `KernelArch` contract of `apply` and the entry cores stated for any implementor. R5, R36 and R56 stay "no" and carry a `deferred` field in the evidence register, rendered as "not attempted, by decision" with the reason. `cargo xtask check` now runs the Profile_Lint (`verify::lint_command`, shared with the job's step). The UPPAAL corpus step reports an expired licence lease as such (`uppaal_unlicensed`) instead of a differing report.

**Sporadic arrivals on the code, and what of the hand-modelled transitions extracts (2026-11-17, later still).**

*The spacing of a Sporadic_Task's arrivals is derived* (`Sched/SpFrame.lean`, 37 theorems; `Sched/SpIntake.lean`, 7; `Sched/Sporadic.lean`, 14; no `sorry`, axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`). `Timing` keeps `has_last`, `last_due` and `deferred_until` per Sporadic_Task. Every Kernel function of the run but two leaves all three (`SpKept`: the proofs of `TimeKept` with the fields exchanged, generated from them). `intake_release` writes them through `Timing::accept`, only after `intake_decision` answered accept, so with the last due instant at least one MIT before the new one (`intake_decision_accept`), or through `Timing::defer`, which sets the deferral instant to the last due instant plus the MIT; `deferred_release` takes the deferral instant as the due instant (`intake_release_sp`, `deferred_release_sp`). `SpRel` states this per transition with the Release state beside it, and `kstep_sprel` proves it of every code-modelled transition. The run `SReach` lets a Sporadic_Task's Job arrive with the transition that makes its Release pending from none held, at the instant the Kernel records as the Release's due instant (`saccept`), or at the instant a deferred event was deferred to (`darrive`), neither with a spacing condition; `sreach_kreach` shows every such run is a run of `KRun.lean` (invariant `SpAt`: a held Release is deferred to the last due instant plus one MIT, and the last arrival is at or before the last due instant, or waits and is exactly one MIT after it), and `property18_sporadic` is Property 18 over it. Conditions that remain: the instant the Kernel records for an accepted event is the run's instant of the arrival (the Kernel's reading in its handler, R10.6's latency being measured); a Task is periodic or sporadic and not both; the hand-modelled transitions leave the nominal instants and the sporadic timing fields. Mutants `lean-code-deferral-without-the-mit` and `lean-sporadic-hand-step-moves-a-due-instant`.

*What extracts of the hand-modelled transitions.* Nothing of `rsk_logic` is missing: `serve_slot`, `timer_event`, `budget_event`, `enter_safe_state` and `intake` are in `Sched/Kernel.lean` already, so `plain_h`, `bevent_h`, `overrun_h` and `opend` are by hand for want of proof, not of extraction (for each, the transition must be restated as the extracted function applied to the run's Kernel state, and the account of its Kernel section proven). A trial extraction (Charon, then Aeneas, into a scratch directory; `Sched/Kernel.lean` not regenerated) of the rest of `KernelOps` for `Bound` and of the entry cores of `arch/cm4/entry.rs` gave: all 25 `KernelOps` methods named, `Kernel::fault`, `fault_of` and `record_event` among them; 34 of the 45 entry functions reached, with `apply_core`, `svc_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `start_core`, `dispatch_vector_core`, `end_plan_core`, the channel functions (`channel_push`, `channel_pop`, `endpoint_slot`, `endpoint_args`, `copy_in_checked`, `copy_out_checked`) and the EasyDMA cores; and 11 without a body: `endpoint_send_core`, `endpoint_recv_core` (the code `svc_h` stands for), `svc_service_core`, `timer_core`, `intake_core`, `dispatch_job_core`, `epilogue_core`, `fault_core`, `unbound_core`, `stack_violation_core`, `boot_core`. All 11 fail for one reason: the function builds a `Ghost(..)` value in executable code (a ghost argument to a callee, a ghost field written, a ghost half of a returned pair; 25 sites of 7 shapes), which the Verus-erased build compiles to a closure that Aeneas rejects (`fn_def_to_fn_ptr`). The remedy `rsk-logic` already uses for `no_history` applies: build each ghost value in a small function of executable arguments, kept opaque to Charon. That is a change to proven Kernel source (ghost only), to be followed by the Verus run of `rsk-kernel` and a check that the Flight_Build's bytes are unchanged; it was not made here.

**The entry cores extracted, and the Budget event's own entry on the code (2026-11-17, last).**

*Extraction.* The entry cores now build every ghost value through seven small functions of `crates/rsk-kernel/src/arch/cm4/ghostv.rs` (`ghost_snap`, `ghost_int`, `ghost_partition`, `ghost_seq_empty`, `ghost_seq_push_u8`, `ghost_seq_push_usize`, `ghost_seq_drop_last`; 25 sites of `entry.rs` changed, the snapshots of the entry state taken once at the top of each core and handed to `end_plan_core`), and `fault_core`'s last disjunct is `no_job_in_progress`, a function with a declared result. rsk-kernel: 282 verified, 0 errors (274 before, the eight functions added). Placed in `entry.rs` itself the seven made two unrelated proofs fail (`clear_channels`, `endpoint_reinit`: the solver's context changed), which is why they are a module of their own; no proof was altered. The Flight_Build's loadable image (197,912 bytes) differs from the one built from the committed source in 17 places, each the source line number of a panic location record (11 of `entry.rs`, 6 of `bound.rs`, whose lines moved); no instruction and no other datum changed. With the seven kept opaque, Charon and Aeneas translate the whole start set of `proofs/aeneas/README.md`: 315 functions, none without a body, the 11 entry cores that had failed among them; the 179 declarations `Sched/Kernel.lean` held before are unchanged, and every proof of `lean/Sched` builds on the new file. Nothing proven uses the entry cores yet: `svc_h` (an Endpoint operation) and `plain_h` are still by hand.

*The Budget event's own entry.* `bevent_h` and `overrun_h` now take the Kernel state after the entry from the code (`hcode : Kernel::budget_event x.k cfg t i = ok k'`), with the scheduler fact they already stated beside it, and a third transition, `overrun_p`, covers what the pair "`overrun_h` then `resp`" stood for: the overrunning Job on top ended through `Sched::finish_top` and the rest of a Partition's stop or restart applied, in one call of `budget_event`. `Sched/Timer.lean` reads all three scheduler facts from the code (`served_bevent`, `served_bevent_recorded`, `served_overrun`, and new: `end_partition_jobs_top`, `stop_partition_top`, `restart_partition_top`, `apply_response_partition`, `served_overrun_partition`). The condition that a hand-modelled transition leaves the nominal instants and the sporadic timing fields no longer covers a Budget event (`HandShape` has lost its `finish_top` case; `kstep_nomrel` and `kstep_sprel` take these three from `budget_event_tk`, `budget_event_sp` and `budget_event_eff`). Not covered by `overrun_p`: an Overrun_Response that enters the safe state, which ends no Job. Still by hand: `plain_h`, `svc_h`, and `opend`.

**An Endpoint operation on the code (2026-11-17, `Sched/Endpoint.lean`; the first proof over the extracted entry cores).** `endpoint_send_core_kernel` and `endpoint_recv_core_kernel`: for any implementor of `KernelOps`, the hardware interface and the tables, the Kernel after `endpoint_send_core` is the Kernel before or the result of exactly one interface call, `fault` with `EV_API_MISUSE` (bad arguments, no slot), `record_event` with `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL`, or `intake` (the release of the receiving Task); after `endpoint_recv_core` it is the Kernel before or the result of that `fault`. `bound_fault` and `bound_record_event` say what the two calls are for `Bound`: `Kernel::fault` and `Kernel::record_event` on its Kernel state. The run follows: `svc_h` no longer leaves the Kernel free with the scheduler state kept, it takes the Kernel before or `Kernel::record_event`'s result (that the scheduler state is kept is now derived, `record_event_sched`), and two transitions are added, `faulted` (`Kernel::fault`) and `faulted_of` (`Kernel::fault_of`), each a response with no Kernel section of its own as `resp` is (`fault_r`, `fault_of_r`; until now a fault was `resp` applied to the state before, which skipped the log entry `fault` writes first). `KStep.svc_send` and `KStep.svc_recv` show that an Endpoint operation by the Job on top, as the cores return for `Bound`, is `svc_h`, `svc_h` then `faulted`, or, for a send that releases the receiver, `svc_h` with the Kernel as before and the interface's `intake` call remaining. `kstep_nomrel` and `kstep_sprel` take `svc_h` and the two fault transitions from the code (`record_event_tk`, `fault_tk`, `fault_of_tk` and their `SpKept` forms), so the condition on hand-modelled transitions no longer covers an Endpoint operation. Limits: the extraction holds `Bound`'s interface functions one by one and not as one implementor (some are not extracted), so the two link theorems take any implementor whose `fault` and `record_event` are `Bound`'s; the link from `Bound`'s `intake` to the run's `deadline` and `intake` transitions is still by hand; what the cores do to the hardware and to the Endpoint buffers is not read. Axioms: the four theorems over the cores depend, beside `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, on `ghostv.ghost_partition` (and `svc_recv` on `ghostv.ghost_int`): the declarations of the opaque ghost-value functions the cores call, each an uninterpreted function with its type and no stated property (the statements are of the form "if the core returns", whatever those functions return). `property18_kernel`, `property18_timed` and `property18_sporadic` do not depend on them. Still by hand: `plain_h` and `opend`. Mutant `lean-endpoint-entry-state-not-the-codes`.

**The last transitions modelled by hand, the safe-state Overrun, and the intake an Endpoint send ends in (2026-11-17, evening).** Three changes to the run of `Sched/KRun.lean`, after which no transition leaves any part of the Kernel's state to a hypothesis.

*`plain_h` and `opend` on `serve_slot`.* Both now carry `hcode : Kernel::serve_slot x.k cfg i = ok k'` beside the scheduler fact they already stated (the scheduler state kept; the safe state entered from that scheduler state), which `Sched/Timer.lean` reads from the same call (`served_kept`, `served_end`). Two lemmas over the whole of `serve_slot`, a slot of any kind: `serve_slot_nomrel` (`Sched/Arrive.lean`: only a release slot moves a nominal instant, by its Task's period) and `serve_slot_sprel` (`Sched/Sporadic.lean`: the sporadic timing fields and the Release state change only as `SpRel` allows). With them `kstep_nomrel` and `kstep_sprel` hold of every transition with no side condition; `HandShape` is deleted, and `TStep.other`, `SStep.saccept` and `SStep.other` lose the hypotheses that a hand-modelled transition leaves the nominal instants and the sporadic timing fields. `property18_timed` and `property18_sporadic` are therefore stated over runs with one condition fewer each, and their statements are otherwise unchanged.

*An Overrun answered by the safe state.* `Kernel::enter_safe_state` drops every Release and ends no Job, so the entry is neither `overrun_h` nor `overrun_p` (both end the Job on top through `Sched::finish_top`). The new transition `bevent_r` is a Budget event's own entry stated only through the code: the Kernel state is `budget_event`'s, the accounts follow the scheduler state it left (`budget_event_eff`: a response), and no last unit of the Job is counted. It holds for every outcome of `budget_event`, so `KStep.served_bevent_any` needs no premise about the account or the Partition's Overrun_Response. Its Kernel section is bounded by `Kt`, the Budget event's entry, not by `Kov`: for a response that does more than record this is a condition on the measurement, and no rig record was read for it here.

*The reading of the time base, and `Bound`'s `intake`.* `observed` is `Kernel::observe`, the first call of a timer entry and of an intake: it writes the Kernel's reading and nothing else (`observe_sched`, `observe_nom`, `observe_timing`), and has no Kernel section of its own. With it the interface call an Endpoint send ends in when its message releases the receiver is read to the end (`Sched/Endpoint.lean`): `bound_intake` (`Bound`'s `intake` is nothing, for an index out of range, a Task that is not sporadic or an instant past the operating duration, or `Kernel::intake` with the offender replaced by the Task when out of range), `kernel_intake_cases` (`Kernel::intake` is `observe`, then `deadline_miss` when the Task's deadline slot is armed, of the deadline kind and due, then `intake_release`), and `KStep.bound_intake_run` (from a state of the run: nothing, or `observed` followed by the calls that are the Kernel-side premises of the run's `deadline` and `intake` transitions). The model-side premises of those two transitions (which event awaited its entry, the section's bound) are not derived, as for every transition.

*Mutants.* Two entries of the register mutated the conditions that are gone (`lean-timed-hand-step-moves-a-nominal-instant`, `lean-sporadic-hand-step-moves-a-due-instant`). Each is replaced by a mutant of what took the condition's place: `lean-plain-entry-state-not-the-codes` and `lean-end-entry-state-not-the-codes` (the transition without `serve_slot`'s result). `lean-kernel-plain-entry-of-a-deadline-source` is re-anchored, the same mutation. Two are new: `lean-budget-response-entry-state-not-the-codes` and `lean-observed-state-not-the-codes`.

*What is still not covered.* The link theorems of `Sched/Endpoint.lean` over the entry cores depend on the opaque ghost helpers as before. What the cores do to the hardware and the Endpoint buffers is not read. `timer_core` and `intake_core` are not read: that a timer entry is `Bound`'s `timer_event` and an interrupt's intake `Bound`'s `intake` is their Verus contract.

**The Verus facts in Lean: the Budget slot's ownership proven, the deadline slot's disarm not, and a finding (2026-11-17, night).** The table "The Verus facts and the run" lists two facts the Lean proof took from Verus without joining them. Both were attempted on the extracted code.

*An armed Budget slot is the executing Job's* (`Sched/Slots.lean`, 64 theorems, no `sorry`; `kreachb_bo` and `budget_slot_top` on the axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`). The invariant `BO` is `budget_slots_owned` stated by the slot's kind and not by its index: a slot that is armed with the Budget kind names the Task on top of the stack and is that Task's Budget slot. Stated so it needs nothing of the configuration (Verus needs the slots distinct), because only `arm_budget` writes the Budget kind. Every Kernel function of the run keeps it with no precondition (`*_box`, one per function, the loops of a Partition's stop and restart by induction): `dispatch`, `job_return` and `end_top_release` disarm the Budget slot of the Job on top before the stack changes, so that none is armed while it does (`nob_top_disarm`), and `arm_budget` is called only for the Task the caller read from the top of the stack (`dispatch` after `Sched::start`, `job_return` and `end_top` for the resumed Job, `settle`, and `budget_event` after its own test that the Task is on top). `kstep_bo` is the same of every transition of `KStep`, with no condition on the slot index or the Task a timer transition names, and `kreachb_bo` of every state reached from a boot state with no Budget slot armed (`KReachB`; `new_nob` and `start_nob` show `Kernel::new` followed by `Kernel::start` gives one; `new_nob` depends on the extraction's axiom `no_history`, the others do not). `budget_slot_top` is the consequence: when the slot served is armed with the Budget kind, a Job is in progress, the slot's Task is on top, and `topBudgetArmed` holds. `KStep.served_bevent_reach` is `served_bevent_any` without those two premises. So of the condition "a Budget event's own entry finds a state an entry's last step left, with the Budget slot of the Job on top armed", the second half is now a consequence of the code; the first half (`hset`: every entry core ends with `settle`) is still the entry cores' Verus postcondition. "One Budget event per Job" is not a Verus fact and stays a condition (`budget_event` arms the slot again when it finds no Overrun). Mutants `lean-budget-slot-owner-is-any-task` and `lean-code-dispatch-keeps-the-preempted-budget-slot`.

*The deadline slot's disarm: not proven, because the invariant it would give does not hold of the code.* In Verus the fact is a pair of postconditions (`job_return`, `end_top_release`: with no Release of the Task held the ended Job's deadline slot is disarmed, with one held it is kept); there is no invariant over the run. The run's condition "a deadline event has an entry only on a miss" needs one: an armed deadline slot belongs to a Job that is pending or in progress. Stating it exposed a case in which it is false.

**Finding (closed the same night, below): a Job that ended before its deadline is reported as a Deadline miss when a deferred Release of its Task was held at its end.** `job_return` (and `end_top_release`) disarm the deadline slot only when `release == REL_NONE`. The comment and the postcondition treat any held Release as one that "became due while this Job was in progress" and has armed the slot for its own deadline (R9.6, audit finding 5 of 2026-11-13). That is so for a pending Release. A deferred Release (`REL_DEFERRED`, a Sporadic_Task with the deferral policy) arms the deadline slot only when `deferred_release` makes it, so until then the slot holds the ended Job's deadline. When that instant comes, `timer_event` serves it and `deadline_miss` logs `EV_DEADLINE_MISS` and applies the Partition's deadline response, unconditionally. With the deadline equal to the MIT the deferral falls at the same instant, and the deadline slots are served first (KD-R9.6).
