Witness: `crates/rsk-loom/tests/deadline_after_deferral.rs` (the small configuration; an event accepted at 10, the Job dispatched, a second event deferred at 20, the Job ended at 20, the timer entry at 110 logged one Deadline miss; run 2026-11-17 on the Kernel as it was). Reach: an event is deferred while the Task's Job is in progress only when it arrives inside the MIT window, which a masked interrupt source cannot do (DD-06); a Release_Signal raise and an Endpoint send that releases its receiver call `intake` without regard to the mask (`entry.rs`, the signal and send cores), so a Job that raises twice inside the receiver's MIT, the receiver's Job in progress at the second raise, gives the case. No rig record shows it: the stress and timing configurations' sporadic Tasks are released by interrupt sources.

**The Kernel change (2026-11-17, on the user's decision).** `job_return` and `end_top_release` disarm the ended Job's deadline slot unless a Release of the Task is pending (`release != REL_PENDING`, before `release == REL_NONE`), and their postconditions say so: with no Release pending the slot is disarmed (after `job_return`: disarmed or the unmask event of the MIT window), with one pending it is kept. Nothing else was changed, no proof body among them. Verus: rsk-logic 346 verified, 0 errors; rsk-kernel 282 verified, 0 errors (each run fresh). The witness test now asserts the opposite: the slot is disarmed at the Job's end, the timer entry at 110 logs no Deadline miss, makes the deferred Release and arms its deadline event for 210. Host tests of rsk-loom, rsk-logic and rsk-kernel pass. Mutants: `job-return-disarms-held-deadline` re-anchored (the same mutation, on the new condition) and `job-return-keeps-the-deadline-under-a-deferred-release` new (the condition as it was), each run alone and killed by the postcondition. `Sched/Kernel.lean` regenerated: it differs from the translation before in these two conditions and in source line numbers. Not run for this change: the Kani harnesses, the full verification job, the rig (the records under `docs/hil/` are of the Kernel before it).

Also read and not run: after the safe state is entered (`enter_safe_state` drops every Release and disarms no deadline slot), `Kernel::intake` still serves an armed, due deadline slot of the Task before `intake_release`; `timer_event` does not (it returns at once in the safe state).

**The deadline invariant on the code (2026-11-17, late; `Sched/Deadline.lean`, 66 theorems, no `sorry`; `kreachs_di`, `deadline_slot_incomplete` and `KStep.served_deadline_reach` on the axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).** With the Kernel change above the invariant holds and is proven over the run: outside the safe state, a timer slot armed with the deadline kind names a Task of the configuration, is that Task's deadline slot, and the Task has a Release pending or a Job in progress (`DG`, stated by the slot's kind as `BO` is). It is not in Verus, which has the two postconditions only.

**The same two statements in Verus (2026-11-25).** The deadline invariant and the quieted state every entry leaves, until now Lean's alone, are proven on the source as well. `Kernel::deadline_slots_owned` (`crates/rsk-logic/src/logic/kernel.rs`): outside the safe state, a timer slot armed with the deadline kind is the deadline slot of a Task with a Release pending or a Job in progress. `Kernel::new` establishes it and every function of the Kernel keeps it (`deadline_kept`). It is not a clause of `inv`: it is false for a few statements inside `job_return` and `end_top_release` (the Job has ended, its slot is disarmed after the record and a log entry) and between the kill of a Partition's preempted Jobs and the clearing of its Releases, and `inv` is required there; so it is carried beside `inv`, with the exempt Task or Partition named (`deadline_slots_owned_but`), which is the exemption `E` of the Lean proof. `Kernel::quieted` is Lean's `Quieted`, and `lemma_guard_quieted` proves it the same as `dispatch_guard` under `inv`. In Kernel_Arch every operation of `KernelOps` and every entry core keeps both (`spec_deadline_owned`, `spec_dispatch_guard`; `slots_step` on the seven cores the shells call), and `entry::theorem_entries_run` states them of every state between the entries of a run from the start of the system, with `a1_run`'s trust base. No executable line changed. Run: `rsk-logic` 350 verified, `rsk-kernel` 380 verified; five mutants registered and killed (`deadline-owned-forced-end-keeps-the-slot`, `deadline-owned-cleared-release-keeps-the-slot`, `deadline-owned-safe-state-not-excluded`, `quieted-without-the-budget-slot-invariant`, `entries-run-without-the-cores-step`). Not ported: the hold of `Sched/Hold.lean`, a statement about the run's timing model, which Verus has not. Not run: the verification job, Kani, the Lean build (the translation is of the executable code, which is unchanged).

**Three more statements in the other prover (2026-11-25, later).** (1) Register drivers, Verus: `drivers.rs` already had each driver's effect on the register file as its contract; added, to match `now_spec` of `Hw/Model.lean`, that the time base `timer0::now`, `arm` and `rearm` return is the extension of the capture register's reading (`Extend::reads`, `Extend::then`; for `Clock` two uninterpreted functions of a ghost value, nothing assumed of them; `Clock` is built by `Clock::new()` at its three call sites, a zero-sized value as before). `rsk-kernel` 381 verified. (2) The scheduler over a run, Lean, `Sched/Restore.lean` (12 theorems): `ceiling_restored` (`lemma_ceiling_restored`: after a granted lock, along a stretch of the run of `Sched/Run.lean` in which the lock stack never has fewer entries than the lock left, the unlock granted when it is again that long is of the Resource locked and leaves the System_Ceiling at its value before the lock) and `deadlock_free` (`lemma_deadlock_free` parts 2 and 3; part 1 is `lock_entry_finds_free`), also of every state the run over the Kernel's functions reaches. Not done: the ceiling restore for a stretch of the run over the Kernel's functions. (3) Interrupt sources, Lean, `Sched/MaskFrame.lean` (41 theorems) and `Sched/Intakes.lean` (19): every function of the run other than `observe` and `intake_release` leaves the instant and `mask_until` and lifts a mask only with the instant at or past the window's end (`KM`); `intake_release` reads the instant and then returns that state or sets the mask for one MIT (`intake_release_chain`); the invariant `MI` (Verus's `intakes_linked` for one intake) is kept by every transition (`kstep_mi`); two effective intakes of a source are at least one MIT apart and `n` of them span `n - 1` MITs (`irun_spaced`, `irun_count`; `lemma_intake_spacing_over_run`, `lemma_intake_count_bound`). The history being erased, an effective intake is an intake that finds its source unmasked and leaves it masked, and a stretch carries the list of those it names. No `sorry`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. `lake build Sched` passes. Four mutants registered and killed (`timer-now-extends-the-compare-register`, `lean-restore-stretch-may-dip-below-the-lock`, `lean-unmask-event-without-the-window-test`, `lean-effective-intake-from-a-masked-source`). Not run: the verification job, Kani, the rig; the Lean translation was not regenerated (no function it translates changed).

**The Health_Monitor, the interrupt controller and the entry cores' hardware writes in Lean (2026-11-25, night).** `Sched/Contain.lean` (48 theorems): `fault_first` (`fault_recorded`: `Kernel::fault` pushes the fault's event on the state it finds, against the Task on top and its Partition or against the Kernel, and then applies the Partition's declared response or enters the safe state); `apply_response_cases` and `apply_response_contained` (a response other than the safe state is steps of the Tasks of its Partition and of its Task: `CStep`, and `csteps_cont` gives what they leave alone, the scheduler's clauses of `others_unchanged`); `end_partition_jobs_ended` (`partition_ended`, given every Job in progress is on the stack); `stop_partition_flags`, `restart_partition_flags` and the frame `FL` on the other Partitions' flags and counts. Not in Lean: the other Partitions' Budget accounts and timer slots under a response, and that the System_Ceiling does not rise. `Sched/Nvic.lean` (14): the NVIC part of `hw_model` restated (`encodeLevel`, `execPrio`, `nvicTakes`, `basepriFor`, `nvicSelects`) and proven against the extracted scheduler state: `dispatch_takes_iff_elig`, `dispatch_selects_srp`, `dispatch_idle`, `kernel_level_never_masked`, `kernel_level_taken`, `kernel_level_wins`, `kernel_level_not_preempted`; the lowest-identifier tie-break of `select` is a premise there. `Sched/ArchHw.lean` (60): the model's fields these statements read (`HV`, `hstep`) and the trait's contract as a class (`HSpec`, with `KBp` for `basepri_now`); the frame `HQ` (PRIMASK as it was, not cleared, the watchdog not fed) of every core but the start's and the time base's, with `svc_core_hq`, `irq_entry_core_hq` (unless the vector is the time base's), `timer_core_fed` and `start_core_wf`; and A1 after `apply_core` and after the six cores that end in it (`apply_core_a1`, `timer_core_a1`, `intake_core_a1`, `unbound_core_a1`, `stack_violation_core_a1`, `fault_core_a1`, `start_core_a1`). Not in Lean: A1 after the dispatch, unwind, interrupt-entry and supervisor-call cores, `boot_core`'s vector priorities, the floating-point access, and the join of `HSpec` to the register model. No `sorry`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; `lake build Sched` passes; three mutants registered and killed (`lean-response-kill-of-any-task`, `lean-nvic-encoding-order-reversed`, `lean-primask-clear-counted-quiet`). No Rust source changed.

**The three rows' remainders in Lean (2026-11-26).** `Sched/ContainMore.lean` (20 theorems): a response does not raise the System_Ceiling (`cstep_ceil`, `csteps_ceil`) and leaves the Budget account of every Task outside its Partition, other than its own Task, as it was (the frame `BU`, through `apply_response_bu`). Not in Lean: the other Partitions' timer slots and timing fields under a response. `Sched/HwMore.lean` (13): the rest of `hw_model` restated and proven (`basepri_max_only_raises`, `return_from_sole_active`, `job_cannot_set_mask`, `mpu_grant_covered`, `hw_partition_isolation`, `hw_write_isolation`, `kernel_ram_kept`, `entry_frame_is_frame`, `compare_never_too_close`, `elapsed_in_range`, `dma_footprint`). `Sched/ArchHwA1.lean` (7) and `Sched/ArchHwA1Svc.lean` (4): A1 after the dispatch of a Job and after the unwind (`dispatch_job_core_a1`, `epilogue_core_a1`), and kept by a dispatch vector and a supervisor call (`dispatch_vector_core_a1`, `svc_service_core_a1`, `svc_core_a1`; an accepted EasyDMA request or register write returns the Kernel's state it was given, `dma_request_core_kept`, `dma_write_core_kept`). `Sched/ArchHwBoot.lean` (20), over the contract extended with the floating-point access and the lines' priority and enable (`HV2`, `HSpec2`): `boot_vectors_ok` (each line 16 to 16 + `irqs` as its binding declares, an unbound line disabled), `boot_core_hq` (the watchdog not fed, PRIMASK not cleared), `dispatch_job_core_fpu`, `epilogue_core_fpu`. **Kernel RAM, dispatch planning and the time base in Lean (2026-11-26, later).** `Sched/Plan.lean` (12 theorems), on the extracted functions of `plan.rs`: `frame_in_stack_spec` (`spec_frame_in_stack`), `plan_frame_some` and `plan_frame_none` (every clause of `plan_frame`'s contract), `plan_frame_in_stack`, `compare_target_spec` (the value is `compare_for` of `Sched/HwMore.lean`; unlike Verus the statement needs no bound on the reading, since it is of the form "if the function returns"), `top_active_full`; and `storage_kept_between_entries` (`theorem_storage_kept_between_entries`, from `kernel_ram_kept`). `Hw/TimeBase.lean` is a new Aeneas translation, of `ArchState::extend` alone (the Kernel's translation does not reach it: it is called through the trusted accessor of the Kernel's one `ArchState`); `Hw/TimeBaseProofs.lean` (7 theorems) proves its contract (`extend_total`, `extend_spec`), the induction over a sequence of readings the contract names (`extends_true`), `lemma_housekeeping_within_a_period`, and `now_true`: `timer0::now` over the register model returns the true instant (`now_spec` joined to the extension through `archExtend`, which stands for the Rust `Clock`). Assumed as in Verus: two consecutive readings are less than one counter period apart. Not done: joining the extended instants to `Sched/Time.lean` and Property 18's run. No Rust source changed.

**The checksums, the Config_Checker's image functions and the Generator's acceptance check in Lean (2026-11-26, later again).** Aeneas refuses a `return` inside a loop unless the loop is the last statement of its function, and a loop nested in another. So the Rust source changed, with every Verus contract as it was: in `crates/rsk-gen/src/accept_v.rs` the loops of `accept` and `endpoint_accept` end by a flag, and the inner loop over an Endpoint's edges is the function `edges_accept`; in `crates/rsk-confcheck/src/image_v.rs` each loop of the three decoders and of the comparisons is a function of its own (`rd_tasks`, `rd_resources`, `rd_rows`, `rd_partitions`, `rd_windows`, `rd_kernel_windows`, `rd_partition_layouts`, `rd_endpoints`; `tasks_agree`, `resources_agree`, `rows_agree`, `partitions_agree`, `kernel_windows_agree`, `parts_agree`), `rd_endpoint` reads its validation rows with `rd_rows`, and the two loops of `storage_placed_ok` end by a flag. `cargo-verus verus verify`: `rsk-gen` 29 verified, `rsk-confcheck` 126 verified; the two crates' host tests pass. New translations: `Tools/GenAccept.lean` (`accept`) and `Tools/CcImage.lean` (`crc32`, the three image checks and through them the decoders and comparisons, `storage_placed_ok`, `storage_in_kernel_ram_ok`); neither has an axiom. `Tools/CrcSpec.lean`: one definition of CRC-32. `Sched/Crc.lean` (11 theorems): the Kernel's `Crc32::byte`, `u32`, `u64` and `finish` compute it. `Tools/CcImageProofs.lean` (77 theorems): `crc32_spec`; the readers (`Reads`: what a reader passed is the encoding of what it returned), `decode_tables_spec`, `decode_layout_image_spec`, `decode_endpoint_image_spec`; the comparisons and `image_is_spec`, `layout_image_is_spec`, `endpoint_image_is_spec`; `storage_placed_ok_spec` (both directions) and `storage_in_kernel_ram_ok_spec`. `Tools/GenAcceptProofs.lean` (15 theorems): `accept_spec`. `Tools/FlagLoop.lean`: the rules for a checking loop that ends by a flag and for a comparison loop. Every theorem is total. No `sorry`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound` (two byte lemmas first proven with `bv_decide`, which adds axioms of its own, were reproven bit by bit). `lake build` passes. Four mutants registered and killed (`lean-crc-polynomial-changed`, `lean-acceptance-deadline-after-the-period`, `lean-image-word-bytes-swapped`, `lean-ram-apart-without-the-address-space-bound`); two existing mutants' `find` texts were moved to the same statements in the restructured code and are killed (`confcheck-storage-alignment-not-checked`, `gen-acceptance-takes-a-report-on-any-edge`). Not done: the encodings are restated in Lean, not shared with Verus; `config_crc` and `layout_crc` as whole computations; `image` of `accept_v.rs`.

**The assembly stubs' machine code in Lean (2026-11-26, last).** Until now the stub model (5.17; `stub_model.rs`, `Sched/StubModel.lean`) assumed the meaning of its instruction functions (`i_push_r3_r11_lr`, ...). Four files replace that assumption by a proof.

- `Sched/Arm.lean`: a semantics of the Thumb instructions the stubs use, from the Armv7-M Architecture Reference Manual: the encodings as a decoder from halfwords (`decode16`, `decode32`), each instruction's operation on a processor state (R0 to R12, SP_main, LR, PC, PSP, the flags, ITSTATE, PRIMASK, FAULTMASK, CONTROL, IPSR, S0 to S31, memory in bytes), conditional execution and the IT block, and one step of the machine from a code memory. Handler mode, privileged. An unaligned multiple load or store, an FP instruction with lazy state preservation active, and anything the stubs do not use have no result.
- `Sched/StubCode.lean`: the halfwords of each stub as `llvm-objdump` prints them from a linked binary, and 110 theorems, one per instruction: the decoder reads that instruction at that offset. The two halfwords of a `bl` to a Rust half and of a `b.w` to `rsk_unwind` are parameters (they hold a distance that differs per binary); the theorems hold for any that are such a branch.
- `Sched/StubRefine.lean` (78 theorems): the relation `R` between a machine state and a model state, the lemmas about words of byte memory, and for each instruction form the simulation lemma: the machine's instruction from a related state reaches the state the model's function gives.
- `Sched/StubPaths.lean` (33 theorems): for each path of each stub the machine, run on the stub's code, returns and reaches the state the model's path function gives, at the program counter the model's control line names (`irq_to_call_run`, `irq_after_call_run`, `irq_dispatch_run`, `irq_restore_run`, `irq_return_run`, `irq_to_unwind_run`; `unwind_entry_run`, `unwind_to_call_run`, `unwind_after_call_run`, `unwind_exit_run`, `unwind_again_run`; `svc_to_call_run`, `mm_to_call_run`, `fault_to_call_run`, `timer_to_call_run`; the shared tail `<stub>_after_call_run`, `<stub>_return_run`, `<stub>_to_unwind_step`; the calls `<stub>_bl`).

`cargo xtask stubs <binary>` now also compares the binary's halfwords with `Sched/StubCode.lean` (parameters must be a `bl` and a `b.w`); run on four linked binaries of 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-21 (the stubs' source last changed 2026-11-16), each agrees.

**Finding (CONTROL.FPCA).** With FPCCR.ASPEN set, as the boot sets it, a floating-point instruction executed with CONTROL.FPCA clear sets FPCA (`ExecuteFPCheck`, B1.5.7). An exception entry clears FPCA, so `vpusheq` at the head of `rsk_irq_entry`, executed when the preempted context has a floating-point frame, sets it; the `mrs r2, control` that follows reads CONTROL with FPCA set, the Rust half records that value, and `rsk_unwind` writes it back. The stub model's `vpush` and `vpop` leave CONTROL. The theorems state the difference: after such a path the machine's CONTROL is the model's plus `e`, `e` being 0 or 4 and 0 when the entry had no floating-point frame, and so is the R2 handed to the Rust half; `msr control` in `rsk_unwind` and in the dispatch path ends the excess (the value written is exact). No behaviour is known to depend on it: an exception return sets FPCA from EXC_RETURN. The two stub models (Verus and Lean) are unchanged; whether to make their `vpush` and `vpop` set FPCA is open.

**Finding (`rsk_timer_entry`).** Nothing refers to the stub and no binary links it. Its code in `Sched/StubCode.lean` is assembled by hand from the encodings of the same instructions in the fault stubs and compared with no binary.

Assumed still: the semantics of `Sched/Arm.lean` (a transcription by hand, not compared with a reference model of the architecture); the exception model; AAPCS for the Rust halves; that no exception is taken between two instructions of a path; flash is not written (code is read from a memory of its own). The paths' premises: no wrap of the stack pointer over the path's pushes and pops, SP_main a multiple of 4, the PSP value a multiple of 4, the CONTROL value with no bit but nPRIV and FPCA, no HardFault or NMI active (for `cpsid f`), no lazy floating-point state preservation pending. No `sorry`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; `lake build` passes; three mutants registered and killed (`lean-arm-push-highest-register-first`, `lean-stub-code-halfword-changed`, `lean-arm-fp-instruction-leaves-fpca`). No Kernel source changed; `xtask` has the halfword comparison and a test of it.

**The two findings closed and the main-stack bound extended (2026-11-26, after the above; on the user's word).**

1. *The Kernel's main-stack bound* (`crates/rsk-link/src/stack.rs`, `msp_bound`; R37.2). Asked whether CONTROL.FPCA set in a handler makes a nested entry stack the extended frame: it does not in normal operation, since from `vpusheq` to the exception return PRIMASK is set, the dispatch path writes CONTROL = 1 before it unmasks, and every return path sets FAULTMASK before it clears PRIMASK. But the bound had no term at all for a handler that preempts a handler. Two cases now counted: a dispatch stub preempted at its entry instruction by a higher vector or a Kernel-level entry (5.8a), a basic frame per Job level; and a HardFault or NMI taken inside a handler or the start, its frame (the extended one when a Task has a floating-point context) and the fatal handler's own usage. The bound is the largest of the earlier rule, the start with a fault in it, and handlers nested; never below the earlier rule (tested). On the seven linked binaries at hand the bound rises (cycles-fpu 3,880 to 4,680 of 5,004 bytes available; flight 3,896 to 4,768 of 29,308) and each still passes. Not confirmed by a run on the rig: the stack-painting measurement was not repeated. *2026-11-27:* run on the rig builds that night, the bound refused four of them; and the figures here are the unfixed decoder's (on the commit's own timing build it read 4,712 where the frame of the boot, allocated with the unread encoding, makes it 5,064 of 5,004). See "The decoder's unread decrements" below.
2. *CONTROL.FPCA in the stub models.* `vpush` and `vpop` set the FPCA bit (`fpca`) in `stub_model.rs` and in `Sched/StubModel.lean`. Theorems restated, none weakened in what it says of the other registers: where they said CONTROL is the entry's (`lemma_irq_call`, `lemma_irq_called`, `lemma_irq_restore`, `theorem_irq_stub`; Lean `irq_call`, `irq_pro_spec`, `irq_called_spec`, `pops_spec`, `irq_restore_spec`) they say `entry_control`: the entry's CONTROL, with FPCA set when the entry stacked a floating-point frame. `theorem_nested_irq` keeps its statement (a Handler-mode EXC_RETURN has no floating-point frame; `lemma_handler_exc_return` gained that bit). `Sched/StubRefine.lean`'s `sim_vpush_eq` and `sim_vpop_eq` and every theorem of `Sched/StubPaths.lean` are exact: the excess is gone.
3. *`rsk_timer_entry` removed* (5.17's record of 2026-11-13 left it "removed or guarded before a Target binds it"): the stub, the `extern` declaration and `rsk_timer_rust` of `rsk-entry`, the shell `arch::cm4::timer`, justification UJ-025, `timer_to_call` and `theorem_timer_stub` of the Verus model, `timer_to_call`, `timer_stub`, `timer_stub_mem` and `timer_stub_world` in Lean, `timerCode` and its theorems, and the entries of xtask's stub tables. `timer_core` stays: the time base is served through `rsk_irq_entry`. Proofs deleted with the code they were about: `theorem_timer_stub` (Verus), `timer_stub`, `timer_stub_mem`, `timer_stub_world` (Lean), seven decode theorems and five path theorems of the machine-code proof.

**What the stubs' proof still assumed, proven or checked (2026-11-26, the last).** Of the five assumptions the survey listed under the machine-code proof:

1. *The exception model.* `Sched/ArmExc.lean`: the exception entry (`excEntry`: the frame on the stack in use, basic or extended, its alignment, EXC_RETURN in LR, R0 to R3, R12 and the flags UNKNOWN, IPSR, ITSTATE cleared, CONTROL.FPCA and SPSEL cleared) and the return to a handler with a basic frame (`excReturnHandler`), from B1.5.6 and B1.5.8. `Sched/StubExc.lean` (7 theorems) proves the stub model's three statements: `excEntry_hwEntry` (`hwEntry`, HW-06: what LR says of the interrupted context and what was stacked on the main stack), `excEntry_sim` (the entry taken while a handler executes is `exc_enter_handler`, for a frame of 32 or 36 bytes), `excReturn_sim` (the return to it is `exc_return_handler`, HW-07). Premises: not the NMI or the HardFault, room for the frame, CONTROL.FPCA clear at the entry and at the return (as at a stub's entry instruction).
   **Two more model corrections**, in `stub_model.rs` and `Sched/StubModel.lean`: `exc_enter_handler` kept R0 to R3, R12 and Z, which `ExceptionTaken` leaves UNKNOWN: they are `hx`'s now; `exc_return_handler` kept Z, which the processor restores from the stacked xPSR: it is bit 30 of the frame's eighth word now. No theorem of either model needed its statement changed (`theorem_nested_irq` reads R0 to R3 and R12 back from the frame); `rsk-kernel` 381 verified.
2. *No exception between two instructions of a path.* `Sched/StubMask.lean` (12 theorems): the execution priority with PRIMASK and FAULTMASK (`execPriority`), `masked_no_configurable` (with either mask set no exception of priority 0 or more is taken), `execI_masks` (an instruction other than `cps` leaves both masks) and the three `cps` lemmas. Each path theorem of `Sched/StubPaths.lean` now also states, of every state of its run (`AlongRun`): for the dispatch stub and `rsk_unwind`, after their first instruction and up to and including the exception return, a mask is set (`Msk`); for the Kernel-level stubs both masks are as at their entry (`EqM`), so the execution priority is the entry's. What can still be taken is a HardFault or an NMI, which end in the safe state.
3. *The semantics itself.* Not provable; checked in part. The decoders were compared with `llvm-objdump`: every halfword `decode16` accepts and a sweep of the pairs `decode32` accepts (243,021 encodings, and 28 more in a second sweep of `msr`), each read by LLVM as the same instruction with the same operands: 243,049 agree, none differs. The first run found the decoders accepting what the manual gives as UNPREDICTABLE or reserved (a `push` or `pop` of no register, the 32-bit forms with one register, `cps` with neither flag, `it` with the condition 1111, special registers the semantics has no case for, `dsb` and `isb` with an option other than SY); they refuse them now. The comparison was a script, not kept, and is not repeated by any job. What each instruction does (`eff`) was not compared with anything.
4. *AAPCS for the Rust halves*: still assumed. It is a property of the compiler's output, not of source a prover reads.
5. *The Verus model is the Lean model*: still by `cargo xtask check` for the sequences and by reading for the instruction functions.

Not modelled still: the returns to Thread mode (the model stops at them), tail-chaining and late arrival. No `sorry`; axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; `lake build` passes; two mutants registered and killed (`lean-exc-entry-handler-exc-return-thread`, `lean-masked-means-both-masks`).

**The Partition API proven against the Kernel's contracts (2026-11-26).** `rsk` was the one component of a Flight_Build with nothing proven: the App_Prover trusts its contracts, and nothing but reading said that a request word `rsk` builds is the one the Kernel decodes, or that the reply `rsk` reads means what the Kernel put there.

- *One specification, two sides.* `crates/rsk-kernel/src/arch/cm4/svc_abi.rs` states the interface once: the request words (`endpoint_arg`, `violation_word`, `rejection_word`, `dma_request_word`, `dma_write_word`) with lemmas that the Kernel's decoding expressions (those of `endpoint_args`, `violation_core` and the EasyDMA branch) read back what was put in and find no bit they refuse a request for; `svc_ret`, an uninterpreted predicate for "this Job issued supervisor call n with these R0 to R2 and resumed with this R0"; and `svc_served`, what the handler leaves in word 0 of the frame for a request.
- *The Kernel's side.* `svc_core` and `svc_service_core` gained the postcondition `svc_served`: an EasyDMA request is answered 0 exactly when `dma_accepts`; a register write 0 only for a channel of the Partition and an unmediated register; a raise 0 exactly when `raise_accepts`; a send or a receive with its service's code and the service's contract between the state found and the state the service left (`send_served`, `recv_served`: the functional clauses of `endpoint_send_core` and `endpoint_recv_core`, existentially over the state before the fault response is applied); lock and unlock 0 or 1; the report 0. No executable line of the Kernel changed. `rsk-kernel`: 395 verified (381 before).
- *`rsk`'s side.* The crate is a Verus crate now (`[package.metadata.verus]`; the verification job finds it by that). Proven: `endpoint_word` is `endpoint_arg`; `send_request` and `recv_request` issue their service's call with it and return the reading of the reply (`Ok` exactly for `ENDPOINT_OK`, the peer-state error exactly for `ENDPOINT_PEER`, and so on: what the two Kani harnesses check for every code); `Signal::raise`, `dma_write`, `report_protocol_violation`, `report_rejected_message` and `now` likewise; `DmaBuf` (R17.5): `bytes` hands the bytes out exactly when the buffer is not lent, `lend` requests nothing for a lent buffer and otherwise issues the request and marks the buffer lent exactly when the reply is 0, `reclaim` is the only place the mark is cleared and clears it only on `stop_evidence` (a read of ENABLE that was not the enabled value, or a read of the STOPPED event that was not 0), neither changes a byte; `DmaTransfer::stopped` reads at most `DMA_STOP_POLLS` times; `DmaHeld::take` and `put`; `Gate`; `DmaGuarded::arm` and the verdict of `footprint`. 60 verified.
- *Source changed for it.* `crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`: the verified items stand inside `verus!` blocks; `send_result` and `recv_answer` are as they were; `Endpoint::arg` became the free function `endpoint_word` and the two Endpoint shells call `send_request` and `recv_request`; the stubs are called through eleven one-line functions with trusted specifications (`k_lock` to `k_read`, and `DmaBuf::address`), entered in `docs/proof_assumptions.toml`; `DmaTransfer::end(mut self)` rebinds `self` (Verus takes no `mut self`); `DmaGuarded::footprint` and `stopped` gained loop invariants. `vstd` is a dependency of `rsk` as it is of the Kernel (erased from a build).
- *Trusted, and where it is written.* That a supervisor call of a Job is one run of `svc_core` with that number and that frame (`svc_abi.rs`'s header): the stubs' assembly, the exception entry and return, the SVC stub, and the shell that reads the number. With it, the two sides join: an `Ok` of `send_request` is a reply `ENDPOINT_OK` to the request word of that Endpoint, role and peer, for which `svc_served` gives `send_served`.
- *Not done.* The App_Prover's contracts stay `trusted` in Creusot, and that each follows from the Verus statement is by reading (for `Gate` they are the same three sentences; `channel::sent` and the like are opaque there). The generic shells (`Endpoint::send_to`, `recv_from`, `Shared::lock`, `PerTask`, `RoleSlot`) are not verified: `Plain`, `Words` and `ResourceCell` have no specifications. On the Kernel's side: which of 0 and 1 a lock answers, what a report records, and the clock's value. `reclaim`'s test that the transfer is the buffer's own rests on the buffer's address, which has no specification (Kani covers it).

Seven mutants registered and killed (two of the handler's reply, five of `rsk`); the two Kani mutants of `rsk`'s readings were rerun and are killed (396 in the register). Evidence row R7: `svc_core` gained a clause; the row was read against the statement, which it still describes, and the new hash entered.

**Eight components by agents, brought in and checked (2026-11-26, the last).** Eight agents, each in its own copy of the tree, worked on rows of the coverage survey and were stopped before any had reported; their work was brought into the tree, checked, and in places corrected. What each left, as it now stands:

- *Health_Monitor, the remainder* (Lean, `Sched/ContainSlots.lean`, `Sched/SlotsApart.lean`): a response leaves every other Partition's Task its deadline slot, Release slot and timing fields, and its Budget slot unless that Task is the one resumed on top (`apply_response_others`, `fault_others`); the distinct-slots facts are `validate`'s.
- *Entry cores over the hardware model* (Lean, `Sched/ArchHwA1Irq.lean`, `ArchHwBootRet.lean`, `ArchHwSvcCheck.lean`, `Sched/SvcCheck.lean`, `Hw/Lines.lean`): BASEPRI over `irq_entry_core`, a lemma per branch (`irq_entry_core_a1`); the vector lines at `boot_core`'s return (`boot_core_vectors`); `svc_check_core` in a translation of its own (`svc_check_core_t`). Not done: the join of `HSpec` to the register model.
- *Analyzer* (Lean, `Rta/Inputs.lean`, `Sched/Checked.lean`; `Rta/Rta.lean` regenerated with `check_inputs`, `endpoint_sites`, `endpoint_cost_add`): `check_inputs` answers `None` exactly when the inputs are admissible; Property 18's theorems with that answer as their hypothesis (`property18_kernel_checked` and three more).
- *Link_Checker certificate* (Lean, `Tools/Link.lean`, `Tools/LinkCertProofs.lean`): `cert::check` answers true exactly when `Certified` holds (`check_spec`), and then every walk is within the recorded usage.
- *Protocol_Checker* (Lean, `Proto/ProjExec.lean`, `RoleOps.lean`, `CertSpec.lean`, `CertExec.lean`, `Certify.lean`; `Proto/Proto.lean` regenerated): projection, role operations, and `certify_sound`. `progress.rs` and `api.rs` restructured for the translation (each loop a function), every contract kept (573 verified). Not done: the lexer.
- *Shells, entry code facts, the Kernel side of the Partition API* (Verus, `arch/cm4/mod.rs`, `entry.rs`, `bound.rs`, `svc_abi.rs`, `rsk-entry`): each shell is a verified generic function over a trait for the three things it cannot verify (the two safe-state calls, specified as not returning, and the PPI boot check), called from a one-line unverified function that hands it the statics; the boot's order is `reset_shell`. Every entry core states what it pends (`pends_only`, `selection_pended`: the selected Task's vector and no other, with the deferred and own vectors of 5.8a named for the interrupt entry) and the view loaded at a return to a Job. `svc_served` gained the lock's and unlock's answer (`spec_lock_grants`, `spec_unlock_grants`), the report service (`report_served`) and the clock (`now_served`). The entry cores gained clauses and ghost code only. `rsk-kernel`: 417 verified.
- *`rsk`, the remainder* (Verus and Creusot): the generic shells verified over trusted specifications of `Words`, `Plain` and `ResourceCell` (`sent_to`, `received_from`, `Shared::lock` with a token the unlock consumes); `reclaim`'s address test and the dropped transfer's stop stated. `Gate`, `DmaChannel`, `DmaHeld`, `Signal::new`, `Endpoint::new` and `DmaTransfer::channel` are no longer `trusted` in Creusot: a proof workspace of `rsk`'s own bodies (`proofs/creusot-rsk`) proves them. `rsk`: 73 verified.
- *Config_Checker front end and `rsk-elf`* (Verus and Lean): the ELF reader's core is `rsk-elf/src/reader_v.rs` (56 verified; `Tools/Elf.lean`, `Tools/ElfProofs.lean`), the public reader a wrapper of it; the Config_Checker's table builder is `tables_v.rs` (`rsk-confcheck` 179 verified). Not done: the Task_Model's JSON reader.

*Two defects of the Link_Checker's decoder found and corrected* (`crates/rsk-link/src/thumb.rs`; both older than this work, neither met by a binary so far). (1) `vpush` and `vpop` were recognised by the first halfwords `0xED2D` and `0xECBD` only; with the D bit set (`0xED6D`, `0xECFD`: an odd-numbered first single-precision register, or D16 and above) they fell to the floating-point moves, which leave the stack pointer, and the bytes pushed were not counted. (2) Four data-processing cases (`sub.w` and `add.w` on SP, `mov.w`/`movw` to SP, `movw`/`movt`) did not test bit 15 of the second halfword, so a `bl`, `b.w` or `b<cond>.w` whose distance gave its first halfword one of their patterns (323,584 bytes or more) was read as a constant load or a stack-pointer change and its edge was lost from the analysis. Both came out of an agent's unfinished comparison of the decoder with `Sched/Arm.lean`. Tests: the existing 32-bit test extended with the two `vpush`/`vpop` forms; a new test over every first halfword `11110...` with a sample of second halfwords of `bl` and `b.w` (it fails on the code as it was). The decoder's translation was regenerated (the recipe reproduces the file exactly before the change).

*Checked when brought in.* Verus on each crate in the merged tree (`rsk-kernel` 417, `rsk` 73, `rsk-elf` 56, `rsk-link` 10, `rsk-confcheck` 179, `rsk-protocol` 573, `rsk-analyzer` 108, `rsk-gen` 29; 0 errors); `lake build` of every library; the axioms of 26 named theorems (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`); no `sorry`, axiom, `admit` or `assume` added; the 49 Verus mutants the agents registered, each killed; host tests of the changed crates and of `xtask`; the demo on QEMU (`RSK-OK`); the Link_Checker and the Config_Checker, before and after, on that binary: the same output and the same stack bounds; Creusot on `proofs/creusot-rsk` (28 files) and on two application members (`p1-demo-part0-proof`, `pacemaker-comms-nrf52840-proof`). Read in full: the Kernel's and `rsk`'s changes. Read in part: the Lean files (the specifications the main theorems are stated over, and those theorems), the ELF reader's executable changes. Evidence rows R7, R10, R11, R14, R16, R17, R40, R42: twelve cited contracts gained clauses; each row was read against its statement and the hashes entered.

*Trusted, added.* The shells' environment (`ShellEnv`: the safe-state calls do not return); in `rsk`, the one-line functions over `ResourceCell`, `Words` and the message glue, and the lock token (`docs/proof_assumptions.toml`).

**The Link_Checker's decoder against the Armv7-M semantics (2026-11-26, the last).** The comparison an agent had begun, whose draft found the decoder's two defects, was lost with a temporary directory, regenerated from the agent's record (its generator scripts rerun; the one file with a committed copy came out identical), adapted to the corrected decoder, and finished. `Tools/LinkThumbDefs.lean`: what the decoder answers for a halfword, or two, as functions of natural numbers (`D16`, `D32`), and the two agreement statements (`SpAgrees`, `TargetAgrees`). `Tools/LinkThumbProofs.lean` (78 theorems): the compiled `decode16_static` and `decode32_static`, as Aeneas extracts them, return for every input at an address below 2^30 and compute `D16` and `D32` (`decode16_spec`, `decode32_spec`; the second one declaration of some 950 lines, with a larger heartbeat budget for that declaration). `Tools/LinkThumbSpec.lean` (18): for every encoding the semantics' decoders accept, `D16` and `D32` read the stack-pointer change the operation makes and the branch target it loads (`d16_agrees`, `d32_agrees`). `Tools/LinkThumbArm.lean` (6): the two joined, on the compiled code (`decode16_arm`, `decode32_arm`), and for one step of the machine, where an instruction whose condition fails in an IT block leaves SP (`decode16_sp_step`, `decode32_sp_step`). The draft's three theorems that stated the two defects as exceptions are gone with the defects: `d32_agrees` and `decode32_arm` have no exception hypothesis, and the theorem that said an odd-register `vpush` reads as a move no longer holds of the corrected decoder. Covered: the instruction forms `Sched/Arm.lean` has (those of the stubs). Not covered: the forms it has not, the literal wrapper, `decode_function`, the call graph and the bounds over it. Axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; no `sorry`. One mutant registered (`lean-link-decoder-vpush-with-the-d-bit-not-read`).

**The Link_Checker's nodes and bounds (2026-11-26, after the decoder).** Between the decoder and the certificate stood the construction of a node and, after the walk, the bounds; both are verified now, with one lemma in Lean for the frame model. `crates/rsk-link/src/bound_v.rs` (Verus; `rsk-link` 27 verified, 10 before): `frame_of`, `dynamic_of`, `direct_of` compute a function's frame, dynamic-SP mark and direct edges from the instructions the decoder read, with contracts that say exactly which (`down_sum`, `edge`); `CallGraph::build` takes the three from them, where it accumulated them inside its loop. `msp_value` and `jobs_bound` compute the main-stack bound's three cases and a Partition's sum with checked arithmetic (`None`, read as a bound no region holds, when a term does not fit 64 bits, where the unchecked sums would have wrapped in a release build); `stack::msp_bound` and `stack::bounds` take the numbers from them. `MspInputs` and the two frame constants moved to the module. The Link_Checker before and after on the demo binary: the same report and the same stack bounds. `Tools/LinkFrame.lean` (5 theorems): along a run whose steps change SP as the decoder read them (`SpStep`, which `decode16_sp_step` and `decode32_sp_step` give), with no dynamic step and no increment above the stack's top, SP descends by no more than the sum of the decrements of the steps (`descent_le`), and by no more than the body's frame when the steps are instructions of the body each at most once (`descent_le_frame`). The premise that no instruction is executed twice is the frame model's and is not checked by the tool (a loop that lowers SP). Three mutants registered and killed. The App_Prover's step of the verification job now also proves `proofs/creusot-rsk`.

**The decoder's unread decrements, the refusal of unread writes of SP, and the main stack's room (2026-11-27).** The night's campaign on 633e414e failed: the verification job with 13 violations, and four rig steps at the build, the Link_Checker refusing the binaries on R37.2. Three causes, the first older than the commit.

*The defect.* `decode32_static` matched `sub sp, sp, #const` by the first halfwords `0xF1AD`, `0xF1BD` and `0xF2AD` (and `add` by `0xF10D`, `0xF11D`, `0xF20D`): the encodings whose immediate has its top bit clear. With the bit set the first halfword is `0xF5AD`, `0xF5BD` or `0xF6AD`, no case matched, and the instruction was read as leaving SP. The compiler uses that encoding for a frame its other encodings cannot express: `sub.w sp, sp, #0x6b0` (1,712 bytes) is the prologue of `Reset` in a rig build of commit 0fb2aa14, and the Link_Checker reported that function's frame as 8 bytes. The Lean comparison of 2026-11-26 did not meet it: constant subtractions from SP in the 32-bit encodings were among the forms `Sched/Arm.lean` had no instruction for, and the plan said so. Stack painting did not meet it either: a measured depth can refute a bound only when it exceeds it.

*What was low.* The Link_Checker as it was (commit 5d6282e4) and as it is, on the same binaries:

| Binary | Region | Before | Fixed | Available |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| demo, timing rig build, commit 0fb2aa14 | Kernel (MSP) | 4,712 | 5,064 | 5,004 |
| demo, isolation rig build, commit 0fb2aa14 | Kernel (MSP) | 4,528 | 4,752 | 4,364 |
| demo, measurement rig build, commit 0fb2aa14 | Kernel (MSP) | 4,760 | 4,760 | 4,772 |
| PACEMAKER, rig build, commit 0fb2aa14 | `therapy` | 3,224 | 15,864 | 12,288 |
| | `comms` | 752 | 1,308 | 2,048 |
| | Kernel (MSP) | 2,272 | 2,380 | 2,340 |
| PACEMAKER on QEMU, 2026-11-27 | `therapy` | 3,248 | 20,176 | 12,288 until that day |
| | Kernel (MSP) | 1,976 | 6,768 | 30,236 |

On the four rig binaries of the night (633e414e: short stress, timing, measurement, cycles) and on ten rig builds and two flight builds of the demo of 2026-11-27 the two agree in every region: which encoding a frame gets is the compiler's choice per build. The demo's Partitions' bounds are the same under both on every binary compared. The PACEMAKER's were not: each Job of `therapy` has a frame of 3.7 to 5 KiB (the timing state by value), allocated with the unread encoding, and the Partition's stack of 12 KiB was below its bound in every build so far. No overflow was observed (a write below the stack is a MemManage fault and the Partition's fault response, R16; no record shows one), and no measurement of that Partition's stack depth exists.

*The repair of the decoder* (`crates/rsk-link/src/thumb.rs`). The two constant forms are matched by mask, the i bit excluded from the test (`a & 0xFBEF == 0xF1AD`, `a & 0xFBFF == 0xF2AD`, and `add` likewise); `ldr Rt, [sp], #imm8`, the pop of one register, is read as raising SP. `thumb_expand_imm` writes the rotation as a shift (the amount is 8 to 31 and the value has 8 bits; tested against `rotate_right` on every modified immediate), so that its translation has a value.

*Fail closed.* Until now an instruction the decoder had no case for was `Other`, which the analysis reads as leaving SP: an unrecognised write of SP lowered a bound in silence, as this one did. `sp_unread(a, b)` answers whether a 32-bit instruction belongs to a class that can write register 13, as a destination, as a loaded register, or as a base register written back, by the encoding classes of A5.3: data processing with a modified or plain immediate; load and store multiple; the exclusive forms; load and store of two registers; data processing with a shifted register; load and store of one register; data processing with registers, multiply and divide; coprocessor load, store and two-register transfers; one-register transfers from a coprocessor. `decode32_static` calls it after every form it reads a stack-pointer change from has returned, and answers `SpUnread` (also for `mrs` to SP); the call graph gives such a function no bound and names the instruction, its callers have none, and the Link_Checker reports a violation for each such instruction wherever it stands, reached or not, with no exemption (`core::fmt` included). The 16-bit instructions need no such test: the only ones that reach register 13 as a destination are `mov` and `add` with a high register (read as a dynamic write), `push`, `pop` and `add`/`sub sp, #imm7`. `msr` to MSP or PSP stays reported as `Msr` (PR-22). No rig or flight binary of 2026-11-27 holds an unread instruction. Tests: the six constant forms and every `i:imm3:imm8` of both encodings of the decrement; the prologue of that `Reset` (frame 1,720, where 8 was reported); 28 instructions that write SP in forms not read, each answered `SpUnread`, and 13 that only use SP as a base or a source, none of them; a function with an unread instruction and its caller unbounded.

*Lean* (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Tools`; `Link.lean` regenerated). `LinkThumbDefs.lean`: `expand12` (the expansion as `thumb_expand_imm` computes it), `imm12`, `Unread` (what `sp_unread` decides, as a statement about two numbers), and `D32` with the constant forms' values, the single pop, `mrs` to SP and the `Unread` case; `AEff.spUnread`, of which `SpAgrees` claims nothing, as of a dynamic write. `LinkThumbUnread.lean` (new): the compiled `thumb_expand_imm` returns for every 12-bit argument and computes `expand12` (`thumb_expand_imm_spec`); the compiled `sp_unread` returns and decides `Unread` (`sp_unread_spec`). `LinkThumbProofs32.lean` (new; `decode32_spec`, moved from `LinkThumbProofs.lean`): the compiled decoder computes `D32`, the constant forms now with their values where the theorem had said nothing. `Sched/Arm.lean` gained the manual's `ThumbExpandImm` (`expandImm`, UNPREDICTABLE for a zero byte in the repeated forms) and four cases of `decode32`: `sub` and `add sp, sp, #const` in their encodings T2/T3 and T3/T4 with the S bit clear, as `subsp` and `addsp` (which leave the flags; the flag-setting forms are not decoded). `LinkThumbSpec.lean`: `rot_shift` (a byte rotated right by 8 to 31 places is the byte shifted left by 32 minus the amount), `expand12_agrees` (the decoder's expansion is the manual's where the manual's has a value), and `d32_agrees` for the four forms, so that `decode32_arm` and `decode32_sp_step` hold of them: the decrement the compiled decoder reads is the one the operation makes. `LinkFrame.lean`: a run has no `spUnread` step, as it has no dynamic one. Not proven: that `Unread` names every 32-bit instruction that can write SP. The semantics has none of those forms, so there is nothing to compare the classes with; the statement is the manual's encoding tables, read by hand, and the tests. Axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound` on the seven theorems printed; no `sorry`. Two mutants registered (`lean-link-decoder-sub-sp-top-immediate-bit-dropped`: the decoder as it was; `lean-link-decoder-unread-immediate-class-not-refused`).

*The boot's frame.* With the frame read, the reset path's use was 3,100 to 3,400 bytes in the demo's rig builds, of which 1,700 one frame. It was the Kernel's state itself: `Kernel<7, 3, 3, 6, 23, 32>` is 1,696 bytes, and `Bound::init` stored `Kernel::new(&self.cfg)`, which builds the whole value in the caller's frame, the boot's, before the store. `Kernel::init(&mut self, cfg)` (`rsk-logic/src/logic/kernel.rs`) writes the fields in place, each from the same constructor in the same order, with `new`'s postcondition, and `Bound::init` calls it: the frame of the reset function is 272 bytes (1,716 before), the reset path 1,928 in the timing build. Aeneas translates `init` to the body it gave `new` (the old value is not read), so the Lean theorems over `Kernel.new` (`new_ok`, `new_eff`, `new_nob`, `new_nod`, `kernel_new_t`, and the two over `Bound`'s `init`) are over `Kernel.init kold cfg` for any `kold`, their proofs unchanged; `Kernel::new` is no longer in the translation, nothing linked calls it.

*What was this commit's.* The shells of 2026-11-26 made the reset path one function (`arch::reset`), which kept the boot's frame allocated across the call of the safe state after a failed boot: 224 bytes more of the bound in the measurement build (4,760 to 4,984) and the 256 bytes more that stack painting read. With the in-place `init` the frame is gone and the order is unchanged. `timer_core` was folded into its one caller by the compiler once the shells were generic: the conformance suite's two link mutations (`conf-PR-31-lk`, `conf-PR-35-lk`) alter it by its symbol and found none, and the Kernel's text outgrew the QEMU layout's Kernel flash by 280 bytes in the four fault-injection builds, which no longer linked. It is `#[inline(never)]` now; the symbol is back and the nine fault-injection variants link.

*Room.* After that, the Link_Checker on every rig build of the demo and the PACEMAKER:

| Build | Kernel (MSP) bound | Available |
|---|---|---|
| timing, short timing | 4,712 | 5,004 |
| stress, short stress | 4,592 | 5,004 |
| cycles, cycles with the FPU | 4,560 | 5,004 |
| measurement | 4,720 | 4,772 |
| response | 4,496 | 4,908 |
| boot checks | 4,728 | 5,004 |
| base | 4,720 | 5,004 |
| isolation | 4,528 | 4,364, then 37,132 |
| PACEMAKER | 2,328 | 2,340, then 35,108 |

The isolation build is over on the term for nested handlers (worst handler 1,928, level records 728, a fault in it 1,512), as it was on commit 0fb2aa14; the PACEMAKER's fits by 12 bytes. On the user's word neither build's no-init records were made smaller: both are declared on the Target with the enlarged Kernel regions (`nrf52840_coverage`: 64 KiB of Kernel RAM, 256 KiB of Kernel flash, the Target of the Kernel coverage build since task 19.5, the same silicon; `apps/p1-demo/decl/src/nrf52840_isolation.rs`, every `apps/pacemaker/decl/src/nrf52840*.rs`). `therapy`'s stack is 16 KiB on the board (bound 15,864) and 24 KiB on QEMU (bound 20,176). `cargo xtask gen` regenerated the eight configurations. The measurement build's margin is 52 bytes.

*The AAI run of the PACEMAKER on QEMU* (`PM-FAIL`; the cause reported to the user, then the check changed on the user's word: it expects AT at that instant in AAI, and names each kind it prints; the four Modes pass). The script's atrial Sense at 1,700,000 comes 400 ms after the atrial Pace at 1,300,000, shorter than the URL interval (500 ms at the nominal 120 ppm), so since the AT Marker of waves 30 and 31 (PM18.5, commit 18860afe) `therapy` records it with kind AT. The check of `apps/pacemaker/system/src/lib.rs` still expects kind SENSE there (`INHIBITED`) and prints every kind it has no name for as `refractory_sense_at`. The Paces and the pending Pace are the expected ones. The run fails the same way on commit 0fb2aa14.

*The App_Prover's bound on the PACEMAKER's `therapy` member.* The job proves each member once with alt-ergo and z3 alone and once with cvc5 and cvc4 alone, each run bounded at 600 s. The run with alt-ergo and z3 reached the bound twice. By hand it proves, in 17 minutes 19 seconds and 4,323 s of processor time: the proofs found took the solvers 396 s over 1,294 goals, of which 390 s and 994 goals are `on_control`'s, reached after 697 splits; the rest went on the attempts that failed before a split. On the user's word the function was parted and the bound left: the Job above the trip level is `served`, with the contract that the level's mark is cleared (`apps/pacemaker/therapy/src/lib.rs`; `docs/pacemaker/findings.md` B49). `on_control` has 363 goals; the member proves in 1 minute 57 seconds with alt-ergo and z3 and in 1 minute 5 seconds with cvc5 and cvc4 (87 files), the board's member in 1 minute 3 seconds and 29 seconds (89 files).

*Run:* Verus `rsk-kernel` 417, `rsk-logic` 351, `rsk-link` 27 verified, 0 errors; the host tests of `rsk-link` (32), `rsk-kernel`, `rsk-logic`, `rsk-gen`; the demo on QEMU (`RSK-OK`) and the PACEMAKER in VVI, AAI, VOO, AOO (`PM-OK`); the Link_Checker on twelve rig builds and four flight builds.

**The body of a function as control reaches it, and the certificate of its flow and stack heights (2026-11-27, last; the survey's row "Link_Checker, the remainder").** Two of the row's items were not properties to prove of the tool as it was: they were wrong or unchecked, and the tool is changed.

*The defect in the walk.* `thumb::decode_function` decoded every halfword of a function's symbol in address order. The table a `tbb` or `tbh [pc, Rm]` branches through lies in the body directly after the instruction, and its bytes were decoded as instructions. In the PACEMAKER's `therapy::event` (QEMU builds) the table `02 b5 02 b5` was read as two `push {r1, lr}`: 16 bytes added to the frame, 2,944 reported where the instructions give 2,928. That direction is safe. The other is not: a table byte of 0xE8 or more in the high half of a halfword starts a 32-bit instruction, which takes the next halfword with it, and at the end of a table that is the first halfword of the code after it, so a stack-lowering instruction there would not be read. No binary in the tree has that case (the old and the new tool give the same frames for every function of 140 built binaries but `event`). The constant data after a function's last instruction was decoded the same way.

*The premise that was not checked.* The frame is the sum of the body's decrements, which bounds the descent when no instruction is executed twice (`Tools/LinkFrame.lean`); nothing looked for a loop. A first check (no stack-lowering instruction under a backward branch) refused the Kernel's own `rsk_unwind`, whose loop lowers SP by 16 and raises it by 16 each time round: the premise is false of a correct stub, and the right condition is about heights, not about repetition.

*What the tool does now.* `flow::body` (`crates/rsk-link/src/flow.rs`, not verified) follows control from the function's first instruction: the next address unless the instruction stops, the target of a branch, the entries of a table (read up to the lowest address reached as code; a table read over an address another path reaches is read again with that address as its end). It marks the instructions under an `it`, and proposes a height for each instruction: the longest path of weights from the entry, where an instruction's weight is its decrement, or minus its increment when it is under no `it`. `flow_v::flow_ok` (`crates/rsk-link/src/flow_v.rs`, Verus) checks the proposal against the body's bytes, and `CallGraph::build` takes the frame, the dynamic mark, the unread instructions and the direct edges from the list it accepted. A function whose body has no certificate has no bound and is a violation (R37.3), wherever it stands.

| What `flow_ok` accepts satisfies (`flow_spec`) | |
|---|---|
| Order and place | The list is in strictly increasing address order and starts at the body's first address; each instruction lies in the body with the length its first halfword gives (`placed`) |
| Closed | From each instruction, every address control can pass to inside the body is an instruction of the list (`lands`): the next address unless the instruction is `hard` and under no `it` (a return, `bx`, a table branch, an unconditional `b`, `udf`, a call of a function on the panic path), or a call that is the body's last instruction; the target of a branch or `cbz`; every entry of a `tbb`/`tbh [pc, Rm]` table, where the bytes from the instruction to the next one of the list are all entries but for the byte that fills a `tbb` table to a halfword (`table_ok`). A table branch through another register, an entry past the body's end and a table that does not end at an instruction are refused |
| `it` | Every instruction of the list that one of the list's `it`s governs is marked (`cond_ok`, `it_addr`: up to four instructions, their lengths read from the bytes) |
| Heights | The first instruction's height is 0; along every passage above the height reached is at least the height left plus the weight (`delta`); every height plus the instruction's own decrement is at most the frame (`node_ok`) |

*What that gives* (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Tools/LinkFlow.lean`, 5 theorems): `step_wt`, one step lowers SP by no more than the weight; `path_height`, along any path of passages SP falls by no more than the difference of the heights, however often the path passes an instruction; `path_within_frame` and `step_within_frame`, from the first instruction SP is never more than the frame below where it started, at an instruction boundary and after the next step. So the premise "no instruction twice" is replaced by a condition the tool checks on every run. That `Cert`'s three conditions are the ones `flow_spec` states is by reading: the check is in Verus and the theorem in Lean.

*Also in the row.* `bound_v::unread_of`: the addresses of the instructions the decoder answered `SpUnread` for, every one of the body and no other (it was a line of `CallGraph::build`'s loop). The semantics has the 16-bit `pop` without the PC (`Arm.decode16`, a last case; `d16_agrees` covers it), so the comparison of the decoder with the semantics no longer leaves it out.

*Taken as given by the certificate* (stated in `flow_v.rs`): that each `Insn` of the list is `thumb::decode`'s answer for the bytes at its address (`flow::body` stores it); that a function on the panic path does not return, and that a call which is the last instruction of a body does not (in the 140 binaries every such call is of the safe state, a fault or panic handler, an exit, or `len_mismatch_fail`); that a call returns with SP as it was; that the bytes between a table branch and the next instruction reached are its table, and that a table does not extend past the next instruction reached; that an instruction under no `it` of the list executes (the processor's IT state is not modelled along a path). What `flow::body` computes is not trusted: a wrong walk or wrong heights fail the check.

*What is left of the row.* The forms the semantics lacks other than the 16-bit `pop`: a `pop` with the PC, the flag-setting `subs`/`adds sp, sp, #const`, the pre- and post-indexed loads and stores, `movw`/`movt` and literal loads as constants, `tbb`, the floating-point classes. Each needs a constructor of `Arm.Instr`, on which the stub proofs (`Sched/StubMask.lean`, `Sched/StubRefine.lean`) do case analysis; not done. That `Unread` names every 32-bit instruction that can write SP: there is no semantics of all of Thumb-2 to compare it with, and no prover here can supply one. The resolution of indirect calls from the read-only tables, the wrapper that reads a literal, and the report's text. The symbol table's reader was already verified (`rsk-elf`, `reader_v.rs`); the row had not said so.

*Existing behaviour changed.* The body of a function is the instructions reached, not every halfword: `event`'s frame in three QEMU builds of the PACEMAKER is 2,928 (2,944), and its callers' usages 16 lower. A function can now be refused for its control flow or heights; none of the 140 binaries has one. `Function` has the field `uncertified`; `thumb::decode_function` is kept for the tests that compare the two walks. No existing test changed. Added: 8 tests of `flow` (a straight body's heights, a balanced loop certified, a lowering loop refused, a conditional increment not credited, an inline table read as data and the old walk's 16 bytes, a table with its padding, a table entry outside the body, a call that does not return) and one of `graph` (a body without its certificate leaves no bound, nor its caller).

*Run.* Verus `rsk-link` 56 verified, 0 errors (27 before); the host tests of `rsk-link` (41, one ignored: the survey); the old tool (built from the commit before, in a worktree) and the new on 140 built binaries with a Task_Model of the same Application: the same report and the same `stack_bounds` for 137, the three QEMU builds of the PACEMAKER differing in `event`'s frame and what follows from it; the conformance suite (46 cases); `lake build` of every library (3,654 jobs, no error, no `sorry`); axioms of `path_height`, `path_within_frame`, `step_within_frame` and `d16_agrees`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. Six mutants of `flow_v.rs` registered and run (472 in the register): a passage landing at any height, a conditional increment credited, no passage after any call, a table entry outside the body, a height over the frame, an `it` block one instruction short; each is killed by the function it is of. Not run: the verification job, Kani, the rig, the campaigns.

**An entry's writes to the Kernel are a sequence of the run's functions (2026-11-27, later; `proofs/aeneas/lean/Sched/Trace.lean`).** The survey's last table had "Lean run vs. the real Kernel": that every execution is a run of the Lean model, and that the Analyzer's input predicate equals the theorem's hypotheses. The second was proven on 2026-11-26 (`Sched/Checked.lean`); of the first, `Sched/Entries.lean` had cut the run into the Kernel's entries and left, read from the code and not proven, that what an entry core does to the Kernel between its two boundaries is transitions of the run.

*Statement.* `LStep cfg k k'`: `k'` is the result on `k` of one function the run's transitions carry (`observe`, the charge of the Job on top, `serve_slot` of a slot armed and due, `deadline_miss`, `intake_release`, `dispatch`, `settle`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `fault`, `fault_of`, `record_event`, `ack_reinit`, `ack_stop_dma`), or of `Kernel::start`, `boot_check_failed` or `boot_info`. `Tr b b'`: the linked Kernel `b'` has `b`'s configuration and its Kernel state is reached from `b`'s by a finite sequence of such steps. `coreret_trace`: when one of the five entry cores, as extracted and with `impl KernelOps for Bound` behind the trait (`IsBoundT`), called on `b` satisfying `BX`, returns `b'`, then `Tr b b'`; `svc_core_trace`, `irq_entry_core_trace`, `start_core_trace`, `fault_core_trace` and `epilogue_core_trace` are the five. `entryof_lsteps` is the same of an entry of the cut run (`EntryOf`). The configuration before and after, which `EntryOf` stated, is derived.

*How.* No core was read again. Each method of `Bound` that returns a Kernel gives `Tr` by its body (15 lemmas: `dispatch` is `Kernel::dispatch` or nothing, then `settle`; `timer_event` is the reading, the charge and the chain of slots served that `timer_event_served` already had; `intake` is the reading, the Task's deadline slot when armed and due, and `intake_release`). The totality proofs of `Sched/CoreTotal.lean` are over an assumption class with one invariant of the Kernel, any predicate every method keeps (`KOpsT`, `okT`); `bound_kopst_tr` gives the class with `BX` and "reached from `b0` by `Tr`" as that invariant, and each core's totality theorem is then the trace theorem. For that the class's `init` field became a class of its own (`KInitT`; only the boot calls `init`, and `init` does not establish "reached from `b0`"); `bound_kopst` has no hypothesis now, `bound_kinit` has the one about `init`. `endpoint_recv_core_kept` of `Sched/Quiet.lean` is renamed `endpoint_recv_core_same`: the name was also `Sched/ArchSvcKeep.lean`'s and the two files could not be imported together.

*What it does not say.* (1) Which functions an entry calls and in what order: the class has one invariant for every method, so the closure admits any sequence, `Kernel::start` and the boot's records in every entry. (2) The model's side of a transition: that the run's clock, its counts and its Kernel sections are in the state the transition asks for; its timing conditions are measurements. (3) The run has no transition for `Kernel::start` or the boot's records (it begins at the state `init` leaves). Assumed as in `Sched/CoreReal.lean`: `HwT`, `TabT`, and for an interrupt entry that the Partition taken as executing is 255 or one of the layout's (`ai_cp` gives it from the entry cores' invariant).

*Run.* `lake build` of every library (3,647 jobs, no error, no `sorry`); axioms of `coreret_trace`, `entryof_lsteps`, `irq_entry_core_trace` and `bound_kopst_tr`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. One mutant registered and run (`lean-code-take-reinit-writes-the-kernel-without-an-acknowledgement`: a write of `take_reinit`'s own): killed, but by `Sched/TotalBound.lean`, a file before this one in the build, so the trace theorem has no mutant that reaches it (462 in the register). No Rust source changed.

**The order of an entry's calls, and the run that follows an execution (2026-11-27, last; `proofs/aeneas/lean/Sched/Script.lean`, `ScriptEpilogue.lean`, `EntryRun.lean`).** The trace theorem above admits any sequence of the run's functions. These files read the order from the cores, state what is left of the row as one assumption for each call, and show that nothing else is needed for a whole execution.

*The order (`Sched/Script.lean`, for any implementor of `KernelOps`).* `Applied k k'`: `settle`, then for each Partition `take_stop_dma` and `take_reinit`, each on the state the one before returned; `apply_core_applied`: this is all `apply_core` does to the Kernel. `Wr l k k'`: the item `l` gave `k'` on `k`, an item being a method of the interface (`start`, `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `intake`, `timer_event`, `fault`, `fault_of`, `record_event`) or `apply_core`. `Sh ls k k'`: the items `ls` in that order, each on the state the one before returned. Each core has a theorem that gives the list of its paths:

| Core | Paths ("applied" is `apply_core`) |
|---|---|
| `start_core` | `start`, applied |
| `timer_core` | `timer_event`, applied |
| `intake_core` | `intake`, applied; `fault`, `intake`, applied (the interrupted Job's stack pointer outside its stack) |
| `unbound_core`, `stack_violation_core`, `fault_core` | `fault`, applied |
| `dispatch_job_core` | `fault`, applied; `dispatch`; `dispatch`, `fault`, applied |
| `dispatch_vector_core` | those, and nothing |
| `irq_entry_core` | those of the four above, and nothing |
| `epilogue_core` | nothing; `fault`, applied; `job_return`, applied; `job_return`, applied, `fault`, applied (the level record of the Task resumed names no exception) |
| `svc_core` | nothing; `svc_lock`; `svc_unlock`, applied; the services' |
| `svc_service_core` | nothing; applied alone; `fault`, `intake`, `record_event`, `fault_of` or `job_return`, applied |

An EasyDMA request or register write and an Endpoint receive are read with their answer: accepted (or an answer other than "refused") and the Kernel as it was, or refused and one `fault` (`dma_request_core_fl`, `dma_write_core_fl`, `endpoint_recv_core_fl`); that is why no service writes the Kernel without `apply_core` after it. The lists are exact for the proof: each path of a core is closed by the lemma of its shape, and an alternative that closes no path is reported by the linter. `coreret_en` (`Sched/EntryRun.lean`): the five entry cores together, 15 paths. The unwind's last path was not expected: the first statement of a path (methods, then `apply_core` or not) could not express it, and `apply_core` became an item of the sequence.

*The Kernel's functions (`Sched/EntryRun.lean`, for the linked Kernel, `IsBoundT`).* `KW cfg l k k'` is the body of `Bound`'s method `l` as a sequence of functions of `rsk-logic` (`wr_kw`): `start` is `Kernel::start` when the Kernel is not started and nothing otherwise; `dispatch` is `Kernel::dispatch` when started and the Task eligible, or nothing, and then `settle`; `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `fault_of`, `record_event` are the Kernel's function or nothing; `intake` is `observe`, `deadline_miss` when the Task's deadline slot is armed and due, and `intake_release`; `timer_event` is `observe`, the charge of the Job on top and the slots served, each armed and due when served (`ServedChain`). `coreret_ksh`: an entry keeps the configuration and takes the Kernel's state along one of the 15 paths over those functions.

*The start.* `start_first`: the state the start entry leaves has the scheduler state `Sched::new` returns when the state it found had (`Kernel::init`'s, `new_eff`), since `Kernel::start`, `settle` and the acknowledgements leave the scheduler state. It is a boot state of the run (`KReach.boot`), so the run begins after the start and has no transition for `Kernel::start`. `Bound::start` calls `Kernel::start` only when the Kernel is not started.

*The run follows an execution.* `Follows I cfg P` has one field for each function an entry calls (16): from a state `x` of the run with `I x` whose Kernel state is the one the function is called on, the run has transitions to a state with `I` whose Kernel state is the function's result. `I` is any predicate on the run's states, the reader's link between the accounts and the system. `exec_follows`: for every boundary of an execution (`Exec`: entry cores of the linked Kernel called one after another from a first boundary), the run has transitions from the first state to a state on that boundary's Kernel state. `exec_kreach`: from the boot state, that state is one the run of `Sched/KRun.lean` reaches. `property18_exec`: Property 18 of it. The served slot a timer entry serves first is given that the Kernel is quieted, which is what the run's Budget-event transitions ask (`Sched/Entries.lean`); each boundary is quieted (`coreret_quieted`).

*What is assumed: `Follows`.* For `observe`, the charge, `settle`, the two acknowledgements, `fault`, `fault_of` and the end of a killed Job's handler the run's transition has no premise but the function's result (`fol_observe` to `fol_unwind`: the field is then only that `I` is kept). For a served slot, `deadline_miss`, `intake_release`, `Kernel::dispatch`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, the end of a Job and `record_event` the transition has premises that are not properties of the Kernel's code: an arrival awaiting its entry, the execution the Job still needs, the bound of the Kernel section, and, for a dispatch, that the Task is the one `Sched::select` returns (the Kernel checks that it is eligible; the interrupt controller chooses it, `Sched/Nvic.lean` over the hardware model), and for a Job's own calls that `Sched::select` returns no Task. `Kernel::start` after the first entry is in `Follows` too; `Bound::start` calls it only on a Kernel that is not started, and the field is given that. Not proven: `hprio`, that the configuration gives each Task the Priority the Analyzer was given (the Analyzer's input is not in Lean).

*How.* Each core is unfolded and its binds peeled into paths (`peel`, `Sched/Entry.lean`), as `Sched/Settle.lean` does; a path is closed by naming its calls. `violation_core` needed its conditionals split by `ite_eq_iff` (the `split` tactic fails on a condition under a bind). No existing file changed but for the pointers in the headers of `Sched/Trace.lean` and `Sched/Entries.lean`.

*Run.* `lake build` of every library (3,650 jobs, no error, no `sorry`); axioms of `coreret_en`, `coreret_ksh`, `start_first`, `exec_follows`, `exec_kreach`, `property18_exec`, `epilogue_core_sh`, `svc_core_sh` and `irq_entry_core_sh`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. Two mutants registered and run (464 in the register): `lean-code-timer-entry-applies-on-the-state-before-the-timer-event` is killed by `timer_core_sh`; `lean-code-apply-core-acknowledges-the-reinit-on-the-state-before-the-stop` is killed by `Sched/Settle.lean`, a file before these in the build. No Rust source changed. Not run: the verification job, Kani, the rig.

**The dispatch choice joined to the interrupt controller (2026-11-27, after the above; `proofs/aeneas/lean/Sched/SelectLow.lean`, `DispatchTask.lean`, `NvicRun.lean`).** The run's `dispatch` transition asks that the Task is the one `Sched::select` returns. The code does not call `select` to choose: a dispatch vector's entry dispatches its own Task when `eligible` admits it, and which vector is taken is the NVIC's arbitration. `Follows` assumed the run follows whichever Task is dispatched. That is replaced as follows.

| Step | Theorem | Says |
|---|---|---|
| `select`'s tie order | `select_loop_low`, `select_some_low`, `select_srp` (`Sched/SelectLow.lean`) | Every pending Task with a lower identifier than the one `select` returns has a strictly lower Priority (the loop replaces its candidate only on `>`), so the Task returned satisfies `Nv.Srp` with its third clause (R7.3), which was a premise in Lean |
| The converse | `srp_unique`, `select_returns`, `select_of_srp` | At most one Task satisfies `Nv.Srp`; `select` returns on a state whose stack holds Task identifiers; in a state of the run the Task that satisfies `Nv.Srp` is the one `select` returns |
| The Task of a dispatch | `dispatch_job_core_task`, `dispatch_vector_core_task`, `irq_entry_core_task` (`Sched/DispatchTask.lean`, any implementor of `KernelOps`) | The interrupt entry given the status word `ipsr` takes a path with no dispatch, or the tables bind `vecOf ipsr` (the low nine bits) to the dispatch of a Task `t`, bit 3 of the return word is set (thread mode), and the entry is `dispatch` of `t` on the Kernel state it was called on, alone or followed by a fault and `apply_core` |
| The check | `bound_dispatch_elig` (`Sched/NvicRun.lean`) | The linked `dispatch` calls `Kernel::dispatch` only when started and for a Task R7.1 admits, then `settle` |
| The join | `Nv.taken_is_select` | In a state with the run's structure, with `Nv.LevelsOk` and `Nv.VectorsOk`: if the vector of the Task `select` returns is pending whenever it returns one, the NVIC selects the vector of `u` against `Nv.dispatchExec`, and R7.1 admits `u`, then `select` returns `u`. No `Nv.NoStale` premise |
| The execution | `FollowsSel`, `Follows.sel`, `Taken`, `ExecN`, `ExecN.exec`, `execn_kreach`, `property18_nvic` | `FollowsSel` is `Follows` with the dispatch field asked only of `select`'s Task (the weaker assumption). `ExecN` is an execution whose interrupt entries that come from thread mode with a number bound to a dispatch carry `Taken` on the scheduler state the entry finds. With `FollowsSel`, every boundary of such an execution from the boot state is the Kernel state of a state the run reaches, and Property 18 holds there |

*What is assumed.* `Taken cfg s vector group n`: there is a set of pending vectors that holds the vector of the Task `select` returns on `s` (if it returns one) and among which the NVIC selects `n` against the execution priority of thread mode with PRIMASK clear and BASEPRI the value for `s` (`Nv.dispatchExec`). So: that the exception number the entry reads is the one the NVIC selected; that BASEPRI held the Kernel's value (the cores' BASEPRI writes are `Sched/ArchHw.lean`'s, not joined here); that the Kernel had pended the vector of `select`'s Task (the pend is `apply_core`'s plan from `settle`'s answer, Verus `selection_pended`; that the bit stays set until taken is the hardware's). Also `hbind` (a number the tables bind to the dispatch of `t` is `vector t`) and `Nv.VectorsOk` (the Generator's tables), `Nv.LevelsOk` (`Config::validate`), and the rest of `Follows`. Not done: that `Sched::select` returns no Task while a Job executes, the premise of the run's lock, unlock and end-of-Job transitions.

*Existing files changed.* `Sched/EntryRun.lean`: `Follows` is now `FollowsRest` (every field but the dispatch) extended by the dispatch field, with the same fields and the same meaning; `kw_fol` is split into `kw_fol_rest` (any item but `dispatch`, from `FollowsRest`) and the dispatch case; `ksh_fol_rest` added; `served_fol`, `kacks_fol` and `kapplied_fol` take `FollowsRest`. No statement weakened. Headers of `Sched/Nvic.lean` and `Sched/EntryRun.lean` (pointers).

*Run.* `lake build` of every library (3,653 jobs, no error, no `sorry`); axioms of `Nv.taken_is_select`, `select_srp`, `select_of_srp`, `irq_entry_core_task`, `bound_dispatch_elig`, `execn_kreach`, `property18_nvic` and `property18_exec`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. Two mutants registered and run (466 in the register): `lean-nvic-taken-without-the-selected-vector-pending` is killed by `execn_kreach`; `lean-code-select-keeps-the-later-task-among-equals` (`>=` for `>` in `select`'s loop) is killed by `Sched/Select.lean`, a file before these in the build, whose proof step fails although its statement (the highest Priority) is still true of the mutant: the tie order has no mutant that reaches `select_loop_low`. No Rust source changed. Not run: the verification job, Kani, the rig.

The statement carries a set of exempt Tasks, empty before and after every Kernel function, for the places where it is false for a moment: `Sched::finish_top` has ended the Job and the slot is disarmed a few statements later (`job_return`, `end_top_release`; when a Release of the Task is pending the exemption is discharged by that Release, `dx_pending`, and this is the branch the finding was in); `kill_partition_jobs` has killed the Partition's Jobs and `clear_partition_releases` then drops each Task's Release and disarms or overwrites its deadline slot (`kill_loop_dg` with the Partition's Tasks exempt, `clear_loop_dg` with those from the loop's index on). A deadline slot is armed only after `Sched::make_pending` for the same Task (`periodic_release`, `intake_release`, `deferred_release`: `dx_arm_deadline`); `Sched::defer` is called only with no Release held and `deferred_release` drops only a deferred one (`mono_defer`, `mono_clear_deferred`), so neither takes a pending Release from a Task whose slot is armed. `kstep_di` is the invariant kept by every transition of `KStep` with no condition on what the transition names; `kreachs_di` holds of every state reached from a boot state with no Budget slot and no deadline slot armed (`KReachS`; `new_nod`, `start_nod`).

*Two limits, both in the statement.* The safe state is excluded: `enter_safe_state` drops every Release and disarms no slot, and `Kernel::intake` (not `timer_event`) can still serve an armed, due deadline slot there. The Tasks number at most 256 (`Fact (NT.val ≤ 256)`): `clear_partition_releases` indexes Tasks by `usize` and clears the Release by the `u8` cast of the index.
