The checker now applies the same floor (the unverified shell of `recompute.rs`; rsk-confcheck: 19 verified, 0 errors), with a test on the stimulus Application's model. The demo never showed it, every one of its configurations having Resources.

*A Kani kind in the mutation register (R52.2).* `prover = "kani"` runs the package's harnesses, or the one named, on the mutated tree under the job's flags, and the mutant is killed when a check fails and the output carries the register's text. The two defects of 6.1 that were replayed by hand on 2026-11-06 are registered and were each run alone and killed, by the checks the plan names: `kani-nvic-line-of-a-system-exception` and `kani-dma-program-guarded-by-plausible` (both by `apply_every_action_is_total` and the register double's own assertion in `write`).

*The Kernel's version symbols (R6.7).* Not implemented until this date: the version constants existed and nothing was linked or compared. The Kernel now carries `RSK_KERNEL_VERSION`, `RSK_PROFILE_VERSION` and `RSK_PROFILE_VARIANT` (16-byte strings in the Kernel_Unsafe_Module, a symbol name and a section being link-level declarations; the section `.rsk.version` is kept by the generated linker script, every `link.x` regenerated), and the Config_Checker reports each when absent or different from the Task_Model's `kernel`, `profile.version` and `profile.variant`. The first build, before the section was kept, lost the three to the linker and was reported as R6.7 asks ("absent from the binary"). Three cases of the Config_Checker's mutation suite alter the model; all are reported. rsk-kernel: 282 verified, 0 errors. The Flight_Build's image grows by the 48 bytes.

*The `derived` marker (R23.5).* The Task_Model has a top-level `derived` list naming `kernel_levels` and `priority_map`, required by the schema and the reader; the Config_Checker requires both names; one mutation case. Every committed model is regenerated. The schema identifier stays `rsk-task-model/1`: the field is added, none is changed, and a reader of this revision refuses a model without it.

*Rig tooling (code and host tests; each needs one run on the boards to be confirmed).*
- The release-timing and stress reports print the target binary's hash, and the campaign items take the report's own binary when it names one (a report of another binary beside a fresh timing record is a stale campaign).
- Δ_wake: the Kernel's record gains two words per Task (60 from 58, both sides), the edge-to-timestamp lag over the stimulus edges whose intake found no Job executing; the release-timing record exports it as `delta_wake`, an upper bound of the wake-up (the exception entry and the handler's read are inside it).
- Δ_hm and Δ_stop: the entry kinds existed and no measurement configuration takes a fault entry or stops a Partition. The response suite, which does, now writes `docs/hil/kernel_timing_nrf52840_response.json` after a passed run, and its configuration is one of the job's HIL builds, so that the record is of a binary the manifest lists. With these three, R7.8, R14.8 and R14.10 get an item from the records of the next runs.
- R11.8: the isolation suite's FPU Task sets round-toward-zero as the last floating-point act of every Job (a new probe, `fpscr_set_round_toward_zero`, under the justification of the other register probes) and every later Job compares the FPSCR it finds with the first Job's; the count, or a mark for a difference or for a rounding mode that would not change, is reported and judged as a row of HW-10. The App_Prover member of the changed Task body (`p1-demo-part2-isolation-proof`) was run alone from an empty session: Proved (19 files).
- R11.5, the Partition's half: conformance case `fi-R11-5-fp-stack-bottom`. The FPU Task issues an SVC with its floating-point context in use and 16 bytes of stack. Run on QEMU: pass, 8 stack violations of part2 in 18 events (its response restarts the Partition, whose first Job injects again), no HardFault and no BusFault.

## 14. Structural coverage (R53; task 19.5, 2026-11-06)

**The evaluation (R53.1, ORQ-05).** Four ways of obtaining structural coverage of the Rust code were weighed against what the project can run today, on the host and on the Target, for the three levels of coverage the objectives need (MC/DC for DAL A, decision for DAL B, statement for DAL C):

| Method | What it gives | Host | Target | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rustc's source-level instrumentation (`-C instrument-coverage`, LLVM's coverage mapping; `llvm-cov`) | statement (regions and lines) and, with `-Z coverage-options=branch`, the outcomes of every branch, which is decision coverage of `if`, `match`, `while`, and the short-circuit operators; `-Z coverage-options=condition` counts each condition of a boolean expression separately (its true and false outcomes, not MC/DC's independence pairs) | yes, from the test binaries | yes: the Partition crates in the coverage build (11.13), their counters read over the probe, the raw profile assembled on the host | selected; branch and condition options need the nightly of `toolchain/decision.toml [coverage]`, the pinned 1.95.0 gives regions and lines only |
| rustc's MC/DC instrumentation (`-Z coverage-options=mcdc`, LLVM's MC/DC bitmaps) | MC/DC at source level | - | - | not available: the option was removed from rustc in late 2025 and this nightly rejects it (`block`, `branch`, `condition` are what it offers); restoring it is an upstream 2026 goal (RN-40) |
| GNATcoverage (object- and source-level, documents Rust MC/DC, RN-40) | MC/DC, decision, statement, on the Target by trace or instrumentation | - | - | not evaluated here: a commercial tool outside the project's toolchain; the candidate for DAL A MC/DC when the applicant procures it, with Ferrocene (GAP-01) |
| object-level coverage from an instruction trace (ETM or the trace pins) | statement coverage of the object code, no source decisions | - | the DK's on-board J-Link has no ETM capture; the trace pins give the executing Task, not addresses | not possible on this rig |
| formal-analysis credit (DO-333, R52.4, section 9.4) | the objectives the proofs replace, not coverage figures | the Verus proofs of the logic (every exec path under the contracts) and the Kani harnesses (every path of the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's leaves under the bounds) | - | claimed as section 9.4 states (R53.4, below) |

**The selection.** Source-level instrumentation by rustc, measured on the Target for the Partition code (the coverage build of 11.13: levels A, B, and C are the three Partition crates, under the inputs of the measurement configurations and, as the suites gain coverage builds, the fault-injection ones) and on the host for the Kernel crates' tests; branch outcomes as the decision measure for DAL B, lines and regions as the statement measure for DAL C, and for DAL A the branch and condition outcomes with MC/DC recorded as open (GAP-02) until a tool provides it, the multi-condition decisions of the level A code being few enough to list: `control`'s one guard and `sensor`'s loop guards are `a && b` forms whose condition outcomes the `condition` option counts. The coverage toolchain is the nightly of `[coverage]`, a measurement aid like the coverage build; the Flight_Build's toolchain stays the pinned one (R53.1's record: tool rustc/llvm-cov of that nightly, source level, host for the Kernel and Target for the Partitions).

**The first reports (R53.3).** The job's step "Structural coverage" writes two kinds of Evidence_Item. `coverage-partitions-<variant>`: the committed on-target reports of 11.13 per crate (level A `p1_demo_part0`, level B `p1_demo_part2`, level C `p1_demo_part1`) and per Task entry against the recorded threshold (timing-short: control 100 %, echo 100 % with no branch, sensor 85 %, log 92 %, pulse 100 %, filter 50 %, sampler 50 %; stress-short: 100, 100, 83, 90, 100, 70, 63 %; the plain measurement configuration of 11.14: 100, 100, 83, 92, 100, 50, 50 %; the uncovered outcomes as 11.13 lists them). `coverage-kernel-host`: the Kernel crates' host tests (rsk-logic's property and unit tests, rsk-kernel's) under the same instrumentation, llvm-cov's totals over the Kernel sources: lines 7.0 % (267 of 3,798), branch outcomes 68.9 % (62 of 90 the test binaries carry), functions 9.2 % (30 of 327), regions 11.0 % (2026-11-06; `hw.rs` and `sim.rs`, the host doubles the tests drive, at 31 to 39 % of lines; the logic crate at 0 %, having no host test). The host figure is low and says so: the Kernel's requirements-based tests are the HIL suites on the Target and the Kani harnesses, neither of which the host instrumentation reaches, and its logic is proven rather than tested (section 9); the number measures what the host tests touch. **The Kernel on the Target (`coverage-kernel-target`, the same day).** The instrumented Kernel does not fit the Flight layout: its code is 125 KiB against the Kernel's flash (64 KiB until the same day, 128 KiB since: the HIL isolation build, with the differential-test probes, the trace, and the Δ_stamp probe, outgrew 64 KiB by 840 bytes in the day's last campaign, and the nRF52840 Target's Kernel flash was doubled; every layout, checksum, and record of the day is of the 128 KiB layout) and its 1,105 counters 8.8 KiB of RAM. The Target table therefore carries `nrf52840_coverage`, the same silicon with the Kernel's regions enlarged to 256 KiB of flash and 64 KiB of RAM (powers of two, as the layout's intervals are; the Partitions' regions follow), and the declaration variant `nrf52840_coverage` is the plain measurement configuration on it with every Budget two to three times the demo's (the instrumented Kernel's services, charged to the calling Job, run about three times slower: `control`'s 1 ms Budget overran at 1.24 ms). `cargo xtask hil --coverage --kernel` builds it with the Kernel crates (rsk-kernel, rsk-logic, rsk-entry, the system crate) instrumented beside the Partition crates, the Kernel's counters in `.kernel.cov` of its RAM (zeroed at instant 0 with the Partitions'); the functions Partition code runs inline from the Kernel crate (the SVC stubs, the register leaves, the state cells' accessors, the probes) carry `#[coverage(off)]` under the `coverage` feature, since their counters would be the Kernel's and unreachable under a Partition's view, the attribute's gate coming from the build's command line (`-Z crate-attr`) and never from the source (PR-36); the libcall of 11.13 is off too, or it would count itself. The run is the plain measurement run with its timing bounds reported and not judged (the instrumented Kernel is no timing evidence: J_rel 31,593 cycles there against 6,559, Δ_stamp 327 µs against 48.6). Result (2026-11-06, `docs/hil/coverage_kernel_nrf52840_coverage.json`, the job's `coverage-kernel-target`): **lines 68.6 % (3,017 of 4,400), branch outcomes 55.4 % (716 of 1,292), functions 75.5 % (280 of 371) of the Kernel sources** under the plain demo's inputs; what the plain run does not reach is the fault, restart, Overrun, violation, and boot-failure handling, which the response, stress, isolation, and boot-check suites exercise and which coverage builds of those configurations would add (section 13).

**The fault-injection suites' coverage (2026-11-07).** `cargo xtask hil --coverage --response` and `--coverage --isolation` run the response suite (11.4) and the isolation suite (11.7, 11.8) over the Partition crates instrumented as 11.13 describes, read the counters after the suite, and write `docs/hil/coverage_nrf52840_{response,isolation}.{md,json}`, which the job's step reads as `coverage-partitions-nrf52840_response` and `-nrf52840_isolation` beside the measurement configurations' items; the nightly runs both. The boot-check suite has no coverage build: it boots the Target once per mutated check and the counters are zeroed at each instant 0. Results: both suites pass under instrumentation with every step as on the plain build (the response suite's 29 events and every response as declared; the isolation suite's 75 refused attempts, 5 EasyDMA refusals, 14 Hardware_Model rules). Response: control 92 % (13 of 14 branch outcomes), echo 83 %, sensor 83 %, log 95 %, pulse 91 %, filter 83 %, sampler 70 %; isolation: control 87 % (7 of 8), echo 75 %, sensor 75 %, log 100 %, pulse 100 %, filter 72 %, sampler 66 %; none below the threshold. The outcomes the measurement configurations left at 50 % (`filter`'s and `sampler`'s refusals and restarts) are reached here, where the suites drive them; the branch counts differ between the configurations because each declaration variant compiles its own feature-gated bodies, so the figures are per configuration, as 11.13's are, and are read beside them.

**Source-to-object traceability (R53.2).** Source-level coverage of DAL A code needs an analysis of the object code not directly traceable to source. The Link_Checker's scans of the Flight_Build are its basis (section 1, `analysis-stack-p1-demo`; task 12.3): every compiler-generated check is listed (the implicit panic kinds, a bounds check through 21 Kernel functions and a remainder by zero through 2, no explicit Kernel panic), every `core` and `compiler_builtins` symbol linked is mapped to a Core_Subset item (17, the `core::fmt` ones unreachable), the Generator's output is the generated sources the lint and the coverage see as source, and the naked stubs are the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's listed assembly (13, no cross-section branch). What the analysis does not yet do: match the coverage mapping's regions to the object code of each function (the inlined `rsk` API and the generated wrappers appear under their own source names, 11.13), and decide the object-code verification of GAP-01; both stay with GAP-01 and GAP-02.

**Formal-analysis credit (R53.4).** As section 9.4 records: for the Kernel's logic the Verus proofs replace the structural-coverage objectives' purpose (every path of the exec code is covered by the proof of its contracts, DO-333 FM.6.4.4) and the testing that remains is listed there; for the Kernel_Unsafe_Module the Kani harnesses cover every path of the leaves under the bounds of 6.3; for the Partition code no credit is claimed and the coverage above is the evidence. No objective is claimed replaced for the data and control coupling coverage (Annex A table A-7, objective 8), which stays with GAP-02.

still standing with their reasons (5.15): that the Jobs gate their roles' operations only as `after` declares (an App_Prover obligation on the generated Job wrapper, or a lint on the bodies, would discharge it), that the bodies follow the projections (A-P1, narrowed to the rendering of the verified tables and finding 4), and, for A-P2, only that nothing but the Kernel's `copy_in` writes the Endpoint storage (R16.1; the role API's half is an App_Prover obligation since 2026-11-09, `rsk::channel`); the general theorem of R41.3 is proven for the two-role fragment (`theory::theorem_two_role_progress`, 5.15; every Endpoint of the Profile binds two roles) with its bridge to the flattened automaton that `compose.rs` takes; **the multiparty case with the full merge (three or more roles on one Endpoint, task 21.7) is proven since 2026-11-09 (`multiparty::theorem_multiparty_progress`, over the acceptance conditions `acceptn` of the syntax, 5.15);** the lexer verified too the same night (`lex_v`; `chars_of`, the standard library's decoding of the text into characters, is the one external body of the path); for two roles nothing else (the acceptance `accept_two_role`, the conversion `to_theory`, the flattening `flatten_two`, and the exploration `explore_system` are verified); for three or four roles nothing else either since later that day (`acceptn_exec`, `flatten_n`, and `explore_system` run by `check_protocol`; the plain-Rust `explore::certify` is a cross-check); (`log` has an App_Prover member since 2026-11-09, `proofs/creusot/part1`); its counterexample search (`tests/r41_3_search.rs`) found none in 100,000 generated types and its exhaustive enumeration (`tests/r41_3_enumeration.rs`) none in 1,305,724 small Protocols (5.15; a random run at depth 4 and capacity 8 exhausted the explorer's memory, so both run at capacity 2);
- (closed 2026-11-16: the HIL protocol-violation and rejected-message cases of tasks 21.3 and 21.4 are injected on the board by the response configuration and judged, 11.4; the peer-state step passes with them; Δ_send and Δ_recv measured by the campaign of the same day on the plain measurement configuration: 2,306 cycles observed over 89,104 sends, 2,883 with the margin, and 902 observed, 1,128 with the margin);
- (closed 2026-11-15 by argument, no Kernel change: the Analyzer's count of Endpoint operations per Job is the declaration's `ops` (5.14), checked by nothing at run time, and needs no check for R18.7. A Job's Endpoint calls take no charge point (`svc_core`: `number != SVC_JOB_END ==> final(state).last_switch == old(state).last_switch`, Verus, with the mutant `svc-lock-takes-a-charge-point`; 7.3, HW-13), so the Kernel's time in every such call is consumed from the calling Job's Budget: a Job that performs more operations than it declares reaches its Budget sooner and is answered by its Partition's Overrun_Response (R18.2, R18.3), and what it takes from other Tasks stays within E_j. Blocking does not depend on the count: each operation is one Kernel-level section, and a Task is blocked by at most one (R8.4). The `ops` × max(Δ_send, Δ_recv) charged in O_i on top of the Budget is therefore pessimism for a measured Budget that already contains the calls, kept as it is; a Kernel-side count would only name the misuse earlier than the Overrun does);
- the HIL findings without an explanation: one pulse unanswered in the first hour-long timing run (11.3); one UsageFault of `control` at a release instant in a short timing run of 2026-11-05 (**reproduced 2026-11-06 in the evening campaign's timing-short run, this time with its diagnostics:** CFSR 0x00040000, UFSR.INVPC, at 168,860.008 ms, a nominal instant of `control`; the trace ring shows a GPIOTE intake of `pulse`'s source 39 µs before the time-base entry, the dispatch of `control` on a fresh frame at the stack top 100 µs later, and the fault 12 µs after it, before any instruction of the Job's body traced; INVPC is the processor's exception-return integrity check, which with NONBASETHRDENA set (DD-01) fails on an invalid EXC_RETURN or an exception state inconsistent with the returning frame, so the suspect is the dispatch return of DD-01 in the coincidence of an intake, a timed release, and a dispatch within 150 µs; `docs/hil/findings/timing_short_usagefault_2026-11-07/` holds the report, the 512-entry trace, and the events; **the same campaign's hour-long timing run reproduced it a second time** at 2,232,060.008 ms with the identical trace pattern, an intake 40 µs before the tick, `..._hour_usagefault_2026-11-07/`; twice in one evening under the timing configuration, never in the plain, stress, or coverage runs; the Partition restarted as declared, the runs otherwise clean. **Diagnosed the same evening (5.8a):** the UsageFault handler of HIL builds now records the processor's exception state and the faulting frame, a third reproduction (the sixth 3-minute run of a loop, this time `pulse`'s dispatch 14 µs after `filter`'s) showed RETTOBASE clear in ICSR, `filter`'s dispatch vector active in NVIC_IABR0, and NONBASETHRDENA clear in CCR: a dispatch vector taken in another dispatch stub's return window had returned to Thread mode with that exception active. **Fixed 2026-11-07, in three steps (5.8a):** the first fix (the vector re-pending itself) was a livelock that the widened-window witness build exposed before it ever ran in a Flight stub; the return window is closed with FAULTMASK, which the exception return clears; and the records' refresh then showed the interleaving at the stub's entry instruction, which no mask can precede, so a vector taken there disables itself and is re-enabled, pending, by the preempted stub's own entry. **Closed 2026-11-07 (5.8a):** the witness build without the fence and the deferral reproduces the fault at will (340 fault-record entries in 61 s) and with them handles both widened windows (189 occurrences in 180 s, no fault); the campaign's runs on the fixed Kernel are clean (the records refreshed on it), and the rig's step "dispatch deferrals (5.8a)" reports the deferrals of every timing and stress run, each a handled occurrence), its CFSR unread (11.3; the timing and stress rigs now print the fault record); the two one-offs of the stress configuration (11.6: the completed `log` Job above its Budget recurred on 2026-11-05 at 986,501 cycles, twice with one value to the cycle, on the 33cb41c binary alone (bisected: absent on its parent's and on the next binary's, so a latent condition that the binary's timing alignment hits, 11.6); the trace had frozen and the rig misjudged it (fixed, and the mirror now stops at the freeze); the archived trace of that binary (`docs/hil/findings/`) showed a `log` Job of 23 ms wall time through six preemptions and a part2 restart charged 986,501 cycles with no Overrun, the cause being `budget_event`'s "Overrun recorded once" inference from the account, replaced by `Budget::reported` and proven (11.6); the one-off is closed as a defect found and fixed; part0's counters zeroed, not reproduced); the stress configuration's lower-criticality Tasks are not schedulable under the measured parameters: its documented claim since 2026-11-07 is the protected Partition's schedulability alone (11.14), judged by the job;
- (closed 2026-11-16: stack painting on the rig for the Link_Checker's bounds, 11.15: every region within its bound on the short stress, the short release-timing and the measurement configurations, and the job's Evidence_Item `hil-stack`; the response and isolation configurations are not done);

- the doc comments of tests that establish a criterion without naming it: 55 of the 109 tests that named none were given their criteria on 2026-10-24 (the remaining 54 are tests of helpers with no criterion behind them: tokenizers, decoders, hash vectors, parsers); the test and Kani items' `covers` lists are derived from these names since 2026-10-23;
- property-based tests now exist for Properties 12 to 15 (proptest, 2026-10-26: `rsk-gen`, `rsk-model`, `rsk-analyzer` test suites); the Analyzer's Properties 16 to 18 run under proptest as well since 2026-11-15 (`tests/properties.rs`, module `generated`: each property's case is one function of a list of draws, which proptest generates and shrinks, 256 cases each; the fixed-seed runs stay for the counts that show the cases are not vacuous);
- confirmation of the Gap_Register's Annex A applicability, independence, and DO-333 and DO-330 items against the standards' texts (OPEN-01, OPEN-02);
- a differential test of the TIMER compare rule under the Kernel's own path (11.8): `MIN_COMPARE_DISTANCE` is now the measured 16 ticks, and the driver's post-write check is the safeguard; the probe for the Kernel's own path is built since 2026-11-18 (the paragraph on ASM-19 at the end of this document) and has not run on the boards;
- the subregion-disable and overlap rules of HW-09 and the late-arrival rule of HW-14 have no silicon test (11.8, dispositions recorded); a layout that uses SRD bits would exercise the former;
- L_kernel is measured (the longest masked span end to end plus Δ_entry since 2026-11-07, 11.13; the longest single Kernel entry before), not statically bounded in cycles: the Profile_Lint's loop-bound report gives every Kernel loop's iteration bound (PR-16), but a static cycle bound needs an instruction-timing model of the Cortex-M4 with the flash's wait states, which the toolchain does not have (11.5);
- nRF52840 anomaly 155 (a GPIOTE IN event twice on edges closer than 1.3 µs) is masked by the MIT policy, and the Generator could set the anomaly's register for sources that may meet the condition; an Application owning SPIM3 inherits anomalies 174, 193, 195, and 198;
- two one-offs of the stress configuration (11.6): a completed `log` Job of 13.8 ms under a 6 ms Budget (R18.2) and part0's counters zeroed mid-run as by an unlogged restart; neither reproduced with the diagnostics now armed (a trace freeze on a completion above the Budget with an execution histogram, and a freeze on any event of part0 by Partition);
- every hardware-pending item of section 11.

**Two findings of the campaign of 2026-11-18, fixed the same day; neither fix has been run on the boards or through the job.**

- **The isolation build's indirect call passed PR-18 by coincidence.** `rsk::matrix::perform` makes one indirect call, `hw_dt::fetch` (HW-09): a `blx` to an address outside the Partition's view, which the MPU refuses. The Link_Checker resolves an indirect call that materialises no function address to the function addresses found as words of the data. In every isolation build before 2026-11-18 exactly one such word existed, and it was not a table entry: a word of `.kernel.data` happened to equal the address of `rsk_busfault_entry`. The version symbols of 2026-11-17 moved the Kernel's text, the word no longer equals a function address, and the call was reported (10 violations). The earlier bound was therefore not evidence of anything about that call. Since 2026-11-18 the call is declared: `rsk-link --fetch-probe rsk::matrix::perform` (`rsk_link::check_probed`) takes that function's one indirect call as having no callee. The declaration is passed for a HIL build with the `isolation` feature only, never for a Flight_Build; it is refused when the binary has no such function, more than one, a different number of indirect call sites, or when the function is Kernel code (test `a_fetch_probe_is_one_named_function_with_one_indirect_call`). `perform` is `#[inline(never)]` so that the name stays in the binary. The resolution itself was narrowed the same day: only the read-only table sections (`.rodata`, `.rsk.tables`; `rsk_link::graph::TABLE_SECTIONS`) are read for function addresses, not the Kernel's or anyone's initialised data, which is state (test `a_table_target_is_a_word_of_a_read_only_table_section`), and the report lists every candidate word with its section, address and function, or says that there is none. On the thirteen builds of the campaign (the QEMU Flight_Build, the plain HIL build, nine measurement configurations, the stimulus, the coverage build) there is none and every function is bounded; the isolation build of 2026-11-16, which the coincidence had bounded, now shows its 10 violations without the declaration and none with it. What remains: a word of `.rodata` that equals a function address would still be a candidate, visibly; and read-only data the linker script places inside a Partition's or the Kernel's text section is not read (the Profile_Lint rejects function pointers and `dyn` in that code, PR-18 LN).
- **The timing Application judged the deliberate overload case by where it fell in the log.** The Kernel's log holds 32 entries. A `control` deadline miss after a late `sensor` Job (the case `Events::overload_misses` accepts on the rig) failed the run when it was among the first 32 events (coverage run of 2026-11-18, 123.040 s) and went unseen after them (hour-long run, 738.040 s, RSK-OK). The Application now applies the rig's rule: a deadline miss of Task 0 within 60 ms of an overrun, deadline miss or held Release of Task 2, and Task 0's held Release at that same instant, are counted apart and printed (`overload_misses`, `overload_miss_at_ms`); any other miss or event of `control` still fails the run.

The Config_Checker's own tables did not list the Target `nrf52840_coverage` (two false discrepancies on the coverage build, the first time the checker ran on it); it is listed since 2026-11-18. The measured-analysis FAIL of the measure and stress configurations in that campaign comes from the 25 % margin on measured terms and is accepted.

**ASM-19 on the Kernel's own path: the probe is built (2026-11-18); no board has run it.** HW-12's test of 2026-11-02 measured a compare's distance from a counter captured before the write, on a channel the time base does not use; the Kernel's compares go through `arm_compare` and the driver, whose capture-to-write time is longer and was never measured, so `MIN_COMPARE_DISTANCE` (16 ticks from the Kernel's capture) said nothing certain about the distance the hardware is left with. Both sides now measure the same quantity, the compare value less the counter captured right after the write:

- the driver (`hw::timebase::arm` and `rearm`, nRF52840) already captured the counter after the write to decide whether to pend the time base; under `trace-ram` that capture is recorded (`stats_arm`: the compares left to the hardware, the smallest distance among them, the counts below 4, 8 and 16 ticks, and the compares pended by software), eight words after the Release ring of `RELEASE_STATS`;
- the probe (`dt::late`, words 140 to 151 of `DT_RECORD`) takes one more capture after each trial's write;
- the rig's row (`rsk_hil::dt::kernel_compare_row`, in the isolation suite, where the same run has both records) matches when the probe's outcomes are ordered and every compare the Kernel left to the hardware was at least as far ahead as a probe compare that fired; a compare left closer is a mismatch of HW-12, which fails the suite. Every release-timing, stress and measurement run reports the driver's record as a step (`compare arms (HW-12, ASM-19)`).

Checked without the boards: the rig crate's tests (the record's decoding, the row's four outcomes), the HIL builds of the plain, timing-short and isolation configurations with both checkers, the nRF52840 Flight_Build (which has no such code). ASM-19 stays open until the isolation suite has run with the probe.

**`Sched/Kernel.lean` regenerated (2026-11-18)** after the contract changes of 2026-11-17 in `arch/bound.rs` and `arch/cm4/entry.rs`: the new translation differs from the committed one in source-line comments only (216 lines), and the Lean run was rebuilt over it.

**Three items of 2026-11-18 (later). No board and no job run; each says what was checked.**

*The Link_Checker reads the read-only data inside the code sections.* The linker script places a Partition's `.rodata` in its code section (`.pN.text`) and the shared crates' in `.text`; a function table there was not read, so an indirect call through one had no candidate and, had another section held any, the wrong ones. `rsk_link::graph::embedded_words` reads the words of those sections outside every function body (a function's own literal pool stays that function's materialised addresses), and a caller's candidates are the table sections' words and the embedded words of the sections it can read: its own and the shared code; shared code reads every Partition's (`table_readable`). The Kernel's code section is not read: no indirect call is allowed in Kernel code (PR-18, the scan), and the addresses its tables hold are entry points. Test `a_table_inside_a_partitions_code_section_bounds_its_own_callers`. On the thirteen builds there is no such word and nothing changes.

*`Sched/Settle.lean`: what an entry leaves, read from the entry cores (22 theorems, no `sorry`; `Quieted` and `settle_quieted` are in `Sched/Calls.lean`).* The task was to derive the run's premise "a Budget event's own timer entry finds the state `settle` returned" from the extracted cores. The premise is false of the code as it was stated, in three ways, none of them a defect of the Kernel:

- `apply_core` calls `settle` first and then acknowledges each Partition's stop and re-initialization requests (`take_stop_dma`, `take_reinit`: `Kernel::ack_stop_dma`, `ack_reinit`), which write the Kernel;
- a timer entry reads the time base (`Kernel::observe`) before it serves the slot;
- five paths return without `apply_core` at all: the dispatch entry (`dispatch_job_core`: the Kernel it leaves is `KernelOps::dispatch`'s), and in `svc_service_core` the lock SVC, the DMA request, the DMA register write and the Endpoint send and receive. The commonest state a Budget event's own entry finds, the one the dispatch of that Job left, is one of them.

What the run took from the premise was one fact: with the Budget slot of the Job on top armed, `Sched::select` returns none. The premise of the four Budget-event transitions (`bevent_h`, `overrun_h`, `overrun_p`, `bevent_r`) is now that fact (`Quieted`, `Sched/Calls.lean`), which the old premise implies (`settle_quieted`), so every theorem of the run holds as before and of more runs. Proven on the extracted code:

- `apply_core_inv`, `apply_core_quieted`: the Kernel `apply_core` returns is quieted whatever state it was given, for an implementor whose `settle` and two acknowledgements are `Bound`'s (the acknowledgements keep the scheduler state and the slots; the loop over the Partitions by induction);
- `observe_quieted`: `observe` keeps it;
- per entry core, what Kernel it returns: the one it found or one `apply_core` returned for `start_core`, `timer_core`, `intake_core`, `unbound_core`, `stack_violation_core`, `fault_core`, `epilogue_core`; those or `dispatch`'s result for `dispatch_job_core`, `dispatch_vector_core`, `irq_entry_core` (`DispLeft`); those or the result of one of the five services for `svc_service_core`, `svc_core` (`SvcLeft`).

*(Closed 2026-11-21, `Sched/Quiet.lean`: the section "The third three of the larger items". The paragraph is kept as written.)* **Not proven, and the premise stays a premise for them:** that the Kernel is quieted after `Kernel::dispatch` of the Task `select` returned (it needs: after the dispatch of the highest admitted Task, R7.1 admits none), after `svc_lock` (raising the System_Ceiling admits no Task), and after the two DMA services and the two Endpoint operations (`Sched/Endpoint.lean` already reads what the latter do to the Kernel). The run has no entry boundaries, so it cannot derive the premise itself; these lemmas are what would let a caller discharge it for every entry. The mutant `lean-kernel-budget-entry-in-an-unsettled-state` now drops the new premise.

*HW-09's subregion-disable rule has a silicon test, built and not run.* The isolation configuration's `part1` declares 24 KiB of RAM: a 32 KiB region with its two top subregions disabled (SRD 0xC0, at 0x20018000). The access matrix has an eleventh Kernel-class attempt (`rsk::matrix::target`, 10): a write into the first disabled subregion of the walker's own RAM region (0x2001E010 for `log`), skipped by the walkers whose region has none. The region matches the address and the subregion is disabled, so the hardware must refuse it; an MPU that ignored SRD would grant it. The rig enumerates the attempt (`isolation::attempts`), requires its MemManage fault with the address like every other, and the differential-test row for HW-09 reports it (`srd_refused`) where it said "exercised by the matrix" for any view with SRD bits, which no attempt backed. The generated layout exports `RAM_SRD`. Overlap precedence stays untested (no view has two enabled regions over one address). Checked without the boards: the rig's tests (the attempt's address from the Task_Model, the judge with and without its fault), the isolation and plain HIL builds with both checkers, the regenerated configurations. The App_Prover was rerun on the three isolation members, whose callers of `matrix::target` have one more table in `wf`: each proved (16, 15 and 20 files).

**The register drivers against the Hardware_Model's step: Kani, then Lean on the extracted code (2026-11-18, later). No board has run the refactored Kernel.**

`Cm4Hw::apply` is trusted by the verified entry cores to take the model's step for each `Action` (the one `external_body` between the cores and the registers). Two things were done for the actions whose step changes a state the hardware keeps: the NVIC's enabled and pending lines and priority bytes, BASEPRI and PRIMASK, the nRF52840's TIMER0 compare, and the boot configuration bits.

*Kani (the simulator given the registers' semantics).* The stub KS-01 kept the last value written to each register, so a clear-enable write left the enable register's cell as it was and no harness could say that a line was enabled after one call and not after the next. KS-11 (`sim::track`) keeps beside the cells what the set and clear registers stand for: the enabled and the pending lines by word, and TIMER0's interrupt enable. Four harnesses run `Cm4Hw::apply` from any such state, for any argument, and assert the step and that everything else is as it was: `nvic_actions_step_the_enabled_and_pending_sets`, `mask_and_priority_actions_step_one_field`, `compare_actions_leave_the_compare_armed_or_the_time_base_pended`, `configuration_actions_set_their_bits_and_keep_the_rest`; a fifth, `uj_063_all_bytes_zero_reads_exactly_the_value`, gives UJ-063 a machine check. Each passed alone under the job's flags.

*Lean.* The drivers' bodies moved to `arch/cm4/drivers.rs`, a module with no `unsafe`, as functions of a register file (`Regs`: word read, word write, the priority array's byte write) and of the time base's extension (`Extend`). `unsafe_module::hw` keeps the functions the Kernel calls, each now the driver applied to `Mmio`, whose three methods are the volatile accesses of UJ-001, UJ-002 and UJ-009; the barriers and the system-register instructions stay there. Aeneas reads the seventeen drivers (`proofs/aeneas/lean/Hw/Drivers.lean`, no error), and `Hw/Model.lean` (86 theorems, no `sorry`) gives them a register model, `RegFile`, with the same semantics as KS-11 and proves on the code as extracted:

- `nvic_enable_spec`, `nvic_disable_spec`, `nvic_set_pending_spec`, `nvic_clear_pending_spec`: a number that names a line sets or clears `1 <<< (line % 32)` in word `line / 32` of the set the action names and changes nothing else; any other number changes nothing. `nvic_set_priority_spec`: one priority byte.
- `set_prigroup_all_preempt_spec`, `set_nonbasethrdena_spec`, `set_disdefwbuf_spec`, `set_kernel_exception_priorities_spec`, `fpu_configure_eager_spec`, `mpu_enable_background_spec`, `dwt_enable_spec`: equations, so the driver also never fails: the returned state is the given one with the named register's bits changed as stated.
- `arm_spec`, `rearm_spec`, `cancel_spec`, `ack_spec`: after an arm or a re-arm the compare register holds the instant's low word, the compare interrupt is enabled, and the time base's line is set pending exactly when the one reading taken after the writes has reached the instant; a re-arm and a cancel clear the compare event and the line's pending bit first. The reading is whatever `Extend` returns for the capture register's contents: the theorems hold for any reading.

Three changes of the extracted drivers were each tried and break the proofs (the disable driver writing the set register; `arm` pending when the instant is not reached; the CCR driver setting bit 1). After the refactor: Verus on `rsk-kernel` 282 verified, the QEMU demo `RSK-OK`, the HIL and Flight_Builds of the nRF52840 through the Link_Checker, the static checks.

What this does not give. The model's step (`entry::step`) is a Verus specification and the Lean statements are written against `RegFile`, not against it: that `RegFile`'s fields are `HwModel`'s is read, not proven. `Cm4Hw::apply` itself, the match from `Action` to driver, is not extracted, nor are `Mmio`'s three leaves, BASEPRI, PRIMASK, the barriers, the MPU load, the watchdog and DMA drivers, or the QEMU board's timer. The model keeps what the Kernel writes: no interrupt source, exception entry or compare match sets a pending bit in it, and it has no counter. Whether the silicon's set and clear registers behave as the model says is HW-05's and HW-12's differential tests. The timing records predate the refactor.

**The register drivers in Verus as well (2026-11-18, evening): the first row proven in both provers by statement.** On the request that what one prover shows the other show too. `arch/cm4/drivers.rs` is inside `verus!`: `RegState` and `write_step` are the register file's specification (the same function as Lean's `RegFile.write` and the Kani stub KS-11), `Regs` carries it as the contract of `read`, `write` and `write_u8`, and each of the seventeen drivers has the postcondition Lean proves of it: the line drivers through `set_line` and `clear_line`, the configuration drivers as the state with the named register written (`with_mem`), `arm` and `rearm` as the armed state with the time base's line pending exactly when the returned reading has reached the instant. Verus on `rsk-kernel`: 329 verified, 0 errors (282 before). The registers' addresses moved into the module (`drivers::addr`, re-exported by `unsafe_module::hw::addr`) so that the proofs see their values. `Mmio`, the memory-mapped registers as a `Regs`, and `Clock` are the trusted surface: four `external_body` functions listed in `docs/proof_assumptions.toml`. The translation was regenerated from the annotated source (the ghost code erased) and `Hw/Model.lean` builds on it unchanged but for one `open`.

Where the two provers stand against each other, by size (2026-11-18): Kernel logic and entry code, Verus 141 proof functions and 352 postcondition clauses, Lean 479 theorems, which are mostly Property 18 and its invariants and are not the same statements; the Analyzer's numeric chain and the Protocol checker's progress theorem, both; the drivers, both since today; the Generator's derivation arithmetic and the Config_Checker's core, Verus only; Property 18, Lean only. The order taken: the two small Verus-only crates in Lean, then the Kernel's properties in Lean one invariant at a time (mutual exclusion and the ceiling protocol first, the lock lemmas of `Sched/Locks.lean` being there), and Property 18 in Verus last.

**The Generator's derivation arithmetic and the Config_Checker's core in Lean (2026-11-18, night): every verified tool crate is now proven in both provers.** `proofs/aeneas/lean/Tools`: `Gen.lean` and `Cc.lean` are the Aeneas translations of `crates/rsk-gen/src/derive_v.rs` (5 functions) and `crates/rsk-confcheck/src/derive_v.rs` (10 functions), no error; `GenProofs.lean` (18 theorems) and `CcProofs.lean` (25 theorems), no `sorry`, state of each function what its Verus contract states, as a total specification: the function returns for every input the statement admits and its result satisfies the postcondition.

| Function | Lean theorem | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| `priority_byte`, `encode_level` | `priority_byte_spec`, `encode_level_spec` | `(8 - level) * 32` for a level 1 to 8, 0 otherwise |
| `ceiling` | `ceiling_spec` | an upper bound of the Priorities that one of them attains; 0 for none |
| `ceiling_of` | `ceiling_of_spec` (`maxP_bound`, `maxP_attained`) | the maximum |
| `slot_plan` | `slot_plan_spec`, `plan_ok_of_exact` | the consecutive plan and its count, from which Verus's `plan_ok` follows (distinct slots per Task, all below the count less two, no slot given twice); none when the Tasks need more than 251 |
| `scale` | `scale_spec` (`rounded_div`) | the product over the denominator rounded down or up as asked, and whether rounding changed it; none exactly on overflow |
| `region_log2` | `region_log2_spec` | the smallest exponent from the minimum to 31 whose power of two holds the bytes |
| `sum_checked`, `timer_slots`, `slot_index` | `*_spec` | the sum, `3n + 2`, `3i + k`; none exactly when it does not fit 64 bits |
| `levels_of` | `levels_of_spec` | ascending, and the same members as the input |
| `size_of`, `aligned`, `stack_fits`, `disjoint` | `*_spec` (`intervals_disjoint`) | two to the exponent below 64; aligned exactly when the exponent is 5 to 32 and the base a multiple; the stack non-empty and within the region; disjoint exactly when no address lies in both |

Three mutants, each tried against both provers and failing in both: `scale` rounding up without a remainder, `region_log2` stopping one size late, `disjoint` with a strict comparison.

One change of source for the translation: `levels_of` pushes at the end and inserts elsewhere. Rust's `Vec::insert` admits the vector's length as an index; the Aeneas library's model of it (the pinned checkout) admits only an index below the length, so with `insert` alone the translated function fails on every non-empty input and no total statement of it holds. Verus on `rsk-confcheck` after the change: 19 verified, 0 errors, with no proof text changed; its tests pass.

Where the two provers stand now: the tool crates (Analyzer's numeric chain, Protocol checker's progress theorem, Generator arithmetic, Config_Checker core) and the register drivers are proven in both. The Kernel's properties 1 to 12 and 21 are Verus with parts in Lean; Property 18 is Lean only.

**Properties 3 and 4 in Lean, on the Kernel's code (2026-11-18, night).** `proofs/aeneas/lean/Sched/Mutex.lean` (16 theorems, no `sorry`; the whole `Sched` library built with it, 1,823 jobs, which also rebuilt the two files whose comments had changed). Verus proves both of `rsk_logic` (`Sched::inv`, `lock`, `unlock`); these are the statements over the run of the Kernel's functions as extracted.

| Property | Lean | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| 4, no Resource held by two Jobs | `kreach_excl` (`excl_step`, `reach_excl`) | in every state a run reaches, no Resource is in two entries of the lock stack; each entry has one holder, so none is held twice by one Job or by two |
| 4, every lock entry finds its Resource free | `kreach_lock_entry_finds_free`, `lock_free` | an entry holding a Resource the Job on top may access is that Job's own (from the invariant's Stack Resource Policy clause, under the Generator's rule that a Ceiling is at least each accessor's Priority, R23.1, taken as a hypothesis on the configuration); and a granted `Sched::lock` found the Resource in no entry at all (its search loop, by induction) with its caller a declared accessor |
| 3, the ceiling is the held Resources' maximum | `kreach_ceiling`, `IsCeil.unique` | in every reached state, and that value is determined by the lock stack |
| 3, a lock raises it | `lock_raises` | to the maximum of its value and the Resource's Ceiling |
| 3, the matching unlock restores it | `unlock_restores` | for a lock granted in a state whose ceiling is its lock stack's, the unlock granted in any later state with the lock stack the lock left returns the ceiling to its value before the lock, and names the same Resource |

What was already there and is used: the run's invariant `Wf` (`reach_wf`: the lock stack's holders, their order, the Stack Resource Policy clause, the ceiling), `lock_views`, `unlock_views`, `finish_top_views` and the frame lemmas of the other scheduler functions. What is new: the exclusion invariant with its preservation by all seventeen transitions of the run, the lock's search loop, and the two statements of Property 3 across a lock and its unlock.

A mutant in Verus: with the re-entry check of `Sched::lock` removed, `rsk-logic` fails (345 verified, 1 error; 346 restored). The same mutant was not run against Lean: it changes `Sched/Kernel.lean`, which rebuilds the whole library, and restoring rebuilds it again.

Not in Lean yet of the Kernel's properties: 1 (the start condition: the run takes the dispatched Task from `Sched::select`, and `select_some` states its choice, but the property is not stated as one theorem), 2 (context preservation: the stubs, outside the extraction), 5, 6, 10, 11, 12 and 21.

**Properties 1, 6 and 12 in Lean, on the Kernel's code (2026-11-19).** Two files, no `sorry`; the `Sched` library built with them (1,825 jobs).

`Sched/Start.lean` (13 theorems):

| Property | Lean | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| 1, the start condition | `dispatch_starts_selected` | in a reached state, the Task `Kernel::dispatch` is given, the one `Sched::select` returns, is eligible (released, no Job, a Priority above the System_Ceiling and above every Job in progress, the last from the stack's order in the run's invariant), no eligible Task has a higher Priority, and after the dispatch its Job is in progress on top of the stack |
| 1, nothing else starts a Job | `start_only_eligible` | in the run every Kernel run is (`ksim`), a Job comes to be in progress by the dispatch transition alone; with `none_elig` (no Task eligible when `select` returns none) this is R7.1 to R7.3 |
| 6, a forced end releases | `finish_top_releases`, `job_return_releases`, `end_top_releases` | the end of the Job on top (`Sched::finish_top`; reached from `Kernel::job_return` and `Kernel::end_top`) removes from the lock stack exactly that Job's entries, leaves the others as they were with their holders on the stack, and the System_Ceiling is the greatest Ceiling of what remains |
| 6, a killed Job | `kill_keeps_locks` | `Sched::kill` (a Partition's stop or restart, a Job below the top) changes neither the lock stack nor the System_Ceiling: the killed Job's Resources are released when it is unwound, by `finish_top`. This is the code's behaviour and Verus's statement of it; until the unwind the System_Ceiling still counts them |

Not stated in Lean of Property 1: the order among equal Priorities (DD-03, which is the vectors' order in the Hardware_Model, outside the extraction). Not stated of Property 6: that each released Resource is reported in the log.

`Sched/Isolation.lean` (12 theorems), of `Layout::view` as extracted:

| Property 12 | Lean | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| what a view is | `view_ok` (`view_loop_ok`) | the Partition exists and every region of the view is disabled or is the shared code (not writable), the Partition's code (not writable), its RAM (not executable), or one of its windows (not executable, writable only where the layout does not say read-only); the loop over the windows by induction |
| (a) within the layout | `view_within_layout` | an address the view grants lies in the shared code or one of the Partition's intervals; a writable one in its RAM or a window; an executable one in the shared code or its code |
| (b) no two views | `views_disjoint` | no address is writable in one Partition's view and accessible in another's |
| (b) no Kernel memory | `view_no_kernel_memory` | no view grants an address of the Kernel's flash or RAM |
| (d) no Kernel peripheral | `view_no_kernel_window` | nor of a Kernel peripheral interval |
| read-only windows (DD-08) | `ro_window_not_writable` | an address of a read-only window is writable in no view, its own Partition's included |
| the stack (R16.5) | `stack_overflow_faults` | the word below a Partition's RAM is not writable in its view |

The MPU's region decision is `covers` (enabled, the address in the interval, its subregion not disabled); the theorems use only that a covering region contains the address, so they hold whatever the subregion bits. The layout's disjointness is a hypothesis, `LayoutOk`: the disjointness clauses of Verus's `Layout::wf`, not its size and alignment rules, which these statements do not need. That a booted layout satisfies them is `Layout::validate`, which Verus proves and Aeneas does not translate (its loops), so in Lean it stays a hypothesis. Weaker than Verus's `is_view_of` in one respect: `view_ok` says what each region may be, not which index holds which.

Mutants in Verus on `rsk-logic` (346 verified): a read-only window made writable in the view, and `select` admitting a Task at the System_Ceiling, each 345 verified, 1 error. Not run against Lean: each changes `Sched/Kernel.lean` and costs two builds of the library.


**Property 5 in Lean, on the Kernel's code (2026-11-19).** `Sched/Blocking.lean` (20 theorems; `lake build Sched.Blocking`, 1,805 jobs, no `sorry`), over the run of `Sched/Run.lean`, whose scheduler state changes only through the extracted `make_pending`, `defer`, `start`, `lock`, `unlock`, `finish_top`, `kill` and `clear_release`.

| Verus lemma | Lean theorem | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| `lemma_blocked_by_one_holder` | `waiting_blocked` | a waiting Task the eligibility test refuses, with no Job of its Priority or above executing, has a lock entry whose Ceiling is at least its Priority and whose holder's Priority is lower |
| `lemma_one_low_blocker` | `one_low_blocker` | the entries that block a Task are all held by one Job |
| `lemma_low_locker_nested` | `low_locker_nested` | a Job of lower Priority on top, under a System_Ceiling at least the Task's Priority, itself holds a blocking entry |
| `lemma_no_lower_dispatch_while_waiting` | `dispatch_not_lower` | the Task a dispatch starts has at least the Priority of every waiting Task |
| `lemma_no_new_blocking_section` | `blocking_step` | an entry that blocks a waiting Task after a step blocked it before the step, or is a lock of the Job that already held a blocking entry |
| (the argument of 5.1, in prose) | `single_section` | over any stretch of a run during which the Task waits: whenever an entry blocks it, the outermost entry that blocked it at the beginning is still on the lock stack (the same Resource, the same holder), and the blocking entry is that one or one above it, held by the same Job |
| (the same) | `one_interval` | once no entry blocks the waiting Task, none does for as long as it waits |

Two differences from Verus. The promptness premise of A4 ("never eligible before its dispatch") is not a premise here: it is the run's rule that a Job locks only when the eligibility test accepts no Task (`Step.lock`'s `hne`), which is what `blocking_step` uses. And the five lemmas are joined: Verus leaves the composition to the argument of 5.1, `single_section` and `one_interval` state it. `release_keeps_blocking` adds that a Release changes no blocking entry, so the stretch may begin at the Release.

Not stated in this file: the length in time of the blocking Critical_Section. That is `Blk` in `Sched/Stage.lean`, which Property 18's proof carries. As for every theorem over this run, `Wf` (the Stack Resource Policy's structure of the stack and the lock stack) is proven of every reachable state (`reach_wf`), and the run itself is the model `KRun.lean` ties to the Kernel's entry functions.

Checked by two wrong variants of the file, each rejected by Lean: `blocking_step` without its second case (no lock ever adds a blocking entry), and `single_section` without the minimality of the first entry. No mutant of the Kernel's code was run against it (each costs two builds of the library).

**Properties 10 and 2 in Lean (2026-11-19).** Four files of the `Sched` library (`lake build Sched`, 3,486 jobs, no `sorry`): `Sched/Overrun.lean` (48 theorems), `Sched/Lifo.lean` (6), `Sched/Record.lean` (7), `Sched/StubModel.lean` (30).

*Property 10* (`Sched/Overrun.lean`), on the extracted code:

| Part | Verus | Lean theorems | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| The accounts | `Budget::reset`, `charge`, `report`, `overrun`, `remaining` | `reset_ok`, `charge_ok`, `charge_mono`, `report_ok`, `overrun_ok`, `remaining_ok` | a charge adds to one account and no other, saturating, and never lowers it; an Overrun is an account above the Budget |
| The charge points (R18.1) | `Kernel::dispatch`, `job_return`, `timer_event` | `dispatch_budget`, `dispatch_charges_only_preempted`, `job_return_budget`, `timer_event_budget` | a dispatch charges the preempted Job and empties the new Job's account; a Job end charges the ended Job; a timer entry charges the Job on top; each once, with the cycles it is passed |
| Nothing else charges (R18.1, R18.6) | `only_reset_from`, `reported_kept` | `Kept`, `apply_response_kept` and the response chain, `budget_event_kept`, `serve_slot_kept_budget`, `ServedChain.kept` | across a response, a served slot, and every slot a timer entry serves, each account is as it was with its report kept, or has been emptied |
| Detection (R18.2) | `budget_event`, `arm_budget` | `budget_event_detects`, `arm_budget_at` | the Budget event of the executing Job, found above its Budget and unreported, marks it reported, logs it and applies the Overrun_Response; the event is armed for the instant the remaining Budget runs out, in ticks rounded up, capped at the operating duration |
| Whatever the ceiling (DD-02) | `lemma_kernel_level_never_masked`, `lemma_kernel_level_taken_in_every_state`, `lemma_job_cannot_set_mask` | `encode_level_ok`, `basepri_for_ok`, `basepri_for_task_level`, `kernel_level_never_masked`, `kernel_level_taken`, `job_cannot_set_mask` | the BASEPRI value the extracted `basepri_for` computes is 0 or a Task level's encoding, and under the execution-priority rule of HW-02 a pending Kernel-level exception is taken with any such value |

The execution-priority rule (`execPrio`, `nvicTakes`, `maskWrite`) is written out in Lean as it is in `hw_model.rs`: an assumption about the processor in both tools. "Within Δ_detect" is a measured quantity (19.1) in both.

*Property 2*, in three files:

| Part | File | Main theorems | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| The scheduler state | `Sched/Lifo.lean` | `Step.framed`, `lifo_resume` | over a stretch of a run that ends no Job already on the stack, the Jobs below and their lock entries are untouched; when the stack is back at its depth the same Tasks are on it, the lock stack is the same entry by entry, and the System_Ceiling is the one of the beginning |
| The level record | `Sched/Record.lean` | `dispatch_stores_record`, `top_active_spec`, `epilogue_returns_record`, `unwind_returns_dispatched_context` | on the extracted `dispatch_job_core` and `epilogue_core`, for any Kernel, hardware and tables behind their traits: a dispatch that starts a Job stores the PSP, CONTROL and EXC_RETURN it was entered with in a level's record; every path of the unwind returns the context of the record `top_active` names; so with that record unchanged and no higher level active, the unwind returns what the dispatch stored |
| The registers | `Sched/StubModel.lean` | `irq_called_spec`, `irq_restore_spec`, `unwind_round_spec`, `dispatch_unwind_restores`, `unwind_loop_frames`, `svc_stub`, `timer_stub`, `fault_stub`, `memmanage_stub`, `nested_irq_resumed` | the machine model of `stub_model.rs` written in Lean with the seven stubs line by line: a dispatch stub leaves the level's register record on the main stack; without a dispatch it restores every register it pushed; one round of `rsk_unwind` pops exactly the top record; a dispatch followed by the unwind of its level gives the preempted context R3 to R11, S16 to S31, the stack pointer and the memory above it as it had them, with the record's PSP and CONTROL; the same over any number of levels; the Kernel-level stubs preserve R4 to R11, LR and SP; a handler preempted at its entry instruction resumes as it left |

`cargo xtask check` now also compares the Lean stub model with the Verus model, function by function and line by line (`checks::stub_model::compare_lean`, with a test of a changed, a removed and a missing line); the Verus model is compared with the assembly as before, so the Lean model follows the assembly through it.

Weaker than Verus in one respect, stated as hypotheses in Lean: Verus proves that the main stack and the level records agree at every entry (`world`, `arch_inv`), from which the two conditions of `unwind_returns_dispatched_context` (the record unchanged, no higher level active) and the condition of `dispatch_unwind_restores` (the main stack where the dispatch left it) follow. That invariant is over ghost fields of `ArchState` (`chain`, `chain_levels`), which the extraction erases, so it is not restated in Lean. The MPU view the unwind loads is an `Action` handed to the hardware trait, whose effect is the Hardware_Model's; not stated here.

Checked by two wrong variants of the Lean stub model, each rejected: `pop {r3-r11, lr}` reading LR from the wrong word (the proofs fail), and `rsk_unwind` popping the registers before S16 to S31 (the proofs fail and the static check names the line). No mutant of the Kernel's code was run against these files.

**Properties 11 and 21 in Lean (2026-11-19).** Two files of the `Sched` library (`lake build Sched`, 3,488 jobs, no `sorry`): `Sched/Refine.lean` (12 theorems) and `Sched/Total.lean` (42). Both are in the total form (`f args ⦃ post ⦄`: for the inputs the precondition admits the extracted function returns a value, and the value satisfies the postcondition), where the earlier files of this library state "if the function returns, then ...".

*Property 11* (`Sched/Refine.lean`):

| Structure | Verus | Lean theorems | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Timed-event slots | `Slots::arm`, `disarm`, `next` | `arm_spec`, `disarm_spec`, `next_spec` | the table as a map from slot to armed instant (`sview`): `arm` sets one entry, `disarm` removes one, the others are untouched; `next` returns an armed slot of least instant, the lowest index among equals, and none exactly when no slot is armed |
| Endpoint buffer | `Fifo::is_wf`, `push`, `pop`, `clear` | `is_wf_spec`, `push_spec`, `pop_spec`, `clear_spec` | the occupied slots oldest first (`fview`): `push` appends the next free slot or leaves a full channel as it is, `pop` takes the first or leaves an empty channel as it is, the count stays within the capacity |
| Event log | `Log::push` | `log_push_spec` | entry by entry (`lget`): the event is appended; a full log drops its oldest entry, shifts the rest by one and counts the loss, saturating |

*Property 21*, the part of it Verus proves of `rsk-logic` (`Sched/Total.lean`). In Aeneas's translation an overflow, an index out of bounds and a panic are each a monadic step that fails, so "no runtime error" is "the function returns". Proven to return, under the bounds named:

| Layer | Functions | Precondition |
|---|---|---|
| Budget accounts | `reset`, `charge`, `report`, `overrun`, `remaining` | the Task identifier within the Task count |
| Level encoding | `encode_level`, `basepri_for` | a level of at most 8 |
| Slots, Endpoint buffer, log | as in Property 11 | the slot within the table; `Fifo::wf`; `Log::wf` |
| Scheduler state | `make_pending`, `defer`, `clear_release`, `start`, `lock` and its loop, `unlock`, `recompute_ceiling`, `complete`, `end_job` and its loop, `finish_top`, `kill`, `select` and its loop | `SInv`: the stack within the Task count and holding Task identifiers, the lock stack within its capacity and holding Resource identifiers; a Job in progress where one is ended or locks; at most 256 Tasks for `select` |
| Kernel entries of a Job | `Kernel::push_event`, `arm_budget`, `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock`, `end_mit_window`, `unmask_instant` | `SInv`, `CfgOk` (the configuration's slot numbers within the table), `Lwf` of the log, the instant at most 2^62 (`MAX_INSTANT`) |
| Steps every entry shares | `Kernel::observe`, `select`, `settle`, `record_event`, `kill_at` | the same |

`job_return_total` carries the scheduler bounds across the Job end (from `finish_top_views`) to the re-arming of the resumed Job's Budget event.

Not proven to return in Lean, and so still Verus's alone for Property 21: `Kernel::timer_event` with `serve_slot`, `periodic_release`, `deferred_release`, `deadline_miss` and `budget_event`; `intake`; `fault`; the Health_Monitor responses (`apply_response`, `stop_partition`, `restart_partition`, `enter_safe_state` and their loops); `Kernel::start`; the `Timing` arithmetic these use (which needs `Config::wf`'s bounds on periods and offsets); `Layout::view`; and the entry cores of `rsk-kernel`, which are generic over the Kernel, hardware and tables traits and return only if those do. The Kernel_Unsafe_Module half of Property 21 is Kani's; of it, the register drivers are total in Lean already (`Hw/Model.lean`).

Checked by two wrong variants, each rejected: `arm_budget_total` without the bound on the instant (the addition can overflow), and `next` breaking ties to the highest slot.

Every Kernel property of the design now has a Lean statement on the Kernel's code or, for the assembly, on a model tied to it: 1 to 12, 18 and 21, with the limits recorded above for 2 (the stack and record invariant) and 21 (the timer, intake, fault and response entries).

**The stack and record invariant in Lean, first part (2026-11-19; in progress).** The invariant of Kernel_Arch's entry cores (`entry::arch_inv`), which the Lean proof of Property 2 took as hypotheses, is being proven in Lean. Verus states it over two ghost fields of `ArchState` (`chain`, `chain_levels`) that the extraction erases; in Lean it is restated over the level records alone (`Sched/ArchInv.lean`, `AInv`): the chain is the active levels in rising order, each naming the next one below as the Job it preempted, and the Kernel's stack is a prefix of it. No ghost state is needed.

| File | Theorems | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `Sched/LoopInv.lean` | 3 | `loop_inv`: an invariant rule for extracted loops that needs no termination measure (by the induction principle of Aeneas's `loop`), for statements of the form "if the function returns" |
| `Sched/ArchInv.lean` | 18 | the invariant on plain functions and its structural lemmas: a Kernel entry that only ends Jobs (`AInv.entry`), a dispatch (`ChainOk.push`), an unwind (`ChainOk.pop`) and the four outcomes after it (`Popped.idle`, `resume`, `next`, `killed`) |
| `Sched/ArchCores.lean`, `ArchSvc.lean`, `ArchEntry.lean` | 60 | `KSpec`, the `KernelOps` trait's contract as far as the records depend on it; for `apply_core`, the Endpoint, DMA and signal services and the Kernel-level entry cores: the level records, the Partition taken as executing and the owed unwind are untouched (`Frame`) and the Kernel's stack only loses Jobs from the top (`KR`) |
| `Sched/ArchKeep.lean` | 12 | `end_plan_core` computes the plan of `ended_plan`; `timer_core`, `fault_core`, `stack_violation_core`, `intake_core` and `unbound_core` keep the invariant |
| `Sched/ArchUnwind.lean` | 10 | `epilogue_core_keeps`: the unwind core, over its 126 exits, leaves the invariant with the plan it returns |
| `Sched/ArchDispatch.lean` | 2 | `dispatch_job_core_keeps`: a dispatch that starts a Job leaves the invariant with the record it wrote on top |

`lake build Sched`: 3,496 jobs, no `sorry`. Not yet done: `svc_core` and `svc_service_core` (they need that a granted service leaves the stack as deep as it was), `dispatch_vector_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `start_core` and `boot_core`; that the Kernel as linked (`Bound`) satisfies `KSpec`; and the join with the stub model (the main stack holds one register record per active level). Until those are done the hypotheses of `unwind_returns_dispatched_context` and `dispatch_unwind_restores` stand as recorded above.

**The stack and record invariant in Lean, second part (2026-11-20).** The entry cores left open on 2026-11-19 are done, and the contract they are proven against is discharged for the Kernel as extracted.

| File | Theorems | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `Sched/ArchSvcKeep.lean` | 21 | `KE` (the stack is the same Jobs) and `KSpecD`, the four depth clauses of the trait's contract `KSpec` does not carry (lock, unlock, start, a fault attributed to the Kernel); `apply_core` ends no Job; a granted DMA service and a receive that is not refused leave the Kernel as it was; `svc_service_core_keeps`, `svc_core_keeps` |
| `Sched/ArchVector.lean` | 10 | `dispatch_job_core_all` (every exit, the refused dispatches included), `pend_take` (the owed unwind handed to the stub at its entry instruction), `dispatch_vector_core_keeps` |
| `Sched/ArchIrq.lean` | 11 | `irq_entry_core_keeps` (an unwind planned while the interrupt had preempted a handler is left owed: `defer_set`, `defer_kept`), `start_core_keeps`, `boot_core_establishes` (a boot that passes its checks leaves no record active, no Job on the stack, nothing owed; for a Kernel whose `validate` and `init` satisfy `KBoot`) |

Every method but `dispatch` leaves the stack a prefix (`SP`) or the same (`SS`); `dispatch` pushes an eligible Task, one of higher Priority than the Job on top (`push_ok`) |

Every entry core of `entry.rs` now has its `_keeps` theorem. What stays open for Property 2 in Lean:

- `IsBound` takes three methods as hypotheses with their clauses, because the extraction does not hold them: `top`, `boot_failed`, `boot_info`. `KBoot` (what `validate` and `init` give) is likewise assumed: `validate` is not extracted. Extending the `--start-from` list of `proofs/aeneas/README.md` by those methods regenerates `Sched/Kernel.lean` and was not done.
- The join with the stub model: that the main stack holds one register record per active level across every entry (`world` in Verus). The hypotheses of `unwind_returns_dispatched_context` and `dispatch_unwind_restores` stand until it is done.
- `epilogue_core_keeps` covers the exits without the safe state.

**Property 21 in Lean, the Kernel's entries (2026-11-20).** `Sched/Total.lean` (2026-11-19) proved that `dispatch`, `job_return`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock` and `settle` return. The remaining entries of `rsk-logic` are added, each with the invariant it keeps, so that the statement is closed: from a state satisfying `KT`, under a configuration satisfying `CT`, every entry returns a state satisfying `KT`.

`lake build Sched`: 3,504 jobs, no `sorry`; `cargo xtask check` passes. Not covered: `Kernel::new` and `Timing::new` (that the initial state satisfies `KT`), the entry cores of `rsk-kernel` (they return only if the hardware trait's methods do), `Config::wf` implying `CI` and `CT` (they are stated as the hypotheses the proofs use, not derived from the validated configuration), and the bound `now ≤ 2^62` on the instant Kernel_Arch passes in (Verus: `now <= operating_duration`, clamped by `Bound::timer_event`; `Bound::intake` checks it). No mutant of the Kernel's code was run against the new files, `cargo xtask verify` was not run, and nothing was run on the rig.

**The gaps of 2026-11-20 filled (same day, later).** Both lists of open points above are taken up. What changed outside Lean first, because the rest depends on it:

- **`Config::validate`, `validate_partitions`, `validate_slots` rewritten** (`crates/rsk-logic/src/logic/mod.rs`): every loop runs to a verdict (`good`, `fine`) and the function returns after it; five `let x = a == B || ...;` are written with explicit `true`/`false` branches. The contracts are unchanged (`ensures ok == self.wf()` and the two helpers' `ensures`). Reason: Aeneas translates neither a `return` inside a loop nor a break to an outer loop, and types the plain `||` form as a proposition. `cargo verus verify -p rsk-logic`: 346 verified, 0 errors; `cargo test -p rsk-logic -p rsk-loom` passes. Not rerun: Verus on `rsk-kernel`, the Kani harnesses of `validate`, `cargo xtask verify`; the flight binaries change, so the rig's records are older than the code.
- **The extraction's start set extended** by `top`, `boot_failed`, `boot_info`, `validate`, `safe_state`, `task_count`, `partition_count`, `source_masked`, `started` (`proofs/aeneas/README.md`). The regenerated `Sched/Kernel.lean` differs from the previous one by additions only. `Layout::validate` is in it as an `axiom` (its loops return from inside; not rewritten).
- **A wrong precondition found and corrected**: `CfgOk` (2026-11-19) asked for a release slot below `NS` for every Task. `Config::slot_ok` gives a discarding Sporadic_Task `NO_SLOT` (255), so no configuration with one satisfied it. The clause is now conditional on the Task being periodic or deferring, and its three uses were re-proven with that (`clear_partition_releases`, the deferral path of `intake_release` through `intake_decision_defer_policy`, `Kernel::start`).

| `Sched/TotalBound.lean` | 25 | `BT`; every extracted method of `impl KernelOps for Bound` that changes the Kernel returns from a state satisfying `BT`, for any argument, and keeps `BT` (`bdispatch_total` by a pigeonhole on the stack, `elig_depth_lt`); `top`, `job_info`, `select`, `partition_stopped`, `eligible` return |

`lake build Sched`: 3,509 jobs, no error, no `sorry`; `cargo xtask check` passes.

With `World`, the two hypotheses recorded for Property 2 are discharged in this sense: the record a dispatch pushes stays matched with its level through every later stub, and is the one `rsk_unwind` pops when `epilogue_core` clears that level. What is still not covered, stated exactly:

- **Entry cores' totality** (`rsk-kernel/src/arch/cm4/entry.rs` returns): not done. It needs the hardware and table traits' methods to return and the Endpoint and EasyDMA index arithmetic within the tables' bounds, that is, a port of Verus's panic-freedom of the whole file. Closed instead: the boundary the cores call through (`TotalBound.lean`).
- **`Layout::validate`** and so `LayoutOk`, `view_total`'s bound on the window count, and `KBoot` for a layout: still hypotheses.
- **`Kernel::new` returns**: cannot be stated, its ghost history is an opaque function of the translation; `new_ok` is "if it returns".
- **`basepri_now`, `message_permitted`, `next_compare`, `source_masked`** of `Bound`: no totality theorem (`basepri_now` needs the System_Ceiling within the Priority levels, which `KT` does not carry).
- **In `World`**: the MemManage stub (`mm_to_call`) has no theorem of its own; the loop of `rsk_unwind` is covered round by round, not as one statement over `unwind_loop`; the nested-interrupt theorems of `stub_model.rs` (`theorem_nested_irq`, `theorem_nested_fault`) are not ported; that the stub passes its registers as the core's arguments is a hypothesis of each theorem (`hlr`), as is the exception model (`hwEntry`).
- No mutant of the Kernel's code was run against the new Lean files.

The contracts are unchanged. `cargo verus verify -p rsk-logic`: 346 verified, 0 errors; `cargo verus verify -p rsk-kernel`: 329 verified, 0 errors; `cargo test -p rsk-logic -p rsk-loom` passes.

The four registered mutants of `mpu.rs` were run one at a time (`cargo xtask mutations --only`): all four killed.
- **The extraction's start set extended** by `stack_at`, `is_killed`, `partition_view`, `dma_permitted`, `in_ro_window`, `config_checksum`, `layout_checksum`. `Sched/Kernel.lean` is regenerated: 423 definitions, additions only; `Layout::validate` has a body; the eight `axiom`s are `no_history` and the seven ghost helpers.

`lake build Sched`: 3,518 jobs, no error, no `sorry`; `#print axioms` of the theorems of `CoreReal.lean` lists `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound` and the eight opaque functions of the translation. `cargo xtask check` passes.

What is still assumed or not covered, stated exactly:

- **`init` returns** once `validate` accepted the tables: a hypothesis (`hinit`) of `bound_kopst` and of every theorem of `CoreReal.lean`. `Kernel::new` ends in `no_history`, an opaque function of the translation, so its return cannot be proven; `Layout::view` and the constructors `new` calls have no totality theorem either.
- **`HwT`, `TabT`, `GhostT`**: that the hardware operations (`KernelArch`), the generated tables' accessors (`Tables`) and the seven ghost helpers return. None is Kernel logic; the tables' accessors are generated code and are not proven here.
- **`LOk` is bounds only**: that the intervals are disjoint (`Layout::wf`) is proven in Verus, not here.
- **The Property 2 join's modelling assumptions** are unchanged: that a stub passes its registers as the core's arguments, and the exception entry model (`hwEntry`).
- Not run: `cargo xtask verify`, Kani, Creusot, the rig. No mutant of the Kernel's code was run against the new Lean files.

**Lean level with Verus on the boot and the layout (2026-11-20, after the pass above).** Three steps, the first two in Lean.

1. **`init` returns** (`Sched/InitTotal.lean`, 7 theorems). `Layout::view` returns for a Partition of an accepted layout (one region per live window, at most 5 after the three fixed ones, inside a view's 8), `Bound::init`'s loop over the Partitions returns, and `Kernel::new` returns given that `no_history` does. `binit_returns`: once `validate` accepted the tables, `init` returns. The hypothesis `hinit` of `Sched/CoreReal.lean` is gone; in its place stands `NoHist`, one line: the ghost history's constructor returns. It cannot be proven in this translation (the build without Verus turns the constructor's body into a closure, which Aeneas keeps opaque), and `#print axioms` shows `no_history` for that reason.
2. **`Layout::wf` on the extracted code** (`Sched/LayoutTotal.lean`, 35 theorems; `Sched/LayoutIso.lean`, 7). The loops of `Layout::validate` and its helpers now carry the facts their checks establish, not only bounds: `check_rules_t` (`true` only for an interval within the region rules), `check_disjoint_t` (`true` only for disjoint intervals), and through the loops `LRaw`. `lwf_of_raw` turns `LRaw` into `LWf`, written clause by clause after `Layout::wf`, `PartitionLayout::wf`, `partition_ok` and `pair_ok` of `mpu.rs`; `layout_validate_wf` is Verus's `ensures ok ==> self.wf()`. `validate_layoutok`: a layout `validate` accepts satisfies `LayoutOk`, which `Sched/Isolation.lean` (Property 12, `views_disjoint`, `view_no_kernel_memory`, `view_no_kernel_window`, `ro_window_not_writable`, `stack_overflow_faults`) took as a hypothesis until now. `LWf` is my transcription of the Verus definitions; nothing checks the two texts against each other.

The verification job: see the next entry.

`lake build Sched`: 3,520 jobs, no error, no `sorry`; one file importing every `Sched/*.lean` elaborates (no clash of names).
