**The four mutants' verdicts, and the slot scan (2026-11-20, late).**

- **Mutants.** `lean-dma-buffer-at-stack-end-refused`, `lean-message-buffer-end-excluded`, `lean-dma-request-partition-check-dropped` and `lean-pair-code-overlap-check-dropped` were each run and each killed by the theorem its entry names.
- **Where an entry's cycles go** (lean HIL build with added cycle stamps, a scratch worktree, 180 s stress run, 64 cycles per µs; mean / maximum): the next due slot (`Slots::next` through `next_compare`, 23 slots, every entry) 607 / 705; `timer_event` 954 / 1,710; programming the compare 284 / 343; selection 164 / 241; the loop over Tasks 101 / 191; the loop over Partitions 49 / 1,161; `apply_core` as a whole 1,557 / 2,586. The slot table's scans are the largest share, not the Task tables'; a timer entry walks the table three times.
- **`Slots::next`** keeps the best slot's instant beside its index (`have`, `best_at`) instead of reading it back through the index; the contract is unchanged. `cargo verus verify`: `rsk-logic` 346 verified, `rsk-kernel` 329 verified, 0 errors. `Sched/Kernel.lean` re-translated; `next_spec` (`Sched/Refine.lean`) re-proven on the new loop. `lake build Sched Tab Hw`: 3,559 jobs, no error, no `sorry`. `cargo test -p rsk-logic -p rsk-loom -p rsk-kernel` and `cargo xtask check` pass.
- **Measured** (lean build, 180 s stress run, before → after, cycles): dispatch mean 2,783 → 2,529, maximum 3,019 → 2,749; timer mean 2,902 → 2,658, maximum 4,714 → 4,507; intake mean 2,130 → 1,937, maximum 2,338 → 2,190; unlock mean 1,581 → 1,358; Job end mean 1,719 → 1,494, maximum 1,976 → 1,801. The commit of that morning (`bda98e14`) measures as "before" does. Instrumented build, the longest masked span of a 180 s run: 19,964 cycles (311.9 µs) before, 22,960 cycles (358.8 µs) after; an earlier record has 379.5 µs. The 180 s maximum of the masked span varies from run to run by more than the change saves: it is not a measure of this change.
- **Not usable**: link-time optimization (the image overflows the SHARED region).
- **Not run after the change**: the Lean mutants, Kani, Creusot, `cargo xtask verify`, the hour-long rig runs. The 180 s records in `docs/hil` are this code's; the hour-long ones predate it.

**The masked span's composition, and `timer_event`'s first pass (2026-11-20, late; after commit `8ed93084`).**

- **The composition record.** `ARCH_SPAN` (feature `trace-ram`) now also holds the entries of the span that is open and of the longest span: a count and, for the first 16, a descriptor (the entry's kind; whether it started a Job, restarted or stopped a Partition, enforced an Overrun; whether an unwind follows; the exception pending at its return) and the cycles from the span's start to the Rust half's begin and end (`span_enter`, `span_leave`, `span_close` in `arch/cm4`). `rsk-hil` decodes it (`SpanEntry`) and the stress report prints it under "The Kernel's masked spans". The decoder read the per-kind table as 16 kinds; it is 18 (`ENTRY_KINDS`), corrected.
- **What the longest span is made of** (three 180 s stress runs, 64 cycles per µs). Instrumented build, 20,869 cycles (326.1 µs), read from the board's RAM after the run: Job end 2,281, epilogue 1,517, timer 3,354, timer that enforced an Overrun 4,094, epilogue 685, timer 4,290, dispatch 3,352. Lean build, 24,449 cycles (382.0 µs), 9 entries: `now` 442, timer 2,904, timer that enforced an Overrun 3,658, epilogue 518, timer 2,686, intake 2,410, dispatch 3,003, timer 4,062, dispatch 3,231; Rust halves 22,914, between them 1,535. Instrumented build after the change below, 23,523 cycles (367.5 µs), 8 entries: `now` 520, timer that enforced an Overrun 3,944, epilogue 687, timer 2,988, intake 2,562, dispatch 3,334, timer 4,359, dispatch 3,570; Rust halves 21,964, between them 1,559. In each, the time base's exception (24) is pending again when a timer entry returns: three or four timer entries run back to back, and they are 11,291 to 13,310 cycles (176 to 208 µs) of the span; the epilogues are 518 to 2,202 cycles.
- **`timer_event`'s first pass** visits each Task's `slot_deadline` (NT iterations) and no longer every slot (NS); the test of the slot and the contract are unchanged. `cargo verus verify`: `rsk-logic` 346 verified, `rsk-kernel` 329 verified, 0 errors. `Sched/Kernel.lean` re-translated (the loop now returns the configuration with the Kernel); re-proven: `timer_loop0_served` and the new `timer_loops_served` (`Sched/Timer.lean`), `timer_loop0_kt` (`Sched/TotalTime.lean`), `timer_event_loop0_cl` and the new `timer_loops_cl` (`Sched/CeilBound.lean`); `Sched/Overrun.lean` and `Sched/Time.lean` call the joined lemma. `lake build Sched Tab Hw`: 3,559 jobs, no error, no `sorry`. The mutant `lean-code-timer-serves-a-disarmed-deadline-slot` follows the new text and is killed (`timer_loop0_served`). `cargo test -p rsk-logic -p rsk-loom -p rsk-kernel -p rsk-hil`, `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask gen --check` pass; `cargo xtask reqdoc` rewrote three line citations (0 cited statements changed).
- **Measured** (180 s stress run, before → after, cycles). Lean build: timer mean 2,658 → 2,482, maximum 4,507 → 4,208; the entries that enforce an Overrun, mean 3,336 → 3,190; dispatch mean 2,529 → 2,547 and intake 1,937 → 1,953 (the composition record's stores are in "after"). Instrumented build: timer mean 3,002 → 2,784, maximum 5,145 → 4,958.
- **Not run after these changes**: Kani (its logic harnesses cover `timer_event`), Creusot, `cargo xtask verify`, the other Lean mutants, the hour-long rig runs.

**L_kernel is the hold, not the span (2026-11-21; branch `kernel-masking`).**

- **What the span is not.** `ARCH_SPAN` (11.13, 2026-11-07) measures the longest run of Kernel entries with no return to a Job between them. It was taken for the stretch in which a Kernel-level interrupt cannot be taken, and L_kernel was set from it. The stubs do not hold a mask across their returns: the SVC, timer and fault handlers return with `bx lr` at the Kernel's level, and the interrupt stub clears PRIMASK before its own. At each such return the processor takes the highest pending exception, so a Kernel-level interrupt that pended during one entry is served there, before a tail-chained dispatch. What cannot be interrupted is one handler from its beginning to its return, with the epilogues of the unwind it runs (`rsk_unwind` is entered from the handler). The 160 µs stamp lag of 2026-11-07, which prompted the span, is a timer entry with its unwind: one such handler.
- **The hold record.** `ARCH_HOLD` (feature `trace-ram`): opened with a span, closed at every entry's end that is not followed by that handler's own unwind; maximum, count, the closing kind, the longest hold's entries and its first and last descriptors, the maximum per closing kind. It is a few stores an entry: a first version with the span's full layout lengthened the entries enough that `filter`'s measured execution passed its Budget with the margin (R28.4) and the run was not judged.
- **Why the Analyzer may take it.** `terms.rs` charges every Kernel entry to its event: the Jobs of hep(i) through O_j, and every lower Task's arrivals and timed events through its Δ_irq and Δ_timer terms, the housekeeping event too, each with J_rel. B_i (R29.2) is the section in progress when an event arrives, at most one. The span's later entries are those events' entries: with L_kernel the span they were counted in B_i and again as interference. `rsk-hil` (`kernel_timing.rs`) now takes L_kernel from the hold plus Δ_entry where the build records it, the span otherwise; the rig's steps are "hold (L_kernel)" and "masked span (entries back to back)"; `hil-rig.toml`: `l_kernel_cycles` 35,000 → 11,000.
- **Measured** (180 s stress runs, 64 cycles per µs). Span: 20,980 and 20,936 cycles instrumented (327.8, 327.1 µs), 21,258 lean (332.2 µs). Hold: 6,945 and 6,875 instrumented (108.5, 107.4 µs), 6,312 lean (98.6 µs), each a timer entry that enforced an Overrun and restarted the Partition, with the epilogue of the Job it ended; by closing kind (instrumented): epilogue 6,945, timer 5,518, dispatch 3,668, Job end 2,304. Release lag at most 9,208 cycles (143.9 µs). The record's L_kernel: 8,682 cycles with the margin (it was 28,700 from the span of 22,960).
- **Tried and not kept: the timer entry serving what came due while it ran** (`TIMER_ROUNDS` = 4 readings of the time base in `timer_core`; commit `d8d0da69`, reverted by `4efa31db`; not proven). Same runs: timer entry maximum 4,855 → 7,063 cycles instrumented (4,392 → 6,352 lean), mean 2,786 → 3,257 (the scan for the next slot is paid by every timer entry); hold 6,945 → 8,364 instrumented (6,312 → 7,475 lean); span 381.5 µs twice instrumented, 300.5 µs lean, inside the run-to-run spread of the span without it; the instrumented runs were not judged (R28.4). Slots keep coming due after the rounds, so timer entries still follow one another.

**The hold checked: every Kernel entry and its term (2026-11-22).**

The hold change is sound only if every Kernel entry that can run while a Task waits is charged to something other than L_kernel. The table lists every way the processor enters the Kernel, from the vector table (`crates/rsk-entry/src/lib.rs`), the stubs (`unsafe_module/stubs.rs`) and the cores (`arch/cm4/entry.rs`), with the term of `crates/rsk-analyzer/src/terms.rs` that charges it. "Task i waits" means from the nominal instant of a Release of Task i to the end of its Job. hep(i) is the Tasks of equal or higher Priority.

| Entry | Source | Term that charges it to a waiting Task i | When it can occur while Task i waits |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dispatch of a Job | a dispatch vector, `rsk_irq_entry`, `dispatch_vector_core` (Task level, PRIMASK held) | Δ_dispatch in O_j (`spec_o`), with Δ_entry and Δ_fp_save + Δ_fp_restore | For a Job of hep(i) or of i: inside E_j or O_i. For a lower Task only if in progress at i's arrival (Lean: `low_disp`); then it is the one section in progress (J_rel, B_i's floor) |
| Dispatch vector found stale (the Task no longer eligible) | the same core, `!k.eligible(t)` | **none** | After a response dropped a Release that was pended; returns at once |
| Dispatch vector deferred (5.8a) | the same core, taken with a handler as the preempted context: disabled, recorded, re-enabled and pended by the preempted stub | **none** for the deferring entry; the entry that then dispatches is the row above | When a higher dispatch vector arrives at another dispatch stub's entry instruction; at most once per dispatch |
| Dispatch vector re-pended over an owed unwind | the same core, `pending_unwind != 0`: pops, unwinds, pends itself | **none** for the first entry; the second is a dispatch | When a Kernel entry ended a Job while a dispatch stub sat at its entry instruction |
| Stack violation found at a dispatch or an intake | `stack_violation_core` from `dispatch_vector_core`, `intake_core`, `svc_check_core` | Δ_hm in O_j of the violating Job (one fault a Job); a restart or stop through `b_floor` (R14.10) | As a fault of that Job (below) |
| Time-base entry, a Periodic_Task's release | the timer vector, `timer_core`, `Kernel::timer_event` | Δ_timer: in O_j for hep(i) (`timed_events`); ⌈(w + J_rel)/T_j⌉·Δ_timer for a lower Task (`terms_for`, the arrival term) | Once per period of each Periodic_Task |
| Time-base entry, a deadline event | the same | Δ_timer: in O_j for hep(i); for a lower Task in `lower_events` unless the same analysis shows the Task schedulable | hep(i): once per Job. Lower: only on a deadline miss (the slot is disarmed at the Job's end) |
| Time-base entry, a Budget event | the same, `Kernel::budget_event` | Δ_timer in O_j for the Job's own event and one more for the Budget event of the lower Job that blocks it (`timed_events`); what ending an overrunning Job takes beyond that in `overrun_extra`; Δ_detect + Δ_enforce in E_j | The Job's own, and the blocking Job's, once. No other: the Kernel disarms the Budget slot of a preempted Job |
| Time-base entry, MIT unmask and MIT deferral | the same | Δ_timer: in O_j for hep(i); `lower_events`·Δ_timer per MIT for a lower Sporadic_Task | Once per accepted Release of a Sporadic_Task (twice under `defer`) |
| Time-base entry, housekeeping (the watchdog feed, the counter read) | the same | ⌈(w + J_rel)/T_hk⌉·Δ_timer on every Task (`terms_for`) | Every housekeeping period |
| Time-base entry, end of the operating duration | the same, `SLOT_END` | **none**: the Kernel enters the safe state | Once; nothing is scheduled after it |
| Time-base entry pended again at once | `RearmCompare` with an instant already reached (`apply_core`) | The term of the slot that is due: each such entry serves at least that slot | Whenever a slot came due during an entry: these are the entries back to back of the span |
| Intake of a Release_Source | an intake vector, `intake_core`, `Kernel::intake` (Kernel level) | Δ_irq: with Δ_stamp in O_j for hep(i); ⌈(w + J_rel)/T_j⌉·Δ_irq for a lower Sporadic_Task | At most one accepted or deferred event per MIT: the source is masked until the MIT has passed and pending events are cleared at the unmask (`lemma_intake_count_bound`, R19.1) |
| SVC: lock, unlock | `rsk_svc_entry`, `svc_core` | (Δ_lock_in + Δ_lock_out) per Critical_Section site in O_j; inside the site's WCET when the site blocks i (B_i) | By a Job of hep(i), or by the lower Job that holds the blocking section |
| SVC: Job end, with the unwind | the same; `rsk_unwind`, `epilogue_core` | Δ_complete in O_j (the rig measures it as the Job-end SVC plus the epilogue) | Once per Job of hep(i) |
| SVC: Endpoint send, receive | the same, `endpoint_send_core`, `endpoint_recv_core` | The Task's `endpoint_cost` in O_j; the operation as a site in B_i (`endpoint_site`); the Release a send causes is the receiving Task's Δ_irq | By a Job of hep(i); by a lower Job at most the one in progress |
| SVC: raise of a Release_Signal | the same, `raise_core` | The caller's side: inside the calling Job's execution (below). The Release it causes: the released Task's Δ_irq | By a Job of hep(i) |
| SVC: `now`, EasyDMA request and register write, an Endpoint_Library report | the same | **no term of their own.** An SVC that does not end the Job leaves the charge point (`svc_core`: `last_switch` kept), so its cycles are in the Job's measured execution and its Budget, which is in E_j | By a Job of hep(i): inside Budget_j. By a lower Job: at most the one in progress at i's arrival |
| SVC: panic, an unknown number, a refused EasyDMA or Endpoint request | the same, `k.fault` | Δ_hm in O_j (a Job has at most one fault: its Job is ended or its Partition stopped) | As a fault of that Job |
| SVC taken for a Job already ended | `SVC_STALE`; the other entries drop a pending SVCall of a Job they end (`svc_of_ended_job_core`) | **none**; it returns at once | Only when a Kernel entry ended the Job during the SVC's own exception entry |
| MemManage, BusFault, UsageFault of a Job | `rsk_memmanage_entry`, `rsk_busfault_entry`, `rsk_usagefault_entry`, `fault_core` | Δ_hm in O_j; the Partition's restart or stop work as B_i's floor (`b_floor`: the larger of L_kernel, Δ_restart, Δ_stop) | At most once per Job of hep(i); a lower Job faults only while it executes, so at most in progress |
| A fault inside the Kernel; HardFault, NMI, DebugMonitor, PendSV, SysTick; an unbound interrupt | `fault_core` with `in_kernel`, `hard_fault`, `unbound_core` | **none**: a Kernel fault into the safe state | Ends scheduling |
| The unwind's epilogues | `rsk_unwind`, `epilogue_core`: no exception entry of its own, it runs inside the handler that ended the Job, once per level unwound | Inside that handler's parameter: Δ_complete, Δ_detect + Δ_enforce, Δ_hm + Δ_complete for a fault, Δ_restart, Δ_stop | With that handler: this is why the hold is one handler with its unwind |
| Boot and start | `boot_core`, `start_core` | none: before the first Release | Not while a Task waits |

What the table shows.

- **No entry relied on L_kernel being the span.** Every entry that can follow another back to back is the entry of its own event: a timed event (Δ_timer on its Task or on housekeeping), an intake (Δ_irq), a dispatch (Δ_dispatch). With L_kernel the span they were counted once in B_i and again as interference.
- **Entries with no term.** Five return at once without changing the Kernel's state and have no term: a stale dispatch vector, a deferred dispatch vector, a dispatch re-pended over an owed unwind, a stale SVC, and (not reachable with the generated tables, which bind only the time base to the Kernel) a Kernel-bound vector that is not the time base. Each is shorter than a dispatch and happens at most once per dispatch or per ended Job, in a window of one instruction or one exception entry; none is in O_j. They are covered only by the 25 % margin on the measured parameters. This predates the hold change and the span never covered them either, but it is an entry without a term and is recorded here as open.
- **SVCs without a term of their own** (`now`, EasyDMA, reports, the caller's side of a raise) are charged through the calling Job's Budget, which rests on R28.4 (measured execution at most the Budget) with the entries inside the measurement (2026-11-16).
- **J_rel is not L_kernel.** The wait of an event for its entry is J_rel, measured as the lag from a periodic nominal instant to the Release becoming effective, on its own record; the hold change did not touch it. In the hour-long stress record J_rel is 11,590 cycles and L_kernel 8,805: the lag includes entries taken before the Release's own, the hold does not. J_rel is measured on periodic Releases only and used for every event source.

**The Lean blocking bound (2026-11-22).**

- *Where Property 18 bounds Kernel blocking.* Not through L_kernel: it is not a parameter of the run (`Params`, `Sched/Run.lean`; `runParams`, `Sched/Link.lean`), and the Analyzer's B_i enters the proof only as a bound on the blocking Critical_Sections (`hL`; `maxCandidate_ge`). `bFloor`, which holds L_kernel, is the base of the maximum and no step of the proof uses it. Three things bound the Kernel's effect on a waiting Task:
  1. Each Kernel section is within its own parameter (`hc : c ≤ P.Kd`, `P.Kc`, `P.Kin`, `P.Kout`, `P.Kt`, `P.Kov`, `P.EC q`) and is on the account of its Job or event source, where the potential counts it.
  2. A section that a lower Job began is in the blocking allowance while the window is open (`ksLow` in `Blk`, `Sched/Stage.lean`), and while the level has no released work it leaves the level quiet (`Quiet`: `st.ks = 0 ∨ lowJob cfg i st`).
  3. The wait of an arrival for its entry is the hypothesis of every transition in which time passes:

     ```lean
     def JitterOk (P : Params) (st : St NT NR NL) : Prop :=
       (∀ k a, st.jwait k = true → st.jl k = some a → st.now + 1 ≤ a + P.J k) ∧
       (∀ q a, st.ewait q = true → st.el q = some a → st.now + 1 ≤ a + P.EJ q)
     ```

     with `J` and `EJ` both J_rel. It is assumed and measured, not derived from a bound on one handler.
- *Is it one handler with its unwind?* The run's shape is: a section starts only with none in progress (`hk : st.ks = 0`), and the unwinding of the Jobs an entry ended (`kend`, `kkill`, `kclear`; `unwind` in `KStep`) has no section of its own. That was a property of the definitions, not a statement. It is one now, in `Sched/Hold.lean`:
  - `reach_hold`, `kreach_hold`: in every state a run reaches, the section in progress has at most `holdOf` left, the largest section the parameters allow (`endHold` is the two entries that end a Job and unwind it, `Kc` and `Kov`).
  - `step_in_section`, `in_section`: while a section is in progress time passes only by its own units; an arrival and the unwinding leave the instant and the section as they are, and no other entry begins. From a state with `ks` left the Kernel is free exactly `ks` later, at most `holdOf` later.
  - `runParams_hold`: with the Analyzer's inputs `holdOf` is at most the largest of Δ_dispatch, Δ_complete, Δ_lock_in, Δ_lock_out, Δ_timer, Δ_detect + Δ_enforce, Δ_irq and a Task's Endpoint cost.
- *What this means for the hold change.* Property 18 holds for any value of L_kernel, so lowering it from the span to the hold cannot make the theorem false. What the theorem needs in its place is that J_rel bounds the entry lag and that every section is within its parameter, both measured. The run's own hold is not L_kernel either: its largest section is `Kov` = Δ_detect + Δ_enforce, which the rig measures as two maxima of overlapping entries (12,398 cycles in the hour-long stress record, against L_kernel 8,805).
- *Not done.* `JitterOk` is not derived from the hold. Doing so needs the order in which pending Kernel-level events are served in the run, with the entries taken first charged inside the window; the run leaves that order free.
- Mutant: `lean-hold-without-the-job-ending-entries` (`endHold` taken as 0: the bound without the entries that unwind).

### The time base's wrap extension proven; the late Jobs moved past the first wrap (2026-11-21)

**The extension (R9.5, DD-05).** The six lines that extend the 32-bit counter to the 64-bit time base were hand-written in `unsafe_module::extend` and carried no specification; the proofs above them (P-07, the Lean timer theorems) take the 64-bit instant as given. They are now `ArchState::extend` in `arch/cm4/plan.rs`, inside `verus!`, and `unsafe_module::extend` only calls it on the Kernel_Arch state. Contract: for every true instant `at` with `counter_reads(previous, low, at)` (at most one counter period after the previous reading's instant, inside 64 bits, `low` its low 32 bits) the result is `at` and the state stands for `at`; nothing else of the state changes. By induction over the readings the time base equals the true tick count across every wrap. rsk-kernel: 332 items verified, 0 errors (329 before). Mutants `time-base-wrap-not-counted` and `time-base-wrap-counted-on-an-equal-reading`, both killed.

**Still assumed.** (1) Two consecutive readings are less than one period (268 s) apart: every timer entry reads the counter and the housekeeping slot is due every 125 ms (`lemma_housekeeping_within_a_period` checks the numbers: the period plus any latency up to 268.1 s stays inside one counter period); that a timer entry does run is the rig's evidence. (2) The counter itself counts ticks modulo 2^32 (HW-12). (3) The one-line link from the static state to the verified method, and the trait method `Extend::extend`, which keeps no specification (docs/proof_assumptions.toml). Not rerun for this change: Kani, the Lean build (the method is outside the translation's start set, `Kernel.lean` is unchanged), the full verify job.

**The release-timing test's schedule.** The rig's counter-wrap step reads the wrap count from the last tick of the Kernel trace, and the trace freezes at the first `control` deadline miss of the deliberate overload case. That miss falls at a different late Job every run (none, 3,075 s, 1,107 s, 738 s in the records of 2026-11-15 to 20); on 2026-11-21 it fell at 246 s, before the first wrap, and the step failed with nothing wrong in the Kernel (13 of the 178 late Jobs preceded the wrap). The hour-long configuration now injects its late Jobs from the 14th multiple of 1024 Jobs on (`LATE_FROM`, Partition 0's feature `timing-long`; 286.7 s), 165 late Jobs instead of 178; the 3-minute configuration is unchanged. The check itself is unchanged. `part0-timing` (Creusot) proved again on the changed body, with `timing-long`. Not yet confirmed by an hour-long run. Left open: after the freeze the trace and the event mirror record nothing more, so the Kernel-events step judges only the run up to the first overload miss.

### The UPPAAL corpus: two possible misses examined, the export corrected, the corpus off the job (2026-11-21)

**The two discrepancies (m01, m49; R31.5).** Both were "a miss may be reachable" from verifyta's stopwatch over-approximation against an RTA PASS, open since 2026-11-14.

- *The export was wrong (rsk-uppaal/4).* A Job in `Running` at its deadline was judged missed while it had executed for less than `EXEC[i]`, which is E_i, the bound on what the Task takes from the Jobs it preempts (with the overrun allowance where the Partition stops overrunning Jobs). The RTA's bound is for the Job's own demand C_i + O_i. The export now carries `OWN[i]` and judges by it. On m49 the miss was a real behaviour of the model as exported: part1.t1 behind one Job of part1.t2 (132,200 cycles), its blocking interval (128,000), then 68,200 of "its own" execution, 328,900 with the jitter against a deadline of 320,000; with C + O = 35,600 the same sequence is 296,300 against the Analyzer's R of 297,800.
- *A confirmation model* (`uppaal::confirmation_of`). The same automata without stopwatches: a wall clock per Job and an integer bound `W` (the Job's E and the E of every Job that started while it was started). It admits every behaviour of the export, so no miss in it means none in the export, and verifyta checks it exactly; a second query checks that its invariants never stop time short of a miss. The cross-check runs it only on a possible miss against an RTA PASS. It refuted m01 (about 10 s).
- *m49 stays open.* After the correction both models still report a miss. The confirmation model's trace was read and is an artefact (it judges part0.t0 missed in a run in which the Job had executed 128,000 cycles against a demand of 67,600; the model does not track executed time). A tighter stopwatch-free model did not finish in 170 s. The hand calculation agrees with the analysis. Recorded in `docs/uppaal_dispositions.toml` as neither confirmed nor refuted.

**The corpus after the correction:** 42 agree, 3 pessimism, 4 inconclusive (the verifier's 120 s limit), 1 discrepancy with its disposition (before: 40, 2, 6, 2).

**Off the job.** The verification job's step "UPPAAL cross-check corpus" no longer runs the corpus (13 minutes 41 seconds of the job of 2026-11-21, a licensed external tool, and an expired lease had failed the job of that morning). It reads the committed report, which now carries a digest of the sources it depends on (the Analyzer, the Generator, the Task_Model crate, the corpus generator, the dispositions), and writes `check-uppaal-corpus` from it: inconclusive when that digest is not the tree's, when the verifier was absent or hit its limits; fail on a discrepancy without a disposition or a missing report. `cargo xtask uppaal-corpus` brings the report up to date and refuses to write one when the licence lease has lapsed. What this gives up: a change to the Analyzer no longer fails the job when it changes a cross-check's outcome; it makes the item inconclusive until the command is run. The cross-check is a sanity check beside the proofs of R29 and R32 (it leaves out the ceiling protocol, takes the Analyzer's own terms as parameters, and runs on placeholder timing), not the evidence for the Analyzer.

**The full job** passed on `bdd582b8` earlier the same day (0 violations, 100 minutes), before these changes; not rerun after them.

Read with `docs/requirements_evidence.md`, whose rows carry the same facts per requirement. The executable code of the Kernel is unchanged by all of it: the Flight_Build's loadable image before and after differs in 16 bytes, each the line number of a panic location in a file that gained lines.

It now rejects it citing NG-01 (`inputs.rs`; test `r27_2_a_global_resource_and_an_endpoint_on_another_core_are_rejected_citing_ng_01`).

| Requirement | Statement | What it says | Mutants |
|---|---|---|---|
| R11.1, R11.7 | `epilogue_core`, with a contract for `KernelOps::stack_at` (it had none) | When an unwind stops at a Job, the hardware model's floating-point access is that Job's Task's declared use | `unwind-leaves-the-fpu-access-of-the-ended-job`, `unwind-takes-the-fpu-access-of-the-bottom-job` |
| R12.2 | `boot_core`, `KernelOps::boot_failed` | A boot that fails leaves the Kernel in its safe state, or has not touched the Kernel's state (the tables were refused before initialization) | `failed-boot-check-without-the-safe-state` |
| R10.5, R10.7 | `raise_core`, `raise_accepts` | A Release_Signal raise is taken as an event exactly when a Job executes, the signal is in the table, the executing Job's Partition declares it, and it is bound to a Task. An accepted raise leaves the state `KernelOps::spec_intake_of` describes: the Release_Source event of the signal's bound Task alone, attributed to the declaring Partition, a threshold response for the raising Job (`KernelOps::intake` now ensures that relation; on `Bound` it is the logic's `intake` contract: `overdue_deadline_served`, then `intake_outcome`). The instant is existential (the clock read has no specification). `intake_core` and the Endpoint send do not state the same link | `signal-of-another-partition-accepted`, `raise-releases-the-raising-task`, `raise-threshold-response-for-the-bound-task`, `intake-attributed-to-partition-zero` |

It is a brittle proof and is recorded as such here. The later addition of the same day (the `spec_intake_of` postcondition on `KernelOps::intake`, which every caller of `intake` in `entry.rs` now receives) left it and the count unchanged (333 items, 0 errors).

### The first three of the larger items (2026-11-21)

Read with the rows R39, R40, R42, R17, R14 and R41 of `docs/requirements_evidence.md`.

**1. Whole messages, not first words (R39.4, R40.3, R40.4, R42.2).**

- *The copy.* `hw::copy_words` was a loop in the Kernel_Unsafe_Module outside every proof, covered by a Kani harness for lengths up to 32 bytes (a harness that has no verdict under a 24 GB cap, as this section records above). The loop is now `Cm4Hw::copy_words` in `entry.rs`, the same statements in the same order, proven by Verus for every length: nothing outside the destination range is written, and under `copy_ok` (both ranges word-aligned, a whole number of words of at most `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES`, neither range past the end of the address space, the ranges disjoint) every word of the destination is the source's word before (`copied`). `hw::copy_words` calls it, so the Kani harness and `Action::Copy` run the proven loop.
- *The model.* `HwModel` gains `mem`, the words of the SRAM as one Kernel entry sees them. `KernelArch::copy_in` and `copy_out` say what they do to it, and their implementations on `Cm4Hw` are verified bodies where they were trusted whole. Trusted now: one word read (`ram_read`), one word write (`ram_write`), and the read of a slot's first word with its link to the label record (`read_label`: what the trusted `copy_out` said before). `docs/proof_assumptions.toml` lists the three; ten trusted functions of `Cm4Hw` where there were nine.
- *The statements.* `endpoint_send_core` ensures `send_message_ok`: the channel's messages (`channel_messages`: every word of each occupied slot, oldest first) are those before followed by the words that stood in the sender's buffer, and the label recorded is the first of them. `endpoint_recv_core` ensures `recv_message_ok`: no word outside the receiver's buffer changes, the buffer holds the oldest message's words, and the remaining messages are as they were, in order. Lemmas `lemma_send_messages`, `lemma_recv_messages`, `lemma_slot_in_channel`, `lemma_slot_ranges_apart`.
- *The premise.* Both hold under `message_premise`: the Endpoint storage's base and the Endpoint's offset are word-aligned, the buffer ends inside the address space, and the buffer lies outside the channel's slots. This is R16.1's layout (the storage is a Kernel static of words; a Partition's buffer lies in its own RAM), which no executable check in the cores states and which is not proven here. The alternative, a run-time test in `endpoint_args`, would have changed code the Lean proofs translate (`Sched/CoreTotal.lean`, `Sched/EndpointValue.lean`) and the measured Endpoint operations, and was not taken; it is the way to make the statements unconditional. Reading the old loop for the proof showed what the premise guards: with a misaligned address it copies nothing and reports nothing.
- *What it does not say.* The model is one Kernel entry: the storage keeping its words from a send's entry to a later receive's is R16.1, as for the labels. A message is its words: the typed payload's encoding is `uj_058_059_message_words_round_trip`, bounded.
- *One statement changed.* `recv_labels_ok` said `h1 == h0`, true of a model with no memory; it now says everything of the model but the memory is as it was. Nothing it said of the fields that existed is lost.
- *Two proofs steadied.* `clear_channels` and `lemma_reinit_round`, which use nothing that changed, stopped verifying with the new declarations in the file, wherever they were put. Each has a solver of its own now (`spinoff_prover`), and the lemma three steps spelled out in one branch. Their statements are unchanged.
- *The executable code.* The Flight_Build's loadable image differs from the commit before in `Cm4Hw::copy_out` alone (the slot's first word is read before the copy and not after: one load moved, the function the same size) and in the line numbers of panic locations. `copy_in`, the loop and the entry cores compile to the same instructions. No function of the Lean translation's start set changed.
- Mutants: `message-copy-writes-zero`, `message-copy-skips-every-other-word`, `copy-out-reads-the-label-after-the-copy`, `kernel-send-messages-stale-slot`, `receive-without-the-message-lemma`, all killed.

**2. What a peer sees after a Partition's stop or restart (A-P6; R42.3, R14.9, R41.3).** The Kernel half that sets the flags was a contract already (`endpoint_reinit`). Added:

- *In the Kernel.* `endpoint_send_core` and `endpoint_recv_core` ensure, for the flag the argument names (`spec_role_slot`): the report (`ENDPOINT_PEER`) is returned only when the flag was set, clears that flag alone and changes no channel, no stored word and nothing of the Kernel; any result but the report and a refusal means the flag was clear, so no message is sent or received by a role that has not been told; and the flags are unchanged unless reported. Mutants `send-reports-the-peer-flag-without-clearing-it`, `receive-ignores-the-peer-flag`.
- *In the role API.* `rsk::Endpoint::send_words` and `recv_words` establish `rsk::channel::peer_told` for the peer-state error and `not_told` for every other answer (trusted contracts, as `sent` and `received`); every generated `send_<label>` and `try_recv` ensures the same of its own result, so it returns `PeerChanged`, which holds the role in `S0` by its type, exactly when the Kernel reported. Proven by the App_Prover on the three QEMU members (`part0` 51 files, `part1` 50, `part2` 52); the 23 HIL members were not rerun. Mutant `roles-peer-change-keeps-the-state` (creusot).
- *Still by reading.* The wrapper that turns the Kernel's code 3 into `SendError::PeerChanged` (`send_to`, `recv_from`: trusted); that `apply_core` calls `endpoint_reinit`; and that a re-initialized Partition's own role values start as `S0` (zeroed memory, `#[repr(u8)]` with `S0 = 0`).

**3. The lending library for sequences of any length (R17.5).** See 11.7's entry ("The lending rules"), extended this day: an inductive harness (any state, any transfer value, any one operation) and its base case replace the bound of four operations; four Kani mutants killed.

**Also this day (R10.5's effect).** `KernelOps::intake` ensures `spec_intake_of`, and `raise_core` that an accepted raise leaves that outcome for the signal's bound Task (the table above).

rsk-kernel: 348 items verified, 0 errors (333 before). **Not rerun:** the full verification job, the Kani run of the Kernel crate (`copy_words_is_total_and_in_bounds` was started on the moved loop and stopped at 23 GB without a verdict, as when the heavy harnesses were last run alone), the Lean build, the 23 HIL members of the App_Prover, the rig. The measured records for the Endpoint operations predate the change to `copy_out`.

### The next three of the larger items (2026-11-21)

Read with the rows R38, R26 and R7 of `docs/requirements_evidence.md`. The fourth item of the earlier list ("one Budget event per Job", R29 and R18) is not among the open points of those rows any more and was not taken up.

**1. The operations of a role's states (R38.4).**

- *Before.* `flatten_two` and `flatten_n` prove the role tables and the automaton's transitions (`flattens`, `flattens_n`). From there to the role module two steps of plain Rust read them: `entry_of` (a walk of an entry's branch list) and `states_from_tables` in the Generator, which found the state after an operation by searching the transitions, with state 0 where the search found nothing.
- *Now.* `rsk_protocol::api::role_ops` derives, per state, its kind, its peer, and its operations (label, next state), and is proven to give `role_api`: one entry per state of the table, each of which `offers` exactly what the state's local type allows (the kind and peer are the type's; the labels are the type's branches in order, none added, none left out; each next state is the table entry equal to the head of that branch's continuation). `flatten_two_api` and `flatten_n_api` run the flattening and `role_ops` for every role and carry `flattens` / `flattens_n` together with `role_api`, so the checker's plain code calls one verified function and discharges no precondition by hand. `entry_of`, `RoleEntry` and the search are gone; `rsk_gen::roles::states_from_api` maps indices to names and nothing else.
- *Unchanged outputs.* The generated files of all 14 declarations are byte-identical (`cargo xtask gen --check`).
- *What it does not say.* The names given to labels and roles, the payload types, and the Rust text of the role module are the Generator's plain code (the compile-fail fixtures, the skeleton tests). The recursion-head flag of a state (whether dropping a role value there is reported, R41.2) is computed from the proven operations by plain code.
- Verus: `rsk-protocol` 570 verified. Mutants: `role-ops-next-state-of-another-label`, `role-ops-leaves-out-a-branch`, `role-ops-send-offered-as-receive`, all killed.

**2. The Config_Checker's decoding and comparison (R26.4, R16.7).** New module `crates/rsk-confcheck/src/image_v.rs`.

- *The encodings.* `spec_image`, `spec_layout_image` and `spec_endpoint_image` state the three images field by field, in this crate, from the Kernel's documentation of them (R26.1: nothing imported).
- *The decoders.* `decode_tables`, `decode_layout_image` and `decode_endpoint_image` return for every byte string, and when they accept, the tables are well formed (each table as long as its count says, every window and role table with all its slots) and the input is exactly the encoding of the tables returned: every byte read in order, none skipped, none left over. A flag byte other than 0 or 1 is refused, which the decoders of the `CONFIG` and Endpoint images did not do before (any non-zero byte read as true; two images could decode alike).
- *The comparison.* `tables_agree`, `layouts_agree` and `endpoint_agrees` answer yes only for tables equal in every field of every entry. Composed: `image_is`, `layout_image_is`, `endpoint_image_is` answer yes only when the bytes are exactly the encoding of the expected tables.
- *The verdict.* `recompute` builds the expected tables from the Task_Model (`expected_tables`, `expected_layout`, `expected_endpoints`: plain code) and records a discrepancy unless the proven function says yes; the per-field items stay, for the reader. So a run without a discrepancy means each image in the binary is, byte for byte, the encoding of the tables computed from the Task_Model.
- *What it does not say.* That the encoding stated here is the Kernel's (shown by the checker's runs on linked binaries: this day the QEMU demo, 546 values; the flood configuration rebuilt, 546; a stimulus and a cycles build of earlier dates). The Task_Model reader, the building of the expected tables, the ELF lookup and CRC-32 are plain code. The converse (a correct image is always accepted) is not stated: a refusal is a reported failure, not a missed one.
- Verus: `rsk-confcheck` 93 verified (the recomputation core included). Tests: with any one byte of a synthetic image of each kind changed, the whole-image comparison says no. Mutants: `confcheck-decoded-field-not-the-bytes`, `confcheck-accepts-trailing-bytes`, `confcheck-flag-byte-any-value`, `confcheck-u64-halves-swapped`, `confcheck-comparison-forgets-the-budget`, `confcheck-layout-read-only-not-compared`, `confcheck-endpoint-validation-edges-not-compared`, all killed.

**3. BASEPRI after every entry (A1; R7).**

- *Before.* `basepri_matches` was a postcondition of `apply_core`, the dispatch path, lock and unlock, the timer, the unwind and the fault entries. The other services of the supervisor call, the interrupt intake, an unbound vector, a vector that does not dispatch and the stack check stated the mask only for the safe state; that they leave it right was read from the code.
- *Now.* Every one of them states it. `intake_core`, `unbound_core`, `dispatch_job_core` and a reported violation of `svc_check_core` end with `basepri_matches` whenever the Kernel is not in the safe state (each ends in `apply_core`). `svc_service_core`, `svc_core`, `dispatch_vector_core` and `irq_entry_core` keep it: if it held at the entry and the Kernel is not in the safe state, it holds at the end. The premise is needed only on the paths that write nothing (an accepted EasyDMA request, a receive that is not refused, the clock, a stale request, a deferred or stale vector, a Kernel vector other than the timer), where the Kernel's state is also proven unchanged.
- *What it does not say.* The clauses are per entry. That the mask holds from one entry to the next rests on a Job being unable to write BASEPRI (unprivileged, HW-03) and on the stubs running the unwind entry when a plan names one; this composition is argued, not a theorem over the stub model. `Cm4Hw::apply(SetBasepri(v))` writing `v` stays trusted (UJ-010).
- *The proof.* Postconditions only: no executable statement of the Kernel changed, so the Flight_Build and the Lean translation are as they were. `rsk-kernel` 348 verified, 0 errors; no proof needed steadying this time.
- Mutants: `clock-service-clears-basepri`, `intake-leaves-basepri-of-the-state-before`, `refused-receive-returns-without-applying`, all killed.

Not rerun for this section: the full verification job, the Lean build, Kani, the Creusot members, the rig.

### The third three of the larger items (2026-11-21)

Read with the rows R39, R18 and R7 of `docs/requirements_evidence.md`. Each removes a premise a proven statement carried.

**1. The premise of the whole-message statements (R39.4, R40.3, R40.4; assumption A14).**

- *Before.* `send_message_ok` and `recv_message_ok` held under `message_premise`, a condition on each call: the storage and the Endpoint's offset word-aligned, the caller's buffer ending inside the address space and lying outside the channel's slots. Nothing proved it of any call.
- *Now.* `storage_placed` states one fact of the linked configuration (assumption A14 above), and `lemma_placed_premise` proves the premise from it for every operation `endpoint_args` admits: the Endpoint is one of the table's (a clause `endpoint_args` gained), the channel index is below `roles * roles`, so the channel's slots lie inside the Endpoint's extent and that inside the storage, and the buffer is one `message_permitted` admits, so it is apart from the storage. `endpoint_send_core` and `endpoint_recv_core` ensure `send_premise` / `recv_premise` of every accepted operation under `storage_placed`. So the message statements depend on the configuration and on nothing of the call.
- *Where the fact comes from.* Not from Kernel code. A boot check was the alternative and was not taken: it changes `boot_core`, which the Lean translation covers (`boot_core_establishes`, `boot_core_real`), and needs the layout through a new `KernelOps` method. The Config_Checker establishes it of each binary instead: `image_v::storage_placed_ok` (proven) on the decoded `LAYOUT` image, the decoded Endpoint table, and the address and size of the storage static (`rsk_system::ENDPOINT_STORAGE`, found by the path in its mangled name; the size compared with the image's `ENDPOINT_STORAGE_BYTES`). A binary with Endpoints and without the symbol is a discrepancy. The checker's statement is its own (R26.1): partitions' RAM intervals of the image where the Kernel's speaks of `message_permitted`; that the two agree is by reading.
- *What reading the code for this showed.* The storage is `UnsafeCell<[u8; N]>`: its type gives it an alignment of 1, not 4 as the premise's comment said ("a static of words"). Its word alignment comes from the generated linker script alone (`.kernel.bss`, `ALIGN(4)`, the storage its only input), as the audit of 2026-11-13 recorded when it refuted the finding. The checker now tests the address of each binary, so a change of the script that moved the storage off a word is reported and not silent (a misaligned storage makes the copy do nothing).
- *No executable statement of the Kernel changed.* Postconditions, specifications and one lemma.
- Verus: `rsk-kernel` 352 verified at this point; `rsk-confcheck` 106 verified. Config_Checker on the QEMU demo's Flight_Build: 565 values, every one matches (546 before the placement items). Test `the_endpoint_storage_placement_is_checked_r16_1` (seven ways of breaking the placement, each reported). Mutants: `storage-placed-without-the-word-alignment`, `endpoint-placed-without-its-extent`, `send-premise-of-another-partition`, `confcheck-storage-alignment-not-checked`, `confcheck-storage-ram-may-reach-the-top`, `confcheck-storage-overlap-one-side-only`, `confcheck-endpoint-offset-alignment-not-checked`, all killed.

**2. Every entry leaves the linked Kernel quieted (Property 18's premise `Quieted`; R18, R29).** New file `proofs/aeneas/lean/Sched/Quiet.lean`, on the extracted code.

- *Before.* `Sched/Settle.lean` proved `Quieted` of every Kernel `apply_core` returns and listed the entries that return without it: the dispatch, the lock, the two EasyDMA services, the two Endpoint operations. For those the run's premise stayed a premise.
- *Now.* `bound_dispatch_q`: `Bound`'s `dispatch` ends in `Kernel::settle`, so its result is quieted whatever it was given. `svc_lock_quieted`, `bound_svc_lock_q`: the lock keeps the slots and either refuses and leaves the scheduler state or raises the System_Ceiling and changes nothing else `select` reads, and `select_raised` proves that `Sched::select` returns none on such a state where it returned none before (`select_loop_congr`: the loop reads the release marks only). `dma_request_core_true`, `dma_write_core_true`, `endpoint_recv_core_kept`: an accepted EasyDMA request or write and a receive that is not refused return the Kernel they were given. A send always ends in `apply_core`. So `svc_core_left`: an SVC leaves the Kernel it found, one `apply_core` returned, or the lock's result (`SvcLeft`'s four service cases are gone from the statement).
- *The statements.* For the linked Kernel (`QBound`: the interface's `settle`, two acknowledgements, `dispatch` and `svc_lock` are `Bound`'s, by equations as in `apply_core_quieted`): `svc_core_quieted`, `irq_entry_core_quieted`, `start_core_quieted`, `fault_core_quieted`, `epilogue_core_quieted`: each entry core returns a quieted Kernel when it was given one. With `apply_core_quieted` for the boot's last step, the premise holds of the state every entry leaves, by induction over the entries.
- *What it does not do.* The run (`KStep`) has no entry boundaries, so its four Budget-event transitions still carry `Quieted` as a premise: the theorems are what discharges it for a run cut into the Kernel's entries, and that cut is not formalized. `svc_check_core` and `timer_core` called directly are covered by their `_q` theorems of `Settle.lean` (the Kernel found or one `apply_core` returned), not restated here.
- No `sorry`; `#print axioms` of the three main theorems: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. `lake build Sched.Quiet` passes on the library as built. Mutants (`prover = "lean"`): `lean-select-raised-with-any-ceiling`, `lean-receive-kept-even-when-refused`, `lean-lock-quieted-from-any-state`, all killed.

**3. BASEPRI from one entry to the next (A1; R7).**

- *Before.* Each entry core stated the mask for its own call. That it holds between entries was an argument.
- *Now, in the cores.* `a1_step` is the step every entry makes: unless it ends in the safe state, an entry that finds BASEPRI at the value the Kernel's state calls for leaves it at the value the state it leaves calls for. Each core the shells call ensures it of its own call: `start_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `timer_core`, `svc_check_core` (for the check that passes and the one that reports), `svc_core`, `epilogue_core`, `fault_core`. `theorem_a1_run`: in a run of entries from the start of the system (`a1_run`) the mask is right at the return of every entry that does not end in the safe state, and at the call of every entry after the first; so whatever executes between two entries executes under the mask of the state the entry before it left.
- *Now, in the stubs.* The machine model of the assembly stubs (`stub_model::M`) has BASEPRI. `lemma_stubs_keep_basepri`: every instruction sequence of the model leaves it, and a call of a Rust half leaves what the half's core wrote; `lemma_irq_stub_basepri`, `lemma_svc_stub_basepri`, `lemma_unwind_basepri`: on every way out of the interrupt stub, of a Kernel-level stub and of `rsk_unwind` (after all its rounds) the mask is what the last core left. The static check holds the model to the assembly line by line, so an instruction that wrote BASEPRI would have no model function.
- *What `a1_run` assumes, and it is the trust base of the composition.* Between two entries neither the Kernel's state nor BASEPRI changes: a Job is unprivileged and can write neither (R16.1, HW-03); the shells call their core once; the processor keeps BASEPRI across exception entry and return (the model's `exc_enter_handler` and `exc_return_handler` leave it: assumed, with the rest of the exception model); the cores run one at a time (PRIMASK, and `theorem_nested_irq` for an entry preempted in a stub's window). And no entry follows the safe state. `Cm4Hw::apply(SetBasepri(v))` writing `v` stays trusted (UJ-010).
- *No executable statement of the Kernel changed.* The existing stub theorems verify unchanged with the new field.
- Verus: `rsk-kernel` 359 verified, 0 errors. Mutants: `a1-run-basepri-not-carried-between-entries`, `a1-run-start-does-not-establish-the-mask`, `stub-call-keeps-the-callers-basepri`, all killed.

The register holds 288 proof mutations after this section. The full verification job and the rig runs that followed this section are recorded with the commit after it.

### The fourth round: three evidence rows, then the list of what was unproven (2026-11-22)

Read with the rows R24, R40, R42, R10, R14, R9, R16, R37 and R39 of `docs/requirements_evidence.md`. No row's strength changed: after the work each of R24, R40 and R42 still has criteria that rest on tests of the Generator or the Analyzer (R24.3, R24.4, R24.6; R40.1, R40.2; R42.1, R42.4). The second half of this section says, item by item, what of the unproven parts was proven, and what was looked at and left.

**1. R24: the read-back reaches the last two tables.** `readback::check_vectors` reads the per-Task dispatch and intake vector arrays and the Release_Signal table out of the emitted system source and compares them with the derivation (the dispatch line from the Target description, the intake line from the Task's interrupt source, each signal's Partition and bound Task from the derived Partitions and Tasks, written apart from the emitter). `outputs` refuses a difference (R24.1). Test `r24_1_the_emitted_vectors_and_signals_read_back_and_altered_ones_are_refused` (two dispatch lines exchanged, an intake line added and removed, an entry dropped, a signal given to another Partition, bound to another Task, left out). A signal bound to no Task cannot be exercised: the Generator refuses it (R22.5). The 14 generated Applications of the tree pass the new check (`cargo xtask gen --check`).

**2. R40: one supervisor call is one uninterrupted Kernel entry (R40.6).**

- *What reading the code showed.* A comment on the send service said the operation runs with PRIMASK held. It does not: the supervisor call's stub (`rsk_svc_entry`) does not set PRIMASK, and the handler is protected by its level, the Kernel level, which `boot_core` gives SVCall, the fault handlers, the time base and the intake lines. The comment and the note on `a1_run` are corrected.
- *Now.* `hw_model::lemma_kernel_level_not_preempted`: while a Kernel-level exception is active the interrupt controller's selection rule selects no configurable exception, whatever BASEPRI and PRIMASK are. `entry::theorem_svc_not_preempted` states it on the Kernel's hardware model. `svc_core` and `svc_service_core` ensure `mask_untouched`: PRIMASK as before, no action cleared it on the way (a new ghost flag `HwModel::primask_cleared`, set by `Action::PrimaskClear` only), and the two boot settings of the Kernel level as before. The frame was added wherever PRIMASK was already framed; `endpoint_recv_core` needed its own prover instance (`spinoff_prover`) after it.
- *Outside the statement.* That SVCall is at that level in the silicon (the boot's register writes, the drivers' Lean model), and NMI and HardFault, which are Kernel handlers that do not return into the entry.
- Mutants: `svc-clears-primask-on-the-way`, `kernel-level-not-preempted-without-an-active-kernel-handler`, `kernel-level-not-preempted-by-a-level-above-it`.

**3. R42: the reading of the Kernel's result code (R42.3).** The trusted wrapper matched the literals 0, 1 and 3. `rsk::send_result` and `rsk::recv_answer` now read the code against the Kernel's own constants (`ENDPOINT_OK`, `ENDPOINT_FULL`, `ENDPOINT_PEER`), and two Kani_Harnesses check them for every 32-bit code (`the_send_result_is_the_kernels_answer_for_every_code`, `the_receive_answer_is_the_kernels_answer_for_every_code`; run alone, as the library's EasyDMA harness is: the job's Kani step runs the Kernel crates). The supervisor call and the delivery of a message only when the validation function accepts it stay trusted. The Partitions' object code changes with this (the two functions are inlined into `send_to` and `recv_from`); the rig's records predate it. The other half of the row's gap was already closed: the verification job of 2026-11-21 ran the App_Prover on the 23 HIL members with the peer-state contracts in the tree. Mutants: `kani-send-refusal-read-as-the-peer-report`, `kani-receive-peer-report-read-as-empty`.

**4. What else was unproven: done.**

- *The Release_Source event at the two entries that did not state it (R10.5, R40.3).* `intake_core` and `irq_entry_core` ensure `intake_entry`: the entry of an intake vector is `KernelOps::spec_intake_of` for the bound Task and the Partition the binding names, on the state the entry found (after the stack-violation report when the interrupted Job's stack pointer is out of its region), and what is returned is that outcome after `apply_core`, of which `applied_from` keeps the Jobs in progress, the levels and the safe-state flag. `endpoint_send_core` ensures `send_release`: the Release an accepted send causes is the receiving Task's event, attributed to the sending role's Partition; without the release flag the Kernel's state is unchanged. Mutants: `intake-entry-threshold-response-for-another-task`, `send-release-charged-to-the-receivers-partition`. The existing mutant `intake-leaves-basepri-of-the-state-before` was re-anchored to the edited text and is killed as before.
- *The fault path's order and fields (R14.3, R14.4, R14.6).* `Kernel::fault` ensures `fault_recorded`: the fault's event is pushed first, on the state as found, against the executing Job's Task and that Task's Partition, or (0xFF, 0xFF) for the Kernel; then the response is applied to that state. It is stated with a witness state and the existing contracts of `push_event` and `apply_response`, not by threading the log through every response. `KernelOps::spec_fault_of` carries it to `fault_core` (`fault_entry`, `fault_is_kernels`). Whether a later event of the response overwrites the fault's is the ring's (R14.7). Mutants: `kernel-fault-recorded-against-a-task`, `fault-entry-hardfault-attributed-to-the-partition`.
- *Only the time base feeds the watchdog (R9, ORQ-31).* `svc_core`, `fault_core`, `epilogue_core`, and `irq_entry_core` for a vector that is not a Kernel vector leave `watchdog_fed` as found. That the watchdog fires as loaded stays the hardware's. Mutant: `svc-feeds-the-watchdog`.
- *The Kernel's RAM between two entries (R16.1; the storage half of the run's premise, R39).* `hw_model::job_writes_only` states what a Job under a view can have changed (a word none of whose bytes the MPU lets it write is as it was); `lemma_kernel_ram_kept_between_entries` derives that no word of the Kernel's RAM changes; `entry::theorem_storage_kept_between_entries` that the Endpoint storage keeps every word, given that it lies in the Kernel's RAM, which the Config_Checker now checks (`storage_in_kernel_ram_ok`; the QEMU demo's Flight_Build: 566 values match; the measure and response HIL builds on disk: 610 and 491). The Kernel's own state (`K`) is abstract in these proofs, so the other half of `a1_run`'s premise, that the state an entry finds is the one the last entry left, is not derived by this. Mutants: `kernel-ram-kept-for-a-word-that-leaves-the-interval`, `storage-kept-without-being-in-the-kernels-ram`, `confcheck-storage-may-start-below-the-kernels-ram`.
- *The MPU register pair as a whole (R16).* Kani_Harness `mpu_register_pair_decodes_to_the_region`: `rbar_encode` and `rasr_encode` of any region within the size and alignment rules decode, by B3.5.8 and B3.5.9, to exactly that region, with no bit outside the fields. Bit-precise over every input, no loop. The decoder is this project's reading of the manual. Mutant: `kani-mpu-normal-memory-marked-shareable` (a bit the two field-level harnesses do not read).
- *The Link_Checker's walk (R37.1).* `rsk-link` opts into Verus. `cert::check` (proven) checks the walk's result on every run: `certified` holds of the nodes, and under it `theorem_walk_within_usage` and `theorem_one_panic_within_total` bound every chain of calls. `CallGraph::analyse` builds the nodes from `callees` and `is_panic_path`, apart from `visit`, and a refused certificate leaves no function a bound. Accepted on the QEMU demo's Flight_Build and on the measure and timing HIL builds on disk; test `the_usage_certificate_accepts_the_walk_and_refuses_an_altered_result`. Mutants: `stack-certificate-ignores-the-callers-frame`, `stack-certificate-accepts-an-unbounded-callee`, `stack-certificate-panic-charge-unchecked`.

**5. Looked at and left, with the reason.**

- *Entry boundaries in the Lean run (the `Quieted` premise of `KStep`).* Not started. The run's transitions are single calls of the logic's functions with the response-time bookkeeping attached; giving it entry boundaries restates `KStep` and reproves `property18_kernel` over it. It is the largest item of the list.
- *The nine trusted hardware leaves and `Cm4Hw::apply`.* They are the boundary to the registers: a proof of them needs a register-level model inside Verus, which is what the drivers' Lean model is for the functions it covers. Not reduced.
- *The stub model against the assembly.* Already checked line by line against the source of the seven stubs by `cargo xtask check` (R44.6). A check against each binary's disassembly was not added.
- *The EasyDMA library's supervisor-call doubles and two-read wait; the typed payload's encoding for every type.* The first are what a host harness can run; the second is one `unsafe` read or write of a `Plain` value, whose justification is Rust's memory model, checked per instantiation. Not provable as stated with the tools in the tree.
- *The validation functions rejecting what they should.* There is no statement of what should be rejected: it is the Application's choice.
- *A-P1, A-P4, A-P9, A-P10.* Liveness premises on the bodies, a property of the Profile, the parser's input side, and the tools. Unchanged.
- *The Generator's validation and emission, the Analyzer's validation, the Config_Checker's Task_Model reader, table builder, ELF lookup and checksum, the Profile enforcers' completeness.* Not attempted beyond item 1: each is a verification of a host tool's front end.
- *Static execution-time bounds for the Kernel's entries (R13).* Outside what a prover does.

Verus on this tree, each crate alone: `rsk-logic` 348 verified, `rsk-kernel` 363, `rsk-confcheck` 107, `rsk-link` 7, 0 errors. Kani: the three new harnesses, each run alone, successful. The register holds 305 proof mutations after this section; the 18 added and the one re-anchored were each run and killed. Not run after this section: the full verification job, the App_Prover (the library's contracts are unchanged, its two wrappers' bodies are not), the Lean build (no executable statement of the Kernel changed), and the rig.

### The fifth round: the run cut into the Kernel's entries, then three items of the fourth round's "left" list (2026-11-22)

No evidence row changed strength. No executable statement of the Kernel changed; the Analyzer, the Generator and the Config_Checker each gained a call of a proven function.

**1. The run cut into the Kernel's entries (R18, R29; Property 18).**

- *Before.* The run over the Kernel's functions (`KStep`, `Sched/KRun.lean`) had no entry boundaries. Its four transitions for a Budget event's own timer entry asked as a premise that the Kernel state they start from is quieted; `Sched/Quiet.lean` proved that every entry core of the linked Kernel returns a quieted state when given one, and the two were not joined.
- *What stood in the way.* A run whose Kernel state between two boundaries is literally what an entry core returned has to make every write the entry makes. Four of the Kernel's writes were no transition of the run: `Budget::charge` (a timer entry charges the Job on top before it serves a slot), `Kernel::ack_reinit` and `ack_stop_dma` (with which `apply_core` ends an entry after a Partition's restart or stop), and `Kernel::push_event` outside a response (a refused lock or unlock). Until now the run's Kernel state silently omitted them, which the old premise tolerated (it reads the scheduler state and the slots only).
- *Now.* `KStep` has four more transitions, `charged`, `acked_reinit`, `acked_stop`, `logged`, each carrying the extracted function's result, with no Kernel section and no change of an account. The five proofs by cases over the run (`ksim`, `kstep_nomrel`, `kstep_sprel`, `kstep_bo`, `kstep_di`) have the four cases. `Sched/Entries.lean`: `EStep` is one step of the run cut into entries (a transition that leaves the Kernel's state, or a whole entry ending in the Kernel state an entry core of the linked Kernel returned from the state the entry found, `EntryOf`); the entry's first transition (`EHead`) admits the four Budget-event transitions **without the premise**; `ereach_kreach` shows every state at a boundary is a state of the old run and the Kernel is quieted there, by induction over the entries from the boot; `property18_entries` is Property 18 over the cut run; `budget_first` shows the state in which a timer entry serves its first slot needs no premise.
- *Stated and not derived.* `EntryOf` says the linked Kernel's configuration is the run's before and after the entry (each method of `Bound` keeps it, `Sched/BoundAll.lean` `*_kp`; that is not composed over the cores), and `QBound` that the five interface functions read are `Bound`'s. That every execution of the Kernel is such a run (each entry's writes are the transitions between its boundaries) is read from the code function by function (`Sched/Timer.lean`, `Sched/Endpoint.lean`), not proven of a whole entry; this is the remaining distance to a run that is the code's by construction.
- Mutants: `lean-entry-prefix-may-rearm-the-slots`, `lean-entry-with-another-configuration-after`.

**2. The Analyzer's inputs and its Endpoint terms (R28, R29, R40.2).**

- `terms::check_inputs` (proven) answers `None` exactly when `inputs_admissible` holds: every site names a Task, one window per Task, every Budget and Priority positive, no deadline later than its period or MIT. These are the conditions Property 18 takes as hypotheses about the inputs (`hsok`, `hwl`, `hbud`, the positive half of `hprio`, `hDT`). `inputs::prepare` calls it before `analyse_all` and rejects otherwise, naming the Task or site. The link between the Verus predicate and the Lean hypotheses is by reading the two; the function is not in the Lean translation.
- R40.2: `endpoint_sites` builds the two sites of an Endpoint role (`endpoint_site`: Ceiling above every Priority, marked as an Endpoint site); `lemma_endpoint_site_blocks` proves such a site satisfies `blocks` for every Task above its holder; `endpoint_cost_add` proves the cost charged per role. `prepare` uses the two functions where it had its own arithmetic. Which roles a Task has is still resolved from the Task_Model by unproven code.
- Mutants: `analyzer-input-check-skips-the-deadlines`, `analyzer-endpoint-cost-counts-only-sends`, `analyzer-endpoint-site-below-the-kernel-level`.

**3. The Generator's acceptance (R22.1, PR-33, PR-14, R42.4).**

- *The rejection specification.* `accept_v::accepted` states the acceptance conditions that are conditions on numbers: per Task a Priority of 1 to 7, a Partition that exists, a period or MIT and a deadline of at least one tick with the deadline not later, a Budget of at least one cycle; at least one Partition; per Endpoint two to four roles bound to Tasks that exist, a capacity of at least one message, a rejection report accepted only on an edge from another Partition of lower Criticality_Level, and in the plain form a validation function on every such edge.
- `accept_v::accept` (proven) decides it. `generate` runs it on what `derive` produced and returns an error when it answers no, so no Generated_Config exists for a declaration outside `accepted`, whatever `validate` does. All 14 generated applications and the Generator's property tests over generated declarations pass with it.
- *Not covered.* Names and paths, the Target's peripherals and interrupts, the Protocol form's per-label validation functions, R42.1's bound on the payload type (the compiler's, on the emitted assertion), the emission itself (read back for the tables of numbers, R24), the summary (R24.3) and byte-identical output (R24.4), which stay tests.
- Mutants: `gen-acceptance-takes-a-report-on-any-edge`, `gen-acceptance-skips-the-deadline`.

**4. CRC-32 as a function (R12.3, R26.4).** `crc_round`, `crc_rounds`, `crc_byte` state the step; `image_v::crc32` (the Config_Checker's, moved inside `verus!`) is proven to compute `crc32_of` of the image's bytes, and the Kernel's `Crc32::byte` is proven to compute `crc_byte`. The three definitions are restated in each crate (the Config_Checker shares no code with the Kernel, R26.1); nothing checks mechanically that the two texts are the same. Not stated: the order of the fields the Kernel feeds its checksum as a function of the tables. The Config_Checker's ELF reader (`rsk-elf`) and Task_Model reader are still tested, not proven. Mutants: `confcheck-crc-with-another-polynomial`, `kernel-crc-with-seven-rounds`.

**5. The entry stubs of each binary (R44.6).** `cargo xtask stubs <binary>...` (`xtask/src/checks/stub_binary.rs`) disassembles each modelled stub of a linked binary with `llvm-objdump` and compares it with the stub's `naked_asm!` block, instruction for instruction: mnemonic, registers in order, immediates, and the symbol a call or a branch out of the stub names. It runs in the verification job's Link_Checker step on every Flight_Build binary (a difference fails the step; a missing disassembler is a warning). It is a check, not a proof: the encoding is the assembler's and the disassembler's, and the target of a branch to a local label is not compared. On the binaries in the tree it accepted the QEMU demo, the rig's flight and response builds and the verification job's build (seven stubs each: 40, 12, 24, 16, 11, 11 and 2 instructions) and refused a `defer-probe` witness build and one stale build from before the `cpsid f` fence, as it should. `rsk_timer_entry` is linked in none of them: nothing references it.

**Left.** A run that is the code's by construction (item 1's last point); the configuration kept across an entry core, composed; the ELF reader; the Generator's rules outside `accepted` and its emission; the nine trusted hardware functions; the remaining protocol assumptions.
